We have compiled lectures given in various cities and over several years, into chapters based on their subject :

1\. Srila Prabhupada's unique position.

2\. Disciplic succession.

3\. Relationships with a spiritual master.

4\. Devotional service: The most elevated activity.

5\. Chanting the holy name.

6\. The importance of preaching.

7\. Vaisnava qualities.

8\. Relationship between devotees.

9\. Spiritual life of grihasthas.

10\. Sadhana.

11\. Prajalpa.

12\. Attachments and material desires.

13\. The counselor system.

# Lectures from a Disciple. Vol. 1

## Introduction

When I spent the summer of 1987 in Santa Fe, New Mexico, I was introduced to the books of Srila Prabhupada by my roommate. The Bhaktivedanta purports did their damage to my false ego and simultaneously revealed Sri Krsna and Sri Vrndavana-dhama to me. This led me to the very distinct and profound realization that I had spent all of my 27 years living in a desert, looking for water with absolutely no success, and finally, finally somebody handed me a big glass of the most refreshing, delicious and satisfying water, which changed my life forever. This was the effect of hearing from Srila Prabhupada!

Now I have been reading *Lectures from a Disciple* again after a long time and I have to say that the effect is at least the same or even more profound and life-giving, because now I hear the combined voices of *two* instead of one pure devotee. Reading Srila Niranjana Swami’s lectures made me realize how much Maharaja has made his desires, words and actions one with those of his beloved and eternal spiritual master, Srila Prabhupada. Sometimes during my reading I thought I was reading Srila Niranjana Swami’s words, but actually I noticed later that it was an excerpt from a purport by Srila Prabhupada. Over the course of reading this book, I had this same realization again and again: How much the speaking and thinking of the disciple had become one with his spiritual master. As an added bonus, we also have the distinct pleasure of tasting the Bhaktivedanta purports through the mouth of a surrendered disciple who can reveal the import of Srila Prabhupada’s teachings according to our particular time, place and circumstance. In that sense, we are reminded of how the fruit has become sweeter after being tasted by the parrot or how the *Bhagavatam* has become sweeter after being tasted by Sukadeva Gosvami.

This is so instructive for all of us because it shows us how to remain steady on the path of *bhakti* and at the same time reach heights of Krsna consciousness that we could never imagine or seriously hope to achieve. Reading the realizations in this book is so profoundly life-giving that we feel encouraged to really give our very best and completely surrender our lives for the pleasure of Krsna, even though the odds are stacked against us in the material world. Here we can hear the voice and associate with a truly empowered disciple of Srila Prabhupada, whose words and actions lift us out of our habitual thinking, feeling and doing patterns and give us an awareness of a different reality, which is right in front of us, but previously could never be accessed. *Krsna-prema* is in the heart of every living entity and it is revealed through the process of hearing and chanting about Krsna, especially by the advanced devotees.

This book is truly a gold mine of *krsna-katha* at its finest, and whoever reads it will surely experience in his or her heart a surge of devotion to guru and Krsna. I would like to express my deepest gratitude to Srila Niranjana Swami for sustaining our lives by filling our hearts with these most exquisite realizations and pray that they may transform our lives forever. Thank you for showing us how to become a true disciple.

Yajna-purusa dasa

## Srila Prabhupada’s Unique Position

## What is the symptom of the connection to our sampradaya?

**Srila Prabhupada’s Vyasa-puja Alma-Ata, 1995**

Today is Srila Prabhupada’s Vyasa-puja, and, as is traditional, we will speak about Srila Prabhupada. I specifically want to speak about the importance of recognizing his unique position. Srila Prabhupada’s disciples and granddisciples throughout the world have written many offerings, and today devotees everywhere are gathering both to commemorate Srila Prabhupada’s appearance and to express their realization of his unique position. Certainly there are many people whose lives have been changed and hearts touched by the presence of this one pure devotee. Therefore, it is impossible to calculate just how many people are glorifying Srila Prabhupada on this auspicious day. The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* describes that the pure devotee is just like autumn rain, which simultaneously adds to the lakes and rivers and helps them to flow clearly. Similarly, one pure devotee can simultaneously purify many hearts and sprinkle the seeds of devotional service so that people’s spiritual life can begin.

The *Caitanya-caritamrta* states, *kali-kalera dharma*—*krsna-nama-sankirtana, krsna-sakti vina nahe tara pravartana.* The fundamental religious system in the age of Kali is the congregational chanting of the Lord’s holy name. Unless one has been invested with the potency given by Lord Krsna *(krsna-sakti)* to spread the holy name of the Lord, one is not empowered to do that service. Srila Prabhupada quoted this verse many times, emphasizing that preaching is not an ordinary, mundane affair. Krsna consciousness can only be distributed widely by one who is specifically empowered by Krsna. Srila Prabhupada also cited that if milk is touched by the lips of a serpent, it has a poisonous effect. Milk is pure, but if a serpent touches it, it becomes poisonous. Many persons may try to glorify Krsna or give others the holy name, but as it is also described in the *Padma Purana, sampradaya-vihina ye, mantras te nisphala matah,* unless one receives the mantra in proper disciplic succession, it will not provide the desired result. What is the desired result of chanting Hare Krsna? It is that one can obtain love of God. If we receive the Lord’s holy name, it is understood that we must receive it from someone empowered to give it.

To be empowered means to receive Krsna’s potency. Then, when people come in contact with us, it is as good as coming in contact with Krsna. This does not, of course, mean that those who are empowered, like Srila Prabhupada, are Krsna. (When some devotees mistakenly drew this conclusion early in the movement, Srila Prabhupada personally rejected them from his ISKCON society. )

To be invested with Krsna’s potency, a devotee must be highly qualified. Yesterday I was speaking to a devotee about the meaning of being connected to a *sampradaya.* The symptom of someone connected to a *sampradaya* is not that he or she has undergone the formality of initiation. Prabhupada explains in *Caitanya-caritamrta* that one may have received bona fide initiation but can still be found preaching Mayavada philosophy. One who is connected to a *sampradaya* will feel and express the purity of Krsna coming through.

A *sampradaya* is like copper wire. If one copper wire is connected to another, and especially if there is a strong connection between them, then the electricity will flow through both wires. If someone touches either wire, he will receive a shock. Wires are connected ultimately to the powerhouse. Although the powerhouse is far away, we can feel its potency by touching the copper wire. This is what it means to be connected or empowered.

What is the symptom of the connection to our *sampradaya?* How is Krsna coming through? We can tell if we are receiving Krsna if we are strictly following our spiritual master’s orders. If we strictly follow our spiritual master’s orders, we can overcome all the imperfections that come with having accepted a material body.

Those who have accepted a material body have the tendency to make mistakes, to be illusioned, to have imperfect senses, and to cheat. By following the spiritual master strictly, we can clear ourselves of these four imperfections. Then when we present transcendental knowledge, the knowledge will simply come through, as if we are copper wires connected to the powerhouse.

However, our following has to be strict. Otherwise, our connection with our link in the *sampradaya* will be loose. What is the value of a loose connection? Electricity does not flow through disconnected wires. Having accepted formal initiation is not in itself proof that the wires are connected.

If we want to see an example of a perfect connection, we can look at Srila Prabhupada’s example. Just look at the potency of Lord Krsna’s pure devotee. Srila Prabhupada strictly followed the order of Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati. That is his unique position among his Godbrothers. That is why we are glorifying Srila Prabhupada today on his Vyasa-puja day. We glorify him because he was such a strict follower of his spiritual master’s order and was so tightly connected to the powerhouse.

His faith in his guru was firm, which also points to Srila Prabhupada’s unique position among his Godbrothers. By his guru’s order, Prabhupada accepted the truth that Lord Caitanya was the most munificent Supreme Personality of Godhead. He also accepted Lord Caitanya’s mission:

*vande sri-krsna-caitanya-devam tam karunarnavam*  
*kalav apy ati-gudheyam bhaktir yena prakasita*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto Lord Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu. He is an ocean of transcendental mercy, and although the subject matter of** ***bhakti-yoga*** **is very confidential, He has nonetheless manifested it so nicely, even in this Age of Kali, the age of quarrel. ” (Cc.** ***Madhya*** [**22.1**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/22.1)**)**

Lord Caitanya is *patita-pavana,* the most merciful incarnation, and He appeared in order to deliver pure love of God while accepting the role of a devotee. Thus, He is called *channa-avatara,* the “covered incarnation. ” His mission was to teach others the process of devotional service and detachment from anything that did not foster Krsna consciousness. *Bhakti* is such a confidential subject matter, and we see in human society that few people understand it. *Bhakti* is performed without desire for personal return. Nondevotees think that anyone practicing unalloyed devotion to another, without desiring anything in return, is physically or psychologically deficient. Prabhupada showed us the opposite. He proved that *bhakti* is revealed by the devotee.

Srila Prabhupada himself understood the confidential details of *bhakti,* and he is glorious for revealing them and making them accessible to others. Actually, he made *bhakti* available to the whole world. Srila Prabhupada saw the spark of Krsna consciousness in the hearts of all living beings.

I once met one of Srila Prabhupada’s guests, who was a very well-known *yogi* at that time. He met with Srila Prabhupada in Bombay in 1976. During their discussion, this *yogi* told Prabhupada that *bhakti* is very, very difficult to understand, because people are so covered. He asked Prabhupada how he could claim to have made the message known to the general people. He said that he himself had no knowledge of *bhakti,* but his limited understanding made him realize how difficult it would be for most people to accept it.

Srila Prabhupada, of course, spoke strongly, asserting that Lord Caitanya had appeared in the world to spread the *bhakti* science everywhere. Lord Caitanya wanted to make love of Godhead easily accessible.

After their conversation, the *yogi* had no further contact with Srila Prabhupada. Then several years ago, I received a phone call at the temple from one of his followers. This *yogi* has a large *asrama* about one hundred miles from the Boston temple. The woman who called said that her guru had asked her to invite us to his *asrama.* This *yogi* wanted his disciples to experience a Hare Krsna *kirtana.* He had told his disciples that if they wanted to learn about *bhakti,* they should learn about it from the followers of A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami. We were told that we were being invited to chant, but that their guru did not want us to speak. We agreed to go.

We arrived at the *asrama* and were given a warm reception. The *yogi* had about three hundred followers at the *asrama.* His disciples invited us into their main reception hall. They asked us to wait while they finished their ceremonies and then they would invite us to chant. When we began chanting, everyone participated enthusiastically. A few minutes later, this *yogi* entered the room. He looked sullen, and sat on his *asana* to watch his followers chant. He, himself, did not take part. I had the impression that he was trying to show, by his non-participation, that he was not a *bhakti-yogi;* he was maintaining his position as his disciples’ guru, and the whole show was simply designed to help his disciples learn a little about chanting Hare Krsna.

The *kirtana* was in full swing. Then, one of the devotees approached the *yogi* as he sat on his seat, and extended him a hand. He asked, “Why don’t you come? ” The *yogi* maintained his serious demeanor, but the devotee continued to smile at him with his hand outstretched. Finally, the yogi couldn’t resist. He stood up and joined the *kirtana.* Within minutes he was spinning with the devotees. The *kirtana* continued for another thirty minutes, and the *yogi* danced the entire time.

After the *kirtana,* the *yogi* turned and asked us to speak. Because I knew about the discussion he had had with Prabhupada in 1976, I decided to address the doubts he had expressed at that time. I focused my talk on the glories of the holy name. I also spoke about how Srila Prabhupada had been empowered to deliver the holy name. Although the subject matter of *bhakti* is so confidential, Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu wanted to have it understood and practiced in every town and village. Srila Prabhupada was His representative who fulfilled that vision.

The fact that Srila Prabhupada was able to make the subject matter of *bhakti* so easily accessible to almost anyone is proof that he was invested with *krsna-sakti. Krsna-sakti vina nahe tara pravartana:* Unless one is invested with *krsna-sakti,* he cannot deliver the Lord’s name to others and convince them to dedicate their lives to the chanting.

Beyond the teaching of *bhakti* principles, of course, is the power to guide others to actually develop their love for God. What are the symptoms of love of God? One who actually has love is exclusive in his devotion and is not hindered by any obstacles that may appear on the path. There are no material impediments to the expression of pure love. A pure lover of God doesn’t say, “I’ll serve God as long as God gives me something in return. ” To think like that is to have a business mentality with the so-called beloved.

We must eventually realize that the ultimate goal of chanting is not to attain liberation or to become free from the reactions of sinful life. Although those are the byproducts of chanting, they are not the ultimate goal. If we become free from sinful reactions but do not become situated on the platform of love of God and engage in the Lord’s service, we will inevitably commit sinful acts again. The *sastra* states, “These sixteen syllables of the Hare Krsna *mahamantra* are the only way that one can counteract the sinful effects in this age of Kali. To become free from the contamination of this age, there is no alternative but to chant these names of the Lord. After looking at all the *Vedas,* we would not find a method of religion more sublime than the chanting of Hare Krsna. ”

Ultimately, this means that to actually counteract this age’s sinful effects—or our own—we have to become situated on the *bhakti* platform. *Bhakti* is the only process that can uproot the desires for sinful activity from the heart. *Bhakti* is so powerful because it is the most superior activity. No other process is complete until we take shelter at Krsna’s lotus feet.

*ye ’nye ’ravindaksa vimukta-maninas*  
*tvayy asta-bhavad avisuddha-buddhayah*  
*aruhya krcchrena param padam tatah*  
*patanty adho ’nadrta-yusmad-anghrayah*  
*(SB* [*10.2.32*](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/2.32)*)*

Lord Brahma explains that even if one performs severe austerities to achieve the highest position, his intelligence will remain impure and he will fall down from his liberated position if he has failed to take shelter at Krsna’s lotus feet. In order to be successful in the practice of chanting, we have to receive the holy name in disciplic succession from the Lord’s pure devotee.

After our lecture, the *yogi* began to speak. “You know, I met your Srila Prabhupada in 1976. I told him, ‘Swamiji, you try to convince people about this *bhakti,* but who will hear your message? ”’ He said he had doubted that Prabhupada would be able to deliver *bhakti.* He didn’t understand Prabhupada’s empowerment or the connection he had with the disciplic succession back to Caitanya Mahaprabhu. He did not seem to understand that the fullness of this connection gave him the power to deliver love of God to others. However, over the years, he had developed an appreciation for Srila Prabhupada and said, “Therefore, I told my followers that if they wanted to know about *bhakti,* they should learn it from the followers of A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada. ”

The *yogi* couldn’t convince others about *bhakti* because he was still attached to the sinful desires within his own heart. About a year after this meeting, it was discovered that he was having illicit relationships with some of the women in his *asrama,* and the *asrama* underwent major changes as a result of this. If we want to practice *bhakti,* we actually have to become free from the sinful desires in the heart by the power of the Lord’s holy name. Srila Prabhupada had that freedom. Therefore he was qualified to deliver others. Prabhupada demonstrated pure Krsna consciousness by his own behavior, character and example.

Therefore, we want to honor Srila Prabhupada’s unique position on this holy, auspicious Vyasa-puja day. He was unique in his ability to follow Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura’s order so completely and to fulfill his desire to preach in the Western world. The full scope of Srila Bhaktisiddhanta’s desire was revealed not by Srila Prabhupada but by Krsna through Srila Prabhupada. Krsna invested Srila Prabhupada with the potency of strong discipleship, and Krsna revealed to the world through Srila Prabhupada who the strict followers of Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati were. No one else among Srila Bhaktisiddhanta’s followers executed the order to distribute Krsna consciousness around the world. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta had so many disciples, but who among them were empowered like Prabhupada? Those of us who strictly follow Srila Prabhupada’s instructions will also be connected and have the qualification to become empowered.

Understanding all this is the meaning of Vyasa-puja. Vyasapuja means worshiping a person who is carrying Srila Vyasadeva’s potency. Srila Vyasadeva authored the Vedic literature. To be invested with His potency means to realize the conclusion of the *Vedas.* What is the conclusion of all Vedic wisdom? *Vedais ca sarvair aham eva vedyo, vedanta-krd veda-vid eva caham.* Krsna says, “By all the Vedas, I am to be known. Indeed, I am the compiler of Vedanta, and I am the knower of the Vedas. ” (Bg. 15. 15) Srila Prabhupada has both realized the Vedic conclusion and delivered it to the world. Prabhupada is not Krsna, but he has been invested with Krsna’s potency, and Krsna has agreed to appear in his words and instructions. Krsna has also agreed to appear in the holy name that Prabhupada has given to others. This is because Krsna is so attracted to Prabhupada’s devotion; He wants to go wherever Srila

Prabhupada speaks. This is how Srila Prabhupada has been able to give Krsna to the world.

Those who strictly follow Srila Prabhupada can be similarly empowered to give Krsna to the world. That will be the symptom of our connection with Srila Prabhupada. If we want to be connected to Krsna, we must become connected to Srila Prabhupada. We are *Prabhupadanugas*; our lives are meant to be dedicated to rendering service to Srila Prabhupada in the mission he himself established. There is nothing missing. Srila Prabhupada has given us everything, because he has given us Krsna. If we have Krsna, we have nothing else to desire. We should never think that Srila Prabhupada did not give us everything. If we accept the gift, we are never in danger of becoming unchaste. We will know well how Krsna has manifested Himself through Srila Prabhupada.

Therefore, on this blessed occasion let us glorify Srila Prabhupada’s unique position and offer him this humble presentation. We beg all of Srila Prabhupada’s followers to take shelter at his lotus feet and to achieve the perfection of life—going back home, back to Godhead after developing love for Krsna. Anyone who develops love for Krsna has already gone back to Godhead. Srila Prabhupada has invested us with the potential to develop that love. He has given this power freely by his mercy.

## Srila Prabhupada set an example for us to follow

**SB** [**4.1.1**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/1.1) **September 13, 1996**

Srila Prabhupada wanted his followers to know his purports. He wanted us to take Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s message and give it to others. We were not meant to simply become satisfied by our own spiritual advancement. Once, when one of Srila Prabhupada’s disciples left ISKCON to go to Radha-kunda to become a *babaji,* Prabhupada said that perhaps some day this disciple would make enough spiritual advancement to leave Radha-kunda, join our movement, and preach.

Srila Prabhupada made his expectations of his followers clear. He did not want us to simply think of our own advancement; rather, he gave us the means to make solid spiritual advancement by teaching us to preach Krsna consciousness to others. This also shows that while Srila Prabhupada was thinking of his followers, he was also thinking of all the nondevotees. He was thinking of both groups right up until his final breath. In the fourth canto, he called translating the *Bhagavatam*—one of his chief forms of preaching— a “herculean task. ” What was Srila Prabhupada doing during the days immediately before his departure from the planet? He was translating *Srimad-Bhagavatam* and giving us his Bhaktivedanta purports. At that time, he was translating the thirteenth chapter of the tenth canto. His purports reflect his concerns. He spoke about the Krsna consciousness movement—its importance, its mandate to spread Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s teachings, and how essential it was for ISKCON to remain pure. Thus Srila Prabhupada falls into the same category of someone who did not think of his own spiritual advancement but who willingly accepted the burden of preaching for the sake of the spiritual advancement of the whole world.

Obviously, such an exalted person will capture the Lord’s attention. Because Srila Prabhupada performed that herculean task, the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* remains a book that can introduce Krsna consciousness to the world. Prabhupada’s purports have made the *Bhagavatam* accessible to everyone. He was such a realized commentator that he was able to make the *Bhagavatam* accessible without watering down its message and without compromise. Prabhupada always emphasized that we should never compromise the teachings of Krsna consciousness while preaching. A preacher must be convinced of the *Vedas’* conclusive truth. Those who have complete, absolute faith in the conclusive truth of the *Vedas* and who preach that conclusive truth for the enlightenment of human society are actually recognized by the Supreme Lord. Srila Prabhupada didn’t have to compromise the teachings, and he continually emphasized that ISKCON would expand on the strength of its purity—the purity of our desire to give Krsna to others. This attitude is what expands Krsna consciousness, and what expanded Krsna consciousness during Srila Prabhupada’s manifest presence. Preaching is how ISKCON will expand even after his departure. Therefore, preaching is what Srila Prabhupada expected of his followers.

We were recently reading a Vyasa-puja offering in which the devotee quoted Prabhupada during a morning walk conversation. He said that the spiritual master is not actually satisfied by an offering of flowers or prayers, but rather when he sees his disciple going out to preach and defeat the materialistic mentality. “This is the real glorification of the spiritual master. Not simply flowers and words of prayer. ” The devotee went on to ask Prabhupada, “Please make me worthy to glorify you, because I know that this offering is not worthy. If I really want to glorify you, then I must be out preaching and showing everyone that this is what you gave to the world. ”

We say that such words are fit glorification of the spiritual master because they glorify his potency. We can simply appreciate the amazing work Srila Prabhupada undertook for our benefit and for the benefit of everyone in the world. If we want to continue to serve his mission, we must adopt a similar mood—that is, if we actually want to make advancement in Krsna consciousness. Only then will we do something worthy to please the spiritual master; not by thinking only of our own spiritual advancement.

“First of all, let us offer our respectful obeisances unto our spiritual master, Om Visnupada, Sri Srimad Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Gosvami Prabhupada, by whose order I am engaged in this herculean task of writing commentary on the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* as the Bhaktivedanta purports. ” *(SB* [4.1.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/1.1))

That was the example Prabhupada set for us to follow. He didn’t teach us to be interested in studying flowery words or to try to enjoy *krsna-lila* for ourselves. As true followers, we will be willing to stay in the material world birth after birth, serving his order to save others.

## “I am prepared to go to hell to spread this Krsna consciousness movement”

**SB** [**3.18.43**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/18.43) **St. Petersburg, September 16, 1996**

The *Caitanya-caritamrta* states that great Vaisnavas are powerful. Such Vaisnavas are concerned for the welfare of all living entities and willingly accept inconvenience and suffering while attempting to relieve the suffering of others. In his purport to *Caitanya-caritamrta* *(Madhya* [15.163](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/15.163)) Srila Prabhupada cites his Guru Maharaja’s comparison of Jesus Christ and Vasudeva Datta. Vasudeva Datta prayed, “Let me accept the sinful reactions of everyone so that they can be relieved from material suffering. ” Srila Bhaktisiddhanta explained that great personalities like Jesus Christ relieve their followers’ sinful reactions, but someone of Vasudeva Datta’s caliber was interested in the sinful reactions of everyone. Of course, Vasudeva Datta knew that to relieve sinful reactions was not sufficient; even if others become relieved of their reactions, without positive engagement in Krsna’s devotional service, they will simply accumulate more. Therefore, the powerful *acaryas* always think of how to engage others in devotional service, while simultaneously accepting the sinful reactions of those who follow their instructions.

Srila Prabhupada accepted his followers’ reactions. He was such a powerful *acarya* that he would not allow his followers to become satisfied in coming to devotional service simply to achieve liberation. Instead, he taught them to preach. Preaching is a liberated activity, and Srila Prabhupada wanted all of his followers to engage in preaching Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s mission. Prabhupada did not think only of his followers’ welfare, but pushed on the preaching mission in order to benefit everyone.

Srila Prabhupada stated, “I must travel all over the whole world, because I am always concerned with the welfare of my disciples—that they do not fall down. ” He didn’t want us to fall down, and he wanted us properly engaged in devotional service—especially in preaching, because to be engaged in preaching encompasses the whole world. To emphasize this point, he quoted Prahlada Maharaja’s statement in *Srimad-Bhagavatam* ([7.9.44](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/9.44)): “My dear Lord Nrsimhadeva, I see that there are many saintly persons indeed, but they are interested only in their own deliverance. Not caring for the big cities and towns, they go to the Himalayas or the forest to meditate with vows of silence *\[mauna-vrata\].* They are not interested in delivering others. As for me, however, I do not wish to be liberated alone, leaving aside all these poor fools and rascals. I know that without Krsna consciousness, without taking shelter of Your lotus feet, one cannot be happy. Therefore I wish to bring them back to take shelter at Your lotus feet. ”

Like Prahlada Maharaja and Vasudeva Datta, Prabhupada thought more of others’ welfare than of his own liberation. His only concern was to give others the opportunity to perform devotional service. Just as Prahlada Maharaja prayed to Lord Nrsimhadeva, “May all the entities throughout the whole universe become free from envy and become engaged in the service of the Lord, ” so Srila Prabhupada attempted to save the world.

As Prabhupada’s followers, we do not have to think about how to become free from suffering. Srila Prabhupada told us, “If you want Krsna to take care of you, then take care of others and Krsna will automatically take care of you. ” It is our tendency to worry about ourselves and how we will survive, but when we learn to think of the spiritual welfare of others, we will capture Krsna’s attention and Krsna will automatically take care of us. Krsna explains in *Bhagavad-gita* ([18.68](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/18.68)): “For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, pure devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end, he will come back to Me. ”

What does Krsna mean? He means that if we explain the confidential secret of devotional service to others, our own participation and success in devotional service is guaranteed. Will Krsna fail us? He gives His word. Therefore, this is the best guarantee we could possibly receive: “You will come back to Me. There is no servant in this world more dear to Me, and there will never be anyone more dear. ”

This is the power of preaching Krsna consciousness, and this instruction to preach is Srila Prabhupada’s gift to us.

By measuring his gift, we can understand that Srila Prabhupada is a glorious Vaisnava. How can anyone be more glorious than Srila Prabhupada? Prabhupada is unique, because he gave devotional service freely. He is the mercy manifestation of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu. He told us he was prepared to go to hell to preach. “I accept so many unqualified disciples, but what can I do? I am prepared to go to hell to spread this Krsna consciousness movement. ” Prabhupada meant what he said. He did not think about saving himself and returning to the spiritual world without us. He was prepared to go to hell to save us. Because of that consciousness, he captured Krsna’s attention.

We should be equally prepared to accept suffering for the Lord’s service. If Prabhupada was prepared to go to hell for us, we should at least be prepared to accept a little inconvenience. Perhaps our *prasadam* is late, or perhaps it is sometimes cold. Is that hell? Prabhupada was prepared to go to hell for us! We should at least repay him a little by being willing to accept a little inconvenience to preach Krsna consciousness, and to think of other people’s suffering as more significant than our own. Prabhupada certainly thought of them. We should follow his example. If we go out and preach, our lives will become successful and we will not care for liberation. Instead, we will pray to the lotus feet of our spiritual master, “Please engage me in this preaching mission birth after birth. ” Don’t listen to those who say that Srila Prabhupada didn’t give us the highest. Let them come before me and tell me that just one more time!

## “He reasons ill who says that Vaisnavas die. Vaisnavas die to live, and live spreading the holy name around. ”

**September 6, 1996**

When observing Srila Prabhupada’s disappearance day, we can recall the day we learned of his actual disappearance from the planet. The news was very painful for everyone who heard it. I still remember exactly where I was at the time. I still often pass that same phone booth when I am in the United States, and I still cannot pass it without feeling pain.

All the devotees who were with me at the time also experienced deep pain. Our best friend, our worshipful lord and master, the source of all our strength, ability, and everything that was giving us life had disappeared. Everything we knew came from his lotus mouth; every devotee had been nourished and sustained in spiritual life by Prabhupada’s presence. It was too painful to think of him as gone.

I was in charge of fifteen uninitiated devotees when Prabhupada left. All of these devotees, of course, considered Prabhupada their spiritual master. We were book distributors. All of us were meditating on his instructions and giving our lives to preaching. But they had not yet received their initiation. The news of Srila Prabhupada’s departure came as a double shock for them. What were they to do? Where would they go? What was their future? As their authority, it became my duty to solace them, but I could hardly respond. I, myself, was suffering too much. Upon hearing of Prabhupada’s departure, I cried continuously for three hours. I could not stop crying. All I could do was cry and chant *kirtana* with the devotees.

When I received the phone call, most of the devotees were sitting in the car. We had been about to change *sankirtana* spots. When they saw me coming away from the phone in tears, they couldn’t imagine what had happened. After all, I had only called the local temple to find out what time the program started. The temple devotee who had answered the phone immediately told me the news. I announced the disaster to the devotees waiting in the car, and all of us were crying. We somehow managed to arrive at the temple.

That’s when I realized the responsibility that Prabhupada had placed on my shoulders. I was considered a leader; I was going to have to speak. I knew I could do nothing else but repeat Prabhupada’s own words. What could I possibly say on my own? I realized that it was the duty of every disciple to accept the spiritual master’s instruction as his or her life and soul. We actually had to have faith in Prabhupada’s instructions.

As we discussed the meaning of Prabhupada’s disappearance, we spoke about faith in guru and in the Supreme Lord. We quoted the verse, *yasya deve para bhaktir, yatha deve tatha gurau:* To those who have implicit faith in guru and Krsna all the truths of the *Vedas* would be revealed.

I realized, through this experience of having to preach with Prabhupada’s own words in his absence, that Prabhupada’s words were the only words with meaning. His disciples really have nothing else. When Prabhupada disappeared, devotees were thrown into lamentation, but they realized that the preaching had to continue. That was all we had and all we knew. It felt like such a contradiction: The preaching had to continue, but the source of our preaching strength was no longer present. Therefore, we were forced to remember his orders and to take them into our hearts. In that way, we could draw strength to continue. It is the same now. We still have to draw Prabhupada’s order into our hearts to give us strength.

Prabhupada’s instructions will empower us. How can we abandon his instructions? His instructions are our life and the only thing that connects us to him. Prabhupada assures us that all that is required is for us to seek refuge at Krsna’s lotus feet and render service unto Him by chanting His glories and spreading the Krsna consciousness movement to every town and village. Then Krsna’s own promise that we will achieve Him will be fulfilled.

Krsna actually told Arjuna to boldly declare that His devotee would never be vanquished. Here, Prabhupada makes the same declaration. Krsna asked Arjuna to make the declaration, because Krsna knew that he would always choose to fulfill the words of His devotee even more than His own direct words. Therefore we can trust Arjuna’s declaration, and we can trust Srila Prabhupada’s statements too.

To follow the guru’s instructions is the meaning of the verse: “He reasons ill who says that Vaisnavas die. Vaisnavas die to live, and live spreading the holy name around. ” Vaisnavas live in sound and in their instructions.

Therefore, although we are observing Srila Prabhupada’s disappearance day, we are observing it in this spirit. We should continue to observe his disappearance day in the spirit of associating with a departed Vaisnava through his instructions. Then we will be able to think of him—and associate with him—every day. This is the meaning of both the appearance and disappearance anniversaries. Prabhupada told us that these two days were nondifferent and could be observed in the same way. We joyously celebrate the appearance of great Vaisnavas, and we celebrate their disappearance with joy as well. When Vaisnavas appear, we celebrate the mercy they have come to extend to us; when they disappear, we celebrate the fact that they have rejoined Krsna’s *nitya-lila.*

Srila Prabhupada’s purport in coming to this world was to preach Krsna consciousness. When Srila Prabhupada spoke about appearance and disappearance, he was actually speaking about his meditation on Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati’s order. When the disciple preaches, he always meditates on his guru’s order. When

Prabhupada spoke about chanting Krsna’s name and spreading the Krsna consciousness movement to every town and village, and when he spoke about our associating with him by *vani* and not by physical presence, what platform was he speaking from? Prabhupada spoke from his own vital realization. Srila Prabhupada lived in our presence based on his guru’s order; his spiritual master had already been gone from the world for over thirty years before Srila Prabhupada even came to the West. He understood the principle of following the guru’s *vani.* His guru’s instructions nourished him and fueled his meditation. He could not think of anything separate from his guru’s order. In that way, he lived with Srila Bhaktisiddhanta constantly.

We must now live in this world in the same way, following Srila Prabhupada’s example. That is the meaning of *acarya*—that he teaches both by words and example. The *acarya* knows that the material world is a dangerous place. Sticking to his instructions is the only way to live safely here.

We have already said that preaching was Prabhupada’s life. According to Srila Bhaktisiddhanta, only one who had life could preach. Preaching gives a devotee life. Preaching means speaking about Krsna. Speaking about Krsna brings a devotee solace. When Arjuna heard that Lord Krsna had departed, he immediately began to speak about Krsna. That speaking became his shelter. He remembered how he and Krsna had shared so many experiences. Then he remembered the instructions Krsna had left him, and the burning in his heart eased.

Today we should also speak about Krsna to ease our hearts. Prabhupada himself thought of Krsna at the end. In *Bhagavad-gita* ([8.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/8.5)) Krsna states, “And whoever, at the end of his life, quits his body, remembering Me alone, at once attains My nature. Of this there is no doubt. ” Srila Prabhupada showed us how to die, speaking about Krsna until the end. Prabhupada was not thinking about the comforts of home and family at the end. Krsna was his real shelter.

His ability to claim that shelter came from his willingness to absorb himself in his guru’s instructions.

When we remember the instructions and example of such great Vaisnavas, then we can learn the meaning of life. This is real life; there is no other life. The bellows in a blacksmith’s shop appear to breathe, but they are dead unless brought to life by the blacksmith. We are dead unless brought to life by Krsna consciousness, including hearing and chanting about Krsna and remembering Him and His pure devotees. Any other kind of “life” is simply a vain attempt to maintain a material body destined to die. If we die forgetting Krsna because we never learned to practice Krsna consciousness, we will simply return to this world again and again.

Let us observe Srila Prabhupada’s disappearance day by remembering his activities, pastimes, instructions and example in this world. Let us discuss these pastimes and read from *Prabhupada-lilamrta* and *Prabhupada Nectar.* Everywhere in his pastimes we will see the perfect example and hear the perfect instructions that will give us real cause for celebration. We will know that one of Lord Caitanya’s most confidential associates rejoined his Lord on this day nineteen years ago, leaving us here to smash our heads against the rock in separation.

## Krsna will manifest Himself to a person who is always absorbed in his duty to his spiritual master

**Prabhupada-lilamrta, Boston, November 1995**

If we study the events leading up to Prabhupada’s departure, we can see how he was absorbed in full Krsna consciousness. Not only that, but he never neglected his mission. Even at the time of his departure, he thought of the responsibilities he had for his institution and his disciples. Thus Srila Prabhupada demonstrated the ideal of how a devotee departs from the world in full meditation upon Krsna.

*anta-kale ca mam eva, smaran muktva kalevaram*  
*yah prayati sa mad-bhavam, yati nasty atra samsayah*

**“And whoever, at the end of his life, quits his body remembering Me alone at once attains My nature. Of this there is no doubt. ”** ***(Bg.*** [**8.5**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/8.5)**)**

This is an important instruction. Krsna does not say, “Whoever remembers Me after the time of death... ” In other words, remembrance of Krsna must manifest at the time of our departure. If we wish to remember Krsna at the end, we will have to be dedicated to Him while we live. Absorption in Krsna is not something that can wait until later. We can most easily become absorbed, as Prabhupada did, through preaching. Srila Prabhupada demonstrated how he could be absorbed in Krsna consciousness and be very much with his followers right up until the time of death. Prabhupada never left us to perform *nirjana bhajana.* Prabhupada’s *bhajana* was his intimate exchange with his disciples.

This is because Srila Prabhupada was always meditating on his Guru Maharaja’s order. He writes in the fourth canto, “Disciples should not try to look for Krsna behind some bush in Vrndavana. If one actually wants to see Him, the best way is to see Krsna personally in the instructions of the spiritual master. In this way, Krsna will manifest Himself to a person who is always absorbed in his duty to his spiritual master. ”

Srila Prabhupada never retreated or showed signs of disinterest in his disciples. Rather, he showed us how to be constantly engaged in Krsna consciousness by his own constant engagement. He then taught us how to give up our bodies in full Krsna consciousness by his own example. He taught us how to aspire. If ever we think it is time to retire, all we have to do is to look at Srila Prabhupada’s example to understand that there is no retirement in spiritual life.

Although some people speak of the renounced order of life almost as a retirement to perform *bhajana,* Srila Prabhupada never spoke of it in that way. Rather, *sannyasa* was for preaching. To preach, we have to give up all material considerations and attachments. Prabhupada retired by giving himself exclusively to Krsna’s service. We must always remember his example.

It is very important for Prabhupada’s followers to understand his mood and purpose in setting up ISKCON. His primary goal was to teach everyone who came in contact with ISKCON that the ultimate goal was to develop love of God. He wanted his followers to know how to develop their own love for the Lord. Therefore, Srila Prabhupada demonstrated the nature of his own love for Krsna by revealing the incredible compassion he felt for those who were suffering. The real manifestation of a pure Vaisnava is his or her selflessness.

We should never think that management couldn’t be done in love of God. Srila Prabhupada taught that love for Krsna should be extended to all humanity. To do that, he revealed his complete selflessness. If we are to follow him, we must do this too.

Only one who loves Krsna can be selfless. If we do not love Krsna, then it is a sign that there are subtle material motives in our hearts. We may want to prepare for our own futures, but Srila Prabhupada never prepared for his.

Prabhupada brought everyone together to teach them how to love Krsna. He taught us how to strive for the ultimate goal. If we want to develop love for Krsna, we have to have the same conviction Srila Prabhupada has—that by giving our lives to preaching, by thinking of the upliftment of others, we will attain devotional service. When Krsna sees the selfless behavior of His devotees, He becomes attracted and offers them the highest perfection.

Srila Prabhupada told us that our love for him would be shown by how we cooperated after he was gone. If we wish to cooperate, it is essential that we first understand Srila Prabhupada’s mood.

Then, we will be better able to devote ourselves to him and work with others who are similarly dedicated. His main objective was to make Krsna consciousness accessible to those who previously had no inclination for spiritual life, and to do so without discrimination.

## Anyone who comes in contact with Krsna consciousness through Prabhupada’s empowerment must especially honor his teachings as the founder-acarya

**Moscow, September 15, 1996**

Two days ago, we spoke about Srila Prabhupada’s unique position, comparing him to the great Vaisnava, Vasudeva Datta, who felt the burden of suffering humanity and was willing to accept all personal inconvenience in order to deliver the conditioned souls. We also spoke about Prabhupada’s ability to carry his own weight of compassion by instilling the preaching spirit in his followers’ hearts. We emphasized that this was different than other spiritual leaders who instead instill in their followers the desire for liberation. Srila Prabhupada taught us that real liberation means engaging in liberated activities in the material world, and that the most liberated activity is fully and whole-heartedly taking part in Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s *sankirtana* mission. By preaching, one can simultaneously achieve the platform of liberation and deliver fallen souls from material suffering. We were inspired by Srila Prabhupada’s purport in the first chapter of the fourth canto that addressed these points.

Yesterday, we spoke about submission. In order for a person to be an effective preacher, he must be submissive to his own guru. Otherwise, he will not be endowed with the necessary potency to spread the pure teachings of Krsna consciousness. We also noted how Srila Prabhupada explained how regrettable it is that many people try to preach the *Vedas* but are unable to spread their essential meaning purely.

Today I would like to continue that theme, but instead of reading from the *Bhagavatam* I would like to read a Vyasa-puja homage that appeared in this year’s Vyasa-puja book. I am convinced that by glorifying Srila Prabhupada and seeing his uniqueness, his followers for generations to come will be able to understand the importance of remaining chaste to his teachings. Certainly Srila Prabhupada is unique among spiritual masters. It’s nice to read Vyasa-puja homages, because on such auspicious days as the spiritual master’s appearance, the followers will praise his unique qualities and characteristics. Homages are a good place to look for inspiration.

Although there are many wonderful and noteworthy homages in this year’s book, I have selected one that I feel nicely illustrates the importance of being chaste to Srila Prabhupada’s teachings. His Holiness Sivarama Swami wrote this homage:

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

All glories to Your Divine Grace.

It is midday. I am standing on the roof overlooking Sridhama Mayapur. Your Samadhi stands prominent in the foreground. Below, final preparations are being made to accommodate devotees coming from around the globe. It will be the celebration of the Centennial of your appearance. There are others with me, but we do not converse. We are waiting. While meditating on a Centennial contribution of my own I open the book in my hand and read.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura is saying, “The teachings left by the founder-acarya of the *sampradayas* are to be especially honored.

Sri Ramanuja, Sri Madhvacarya, Sri Nimbarkacarya, and Sri Visnu Swami are the four original *acaryas* of the four Vaisnava lines. One should accept only their teaching and conclusions and not others. ”

You are the founder-acarya of ISKCON. That everyone knows today, although it was not so in the past. In 1970 devotees forgot your title on the new BBT building. You called it “a conspiracy. ”

There were other incidents, and each time you were emphatic that devotees not neglect your position.

Having heard of these events from a distance, I used to wonder, “Why was Srila Prabhupada so insistent? ” Even the breeze passing over pride never blew your way. You were the humble servant of your Guru Maharaja. I thought, “It is a minimization of Srila Prabhupada. ” But I did not delve further into it. It was not clear to me what the founder-acarya of a *sampradaya* is.

Here \[in this reference from Bhaktivinoda Thakura\] it states that the teachings of the founder-acarya are to be especially honored. “Which teachings are especially honored? After all, Srila Prabhupada is repeating the message of the disciplic succession. ”

But you were so emphatic. There must be more to it all. A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada was not just the spiritual master of ISKCON or another great devotee. He was the founder-acarya of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Yet “the teachings... are to be especially honored. ”

I look up past the Jalangi to the large banyan tree. Behind it, unseen from my vantage point, is Thakura Bhaktivinoda’s house. Sounds of the meeting are behind me.

Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu had come to distribute spontaneous devotional service without discrimination. While touring South India He instructed that this unique gift be distributed worldwide.

By His order, devotional service was forever connected with its distribution. *Sadhana* and *sadhya* were wed with *pracara.*

In other words, Lord Caitanya’s order is explained: Devotional service is completely connected with the distribution of devotional service.

This unique blend of practice and teaching was put into practice by Thakura Bhaktivinoda and reemphasized with greater strength by Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati. Prior to their time, Gaudiya Vaisnavism was generally dominated by *babajis,* their distinctive trait being solitary and secluded practice of *nama-bhajana.* As a consequence, the dissemination of Lord Caitanya’s mission in India ceased. Bhaktivinoda Thakura and Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura both desired to re-establish the course of the *sampradaya.*

They wanted active worldwide missionary activity. But at the disappearance of Sarasvati Thakura the great success of the Gaudiya Matha in serving these aims abruptly came to an end.

Yes, it was you, Srila Prabhupada, who gave new life to Gaudiya Vaisnavism by stressing preaching as well as practice. Although you did not introduce anything ‘new’, you redirected the disciplic succession, which in your own words had become *asara* (or useless). Though others may have not deviated in their practices, they had neglected preaching. In your eyes they had ‘no brain’ for spreading Krsna consciousness. Thus by your efforts you once again established the integrity of the message and practices of the *parampara.*

Bhaktivinoda Thakura further states that one should accept only the teachings and conclusions of the founder-acarya and not others.

Though the *siddhanta* may remain unchanged, there is methodology in its presentation. Preaching is not just a matter of transmitting knowledge. The founder-acarya is empowered by the Lord to distribute the message according to time, place, and circumstance.

I explained this point yesterday. Preaching is not just a matter of repeating Vedic statements. A preacher must also be empowered, because one who has been recognized and empowered to teach the conclusive truth of the *Vedas* is capable of distributing the message according to time, place, and circumstance without changing the truth. This is the core, something essential to understand.

When we actually understand the uniqueness of the founder*acarya's* teachings, we will come to appreciate what empowerment is.

You say, “An *acarya* should devise means by which people may somehow or other come to Krsna consciousness... In this connection, *desa, kala, patra* should be taken into consideration. ” Those not following the founder-acarya are not qualified to criticize such methods.

In other words, even though someone may understand *siddhanta,* he may not be recognized by Krsna to distribute that *siddhanta* according to time, place and circumstance. A founder-acarya is one who is obviously empowered to distribute knowledge according to time, place and circumstance. Therefore, unless we strictly follow the founder-acarya’s teachings, we are not qualified to criticize how he distributes Krsna consciousness, because we cannot see; we have no eyes to see. The only way we can actually have the eyes to see and understand the conclusion is to submit to the founder-acarya.

We saw during Srila Prabhupada’s manifest pastimes how people would criticize his methods of spreading Krsna consciousness. Srila Prabhupada acknowledged the critics in purports—both in the *Bhagavatam* and *Caitanya-caritamrta.* But he emphasized that he had received the order to preach in disciplic succession and that he was strictly following that order. As his followers, we must have complete faith that by distributing Krsna consciousness according to Prabhupada’s order, we are presenting the true conclusions of the disciplic succession perfectly. Those who are outside that realm, who cannot understand or see through Prabhupada’s empowerment, have no right to criticize him. It was Srila Prabhupada who founded ISKCON and led the devotees forward in spiritual life. It was Srila Prabhupada who was empowered to establish the Society for Krsna Consciousness all over the world. Anyone who comes in contact with Krsna consciousness through Prabhupada’s empowerment must especially honor his teachings as the founder-acarya.

By directing all members of ISKCON to preach, you set on course the direction of the *parampara.* This instruction is your hallmark, and bears its imprint on ISKCON society up to the present day. To facilitate and protect this preaching on a worldwide scale, you have harmoniously expressed the teaching of Lord Caitanya in a unique way.

The system of *siddha-pranali* of the *babajis* was replaced by *nama-sankirtana* in the mood of the third *sloka* of Sri *Siksastakam.*

*Siddha-pranali* is a system by which a *babaji* initiates a devotee into his or her *rasa* with Krsna, giving the disciple practices related to that *rasa,* particular meditations, etc. Srila Prabhupada always spoke furiously against such practices, calling them *apa-sampradaya.*

As a sannyasi you performed marriage ceremonies to inspire the youth of the West. You emphasized that *raganuga-bhakti* as taught by Caitanya Mahaprabhu should not be imitated at the stage of *Sraddha* but would transform through grace after one practices *vaidhi* to the stage of *asakti.*

In other words, this is the way to attract Krsna’s attention. If one wants to come to the highest stages of devotional service, he must attract Krsna’s attention and then also attract His mercy. But in order to attract Krsna’s attention, a *sadhaka’s* preaching activities must equal his efforts to achieve *raganuga-bhakti.* If he thinks that he can achieve *raganuga-bhakti* by abandoning his preaching, he will be mistaken. He will not be able to catch Krsna’s attention or mercy, and thus he won’t be able to advance to the elevated states he is trying to attain.

Other practices that were conducive to preaching you adopted in the mood of *yukta-vairagya.* Thus it was at your request that *sastra* be made available for public distribution. You gave an institutional identity to Lord Caitanya’s *sankirtana* and as a jetage *parivrajakacarya,* you traveled to the land of the cow-eaters.

It was Srila Prabhupada who gave an institutional identity to the dissemination of Lord Caitanya’s mercy. Maharaja explains that Prabhupada’s work closely followed the teachings of Bhaktivinoda Thakura, which were also emphasized by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati. In that sense, Prabhupada reestablished the disciplic succession, because the emphasis of the previous *acaryas* was a missionary one. This missionary mood, coming from Lord Caitanya, had been obscured over time. It was reawakened by Bhaktivinoda Thakura, Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, and carried to fruition around the world by our Srila Prabhupada. Sivarama Maharaja continues:

A gentle breeze blows, and with it the sound of Your amplified *kirtana.* I look up. The meeting is still under way.

“One should receive spiritual initiation in one these of four *sampradayas* and not others. A devotee must understand that the *adi-guru,* original spiritual master of a *sampradaya,* is the *siksaguru,* and only his teachings are to be accepted and not those of any other scholar or teacher”.

I received initiation but I did not understand the *adi-guru.* How it was that only your teachings are to be accepted, and not those of any other scholar or teacher. You are the *siksa-guru,* for you have established the tenor of the teaching and practice from all members of ISKCON. Being faithful to you means being faithful to your teachings. These teachings are the heritage of anyone who claims to be your true follower. Responsibility is with the devotee to ‘know’ you by knowing your teachings. To fail to do so is negligence in one’s role as a member and disciple. Such a person will be easily distracted by other ‘teachers’, not being fixed in your *siksa.*

(Reading again from Bhaktivinoda Thakura): “Only a saintly devotee who has understood the teachings of the *siksa-guru* is eligible to be a *diksa-guru* for others. ”

You instructed that to serve the mission, everyone should take responsibility for others and become guru. Although one may not be fully realized, by representing your teachings one would be empowered. Through the process of Krsna consciousness, one will no doubt achieve perfection. Bhaktivinoda Thakura emphasizes that all subsequent gurus in this line derive their authority by fidelity to the *adi-guru.* A person may be conversant in philosophy, but if he is not true to the teachings of the founder-acarya he has no right to act as guru. Both spiritual master and disciple have equal responsibility to be your true followers, for you remain the *siksa-guru* for all subsequent generations of devotees.

Some time has passed. It is almost lunchtime. We are called to return to the meeting. It was an instructive break. I understand something I had not understood before. Behavior is connected to understanding, and understanding to revelation. By your grace I understand a little more the meaning of the founder-acarya. By that understanding, may I be more faithful to you, your mission, and your teachings.

It is an insignificant offering for your Centennial. I offer my obeisances to those who are strict followers of your will. By their grace may I become more faithful to you still.

Your servant, Sivarama Swami

## Disciplic Succession

## How can we develop the discrimination to know who is following Krsna consciousness strictly?

**SB** [**4.2.21**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/2.21) **Moscow**

We should begin by understanding what “strict following” means. It means to follow in *parampara.* One who is actually following the disciplic succession strictly is authorized. Srila Prabhupada taught us that this is how we distinguish between who is genuine and who is bogus. Our disciplic succession begins with Lord Brahma, moves through Narada to Vyasadeva, on down through Lord Caitanya, and eventually to Srila Prabhupada himself. Prabhupada writes, “Therefore, one should not simply follow those who are attached to mental speculation. ” Mental speculators will mislead others and neither the speculators nor their followers will reach the desired goal.

Following the disciplic succession means we are authorized by Krsna to preach. If we want to claim that we are authorized devotees, but we are not recognized by Krsna, then where is our authorization? Srila Prabhupada claimed that his ISKCON society was authorized. He stated this many times in the *Caitanya-caritamrta.* His claim was based on the fact that ISKCON is recognized by Lord Caitanya because it is following Lord Caitanya’s order to preach in every town and village. We can see that we are following and being empowered by the Lord, because so many nondevotees are coming in contact with Krsna consciousness and giving their lives to its pursuit.

Therefore, by giving us this instruction, Srila Prabhupada helped his followers discriminate between what is authorized and what is not. Although we cannot always see clearly this point, Prabhupada added that the real test is not whether someone is a devotee, but whether he is able to bring others to Krsna consciousness. This is the true symptom of a devotee; by his association, he is able to make others devotees.

## If one is not submissive to the previous acaryas, his words are worth nothing

**SB** [**4.1.2**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/1.2) **Moscow, September 1996**

A devotee asked me recently why Srila Prabhupada spoke of himself as “we” instead of “I. ” Srila Prabhupada often said things like, “We are speaking from *Srimad-Bhagavatam, ”* or, “We have a plan to spread Krsna consciousness all over the world. ”

My understanding is that Srila Prabhupada used the word “we” instead of “I” because he was referring not only to himself but the whole disciplic succession. Srila Prabhupada never acted independently of the *parampara.* Therefore, he was as good as the disciplic succession because he was carrying its weight.

“We” means we do not do anything independently of *parampara.* One who has actually realized the conclusive truths of the *Vedas* makes his decisions in accordance with the desires of the previous *acaryas.* Therefore, Srila Prabhupada emphasized that he was not introducing anything new. He did not—and we do not— manufacture or concoct any new religion. Prabhupada said we are simply accepting the foodstuff remnants that have been chewed by the previous *acaryas.*

Srila Prabhupada presented this at the beginning of *Caitanyacaritamrta* when explaining the purpose behind his translation of that work. He emphasized that the *Caitanya-caritamrta* had been authorized by the disciplic succession. Therefore, Srila Prabhupada offers his obeisances to Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati because his purports are based on his authorized instruction.

Those who are submissive to the disciplic succession can understand the conclusive truths of the *Vedas* and thus can explain them to others clearly. The strength of the great Vaisnava *acaryas* is, of course, their realization of these conclusive Vedic truths, and because of their faith in and adherence to the previous *acaryas,* they are able to speak boldly without compromise. Srila Prabhupada did not compromise the teachings but presented the truth. He did not water the truth down in order to have the support of the Indian brahminical community, for example. Instead, he carried the truth stated by the disciplic succession. He had the strength of Srila Rupa Gosvami and was able to carry the weight of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s message because he knew how to hear submissively.

Srila Prabhupada always claimed that his main qualification as a disciple was his ability to hear his spiritual master with submission. Both hearing from an authorized source and hearing submissively are required qualities in an *acarya.* If we are not submissive, we will not be able to understand properly, what to speak of clearly representing the message. Someone may claim to represent the disciplic succession, but if he is not submissive to the previous *acaryas,* his words are worth nothing. Such a person may use the word “we, ” but he is acting as “I, ” the false ego.

Those who misrepresent the *parampara*—or themselves as belonging submissively to it—suffer a reaction. Those who serve the *parampara* are protected, because they are acting on the orders of their seniors. Such devotees can spread Lord Caitanya’s message to every town and village. Therefore, Srila Prabhupada is saying that in order to deliver the real Vedic message to others, we must be both submissive to the spiritual master and properly trained by him. Submission under a bona fide guru will allow us to develop brahminical qualities: “As bell metal is turned to gold when mixed with mercury in an alchemical process, so one who is properly trained and initiated by a bona fide spiritual master immediately becomes a *brahmana.” (Hari-bhakti-vilasa,* [*2.12*](https://vedarama.com/en/books/hbv/2.12)*)*

This is the strength that Srila Prabhupada wanted us to achieve: He wanted us to go from bell metal to gold. Thus he traveled around the world to places where there was no brahminical training or culture and, on the strength of the words of the *parampara,* he expertly mined the people and chose those who were attracted to become Vaisnavas. Then he taught them to be submissive to the disciplic succession and trained them to give Krsna consciousness to others. By this process, he brought forward his followers’ good qualities, all of which were lying dormant in their hearts—even in the hearts of those who had taken low births—and made them qualified *brahmanas.*

## What is the importance of the guidance of the disciplic succession for understanding the Krsna conscious truth?

**SB** [**1.5.10**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.10) **Kiev, March 10, 1991**

We have to accept the previous *acaryas*’ commentaries and should never think we can understand confidential literature without their commentaries. Actually, we should not be interested in reading confidential literature if it is presented without a Vaisnava *acarya’*s commentary. How else will we understand the text if we are not guided by the disciplic succession? This is true of the Six Gosvamis’ books. We may think it attractive to read their descriptions of Krsna’s humanlike and intimate pastimes, but those pastimes and Krsna Himself are so easily misunderstood that we run a risk if we read them without the help of the *acaryas.* Even Srila Prabhupada did not give his purports to the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* without first reading all the Vaisnava *acaryas*’ commentaries. When Srila Prabhupada wrote on the *Bhagavatam,* he kept a large book containing all the *acaryas*’ Sanskrit commentaries before him. He wove his own commentaries with theirs and presented them for the understanding of the Westerners. Prabhupada never presented himself as superior to the previous commentators, but humbly tried to serve their purpose.

When Vallabha Bhatta wrote his own commentary on the *Bhagavatam,* Lord Caitanya called him a prostitute. This was because he had not consulted—and in fact had rejected—Sridhara Svami’s commentary. Lord Caitanya was not interested in hearing a commentary that rejected Sridhara Svami’s contribution. He considered Vallabha Bhatta unfaithful. In time, Vallabha Bhatta came to realize his mistake, although he remained attached to his commentary. In the end, he formed his own disciplic succession and became the leader of the Vallabha *sampradaya.* He broke away from the Gaudiya line because he did not accept Lord Caitanya’s reprimand.

The important point for us to note is that all bona fide Vaisnava *acaryas* stress that we should take the guidance of the disciplic succession if we wish to understand the Krsna conscious truth.

## A mahajana can show us the correct path

**SB** [**4.11.8-10**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/11.8) **Kaunas, September 20, 1995**

**“My dear son, the killing of the sinless Yaksas which you have undertaken is not at all approved by authorities, and it does not befit our family, which is supposed to know the laws of religion and irreligion. My dear son, it has been proved that you are very much affectionate towards your brother and are greatly aggrieved at his being killed by the Yaksas, but just consider—For one Yaksa’s offense, you have killed many others, who are innocent. One should not accept the body as the self and thus, like the animals, kill the bodies of others. This is especially forbidden by saintly persons, who follow the path of devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. ” (SB** [**4.11.8-10**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/11.8)**)**

In these verses, Svayambhuva Manu is speaking to Dhruva Maharaja, his grandson. Svayambhuva Manu is one of the twelve authorities (*mahajanas*) in devotional service. Svayambhuva Manu was concerned that Dhruva Maharaja had been violent beyond what was necessary. Dhruva Maharaja was a king. It was his duty to punish the sinful. To do so he needed to become angry. Now he was angry. Without the force of anger, a king cannot properly protect the citizens, because violence and punishment require anger. That Dhruva Maharaja was angry at the Yaksas was appropriate. However, Svayambhuva Manu saw that Dhruva Maharaja’s anger had blazed out of control. Aside from avenging his brother’s death, he had begun to kill many innocent Yaksas. Why should he wipe out the whole race of Yaksas just because one of them killed his brother?

This was not religious behavior, and was therefore not expected of the king. Such behavior is not appropriate for one who knows religious law. Svayambhuva Manu is a *mahajana*; that is, he is capable of establishing true religion. *Dharmasya tattvam nihitam guhayam mahajano yena gatah sa pantha:* the solid truth of the religious principles is hidden in the hearts of the unadulterated, self-realized devotees. Therefore, we should accept whatever path the *mahajanas* advocate.

The *mahajanas* are seers of the truth; they have the power to discriminate between religion and irreligion. Sometimes, especially in Kali-yuga, it is seen that people confuse irreligion and religion. They have not been trained to discriminate between them. A *mahajana* can help us see our way. He has the proper vision given to him by Krsna and through his pure devotion. He is capable of seeing any discrepancy in the execution of our life’s duties and in showing us the correct path. The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* is full of examples of personalities who established truth and established the proper path.

Svayambhuva Manu appears in the *Bhagavatam* in more than one context. In another place, we see him instructing Priyavrata Maharaja to accept responsibility as universal emperor. Priyavrata Maharaja was his son, but he had accepted another *mahajana,* Narada Muni, as guru.

Priyavrata Maharaja had been inclined to follow Narada’s prescribed path of renunciation, and certainly that path of *nivrtti-marga* was auspicious. When Svayambhuva Manu asked Priyavrata to accept responsibility as emperor, Priyavrata refused. Yet the Lord had a higher plan and that had to be considered. To encourage Priyavrata to accept Svayambhuva Manu’s will, Lord Brahma descended on his swan-carrier. Now there were three *mahajanas* present, and all of them understood the conclusions of devotional service.

Lord Brahma was able to instruct Priyavrata because he was Narada Muni’s spiritual master and the father of Svayambhuva Manu. He told Priyavrata that what Svayambhuva Manu was requesting of him had been ordained by the Lord. He added that this assignment would not last forever, but that when it was complete, Maharaja Priyavrata could then follow the path presented to him by Narada Muni. We can understand from all this that Lord Brahma appeared to establish the proper path for Priyavrata Maharaja. Priyavrata Maharaja accepted it. He followed that path rigidly throughout his life, and, after having properly executed his prescribed duties, achieved perfection. These duties were the means given to him by Krsna Himself to both purify himself and achieve the highest goal of life.

The *mahajanas* know the true path of religion, and whenever they instruct us, we can trust that their instructions will never be against religious principles. Although it appeared that Lord Brahma was instructing Priyavrata to become entangled in social and political affairs, he was presenting the Lord’s will. We might never imagine that the Lord would will such a thing for someone, since it appears to be so entangling and antithetical to the renounced path of devotion. Therefore we say that the truth of religious principles is hidden in the heart of unadulterated devotees. We should follow whatever path such *mahajanas* advocate.

Similarly, Svayambhuva Manu established the proper religious path for Dhruva Maharaja to follow: “It is not befitting one who is in our family to act contrary to the principles of religion. Therefore you should not indulge in killing so many innocent Yaksas. ” He assured Dhruva that he had already fulfilled his purpose in avenging the death of his brother. Now he should stop. Dhruva Maharaja was able to accept Svayambhuva Manu’s good instruction.

When a saintly person who has compassion for living beings instructs others, an amazing effect takes place in the others’ lives. Mrgari the hunter is an example of the kind of transformation that can come under the instruction of a *mahajana.* From birth Mrgari was trained to half-kill animals and to enjoy watching them die slowly. When Narada asked him why he only half-killed the animals, he admitted he found their suffering enjoyable. Narada Muni could not tolerate seeing the animals’ suffering, but even more than that, Narada could not tolerate thinking about Mrgari’s future pain. That is a *sadhus* nature. *Sadhus* are friendly toward all living entities. They do not want to see anyone suffering. Therefore, Narada convinced Mrgari that his behavior was going to cause him to suffer in the future. This was his compassion toward the hunter. In the end, under Narada’s instruction, Mrgari was able to renounce his sinful activities and to become a Vaisnava.

We should trust the *mahajana’s* motives. They are equal and friendly toward all. In this age, it is natural not to trust others, and that can block us from being able to hear. In the tenth canto, Pariksit Maharaja states that the Lord’s pastimes are the right medicine for those who are undergoing the repetition of birth and death. Those who fail to hear those pastimes, he says, are butchers or killers of the self.

I recently had an experience with a few devotees in Southern Russia. I was quite sick, and these devotees wanted to take me to a place where I could recover. They offered to take me to the top of a mountain where there was a curative mineral spring many hours outside the city. Unfortunately, the devotees didn’t actually know what was on top of the mountain. Although they wanted me to stay there for a few days, we only learned when we arrived that the mountaintop had no facilities. We arrived late at night because of the difficulties we experienced on the road. It was a six-hour drive up the mountain road. Partway up, our vehicle’s clutch burnt out and we were stranded. Somehow a second vehicle picked us up, but it got stuck. Then we had to climb into the back of a lorry. We managed to drive for another hour and a half with all the cargo in the back before we got stuck in the river. Then we rode in a jeep, but that too got stuck! Finally, after riding in all these vehicles and still not having arrived, we walked the rest of the way.

We arrived to find no facilities. The devotees were embarrassed, because although they had brought me all that way and through all those austerities to recover, it looked like I would become worse, since the temperature had dropped below zero and we had nowhere to stay. They looked desperately for a shelter. Finally, they convinced a shepherd to allow us to stay in his shack. I told the devotees, “This makes a shack on the streets of Calcutta look like a five-star hotel. ” But it was all we had. The devotees cleaned it as best they could, and we all settled in to tolerate the living conditions. When we woke the following morning, we saw hanging everywhere, outside the shack, sheepskins. We began to talk about what shepherds do to their sheep. The devotees decided to get rid of the skins, so they picked them up on the ends of sticks and tried to hide them in the back. They wanted to convince me that I should stay despite the horrible place so that I could recover my health.

I decided to tolerate the situation since the devotees so much wanted me to be there. The following morning was a Saturday, and a large group of people—friends of the shepherd’s son—came to stay in the shack. This was a very small shack, and in it there were many pots. I told the devotees, “These people look pretty low-class. They have probably not come to cleanse their bodies with the water from the springs. They have probably come here to kill sheep and to drink. ” The devotee replied, “I hadn’t thought of that. ”

“Well, you had better think of it now, because you see that pot right in front of the shack near where you have all been hanging your clothes? I wonder what they are going to do with that pot? ” Two minutes later, the shack owner came up to the devotees and told them to move their clothes. He said they were going to use the pot. I told the devotees it was time to go. “I don’t care how much you want me to get better. I know what’s going into that pot and I don’t want to be here to see it. ”

The devotees gathered our things quickly and just as we were getting ready to leave, I watched three men grab a sheep and drive it up the hill. I turned to a devotee and said, “They’re not taking that sheep for a bath in that pot. ” It was amazing to watch, because it was obvious that the sheep was afraid. They threw the sheep to the ground and cut its throat. Just before the killing, the devotees tried to preach. They later told me how they tried to convince these men that what they were doing was wrong, but they couldn’t hear.

That was an illustration of Pariksit Maharaja’s point: only one who is a butcher or a killer of the self cannot hear good instruction. Those who kill animals, who pay for animals to be killed, or who indulge in eating animal flesh cannot hear spiritual truth. They can barely understand why violence is a crime.
