A saintly person cannot tolerate the thought of a living entity being killed unnecessarily.

Whenever we present the arguments against animal-killing to butchers, they unfailingly respond, “What are we supposed to eat? ” They cannot imagine a vegetarian diet. Most people in this world have come to justify animal-killing with religious arguments. Even Cardinal Danielou, with whom Prabhupada conversed, thought it more than all right to kill animals. Prabhupada challenged him, “How can you kill your mother? ” presenting that the cow is one of the seven mothers because she supplies us with milk. Cardinal Danielou had a respectable leadership position in the Catholic Church, and people would assume he was a deeply religious man, but he used many statements from the Bible to justify his behavior.

Such persons are not *mahajanas.* They do not understand the path of religion. They are too addicted to sense gratification to understand God clearly or even to hear about Him properly. Even when given good instruction, they continue to justify their sinful activities, quoting scripture or standing on religious principles. It says in the Bible that man has dominion over animals, but was Cardinal Danielou’s interpretation that the word “dominion” means “permission to kill” accurate? Prabhupada asked, “Man has dominion over his children. Is it all right to kill our children? ”

Some would answer, “Animals don’t have a soul. ” We can continue this argument—how consciousness is a symptom of the soul, and that the animals have consciousness—but such persons cannot hear. Srila Prabhupada even argued, “You say that God did not know how to use words properly? If He had meant when He said ‘Thou shalt not kill’ that we should not kill human beings, He would have said, ‘Thou shalt not murder. ’ But that’s not what He said.

A truly religious person will never justify his actions based on his desire for sense gratification. Srila Prabhupada explains in a purport in this fourth canto section, “Why should a person, who is already engaged in the service of the Lord, engage in personal sense gratification? ” He is, of course, speaking of Dhruva Maharaja. Dhruva Maharaja was already engaged in the Lord’s service. Why should he now reduce his devotional service in order to satisfy his senses and unjustifiably kill others? This is Svayambhuva Manu’s point. Dhruva Maharaja thought his actions were justified. Someone from this race had killed his brother. Svayambhuva Manu appeared to correct the situation.

Srila Prabhupada tells a story to better illustrate Svayambhuva Manu’s words. He speaks of a serpent who had become a disciple of Narada Muni and been ordered not to bite. But every time someone saw the serpent, they mistreated it, as is the way with people. They threw rocks at it or beat it with sticks. The serpent refused to bite them, because it always remembered Narada Muni’s instructions.

Still, life became difficult for the serpent. People were no longer afraid of it. The serpent returned to Narada Muni and said, “You have instructed me not to bite, and therefore I have stopped all my biting. Now that everyone sees I don’t bite, they mistreat me. ” Narada said, “When they mistreat you, show your hood. Act like you are going to bite, but don’t bite. ”

Svayambhuva Manu told Dhruva that he had already expanded his hood and shown his prowess. Dhruva Maharaj a had already killed or maimed thousands of Yaksas. When they covered Dhruva by their illusory power, he became resolved to kill them all. Therefore, Svayambhuva Manu appeared and stopped him.

A saintly person doesn’t want to see anyone suffer unnecessarily. Instead, he is friendly toward all living beings. *Sadhus* are tolerant, merciful, and equal to all. Why? Because a *sadhu* sees things as they are; a *sadhu* has perfect vision. That is, he sees everything in relation to Krsna. We should trust such persons and follow their instructions.

## Relationships with a Spiritual Master

## How do we actually associate with Krsna?

**SB** [**4.2.21**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/2.21) **Moscow, October 27, 1996**

We should always strive to associate with Krsna through the spiritual master’s instructions. This is how we try to see Krsna in everything. Prabhupada showed us this by his own example. Why do we appreciate Srila Prabhupada so much? It is not because he was a resident of the spiritual world or that he was always talking with Krsna or that he was talking intimately with the *gopis,* because how would we presume to have the eyes to see such things in the first place? We cannot project our lack of vision on the spiritual world. We don’t need to. Srila Prabhupada showed us his greatest glory, something we *could* see. Anyone with eyes can see that Srila Prabhupada was fully dedicated to following the order of his spiritual master. Prabhupada did not show us the example of looking behind every bush in Vrndavana for Krsna; he taught us to see Krsna in the orders of the guru.

We may not always be capable of distinguishing truth from imagination when we hear others say things like, “Oh, I see Krsna! I know Krsna! Every night, Krsna comes to my bedroom and talks to me before I go to sleep. ” We may think such persons are advanced. The real test of advancement however, is what Prabhupada taught by his own example: Right up to his last breath, he remained a servant of Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura. Until his last breath he meditated on his Guru Maharaja’s mission to give Krsna consciousness to the world. That is the example we should follow.

## How should we serve the spiritual master?

We should serve the guru by following his instructions. Srila Prabhupada gives the example that if the spiritual master asks for a glass of water and we think, “Let me bring him a glass of milk, ” then we aren’t serving properly. A disciple may think he is serving by bringing the milk, because milk is better than water, but if we actually want to render service to the spiritual master, we will simply do as he asks. That’s the definition of service. By rendering service in such a way, we come closer to understanding the guru’s heart and thus come closer to the spiritual master.

We must start with obedience, because without obedience it is not possible to understand the guru’s instructions. The spiritual master expects the disciple to maintain an attitude of obedience. He doesn’t want the disciple to concoct his own set of instructions to follow. The spiritual master has already given the instructions. We may or may not understand the import of the instructions, but if we carry them out in pure faith, we can realize everything in Krsna consciousness.

Srila Prabhupada gives another example. He says that the spiritual master is like the captain of a boat. We may be the ones navigating through the water, but a navigator is situated below deck. We cannot physically see the best way to go. The captain, however, is situated on the top deck. He can tell when to turn left and when to go straight. We may not realize that the captain is taking us through all these turns in order to avoid colliding with an iceberg, so if we ask, “What do you mean turn left? You just told me to turn right! Make up your mind! ” Then where is the obedience? And where will be the success in avoiding the collision? ” We have to trust that the captain can see more than we can see. He *knows* the best way to bring us back to Krsna.

Another example: The spiritual master tells us to pick up a rope. Then he says, “Drop it, it’s a snake! ” Obedience means we pick up the rope when it’s a rope and drop it when we’re told it’s a snake. When we have that level of obedience, we can begin to advance in spiritual life. We exhibit our obedience by following the spiritual master’s instructions and rendering service. All other realizations come from that.

## How does the disciple become disciplined?

**SB** [**1.8.29**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/8.29) **St. Petersburg**

The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* explains that Krsna, as *caitya-guru*, helps us surrender to Him. *Caitya-guru* means the Lord in the heart. However, before we are able to perceive the Lord’s dictations, we must purify our existence.

To purify our existence, we must accept some discipline by which our hearts can become cleansed. Lord Brahma is an example of this. He was the first created being within the universe, and he had no one he could accept as spiritual master except Krsna. Lord Brahma wanted to understand his origin. Therefore, he voluntarily accepted the discipline the Lord Himself prescribed. When Lord Brahma entered the Garbhodaka Ocean through the stem of the lotus, he heard the syllables *“ta-pa”.* Brahma accepted that these syllables were of divine origin, so he agreed to follow them and underwent great austerities for one thousand celestial years by sitting on his lotus seat in meditation. The result of Brahma’s obedient austerity was that the Lord personally revealed Himself to him and showed him the expanse of the Vaikuntha planets.

Because Brahma accepted his guru’s discipline, everything was revealed to him: *tene brahma hrda ya adi-kavaye.* The first verse of the *Bhagavatam* states that all Vedic wisdom was revealed to Brahma within his heart. All that wisdom is contained in the four nutshell *slokas* in the second canto. The rest of the *Bhagavatam* is simply an expansion on these four essential verses.

The point to note, however, is that although Lord Brahma agreed to become disciplined, he did not perform whimsical austerities. The austerities he chose to practice were based upon a superior dictation. For ordinary conditioned souls, we voluntarily accept discipline by accepting the guru’s dictation. The spiritual master is the external manifestation of Supersoul, and he instructs us consistently with the dictations of *caitya-guru.* By accepting the guru’s discipline and purifying our existence, we can learn to contact the Lord in the heart.

We should not think that the dictations of Supersoul are in any way different from the dictations of the spiritual master. To think that is foolish. If we find that the two sets of instructions are different, we should know that we are not hearing the Lord in the heart but rather only our imagination shaped by our material attachments.

If we form our lives around the guru’s instructions, we will gradually come to hear the Lord in the heart and follow Him perfectly. Krsna states in *Bhagavad-gita* (6. 7) that “for one who has conquered the mind, the Supersoul is already reached, for he has attained tranquility. ” We conquer the mind not by whimsical activity but by engaging in the Lord’s service under our guru’s direction.

## Why shouldn’t we see the spiritual master as an ordinary person?

**Initiation lecture Novosibirsk, October 13, 1994**

We should never consider the spiritual master to be an ordinary person. He is not simply an advisor, equal in power with our own mind’s desires. We should not think of the spiritual master as someone whose instructions we can accept or reject at will. If we think like this, we have not actually accepted a spiritual master. Srila Prabhupada says that instead we have accepted a “pet guru”. Keeping the guru as a pet is not recommended. A guru is not someone to be taken around and displayed. We are not meant to use the spiritual master to serve our own purposes. We should be careful, therefore, when we accept initiation, to understand the spiritual master properly. He is Krsna’s representative, and his instructions come from Krsna Himself.

When we receive initiation, we take vows before guru and Krsna. When the spiritual master asks the disciple to recite his vows, he is not simply testing to see whether the disciple remembers the four regulative principles. We recite these vows so that we can be sure to understand what it is that we are committing ourselves to before guru, Krsna, the Vaisnavas, and the sacrificial fire.

## Is the relationship between guru and disciple eternal?

**Ista-gosthi Kishinev, March 20, 1991**

The relationship between guru and disciple is eternal. Srila Prabhupada writes that the eternal relationship begins from the moment the disciple hears from his spiritual master. It continues in the spiritual world.

Srila Prabhupada did not speak much of the form the relationship would have in the spiritual world, however. This knowledge is not essential to a strong guru-disciple relationship in this world. For those in the material world, we can maintain our ongoing relationship with guru by remembering his instructions. Srila Prabhupada always emphasized this point: His disciples would always live with him by remembering his instructions even after he was gone.

Srila Prabhupada gave us a mission to fulfill in this world. That mission is simply to execute his order. Therefore, it is a far more important meditation for his disciples to think of his order than to imagine their lives with him in the spiritual world. When we are ready, the spiritual master will reveal more to us about our mutual identities in the spiritual world. We qualify ourselves for that revelation by following his orders in the here and now.

Srila Prabhupada never spoke about his relationship with Srila Bhaktisiddhanta in the spiritual world, nor did he speak to his disciples about our relationship with him in Goloka. I have been asked what Prabhupada said on this matter many times, and therefore I have done much research on the topic. The truth is that I cannot find anything in any of his letters, lectures, or books. He didn’t speak about it. We should understand that he did not speak about such things for a reason; Prabhupada wanted us to live with him by meditating on the instructions he gave us in this world.

There is a saying that Vaisnavas never die, but live in sound. “The Vaisnavas die to live and, living, try to spread the holy name around. ” Srila Prabhupada remains in the hearts of his followers when they remember his instructions and carry them. As Prabhupada said, he lives forever in his books and his sincere follower lives with him.

## How can a disciple feel satisfied?

**SB** [**4.23.12**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/23.12) **Kishinev, June 1996**

*yasya prasadad bhagavat-prasado*  
*yasyaprasadan na gatih kuto ’pi*

**“By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Krsna. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. ” (*****Guruvastaka*****, 8)**

If we actually want to become satisfied, we have to think about how to please Krsna by serving the spiritual master’s instructions. That is the devotee’s business in this world. This is what Srila Prabhupada taught. He never thought of his own liberation. He thought only of giving every breath of his life to executing his Guru Maharaja’s order. Thus Srila Prabhupada set a perfect example. He never thought of retirement. He preached until his last breath. He taught us by his example how to attain the perfection of life.

If we want to please the guru, we have to follow Prabhupada’s example. Srila Prabhupada was so successful in his preaching mission, because he didn’t think of his own liberation; he thought only of his Guru Maharaja’s mission to spread Krsna consciousness all over the world. By carrying out his guru’s order, he automatically became compassionate toward the conditioned souls. He glorified the Lord constantly.

Those who are Prabhupadanugas have this wonderful example to follow and will never think there is a perfection beyond what Srila Prabhupada taught us. This will allow us to become satisfied.

## What is the real criterion for making proper decisions in our conditioned position?

**Unknown date, verse, town, country**

Certainly there must be a criterion against which we can judge right and wrong. We have been given intelligence in order to discriminate between right and wrong. Our intelligence should be superior to our mind. The mind provides ideas for sense gratification, especially when it is not controlled. Intelligence should discriminate between the mind’s dictation and what we are supposed to do.

For someone who doesn’t have strong intelligence, the mind forever seems victorious. That’s why Krsna says that for those who control the mind, the mind becomes their best friend, but for one who fails to control it, the mind remains their worst enemy. We call the mind the “worst enemy” because the mind defeats us and we are forced to follow its dictations and suffer the consequences. We must strengthen our intelligence in order to better discriminate between that which should be done and that which should not.

The criteria upon which we base our discrimination are the standards set by guru, *sadhu,* and *sastra.* We are conditioned and therefore not yet able to hear from the Supersoul directly. We must learn to use our intelligence to discriminate on the basis of what we have heard from these three sources. We must come to understand the spiritual master’s instructions along with the instructions of *sastra* and *sadhus.* We should not place our faith in the mind but in these three authorities.

We may think we are using our minds when we discriminate, but we are actually using the intelligence, which is superior to the mind. We should always follow superior dictation if we want to attain spiritual life. The mind will only encourage us to enjoy our senses. The mind’s business is to present us with ideas for sense gratification.

When Arjuna was bewildered, he did not simply meditate on his problems, but instead presented them before his guru. That’s part of what it means to use the intelligence to discriminate. Perhaps we don’t know who to ask for help. Srila Prabhupada addresses that problem in a *Bhagavad-gita* purport. He writes that someone may say, “Arjuna was able to ask Krsna directly, as He was right there, so there was no problem. But we don’t have Krsna here! ” Prabhupada writes that we can ask our spiritual master.

When we approach the spiritual master, however, we must be careful not to do so with an agenda. We should not be seeking a particular answer, and therefore ask our question in such a way as to bring out of the guru what we want him to say. The spiritual master actually knows what we need to hear, and often he will not tell us what we *want* to hear. In such cases, the mind will protest the spiritual master’s words. The mind cries, “Don’t listen! The guru didn’t give you the correct answer. Maybe you need to ask your question a little differently in order to get the right answer. Just listen to me. I know how to ask the question better than you. Try again! ” But better we approach the spiritual master with submission, with the readiness to accept his answer to our dilemma as being suitable for us.

To be able to submit properly, it helps to understand both how the mind works and how superior dictation can help us. Superior dictation will cause the mind to protest, but if we pursue the order with faith in the superior authority, we will be able to defeat the mind. Such faith and obedience to the guru’s order will purify our intelligence further.

## What does it mean to become submissive to the spiritual master?

**SB** [**3.32.42**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/32.42) **Minsk, June 30, 1998**

Srila Prabhupada writes, “Instruction should be given to the faithful devotee who is respectful to the spiritual master, nonenvious, friendly to all kinds of living entities and eager to render service with faith and sincerity”. Srila Prabhupada adds that a *bhakta* is one who does not hesitate to accept the reformatory process. Devotees are also, as Prabhupada states, friendly to all kinds of living entities and *guru-susrusaya,* faithful servants of the spiritual master.

Srila Prabhupada tells us that we should render personal service and present the spiritual master with comforts. A devotee who does so is a bona fide candidate for instruction. One should not think that because Srila Prabhupada was speaking about “all kinds of comforts” that he was interested in the comforts. A spiritual master has no interest in material facility; he is fully dependent upon Krsna. But if a disciple wants to receive transcendental knowledge from the guru, he must display a submissive service attitude. As Srila Prabhupada explains later in his purport, the spiritual master must not accept a disciple who is not qualified. The guru should not be a professional guru and accept disciples for monetary gain. Real preachers are interested in bringing souls back to Krsna, not with making money.

To impart knowledge into the disciple’s heart, the guru must be qualified and the disciple must become a qualified receptacle. If the disciple is not ready to hear the knowledge the guru has to offer, then even if the spiritual master tries to impart it, the disciple will not hear it. It is important for a disciple to be qualified. A disciple qualifies himself to become receptive by offering submissive service.

When the sages at Naimisaranya praised Suta Gosvami and acknowledged their willingness to hear the *Bhagavatam* from him, they said, “Because you are submissive, your spiritual master endowed upon you all the favors bestowed upon a gentle disciple, and therefore you can explain to us all the transcendental truths which you have heard from him. ”

The spiritual master will test the disciple’s submission. If the disciple appears submissive but maintains an ulterior motive in his service, then that will be discovered through the testing process. It will become obvious that such a disciple is only displaying submission when it is convenient for him. The spiritual master will understand that by making an external show of submission, the disciple thinks the spiritual master will support him in some way. If someone complains to the spiritual master about the disciple, the apparently submissive disciple will hope that the spiritual master will have been taken in by his false submission and take his side. The spiritual master tests for sincerity. Sincerity in the guru-disciple relationship means that the disciple wants nothing from the guru other than Krsna consciousness. He is not interested in buying himself a spiritual master, and the spiritual master is not interested in monetary gain.

Because a spiritual master is attached to the Lord’s service— and he defines his service as glorifying Krsna—he wants to see the qualification of the persons he plans to initiate. The *Bhagavatam* uses the word *diyatam* to explain that the spiritual master should offer Krsna conscious knowledge to his disciples. He should have no other interest. Accepting the service of disciples is secondary to his main service of instructing them. The spiritual master accepts the disciples’ service only so that the disciples can become qualified receptacles for the knowledge he wishes to impart. Krsna Himself has a similar motivation. He is not looking for external surrender but for a depth of faith and love. Krsna says, “As they surrender unto Me, I reward them accordingly. ” That reward is the knowledge given in the heart of a devotee of how to come closer to Him. “Out of compassion for them I am dwelling within their hearts and I destroy with the shining light of knowledge the darkness born of ignorance. ”

Krsna wants to give us the gift of unalloyed devotional service. Similarly, the spiritual master seeks qualified candidates to receive transcendental knowledge. He finds such candidates among those who are submissive enough to render caring and selfless service according to the guru’s orders. The disciple should not think he can only serve the spiritual master as a specialist: “I will render service to the spiritual master only if I can render it in the way I want. ” The spiritual master wants to see a disciple prepared to render *any* kind of service.

It is not easy to become the guru’s personal servant, because a personal servant has to be prepared to do whatever is required according to the guru’s needs. A personal servant has to anticipate the guru’s needs and satisfy them. If the disciple thinks he can only serve in one way, he cannot become the guru’s personal servant. Imagine being with the spiritual master as personal servant and secretary. The secretary takes all the guru’s messages and helps the spiritual master with his correspondence (perhaps he types his letters). Then the spiritual master needs to leave and he can’t find his coat. Should he think, “I am his secretary, so let him find his own coat. If I do only my secretarial service, I’ll please my spiritual master. Someone else will help him find his coat. ” What kind of a personal servant would that be? If we are actually serving someone, Srila Prabhupada says, “We must be prepared to render all kinds of service”—any service that is required. Being prepared to render any kind of service is how we display our submission. Serving another requires sacrifice. Srila Prabhupada’s personal servants can attest to that.

This year in Vrndavana I spent time with my godbrother, Srutakirti Prabhu, who was Srila Prabhupada’s personal servant for about two years. Each day in Vrndavana he was reading from his diary and discussing his life with Prabhupada. I attended every session. Then one day Srutakirti Prabhu and I took a walk on the Vrndavana *parikrama-marga.* During the entire walk he told me stories about Srila Prabhupada. It was so wonderful to hear him speak that I kept asking him for more and more. I had already heard a number of his stories, because Srutakirti Prabhu and I served together in Boston in 1975, but those stories are ever-fresh. What I found most relishable to hear was the variety of things Srutakirti did to serve Prabhupada. I thought, “He was really a personal servant. He knew what it meant to be Prabhupada’s personal servant. ”

For example, he told me that he used to sleep under Prabhupada’s bed at a time when Prabhupada was very ill in Vrndavana. Prabhupada was not so eager to accept help from his disciples, but Srutakirti wanted to be available to him if he needed anything in the middle of the night. He related how one night, Prabhupada got up. He was so weak, however, that he fell to the floor. Srutakirti didn’t get up in time to catch him. Srutakirti immediately jumped out from under the bed to help Prabhupada, asking, “Prabhupada! Why didn’t you call me? Why didn’t you tell me that you needed help? ” Prabhupada dismissed the incident, but I could see from the story that Srutakirti was absorbed in his loving remembrance of Srila Prabhupada.

As the walk continued, he described one incident after another, painting a picture of what it was like to be with Prabhupada as his personal servant. I realized the sacrifice that Srutakirti Prabhu and others made to give Prabhupada comfort. That was especially true when Prabhupada became ill. Although Prabhupada continued to preach strongly throughout his illness, his physical needs increased. Still, whenever Prabhupada saw the anxious expressions on his disciples’ faces as they worried about his condition, he would take the opportunity to preach to them: “Why are you looking anxious? This body is simply a bag of bones and skin. This body is not going to last forever. It’s temporary. The real life is the soul. Real life is preaching Krsna consciousness. Just go out and preach. ”

Sometimes he would preach like a lion, even though his body was frail. During those times, his disciples were only thinking of how to ease Srila Prabhupada’s condition and how to provide him with comforts. Prabhupada in his turn was only thinking about how to give them knowledge.

“The spiritual master must not accept the disciple who is not qualified. He must see the bona fide qualities of a person whom he is going to initiate. An unworthy person should not be initiated. The spiritual master should train his disciple in such a way so that in the future only the Supreme Personality of Godhead will be the dearmost goal of his life. ”

This is an important point to note, especially in reference to initiation. Often, candidates aspiring for initiation think that what they have done up until the point of initiation is their most important service. They think, “Based upon my past surrender, I have become qualified to take initiation. ” We have to be careful not to think that initiation is the goal. Initiation is not an end in itself. It’s not that becoming initiated fulfills all the requirements of pure devotion. Initiation is the beginning of spiritual life; not the end. Only a fool would consider it the goal.

The spiritual master understands that initiation is a step on the path of making Krsna the disciple’s dearmost goal of life. Therefore, it is not only the disciple’s past that the spiritual master examines when considering a devotee for initiation, but also his future potential. Is this disciple sufficiently trained to begin to accept Krsna as the ultimate goal of life? Does he already carry this goal in his heart? At the time of initiation, does his future look as bright as his past? Is he thinking that attaining initiation is the goal instead of attaining Krsna? If the spiritual master sees that the devotee has potential and the interest to advance, he will agree to accept him for further training.

For that potential to be realized, however, the submission that characterized a devotee’s life leading up to initiation should continue after initiation—not only continue, but increase. A disciple should increase his enthusiasm after he has received initiation. Otherwise, it seems obvious that the disciple accepted initiation with an ulterior motive.

A disciple who works toward initiation by practicing submissive inquiry and service and then becomes independent after initiation is living in the greatest illusion. It happens when a disciple does not understand what initiation means. This misunderstanding will lead him to think, “Now that I am initiated, I can become independent. I no longer need authority. I can move out of the temple and live independently, because I got what I wanted—initiation. ” This is illusion. Initiation is not the goal.

Of course, a dependent disciple may move out of the temple to enter household life. Household life provides a license for a more independent lifestyle. Some disciples may live outside the temple even as they come forward for initiation. What the spiritual master is examining is the heartfelt submission in any condition of life, because without submission, there cannot be any transfer of transcendental knowledge. *Tad vidhi pranipatena pariprasnena sevaya:* a disciple must inquire submissively and render service. This is the system that Krsna Himself established for those who wish to give and receive transcendental knowledge.

Srila Prabhupada explains that when a spiritual master sees that his disciple is submissive, he wants to pour his blessings upon him in the form of transcendental knowledge. This is how he trains his disciple. Srila Prabhupada adds, “The candidate must have developed detachment for that which is outside the purview of Krsna consciousness. ” As Queen Kunti states in her prayers, “Those who are on the path of material progress cannot approach Krsna with sincere feeling. ” If a disciple is not careful, his attachments can again take hold of his heart. Therefore, Srila Prabhupada says that when a disciple accepts initiation from a spiritual master, he must break his ties with the materialistic conception of life.

Breaking his ties means that he has to become detached from everything outside the purview of Krsna consciousness. Mrgari the hunter set that example when he broke his bow. Srila Prabhupada said that Mrgari’s breaking his bow was his initiation, because at that time Mrgari asked Narada, “How can I become free from the sinful reaction of having killed so many animals? ” Narada instructed him to give up his profession of killing, give away his possessions, and depend upon the Lord for his maintenance. Mrgari then asked, “I am a hunter. How will I live if I cannot hunt? ” Narada replied, “No, you must stop hunting. The Lord will take care of everything. ” Then he added, “I will arrange to see that you receive your meals. ” Mrgari then broke his bow in half, surrendering it completely. Prabhupada writes in this regard, “This is initiation. ” Initiation takes place when a disciple is willing to break his ties with the bodily concept of life. Mrgari renounced everything and lived simply. Even the people could see what wonderful devotional qualities he began to develop by his service to his Guru Maharaja. After his initiation, people would come daily to bring offerings to the converted hunter. Thus Mrgari had whatever he needed by the Lord’s grace.

The *Bhagavatam* states, “Lord Kapila continued: This instruction is not meant for the envious, for the agnostics or for persons who are unclean in their behavior. Nor is it for hypocrites or for persons who are proud of material possessions. It is not to be instructed to persons who are too greedy and too attached to family life, nor to persons who are nondevotees and who are envious of the devotees and of the Personality of Godhead. Instruction should be given to the faithful devotee who is respectful to the spiritual master, nonenvious, friendly to all kinds of living entities and eager to render service with faith and sincerity. This instruction should be imparted by the spiritual master to persons who have taken the Supreme Personality of Godhead to be more dear than anything, who are not envious of anyone, who are perfectly cleansed and who have developed detachment for that which is outside the purview of Krsna consciousness. ” (SB [3.32.39-41](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/32.39))

Sometimes a disciple cannot act properly and does something he knows will not please his guru. But he doesn’t seem to be able to act in any other way due to ignorance or lack of devotion. Is there any hope that such a disciple could improve and become pleasing to the spiritual master?

There is always hope, because the Lord’s devotees are forgiving. The spiritual master naturally forgives a disciple who realizes he has made a mistake. Even though a disciple may have had difficulty in following his instructions and displeased him, the spiritual master will not reject him unless the disciple rejects the spiritual master. There is a difference between rejecting the spiritual master and being weak-hearted in Krsna consciousness. Disciples often deviate because they are weak-hearted (*hrdaya-daurbalyam*). Weakheartedness is not a disqualification in itself, especially if the disciple realizes his condition. When a disciple realizes his problem, he can sincerely beg forgiveness from the spiritual master. The guru will always accept him.

But it is important for the disciple to come to the point of accepting that he has made a mistake. When a person realizes he has suffered enough for his mistakes, he can realize that he has to beg forgiveness. It seems that suffering may have to come first before the disciple overcomes his weak-heartedness.

The spiritual master doesn’t want to see his disciples suffer. He doesn’t like to see *anyone* suffer. That’s why he preaches Krsna consciousness. He preaches because he hopes that if his disciples hear his instructions, they can avoid suffering. Sometimes the disciples are persistent in their wrongdoing and the spiritual master cannot help them. He must let them go their own way until they have suffered enough to want to again take up his instructions.

Recently one of my godbrothers, who has been away from Prabhupada’s service since the late 1970s, told me that he had suffered enough. He said it took him eighteen years to realize that he was suffering and that Srila Prabhupada’s instructions were the most important thing in his life. This godbrother asked my forgiveness, although he did not break my instructions, because he said that I always tried to help him. He apologized for all the days we spent together on *sankirtana* and how much he was struggling, beginning to deviate even then from Prabhupada’s instructions. I remember going up and grabbing him a number of times, trying to preach to him, and then off he would go again. Even after he left, I tried to encourage him. Therefore he asked my forgiveness for all the inconveniences and disturbances he felt he caused me. I saw again how suffering brings us to the point of realizing we have to reform ourselves, and reform begins with begging forgiveness.

It is natural that the spiritual master will forgive the disciple, especially if the disciple is sincere in his apology. But the forgiveness is not cheap. The disciple has to actually be really prepared to beg forgiveness for his particular mistakes and promise not to do them again. Then real forgiveness is possible. The disciple must be convinced that he doesn’t want to make the same mistake again and the spiritual master must be patient and forgiving.

Srila Prabhupada explains in a *Bhagavad-gita* purport in reference to the verse, *api cet su-duracaro,* that “When one hears these statements of how Krsna is also forgiving, one should not use this as an excuse to deviate. That is not a very high standard of devotional service. ” In fact, it is an offense to the Lord’s holy name to think that we can commit sinful acts on the strength of being easily forgiven. When we hear about the guru’s mercy, we should not think we can use that as an excuse for deviation. Our attempt to improve ourselves must be sincere, and then we can trust that all forgiveness is there.

## What does it mean to associate with the spiritual master?

**Unknown date, verse, town, country**

Association with the spiritual master is in two forms: *vapuh* (personal presence) and *vani* (through his instructions). Associating with the *vapuh* helps develop the ability to associate with *vani.* Thus we should seek our spiritual master’s personal association. Then, when we hear his instructions, we can carry out our real business of following them. We want to associate with the spiritual master so we can hear. By hearing, we will develop our faith in his instructions. If we are in his presence, we will also be able to see his example of applying his own guru’s instructions. That too helps us develop our faith. Certainly if we are fortunate enough to come into our spiritual master’s presence, we should take advantage of the opportunity in order to strengthen our ability to follow his *vani.*

But personally associating with the spiritual master is not cheap. Neophyte disciples rarely know how to take advantage of *vapuh.* They think that *vapuh* is an opportunity for them to show off for their spiritual master. They want him to see how good they are, how surrendered, etc. When the spiritual master comes, they jump towards the spiritual master with their hands up straight, “See how surrendered I am?! ” But when the spiritual master leaves, they immediately fall into *maya.* They don’t understand that *vapuh* is meant to provide them with an opportunity to hear the spiritual master’s instructions and to observe his example.

The more advanced disciples are always associating with the spiritual master’s instructions. Therefore, it is they who receive the spiritual master’s attention. Those who are connected with the spiritual master through his instructions understand the meaning of *vapuh.* Such disciples feel the strength of the guru-disciple bond, because they feel Krsna manifesting in their guru’s instructions. Therefore, when we are blessed by *vapuh* we should take advantage of it to hear. Then, when the spiritual master is not present before us, we can act upon what we have heard. When the spiritual master returns, our relationship with him will have deepened based on our submissive following.

Therefore, we say *vapuh* is important because it helps us develop the ability to follow *vani. Vapuh* is given, however, by the spiritual master’s mercy. We cannot demand it. By guru’s mercy we receive his association.

How do we know when we are receiving our guru’s mercy? We come to know his heart by always meditating upon his order. That’s what it means to understand the heart of the spiritual master. The spiritual master desires to give us Krsna. If we understand this, we have received some of his mercy. *Bhagavad-gita* states, “For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, pure devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come back to Me. ” (Bg. [18.68](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/18.68)) To deliver Krsna consciousness is the spiritual master’s heart.

The disciple who is advancing comes closer to the spiritual master and understands the heart of his mission. When the disciple begins to meditate on how he can help accomplish that mission, then we know he has received the guru’s mercy.

There are devotees who think that coming closer to the spiritual master means getting the opportunity to put on his shoes. But how many disciples can put on the guru’s shoes at one time? Coming close to the spiritual master means understanding his heart, always meditating on his mission, and serving accordingly.

## How can we come closer to the spiritual master?

**Darsana for disciples Moscow, August 1995**

Lately I have been receiving more recommendations for second initiation. Therefore I thought it would be good to discuss the important responsibilities that come along with brahminical initiation, because to receive it is not a sign of prestige, but rather a service. In the seventh canto of *Bhagavatam,* Prahlada Maharaja describes that even if one is decorated with all twelve brahminical qualities but is not firmly situated in devotion to Krsna, he is considered lower than a person born in a family of dog-eaters but who has dedicated body, mind, and words in Krsna’s service. Prahlada Maharaja goes on to say that a so-called *brahmana,* due to his false prestige, cannot even purify himself, what to speak of others.

Srila Prabhupada explains that *brahmana* initiation is meant to bring us closer to our spiritual master and to increase our dedication to his mission. Recently I read in a purport in the eleventh canto a commentary by Jiva Gosvami. He states that a *kanistha-adhikari* cannot properly respect other devotees. He will therefore certainly fail to offer respects to ordinary living entities. A *kanistha-adhikari* is useless when it comes to practical preaching work, because his ability to see himself and others clearly is undeveloped. Because a *kanistha-adhikari s* faith is not based on the Vedic statements, he cannot see the Supreme Personality of Godhead in everyone’s heart. Thus he can neither feel love of God nor understand the exalted position of Krsna’s devotees. Krsna is so glorious that His intimate associates must also be glorious. The *kanistha-adhikari* does not know this. The essential quality of a Vaisnava, to offer respects to others, is absent in him.

Therefore, to become an effective preacher, a devotee must rise above the *kanistha* platform and learn to respect all living entities. When we speak about coming closer to the spiritual master, we do not mean necessarily by physical proximity, but rather through service. This means that we become a more effective instrument in his hands. The spiritual master is interested in preaching, because it is his responsibility to expand the world’s awareness of Krsna’s glories and to give devotional service to others. The disciple who wishes to come closer to him will assist him in his mission. This means that he or she agrees to accept the burden of preaching responsibility so that the spiritual master can more effectively spread Krsna consciousness. If the spiritual master’s disciples remain *kanistha-adhikaris,* how can they teach Krsna’s glories together? The disciples have to come to a higher platform.

Srila Bhaktisiddhanta mentioned before his departure that his temples were becoming filled with *kanistha-adhikaris.* It was obvious by their behavior that they were unable to respect others properly and neither were they able to preach. Seeing so many *kanisthaadhikaris,* Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura decided to leave this world. Of course, not all his disciples were *kanistha-adhikaris;* he had many glorious disciples, among whom Srila Prabhupada was the most glorious. Srila Prabhupada showed himself to be the dearmost servant of his spiritual master by his willingness to accept the burden of preaching in the Western world.

The spiritual master wants to draw his disciples closer to help him in his mission. He does not want to draw them only through sentiment or to render him physical service. He wants to draw them closer in intimacy; he wants them to understand his mission. Then he wants them to help him carry it out.

Becoming a *brahmana* is not simply a position. Becoming a preacher should not be one either. We don’t become preachers by being given a title. *We become recognized as preachers by the way we deal with other living entities.* There is nothing external about it. We become preachers by surpassing the stage of *kanistha* and rising to a more advanced platform.

I have seen a tendency among devotees to have a difficult time overlooking others’ faults. Such devotees often complain about the behavior of others or quarrel among themselves. They also have personal motivations in executing their service. Jiva Gosvami explains that the solution to these problems is to see Krsna within everyone’s heart. A preacher has firm faith in Krsna’s presence in the hearts of others, and he takes whatever opportunity is available to fan the spark of their own awareness of that fact. By seeing Krsna situated in someone else’s heart, a preacher can then direct that person in how to make progress in devotional life.

In other words, preaching does not mean pointing out others’ faults and shortcomings. That isn’t preaching, but faultfinding. Preaching means to encourage the spark of Krsna consciousness in others, and maintaining faith that the development of Krsna consciousness will automatically eliminate any bad qualities they may have. The *Bhagavatam* states, *yasyasti bhaktir bhagavaty akincana,* devotees develop all good qualities.

*Brahmanas* have particular characteristics and qualities. The *sastras* say they should be perfectly religious, truthful, sense controlled, prepared to execute severe austerities, detached, humble, tolerant, nonenvious, expert at performing sacrifices, and prepared to give whatever they have in charity. *Brahmanas* are fixed in devotional service to Krsna and expert in Vedic knowledge. Srila Prabhupada explained that a *brahmana* is recognized by his qualities.

Srila Prabhupada listed those qualities not only as theoretical brahminical attributes but as the attributes he wished his own disciples to attain when they were initiated as *brahmanas.* He said people should be able to recognize a *brahmana* by his or her qualities. The ability to attain these qualities and to become a *brahmana* is not decided by birth.

Why must a *brahmana* be free of envy? Because envious persons are also proud. They cannot see the good qualities in others, nor can they see Krsna in everyone’s heart. This vision is essential to brahminical life, because it is the duty of *brahmanas* to teach. How can one who cannot see Krsna in the hearts of others and only sees their bad qualities preach to them? This is especially damaging if they cannot see the good qualities in the devotees. Such persons will not be able to preach effectively. To preach effectively, we must be able to see the potential for Krsna consciousness within everyone. If we wish to become *brahmanas,* we must give up sectarian or separatist attitudes and not preach simply to fulfill personal ambition. If we think, “I will preach to those who are willing to serve me, and if someone doesn’t serve me, I will reject him, ” then we are not acting on the brahminical platform.

The vision of a preacher is centered on Krsna—what Krsna needs and how to serve His interests. A preacher wants to see everyone serve Krsna, because he sees Krsna within everyone’s heart.

We can recognize a real *brahmana* by his or her dealings with others. We cannot base our judgment simply on external designations. Those who wish to take brahminical initiation should think about how they can come closer to the spiritual master to more intimately serve his mission of sheltering conditioned souls. We cannot force shelter upon conditioned souls. But by our own perfect behavior, we can encourage them to respect what we are doing and to begin to desire the peace a Vaisnava *brahmana* feels. No one can order another to listen to his or her instructions, but we can attract people to Krsna by our nonenvious dealings with one another and with the nondevotees. We can be prepared for people to approach us by increasing our scriptural knowledge and being prepared to speak truthfully and sastrically, without duplicity. Attaining these qualities is the real ladder of success; not climbing from temple devotee to temple president to GBC member.

A devotee recently asked me whether I hadn’t contradicted myself. He said, “When you said we advance in Krsna consciousness by taking on more responsibility and service, but that we shouldn’t try to climb the ladder of success, it seemed like a contradiction. How can we take on more responsibility without climbing the ISKCON ladder? ”

We can take on more service *and* perhaps climb the ISKCON ladder without losing our spiritual focus by always thinking ourselves lower than straw in the street. A devotee accepts more responsibility because he is capable of accepting such responsibility. If he was to abuse his position, then he would not be able to remain in that position. His apparent advancement would be artificial.

There are different types of responsibility. Someone may appear to be responsible because we see him manipulating the material energy. Either he is controlling money or controlling vehicles or controlling other devotees. But is he necessarily responsible? Real responsibility means to take responsibility for the Krsna consciousness of others. As we grow in *that* responsibility, Krsna will send others to assist us. If we want them to assist us, we have to preach to them. Everything ultimately comes from preaching. If we think we have so much responsibility that we can simply push work onto others in order to accomplish everything, and we are not prepared to help them in their own spiritual lives, then we are abusing our power and ultimately being irresponsible. It is important to understand this, because this society has been built on preaching. The Krsna consciousness movement is expanding based on our preaching efforts and the mercy of Krsna. We cannot expand by collecting money or building temples or collecting property assets. Our real expansion means capturing conditioned souls. Everything else happens automatically when that endeavor is made.

## Devotional Service, the Most Elevated Activity

**SB** [**3.26.6**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/26.6) **Novosibirsk, November 15, 1996**

**“Because of his forgetfulness, the transcendental living entity accepts the influence of material energy as his field of activities, and thus actuated, he wrongly applies the activities to himself. ”**

This chapter of the *Bhagavatam* describes the living entity’s entrapment under the three modes of material nature. In other places, Srila Prabhupada translates the word “guna” as rope. It is the rope of the *gunas* that binds us to the material world. These ropes are unbreakable. Prabhupada explains that a rope becomes stronger when it is braided with other ropes. Similarly, the three modes are braided together to form an indestructible rope. The living entity who becomes bound by this rope has little chance of escape. Although by nature the living entity is transcendental to the modes, due to forgetfulness, he accepts their influence over himself as his field of activities. *Bhagavad-gita* states, *prakrteh kriyamanani gunaih karmani sarvasah* / *ahankara-vimudhatma, kartaham iti manyate:* “The spirit soul bewildered by the influence of false ego thinks himself the doer of activities that are in actuality carried out by the three modes of material nature. ” (Bg. [3.27](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.27))

Lord Kapiladeva says that when a living entity comes under the material energy’s influence he is “thus actuated” and “wrongly applies the activities to himself. ” *Actuated* means he starts from that very point. That is, he immediately becomes trapped. He no longer has the power to step back and see how he became entrapped. Like a person trapped in a labyrinth, he tries to find his way out.

Of course, one way to find his way out is to remember how he got in and retrace his steps, but a living entity who becomes trapped by the material nature no longer has the discrimination to do that. He becomes forgetful under the influence of the three modes.

Therefore, since we can understand that we are trapped within the three modes, we can know that all our activities began at the point when we embraced material consciousness. At that moment, we began to center all our thoughts and desires around bodily consciousness, which is the sphere or field of activity. We do not see activity beyond the physical plane, and we no longer have access to the spiritual world. We forget our relationship with Krsna. In many cases, we have not even heard of the transcendental realm from spiritual authorities; we do not even know it exists. If we have heard, we have often not heard with submission. Only by submitting to spiritual authority can we regain access to the spiritual world when we are in material consciousness.

Being trapped by matter and having no access to spiritual truth, we cannot conceive of activities beyond what we can see on the physical plane. They are simply inconceivable. The *Mahabharata* describes that everything transcendental to material nature is inconceivable to us, and arguing about it from this platform is mundane. Since mundane arguments cannot touch transcendental subject matter, we should not try to approach transcendental subject matter through mundane argument. Transcendental subject matter cannot be approached by mental speculation, because mental speculators themselves are trapped in false consciousness. They cannot understand anything about the Supreme Lord or His pastimes. The *Bhagavatam* ([10.9.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/9.21)) states, “The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Krsna, the son of mother Yasoda, is accessible to devotees engaged in spontaneous loving service, but He is not as easily accessible to mental speculators, to those striving for self-realization by severe austerities and penances, or to those who consider the body the same as the self. ” This is a prayer by Lord Brahma. In it he describes that Krsna’s pastimes are accessible only to devotees. Mental speculators cannot understand Krsna’s pastimes because the pastimes are beyond the material realm. Krsna’s pastimes can only be understood with transcendental intelligence. Therefore Krsna says, “One who understands the transcendental nature of My appearance and activities in this world does not have to take his birth again. He attains My eternal abode. ” (Bg. [4.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/4.9))

We may say, “If that’s all I have to do to free myself from this world—understand Krsna’s appearance and activities—then that’s simple. ” But only those who feel love for Krsna can understand His nature. One who can fully understand why Krsna appears in this world will never be able to forget Him. Rather, he will become absorbed in discussing the Lord’s appearance and pastimes. Theoretical understanding is not enough.

Of course, any devotee knows the reasons stated in *sastra* for Lord Caitanya’s appearance. We even know the esoteric reasons. This information is given openly in the *Caitanya-caritamrta.* The *Bhagavad-gita* also tells us that Krsna descends into this world to annihilate the miscreants and to give mercy to His devotees. In a sense, that information is no secret. But to fully understand the *meaning* of these descents requires love. The reasons cannot be understood with the material mind. Transcendental subject matter is inconceivable; the Lord’s appearance and pastimes are inconceivable. We may approach the Lord’s pastimes with the material mind and intelligence, but if we want to realize them fully, they will have to be revealed to us.

That is why, when Srila Prabhupada speaks about the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra,* he says that it is beyond the mind and intelligence. The Hare Krsna *maha-mantra* originates in the spiritual world, just as Krsna and His pastimes descend from there. When the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra* comes into the material world, it descends: *golokera prema-dhana, hari-nama-sankirtana.* The Lord’s holy name is imported from the spiritual world. It is not a material sound vibration.

When Krsna describes in *Bhagavad-gita* that the supreme abode is unmanifest, infallible and the supreme destination, does He mean that the supreme abode does not exist? No, it is unmanifest. How can it be unmanifest? Because there never was a time when it did not exist. That is, there was never a time when it *became* manifest or came into being. The spiritual world is eternal.

This is inconceivable. If we try to conceive of it, we will find it too difficult to understand. Just try to conceive of how the spiritual world has always existed and did not come into being at a particular time. It’s very difficult to understand. Krsna describes the spiritual world as changeless and supreme. In the spiritual world, there is no change; but in the material world, all we see is change. We cannot understand “no change” with our material mind and senses.

What often occurs to people who do try to understand these mysteries is that the spiritual world must be a boring place if it never undergoes change. We tend to think variety is the spice of life. In this world, we work hard to create novelty for ourselves, varieties of engagement, and sense pleasures. We would not want to be faced with an eternity of having to do exactly the same thing day after day. Where would the pleasure be in that?

People seek variety so much that they are willing to give up duty. A husband becomes tired of his wife, so he seeks out another woman with whom to enjoy. Eventually he discovers that this new woman is very much like the old one and thus decides to find a third. A person under material influence uses his field of activities (the body) to seek variety. He tries to widen his field of activities so as to increase his pleasure.

It is natural to want to experience variety, because by nature, material experience is limited and the soul is accustomed to unlimited variety in the spiritual world. The spiritual world is the substance and the material world is the reflection. Enjoyment of any reflection is extremely limited. The spiritual world is eternally manifest and the material manifestation is temporary. It is not actually substantial.

The living entities, attempting to enjoy matter through his particular field of activities, shows that by nature he is meant to experience pleasure. The spiritual world is full of unlimited happiness found in the unlimited varieties of experience. Unfortunately, his pleasure-seeking impulse pushes him to seek pleasure in matter, where it is not ultimately available—this world being the reflection of spirit. When he hears about the variety of enjoyment available in spiritual life, and that to attain it one must restrict himself from material pleasure, a materialist cannot understand. It is inconceivable to him that renunciation can give him access to a greater pleasure.

He also looks at the lives of devotees and wonders how they can follow such a regulated life. How can we find pleasure in doing the same things every day? People question devotees about this all the time. They see us chanting the same mantra, doing the same activities, studying the same books over a lifetime. We discuss the same topics, hear the same information and pastimes, and restrict our senses from everything else. “Why just Krsna, Krsna, Krsna? How do you taste happiness? There is so much to life, but you are simply doing everything for Krsna?! ” They cannot understand spiritual pleasure.

Sometimes neophyte devotees agree with these materialists. They wear devotional clothing and *tilaka,* but gradually they begin to think, “Where *is* the pleasure in Krsna consciousness? I have to do the same thing every day! *Every day* I have to get up for *mangala-arati,* attend *guru-puja,* hear *Bhagavatam* class, and perform my service— the same service. What pleasure am I finding in these activities? ” This is what happens when we begin to think there is no variety in spiritual life. We begin to look for variety in matter, being attracted to the apparent enjoyment that materialists are experiencing.

If we are in this enjoying consciousness and focusing that enjoyment on matter, it will be very difficult for us to understand the Supreme Lord’s appearance and pastimes, and it will be especially difficult to understand the pleasure of spiritual life.

There is great pleasure to be felt in simply discussing Krsna’s activities. We cannot always taste this pleasure because we remain attached to our material field of activities. Krsna says, “One can understand Me as I am, as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, only by devotional service. And when one is in full consciousness of Me by such devotion, he can enter into the kingdom of God. ” (Bg. [18.55](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/18.55)) In other words, we must actually render devotional service. Otherwise, we will have no choice but to continue using our material consciousness as our frame of reference and be trapped. From that position we will not be able to understand the glories of devotional service, the Lord’s pastimes, or the glories of the holy name.

Lord Brahma prays that devotional service to Krsna is the best process for self-realization because it is the only way to realize our actual spiritual identity. In order to understand Lord Krsna’s pastimes, they have to be awakened within the heart. Mental speculation will not work. We must change our consciousness. That is the point Srila Prabhupada makes in his purport to this verse. “In material consciousness, whatever is done under the influence of material energy is accepted by the conditioned soul as self-actuated. ”

In its pure state, the soul can be in nothing other than Krsna consciousness. When a person is not acting in Krsna consciousness, he is understood to be acting in material consciousness. Consciousness itself is never annihilated, because it is the characteristic of the living entity. Consciousness can either be material or spiritual. If it is material, it must be purified. When consciousness is material, the living entity sees life reflected through the senses and the material body; he sees everything in relation to the body. Srila Prabhupada gives the example of the senses and how they are like vents for spiritual awareness. When the senses are directed through the material mind and intelligence, however, they pick up only the colors of the material world, influenced as they are by the modes of material nature. Just as light passes through colored glass and appears to become the color of the glass, so the senses pass through the colored glass of the material modes and appear to take on the modes’ characteristics. The *sastra* confirms that we cannot understand Krsna with our “blunt material senses. ” The senses must be purified so that we are seeing from our original and constitutional position.

One who engages his senses in the Lord’s service begins to experience a superior satisfaction. In *Bhagavad-gita,* Arjuna asks Krsna about the symptoms of a man situated in transcendence. Krsna replies, “When a man gives up all ideas for sense satisfaction, which arise from mental concoction, and finds satisfaction in the self alone, he is said to be in pure transcendental consciousness. ” Krsna is asserting the superiority of spiritual satisfaction.

Such a person is self-satisfied. We think becoming self-satisfied is a difficult task, because in order to do so, we must be satisfied in ourselves alone. Our usual way of becoming satisfied is through material objects. We begin to look for these objects—subtle or gross—to possess, convinced that they will bring us pleasure. But that means we are not satisfied by the self alone. We hanker for association with others. We want something to call ours, whether it be a thing or another person. The truth is, we don’t understand how to become self-satisfied, and we are not even sure we *can* be selfsatisfied—satisfied within the self with no need for pleasure derived outside ourselves. Lord Rsabhadeva calls this an increase in illusion:

*purusah striya mithuni-bhavam etam*  
*tayor mitho hrdaya-granthim ahuh*  
*ato grha-ksetra-sutapta-vittair*  
*janasya moho ’yam aham mameti*

**“The attraction between male and female is the basic principle of material existence. On the basis of this misconception, which ties together the hearts of the male and female, one becomes attracted to his body, home, property, children, relatives and wealth. In this way one increases life’s illusions and thinks in terms of ‘I and mine. ’” (SB** [**5.5.8**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/5.8)**)**

This sex life is what perpetuates material existence and keeps each person dissatisfied and always seeking more. In order to feel “more, ” such persons increase their field of activities through various extensions. They think this will help them feel more complete, more satisfied.

What does it mean, then, to find satisfaction in the self alone? Does it mean that a self-realized soul is always alone? No. Rather, it means that a self-realized soul knows who he is. He knows he is never separate from Krsna. That is “self-realization. ” One who finds satisfaction in the self alone knows he is always with Krsna and therefore forgets neither his true identity nor his Lord. Krsna says about such devotees, “The devotee is in My heart and I am in the heart of My devotee. The devotee is always thinking of Me and I am always thinking of him. ”

This reciprocation is actually the only real source of pleasure. All so-called pleasures in the material world are simply perverted reflections of this one real pleasure. Krsna is the reservoir of pleasure. That means that any pleasure of which we can possibly conceive has its origin in Krsna. The pure devotee is in touch with that reservoir.

One who is self-satisfied has discovered how to please the Lord’s senses and to make that his happiness. This only looks as if it were a bitter fate or monotonous because our eyes are covered by matter. We are diseased. A diseased person is forced to lie in a hospital bed. Any natural movement causes him pain. Similarly, Krsna consciousness is natural, but it causes us pain when we are suffering from the material disease.

What are the natural movements of Krsna consciousness? *Nastaprayesv abhadresu, nityam bhagavata-sevaya, bhagavaty uttama-sloke, bhaktir bhavati naisthiki:* “By regular attendance in classes on the *Bhagavatam* and by rendering of service to the pure devotee, all that is troublesome to the heart is almost completely destroyed, and loving service unto the Personality of Godhead, who is praised with transcendental songs, is established as an irrevocable fact. ” (SB [1.2.18](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.18)) Hearing is a natural movement. It is what the liberated souls are doing: *prayena munayo rajan nivrtta vidhi-sedhatah (SB* [2.1.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/1.7)). The liberated souls who are beyond the rules and regulations find pleasure in always discussing topics of the Lord. “The thoughts of My pure devotees dwell in Me, their lives are fully devoted to My service, and they derive great satisfaction and bliss from always enlightening one another and conversing about Me. ” (Bg. [10.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/10.9)) This is where such devotees find pleasure. This is where *any* of us can find pleasure if we are seriously looking for it.

Does that seem dull or painful? Well, we are still diseased. But hearing and chanting is so pleasurable for the self-realized devotees that they have no other shelter. They see clearly that all other shelters are futile and temporary. They maintain no mundane attachments. They want only to render service to the Lord.

We can do the same thing. If we don’t realize it now, then we should keep serving and continue praying to understand the purpose behind our activities. If we perform our duties with resentment— “Why do I have to do this? Why can’t someone else do this work? ”—then we will not be able to understand the nature of devotional service. We will fail to remember that performing duties in Krsna consciousness, even without full realization, will awaken our understanding and eventually our devotion. Instead of seeking pleasure in matter, we will begin to see it in spirit. We will enter the mystery of how to find pleasure even in doing the same activity every day. Without Krsna consciousness, everything we do is a waste of time *(srama eva hi kevalam).* The goal is simply to remember and serve Krsna. Then we will be happy.

## Surrender is an ongoing process

**SB** [**4.17.28**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/17.28) **Rostov, June 1996**

**“At this time Prthu Maharaja became exactly like Yamaraja, and his whole body appeared very angry. In other words, he was anger personified. After hearing him, the planet earth began to tremble. She surrendered, and with folded hands began to speak as follows. ”** ***(SB***  [**4.17.28**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/17.28)**)**

A devotee who accepts the shelter of the Supreme Lord will always be assured of His protection at all times. Krsna says, “Declare it boldly, Arjuna: My devotee will never be vanquished. ” Therefore, sheltered at Krsna’s lotus feet is the safest position to accept. We should not look for shelter in the material world. A devotee should develop full confidence that if he remembers the Lord in all circumstances, whether he is experiencing happiness or distress, in heaven or hell—whatever condition of life—he will be safe. Thus, we are always anxious to find shelter in Krsna consciousness— praying for the opportunity to hear and chant about and remember the Lord, and to render devotional service.

If we want the Lord’s protection, we must surrender ourselves to His authority. In the material world we tend to challenge authority. However, a devotee accepts the Lord’s authority and remains submissive. In this section of the *Bhagavatam,* Mother Bhumi surrendered herself to Prthu Maharaja and was protected.

Surrender is an ongoing process. We should never foolishly think we have surrendered once and for all. This pride can become the greatest obstacle on our path. Surrender is not something that occurs at a particular point in time; it is a way of life.

Krsna says, “As they surrender unto Me, I reward them accordingly. ” Surrender, followed by Krsna’s reciprocation, is an ever-increasing process. A devotee therefore always seeks opportunities to increase his surrender. We are not free of material desires. We can pray to Krsna for shelter against them. If we think we surrendered five or ten or fifteen years ago, and that we have no further need to surrender, then it is likely that those desires will arise in the heart and we will become attracted to them. We need Krsna’s constant protection.

Sometimes devotees imagined that they surrendered everything when they moved into the temple. Usually they have only surrendered their hair. Krsna wants everything. Those who don’t understand it, tend to let their hair grow back. They think there is no more need to shave it, because they have already surrendered it upon moving into the temple. This is foolishness. Krsna wants everything, especially our independence. If we don’t surrender our independence and try to remain outside the Lord’s authority, patting ourselves on the back because we are wearing three strands of neck beads, we may be in trouble.

Part of our surrendering process is to become disciples. A disciple is one who lives under the guru’s authority. It means he is a submissive servant, always ready to render service to please guru and Krsna. If we want to receive the Lord’s full protection, we must fully renounce our independence. Only fully surrendered souls receive the full protection of the Lord.

A fully surrendered soul is one who gives body, mind, words, and all twenty-four hours in a day to Krsna. Such devotees conquer both time and Krsna. They transcend the material world because they have given everything they have, including themselves, to the Lord’s service. Krsna says He becomes conquered by those who love Him fully.

Therefore, we must always feel the impetus to continue the surrendering process and then take every opportunity to take shelter of the Supreme Authority. That is the only way we will receive the Lord’s full protection. Krsna promises in *Bhagavad-gita* that if we surrender unto Him, He will protect us. Full surrender will capture His attention and we will be safe.

## Devotional service is liberated activity

SB [3.15.48](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/15.48)

**“Persons who are very expert and most intelligent in understanding things as they are, engage in hearing narrations of the auspicious activities and pastimes of the Lord, which are worth chanting and worth hearing. Such persons do not care even for the highest material benediction—namely liberation—what to say of other less important benedictions like the material happiness of the heavenly kingdom. ” (SB** [**3.15.48**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/15.48)**)**

Devotional service is liberated activity. Anyone who becomes attracted to devotional service, even for a moment, can begin to approach pure devotion. The opportunity to engage in devotional service is made available by devotees. Therefore, association with devotees is extremely important. Lord Caitanya quotes a verse from *Hari-bhakti-sudhodaya:* “O greatly learned devotee, although there are many faults in this material world, there is one good opportunity—the association with devotees. Such association brings about great happiness. Due to this good quality, our strong desire to merge into the Brahman effulgence has become weak. ”

Why? Because through the association of devotees we become attracted to devotion. This is what devotee association delivers. Devotional service given by devotees is the most attractive activity. Simply by coming in contact with it, we hanker for more.

## What do we perceive as mercy?

**SB** [**1.8.29**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/8.29) **Boston, January 1998**

*na veda kascid bhagavams cikirsitam*  
*tavehamanasya nrnam vidambanam*  
*na yasya kascid dayito ’sti karhicid*  
*dvesyas ca yasmin visama matir nrnam*

**“O Lord, no one can understand Your transcendental pastimes, which appear to be human and are so misleading. You have no specific object of favor, nor do You have any object of envy. People only imagine that You are partial. ” (SB** [**1.8.29**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/8.29)**)**

PURPORT

The Lord’s mercy upon the fallen souls is equally distributed. He has no one as the specific object of hostility. The very conception of the Personality of Godhead as a human being is misleading. His pastimes *appear* to be exactly like a human being’s, but actually they are transcendental and without any tinge of material contamination. He is undoubtedly known as partial to His pure devotees, but in fact He is never partial, as much as the sun is never partial to anyone. By utilizing the sun rays, sometimes even the stones become valuable, whereas a blind man cannot see the sun, although there are enough sun rays before him. Darkness and light are two opposite conceptions, but this does not mean that the sun is partial in distributing its rays. The sun rays are open to everyone, but the capacities of the receptacles differ. Foolish people think that devotional service is flattering the Lord to get special mercy. Factually the pure devotees who are engaged in the transcendental loving service of the Lord are not a mercantile community. A mercantile house renders service to someone in exchange for values. The pure devotee does not render service unto the Lord for such exchange, and therefore the full mercy of the Lord is open for him. Suffering and needy men, inquisitive persons or philosophers make temporary connections with the Lord to serve a particular purpose. When the purpose is served, there is no more relation with the Lord. A suffering man, if he is pious at all, prays to the Lord for his recovery. But as soon as the recovery is over, in most cases the suffering man no longer cares to keep any connection with the Lord. The mercy of the Lord is open for him, but he is reluctant to receive it. That is the difference between a pure devotee and a mixed devotee. Those who are completely against the service of the Lord are considered to be in abject darkness, those who ask for the Lord’s favor only at the time of necessity are partial recipients of the mercy of the Lord, and those who are cent percent engaged in the service of the Lord are full recipients of the mercy of the Lord. Such partiality in receiving the Lord’s mercy is relative to the recipient, and it is not due to the partiality of the all-merciful Lord.

When the Lord descends on this material world by His allmerciful energy, He plays like a human being, and therefore it appears that the Lord is partial to His devotees only, but that is not a fact. Despite such apparent manifestation of partiality, His mercy is equally distributed. In the Battlefield of Kuruksetra all persons who died in the fight before the presence of the Lord got salvation without the necessary qualifications because death before the presence of the Lord purifies the passing soul from the effects of all sins, and therefore the dying man gets a place somewhere in the transcendental abode. Somehow or other if someone puts himself open in the sun rays, he is sure to get the requisite benefit both by heat and by ultraviolet rays. Therefore, the conclusion is that the Lord is never partial. It is wrong for the people in general to think of Him as partial.

This is a prayer by Queen Kunti. While reading prayers, it is good to understand the motivation of the person offering them. As described in this verse and purport, people sometimes consider devotees a mercantile community, which serves the Lord in exchange for something. Part of serving the Lord is prayer, and when we think of prayer, we usually think of “petition. ” Prayers *are* often requests, but what does a devotee request? What is it we want from the Supreme Lord?

The purport assures us that Krsna is merciful and that He distributes His mercy equally to all conditioned souls. The ability to receive His mercy is based on the quality of the receptacle. Although people may have different opinions about the nature of God’s mercy, a devotee understands that Krsna’s mercy is present in everything that happens. It’s easy to think that when we receive what we want, we have received Krsna’s mercy, but we often find it a little harder to categorize difficulties as the mercy of the Lord.
