Our tendency is to try to find another explanation. The Lord is inclined to bestow His mercy upon His devotees, and that mercy comes in ways that will inevitably draw us toward Him, even if that means we must experience something we don’t consider pleasant.

We lament when we receive something we didn’t expect. It is said that devotees never lament. This is because even when devotees receive something they didn’t expect, they will still accept it as Krsna’s mercy. When we accept things in that spirit, we remain equipoised in happiness and distress and are not disturbed. Therefore, we also say devotees are *titiksa,* tolerant.

So how the Lord’s mercy is everywhere will be perceived differently by devotees and nondevotees. A nondevotee may look at a devotee receiving mercy and feel sorry for him, but a devotee remembers Krsna’s words:

**“The Personality of Godhead said: If I especially favor someone, I gradually deprive him of his wealth. Then the relatives and friends of such a poverty-stricken man abandon him. In this way he suffers one distress after another. When he becomes frustrated in his attempts to make money and instead befriends My devotees, I bestow My special mercy upon him. A person who has thus become sober fully realizes the Absolute as the highest truth, the most subtle and perfect manifestation of spirit, the transcendental existence without end. In this way realizing that the Supreme Truth is the foundation of his own existence, he is freed from the cycle of material life. ” (SB** [**10.88.8-10**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/88.8)**)**

If this happens to devotees, they will think, “Yes, Krsna is so kind. He has placed me in a situation where He has left me with nothing except Himself. ” Devotees consider this the highest form of mercy; because when devotees are fully dependent upon Krsna, He will protect them. Nondevotees may see misfortune even in such a situation; they cannot understand the inner realization of a devotee.

Sometimes we see Vaisnavas who externally appear impoverished or materially unfortunate, and we are amazed at how peaceful they are at heart. Their peace comes from taking advantage of their suffering to constantly remember Krsna. Devotees can be happy in any condition.

There was once a *brahmana* who lived as a beggar. He happily accepted whatever came in the form of alms, which he would beg door to door at the homes of the poorest people. He remained satisfied to offer whatever he had received to Krsna, and to constantly chant His names. This *brahmana* remained peaceful despite his poverty. He had received Krsna’s mercy. The Lord is kind and merciful to persons who simply remain satisfied to be engaged in His service and who make no unnecessary endeavors to enjoy the material world. The Lord reciprocates with such persons by giving Himself to them.

An outsider may mock a devotee who has received Krsna in this way, thinking he is living dependent upon others, but that is only because outsiders cannot see the devotee’s internal relationship with Krsna.

Although we may not be living in such poverty as this *brahmana,* we can think of our own begging activities when we go out on the street to distribute Krsna conscious literature. Our main purpose in such “begging” is to introduce Krsna consciousness to others and to give them an opportunity to learn about God. People look at us and think that we are simply begging for money because we are poor. But this is not true. We accept whatever money comes in, but our primary purpose is to provide God conscious education. It is natural that nondevotees cannot see our inner purpose. They look at the devotees and wonder, “What are these people getting out of this? ” They also tend to mock us: “Why don’t you do something with your life? Why don’t you get a job! ” Sometimes people ask us how much we are paid for book distribution, and when they hear that we volunteer our services, they imagine that we are being exploited. I was once asked this question when I was distributing books.

It was pouring rain that day, and my coat was soaked through. It didn’t matter to me; I was blissful distributing books. When I told my questioner that I was a volunteer, he was shocked and replied, “You’re crazy! ” That man could not understand the mercy I was receiving.

What is real mercy? The opportunity to engage in the Lord’s service. This is not as easily attained as it would seem, and it is impossible to achieve for someone absorbed in the desire for sense gratification. Krsna explains in *Bhagavad-gita* ([2.44](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/2.44)): “In the mind of those who are too attached to sense enjoyment and material opulence, and who are bewildered by such things, the resolute determination for devotional service to the Supreme Lord does not take place. ”

It is inconceivable for nondevotees to understand how anyone can act without expecting something in return. Where we see service opportunity, they see only a black hole—no return for their work. They cannot perceive mercy and do not understand the internal workings of a person who has received it. It is all a mystery to them. They think “mercy” or “reciprocation from God” means a good bank balance, a nice house, a new car, or other material assets. This is what they expect in return for their “service. ” Therefore Prabhupada writes, “Foolish people think that devotional service is flattering the Lord to get special mercy. ” They consider the Lord an ordinary person. Queen Kunti states, “No one can understand Your transcendental pastimes, which appear to be human and so misleading. ”

Can Krsna be flattered? In ordinary dealings, sometimes we flatter others to manipulate them. When a person is flattered, he may think favorably toward the flatterer and be more inclined to satisfy him. When a flatterer then expresses his desire, the person he was flattering may feel used. “Is *that* all he wanted? He didn’t really want to please me and thus spoke untruthful words to make me inclined to give him what he wanted. ” But Krsna is in everyone’s heart and knows the desires we each possess better than we know them ourselves. If someone tries to flatter the Lord, the Lord is not fooled. He doesn’t have to wait two weeks, three weeks, two months until we come out and ask for what we really want. He already knows what we want. He knows the desires within our heart. He does not become enamored by flattery, nor does He fall into illusion. Rather, He is all-knowing, omniscient. The Lord cannot be purchased by flattery.

A devotee does not glorify the Lord to flatter Him, but rather to honestly praise Him. A devotee knows the Lord’s position as well as his own. He glorifies the Lord because He is full of wonderful qualities and pastimes, and because it is his nature to praise. It is the natural position of every living entity to glorify God. Those in the material world have forgotten their natural position and discuss only mundane subject matter. If the urge to glorify arises, they tend to glorify themselves. The desire to glorify is an innate part of human nature, but it is important to glorify the person most worthy of receiving it. That is, of course, the Supreme Lord.

We glorify Krsna because He is supreme. That means we have to understand Krsna’s position. As Krsna says, “I am the source of all spiritual and material worlds. Everything emanates from Me. The wise who perfectly know this engage in My devotional service and worship Me with all their hearts. ” (Bg. [10.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/10.8))

Devotees have no illusions about God’s nature. Being situated in knowledge, they realize that God is great and they are tiny, and that He is supreme and they are His servants. This is just the way it is. It is absolute truth and cannot be changed. When we try to change it, we must enter into illusion. For those who do not choose illusion, praising Krsna is spontaneous.

Krsna has given us each a tongue so that we may speak about Him, glorify Him, and offer Him prayers as Queen Kunti is doing. Queen Kunti’s prayers are the prayers of pure devotion; there is no hook at the end, no material request. She is not flattering Krsna, but speaking of her heart’s understanding. Krsna is not standing there listening to her prayers thinking, “Yeah, yeah, I know. She’s just offering all these prayers because she wants something. ” He recognizes the purity of her devotion and reciprocates with it.

Because Queen Kunti is offering her prayers in full knowledge of the Lord’s position, we glorify these prayers. When someone prays in pure devotion, it is not only the Lord who is glorified by them. The person offering such prayers becomes glorious as well. Therefore, we like to hear and recite the prayers of the pure devotees—the recipients of Krsna’s full mercy.

The Lord may or may not accept the prayers of those who want something material in return. By His divine arrangement, everything goes on perfectly; He fulfills their desires, if He chooses, through the agency of the material nature. The Lord doesn’t have to become personally involved. Yet He feels obliged to give Himself to His pure devotees.

Queen Kunti states, “O Lord, no one can understand Your transcendental pastimes, which appear to be human and are so misleading. You have no specific object of favor, nor do You have any object of envy. People only imagine that You are partial. ” In the seventh canto of the *Bhagavatam,* Pariksit Maharaja asks Sukadeva Gosvami about the Lord’s partiality. It seemed to him that the Lord had been partial because He acted in Indra’s interest when He killed Hiranyakasipu and Hiranyaksa. Sukadeva Gosvami replied that the Lord is always impartial, but that He favors His devotees. The *Bhagavad-gita* confirms, “I envy no one, nor am I partial to anyone. I am equal to all. But whoever renders service unto Me in devotion is a friend, is in Me, and I am also a friend to him. ” (Bg. [9.29](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.29))

Krsna is neutral in the sense that He simply reciprocates according to the attitude of those who approach Him. The Supersoul is situated within the heart of every living being, and He pervades all things just as fire permeates wood. Therefore, it appears that He acts differently although He is the absolute one without a second. He appears to act differently just as fire in wood appears to act according to the nature of the wood. In wet wood, the fire is dull and smoking; when the wood is dry, the fire is bright and hot. When the piece of wood is small, the fire will be small; when the piece of wood is large, the fire will be large. But fire is fire. Similarly, although the Supreme Lord is one, He behaves differently according to the desires of the persons who approach Him.

In that sense, He is like a judge. A judge may sentence one person and reward another. He is the same person, but he acts differently according to the person who happens to sit before him in the courtroom. The example given in *Bhagavad-gita* is rain. Rain is not responsible for the different kinds of vegetation on the planet, although the vegetation could not exist without rain. Similarly, the Supreme Lord is not responsible for the varieties of life’s conditions. He has arranged material nature in such a way that the living entities themselves are responsible. Those who are completely against the Lord’s service remain in darkness. Those who ask for the Lord’s favor only when they are distressed receive His partial mercy. Those who are cent percent engaged in His service receive His full mercy. This apparent partiality in distributing mercy is actually in relation to the ability of the living entities to receive it.

But from the external viewpoint, it appears that the Lord is ordinary because He is partial to His devotees. Actually, He gives the devotees His mercy because they are so dependent upon Him. Krsna guarantees that anyone who engages in His full loving service will be delivered from the pangs of material existence. The assurance the devotee feels in that regard is symptomized by his peaceful state; he is happy in any condition. “Devotees solely engaged in the devotional service of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Narayana, never fear any condition of life. For them the heavenly planets, liberation and the hellish planets are all the same, for such devotees are interested only in the service of the Lord. ” (SB [6.17.28](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/17.28)) The essence of such devotees’ prayers is the request for pure devotional service. They ask, “Now after offering suitable prayers, I have only one request: please engage me in Your loving devotional service. Please allow me to be in the association of Your devotees. ”

Krsna’s mercy is available to all; He has no specific object of His hostility. The *Bhagavad-gita* tells us that He comes to annihilate miscreants, and although that appears to be another act of partiality, whether Krsna is killing demons or bestowing benedictions upon His devotees, He is simply giving everyone exactly what they want. Therefore, the meaning behind Queen Kunti’s verse is that although Krsna appears human, He cannot be flattered. He responds to pure devotion. He is never like a politician who has been flattered and voted into office and must now fulfill his campaign promises.

I had a humorous experience in this regard. I was in a place called Vinnitza with several other devotees for a meeting with the mayor. This meeting took place just before election time, and the mayor couldn’t do anything before election. We sat down with him and he asked us what we wanted. We reminded him that he had previously offered to give us facility for our Food-for-Life distribution. Within minutes, he slammed his fist on the table and said, “O. K., that’s it. Elections are in three months. We’ll see about it then! ” He was thinking that by promising us the facility, we would arrange for all the devotees to vote for him, which would have added significant support to his side. After the election, I asked the devotees if he had given the facility and they said no.

Krsna doesn’t need our votes to become God, and neither does He renege on His promises. He is never distracted by upcoming elections, afraid that He may lose His position. All Krsna wants from us is our love. Therefore, Srila Prabhupada explains in one purport that if the Supreme Personality of Godhead asks for something, it is that we offer Him a leaf, flower, or fruit with devotion. Krsna *is* asking for something, and therefore it appears that He is dependent upon us. But He doesn’t need our love; He simply wants it. He likes to become dependent upon His devotees in loving exchange. That is how He enjoys. He is very much inclined to reciprocate in a personal way with those who love Him.

And Krsna reciprocates in such personal ways that when we hear about them, our hearts become attracted and we feel new inspiration and enthusiasm. We realize that if we could actually practice pure devotional service, we would be fully satisfied.

Sanatana Gosvami taught this principle in one pastime. A beggar heard that Sanatana Gosvami had a valuable touchstone in his possession, but that Sanatana Gosvami kept the touchstone in the rubbish heap. He decided to ask for the touchstone. Sanatana Gosvami gave it easily. While the beggar was walking away with the stone, he wondered why Sanatana Gosvami had kept such a valuable object in the trash. It occurred to him that Sanatana must have something even more valuable. He returned to Sanatana Gosvami and asked what he possessed that could possibly be worth more than the touchstone. Sanatana said he would share his treasure, but first the beggar had to throw the touchstone in the river. Sanatana Gosvami was expert at using everything in Krsna’s service, so he used this touchstone to demonstrate that there is something greater than material opulence. After the beggar had thrown the touchstone into the river, Sanatana Gosvami gave him the Lord’s holy name.

## Chanting the Holy Name

## What is the necessity of receiving the holy name in disciplic succession?

**Initiation Novosibirsk, October 13, 1994**

For the living entity’s gradual advancement, it is essential that he accept the holy name in disciplic succession. By chanting the Lord’s holy name, he will gradually become purified and attain the chanting’s perfection: pure love of God. Pure love of God is the ultimate goal of chanting. As stated in *Caitanya-caritamrta, bahu janma kare yadi Sravana kirtana:* “If one is infested with the ten offenses in the chanting of the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra,* despite his endeavor to chant the holy name for many births, he will not get love of Godhead, which is the ultimate goal of this chanting. ”

Chanting the holy name in disciplic succession is a sufficient endeavor in our attempt to attain pure Krsna consciousness. Although we can chant the holy name without having been initiated and receive benefit, if we actually want to attain pure love of God, we must receive the holy name in disciplic succession.

Chanting the holy name can deliver us from all sinful reactions. Srila Prabhupada often quoted the *Kali-santarana Upanisad* to teach this point. It states, “Hare Krsna, Hare Krsna, Krsna Krsna, Hare Hare/ Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare—these sixteen names composed of thirty-two syllables are the only means to counteract the evil effects of Kali-yuga. In all the *Vedas* it is said that to cross the ocean of nescience there is no alternative to the chanting of the holy name. ” The only way we can become free from the sinful effects of Kali-yuga is to chant the Lord’s holy name. “Sinful effects” includes the tendency to act sinfully.

Of course, we understand that becoming free from the reactions to sin is not the ultimate goal of chanting; it is simply an early byproduct of chanting. It is said that the chanting has two secondary effects: freedom from sinful reaction and liberation. Pure devotees deride the concept of liberation, considering it no better than going to hell. Pure devotees are not interested in achieving freedom from sinful reaction either. Usually, people want liberation and relief from reactions so that they no longer have to suffer, but a devotee, though willing to tolerate the reactions of his past sins, knows that sinful desires are obstacles which prevent him from pleasing the Lord. He wants to become free from them. Pure devotees are desireless; they want only to serve Krsna. Krsna has already assured His devotees that if they surrender to Him, He will protect them from sinful reactions. The pure devotees don’t feel the need to strive for such ends.

Krsna’s promise to relieve His devotees from sinful reactions comes at the end of *Bhagavad-gita.* The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* begins at that point. It is understood that when we surrender, we become free from sinful life. What do we do with our freedom? We are meant to enter the realm of devotional service. By continually cleansing the heart from the dirt accumulated for lifetimes, we try to approach Krsna in pure love and to detach ourselves from the desires that created our material reactions in the first place.

If we do not clean our hearts of material desires, we may again engage in sinful activity. The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* explains, “Only a rare person who has adopted complete, unalloyed devotional service to Krsna can uproot the weeds of sinful actions with no possibility that they will revive. He can do this simply by discharging devotional service, just as the sun can immediately dissipate fog by its rays. ”

Sukadeva Gosvami stresses the importance of uprooting sinful desires as opposed to simply freeing oneself from reactions, because when material desires are uprooted, there is no possibility that they will revive. The example given is that when we burn creepers at the base of a tree, we destroy the visible plant, but if the root remains in the soil, it will grow to attack the tree again.

One way we eradicate sinful desires is to restrain ourselves from committing sinful activities. As soon as we commit sinful activities again, their reactions again begin to accumulate. Therefore, no one should consider his business finished when he frees himself from sinful reactions. Again, this freedom is only a byproduct of chanting.

Freedom from sinful desires while chanting is called *namabhasa.* Before the sun is fully manifest in the sky, it glows on the horizon. This light is capable of dispelling darkness, but the sun has not yet risen. Similarly, when we chant during the stage of clearing sinful reactions, the holy name manifests like the sun just under the horizon. The goal is to bring on the full sunrise; not to be satisfied with the sun below the horizon.

These points are clearly discussed in the conversation between Haridasa Thakura and the scholars who assembled at Balarama Acarya’s house to discuss the glories of the holy name. Haridasa Thakura insisted that liberation and freedom from sinful reactions were not the true glory of the holy name. Instead, the true glory would manifest when the name was received in disciplic succession from one capable of giving the name, and when it was chanted with pure love. For those not yet at the platform of pure love, continuous chanting of the holy name will help clear the offenses that prevent them from attaining it.

In other places in scripture, continuous chanting is thought of as watering the *bhakti* creeper. Sometimes the weeds of material desire attack the plant, and often, these desires can grow up more quickly than the *bhakti-lata* itself and choke it. When the devotional creeper is choked, it cannot flourish or produce pure love of God.

There are ten offenses in chanting, and we tend to commit these offenses out of material attachment. Therefore, we have to cleanse the heart of attachments. Not only must we become freed of gross attachments, but all the subtle ones too. In the first verse of His *Siksastaka,* Lord Caitanya wrote, “Let there be all victory for the chanting of the holy name of Lord Krsna, which can cleanse the mirror of the heart and stop the miseries of the blazing fire of material existence. That chanting is the waxing moon that spreads the white lotus of good fortune for all living entities. It is the life and soul of all education. The chanting of the holy name of Krsna expands the blissful ocean of transcendental life. It gives a cooling effect to everyone and enables one to taste full nectar at every step. ” We should accept Lord Caitanya’s mercy. Lord Caitanya distributed pure love of Godhead by distributing the pure holy name. Everything distributed by Lord Caitanya is completely pure, invested with the full potency to deliver love of God. That He did this is part of Lord Caitanya’s inconceivable nature and the reason behind His appearance in this world. We say “inconceivable” because no other incarnation showed such mercy. Lord Caitanya made the process of *bhakti* accessible in this age. Although previously *bhakti* was strictly confidential and not easily achieved, Lord Caitanya made it free and accessible through the Lord’s holy name.

Lord Caitanya compared Himself to a transcendental gardener. “How many fruits can I alone eat? How many can I distribute Myself? ” Lord Caitanya ordered that we all take up the distribution of the holy name so that the whole world can attain pure love of God.

Everything Lord Caitanya distributed was fully potent and could bestow upon the recipient pure love of God. Therefore, He has been described as the most munificent incarnation *(namo mahavadanyaya, krsna-prema-pradaya te).*

He prays, “Your holy name alone can render all benediction to the living beings and thus You have hundreds and millions of names like Krsna and Govinda. In these transcendental names You invested all of Your transcendental energies, and there are not even hard and fast rules for chanting these holy names. O, My Lord, out of Your kindness You have made Yourself so easily accessible by Your holy names, but I am so unfortunate that I have no attraction for them. ”

In the age of Kali, the Supreme Personality of Godhead has incarnated in His holy name. Anyone who associates with this name has the fortune of associating with Krsna Himself.

We should associate with the Lord by chanting. It is our duty— those of us who are coming in Lord Caitanya’s disciplic succession— to receive what He has given, to try to chant the name without offenses, and to distribute the name to others. In this way we will not only become free from the reactions of sinful life and achieve liberation, but we will attain pure love of God, the ultimate goal of the chanting.

We give the holy name to a disciple when he or she is initiated. Initiation is different from the other *samskaras* in that it requires more. It requires practicing *tapa,* austerity, first, then marking the body with Vaisnava *tilaka,* then receiving *nama.* Chanting the holy name will help the disciple recognize him or herself as Krsna’s servant. Later, the disciple receives a mantra. This is called *pancasamskara,* five *samskaras,* all meant to situate us on the platform of *bhajana-kriya.*

*Bhajana-kriya* is the third stage on the path toward *prema,* and it comes after *Sraddha* and *sadhu-sanga.* We attain it when we have learned to hear with a certain amount of faith and developed a desire to make progress in spiritual life. Such hearing helps us become situated in a mood of worship of the Supreme Lord. Gradually, from this point, we will progress through *anartha-nivrtti,* the struggle to free ourselves from unwanted things, and move further into *bhakti.*

## How can we overcome mechanical chanting?

**SB** [**4.30.3**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/30.3) **Belgium, May 1990**

This is one of those “how do you do it” questions, and most of them have the same answer: you do it by doing it. We are mechanical because we do not listen attentively. We can do anything in devotional service mechanically, whether we’re chanting rounds, preaching, distributing books, cooking, or worshipping the Deity. We are mechanical in our practices because we misunderstand what the process is. We think that simply by following rules and regulations, we can achieve success. The fact is that anyone who follows the rules and regulations without understanding that the purpose behind them is to remember Krsna is wasting his time. We may perform so many duties, but if they don’t evoke attraction for constant remembrance of Krsna, then we are simply wasting our time.

Therefore, we have to be very careful in *all* of our activities— not only our chanting—to be sure we don’t be ritualists. Devotional service is not simply a ritual we perform to become purified. We actually have to see its connection with becoming Krsna conscious. The essence of all the rules and regulations is to remember Krsna. Chanting is not a mechanical activity but the most potent activity for remembering Krsna. It is the method by which we can advance in spiritual life in this age. Associating with the Lord’s holy name is as good as associating with the Lord Himself.

We have been given the opportunity to associate directly with the Lord! Such an experience is available to us, yet we turn our chanting into a thoughtless ritual, simply moving our fingers on the beads for two hours a day to make sure we get our rounds done. We think the pinnacle of success is that we have finished our rounds!

Our success is not to complete our rounds, but rather to remember Krsna. Chanting therefore has to be a conscious and deliberate practice to worship the Lord. Don’t reduce it to a ritual.

Chanting should teach us how to become dependent on Krsna in everything we do. To chant properly, we have to control the mind and senses, we have to listen attentively to the sound vibration, and we have to focus our minds on Krsna. Sometimes we excuse ourselves by saying that we’re thinking of our service, and that’s just as good as thinking of Krsna since it’s all one, but that is not much of an excuse.

If we want to avoid becoming mechanical, we must become deliberate, conscious of what we are doing, ready to hear attentively, and to remain fixed on Krsna’s name. Krsna will help us if we try. We cannot be successful by our own endeavor; we must invoke the Lord’s mercy by our sincere attempt and dependence upon Him. When Krsna sees our determination, He will give us a more direct experience of Krsna consciousness. We should not follow rules and regulations simply for the sake of following them, but for fulfilling the purpose behind them.

## The treasure of love of God descends from the spiritual world

**SB** [**3.26.6**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/26.6) **Novosibirsk, November 1996**

There are many reasons for the Lord’s descent, but to fully understand them we must apply spiritual intelligence. These reasons cannot be understood with the material mind, because transcendental subject matter is inconceivable. The Lord’s pastimes are inconceivable. We may approach the Lord’s pastimes with our mind and intelligence, but if we want to realize them fully, we must ourselves become fully realized. That is why when Prabhupada speaks about the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra*, he states that it is beyond the mind and intelligence. The Hare Krsna *maha-mantra* has its origin in the spiritual world, just as Krsna has His origin in the spiritual world. When the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra* descends to the material world (*golokera prema-dhana hari-nama-sankirtana)* it does not become a material sound vibration: “The treasure of love of God has descended from the spiritual world of Goloka Vrndavana. ” *(Prarthana)*

## We must chant without offense

**SB** [**3.18.43**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/18.43) **St. Petersburg, September 16, 1996**

There are ten offenses that can be committed when chanting the holy name. If we want to achieve pure love of God, we must free ourselves from all of them. If we maintain material attachments even after hearing so many instructions on the matter, those attachments will choke our devotional creeper.

Of course, we know that whatever progress a devotee makes is permanent, and even if his creeper is temporarily choked, it will not die. Having once tasted the nectar of Krsna’s lotus feet, the devotee will never be able to forget the ecstasy.

Nonetheless, we should remain vigilant against the ten offenses. Therefore, when Srila Prabhupada gave his disciples the holy name at their initiation, he always emphasized the need to recite the ten offenses.

We can become free from the ten offenses by continuous chanting. The *Padma Purana* states that the chanting of the Hare Krsna mantra is recommended even for those who chant with offenses, because if we continue to chant, the holy name itself will purify us and we will gradually become free of our offensiveness.

In order to be qualified to chant continuously, we must receive the pure holy name from the Lord through the disciplic succession. Then we should systematically uproot the offenses. We should hear the offenses as if they were warning signs. Sometimes while driving on the highway we pass flashing signs that indicate danger ahead. When we see the warning signs, we tend to be more cautious. Hearing the offenses recited should make us cautious about committing them.

For example, the first offense is to blaspheme those devotees who have dedicated their lives to propagating the Lord’s holy name.

We know this, and so we should be careful not to commit this offense. If we unnecessarily criticize devotees, we are affecting our chanting.

When Gopala Capala criticized Srivasa Thakura, he suffered from an attack of leprosy. Although he begged Lord Caitanya to relieve him, Lord Caitanya would not. Instead He told him He would not consider relieving him for ten million births. The Lord took the offense toward His devotee that seriously. Gopala Capala then realized what he had done. The next time he saw Lord Caitanya, he once again sought the Lord’s instruction. This time Lord Caitanya instructed him to fall at the feet of the Vaisnava he had offended and beg forgiveness. Srivasa Thakura forgave Gopala Capala, who then became free of his sinful reactions.

This story illustrates that it is a grave offense to criticize a devotee who has dedicated his or her life to chanting the holy name and giving the holy name to others. Even if we unknowingly criticize a devotee we will suffer a reaction.

There once was a *brahmana* who approached Caitanya Mahaprabhu. He had previously spoken critically of the devotees, but having seen Lord Caitanya, he realized that he had been mistaken. All he had said was that the Vaisnavas were chanting like sentimentalists in the street, but when he saw Lord Caitanya, he recognized His pure love of God. He realized that what he had said in ignorance was an offense and he fell at Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s feet and begged forgiveness. He asked, “How can I become free from having unknowingly criticized the Vaisnavas? ” Lord Caitanya responded, “Just as a person who drinks poison can counteract its effect by drinking the nectar of immortality, so your ignorant criticism of the devotees was poison; it will have a poisonous effect, and you have to counteract it. With the same tongue you used to criticize the devotees, you should now use to glorify them with transcendental hymns, prayers and songs. Always glorify the Lord. This is how you drink the nectar of immortality and it will save you. ”

## How can we avoid losing our taste for devotional service?

**SB** [**3.29.9**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/29.9) **Dnepropetrovsk, June 1996**

That we are in danger of losing our taste is why the *acaryas* have so closely defined the ten offenses to the holy name. The offenses are like caution signs on the road. They warn us of the sharp turns and sudden bumps on the road. We may think that all we have to do is remain on the road, but if a sign warns us of danger, we can take advantage of the warning to protect ourselves from danger.

If you want to maintain your taste in devotional service, continue chanting, but don’t ignore the ten offenses as if they don’t exist. Take the warning that they indicate and avoid them. The Lord knows there will be obstacles on the path. Proceed with caution and do not commit offenses. This will help us travel a smoother road. If you miss the signs that indicate curves, we might drive straight and fall off the road. We have to pay attention so that we stay on course.

## Chanting is our meditation

**Bg.** [**8.8**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/8.8) **Kiev, October 1990**

Chanting Hare Krsna is not an activity performed only in the morning when we pick up our *japa* beads. It is something we dedicate our lives to. We are chanting by all our activities.

Krsna also explains that we must remain undeviated in our determination, our mind fixed on the goal by constant meditation. Krsna assures us that if we maintain our determination to reach Him, we will succeed. Srila Prabhupada describes such meditation as meditation on the Lord’s name. He calls it “mystic-meditation” and tells us that it is easy to practice. Chanting will enable us to gain the Lord’s association.

**“O King, constant chanting of the holy name of the Lord after the ways of the great authorities is the doubtless and fearless way of success for all, including those who are free from all material desires, those who are desirous of all material enjoyment, and also those who are self-satisfied by dint of transcendental knowledge. ” (SB** [**2.1.11**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/1.11)**)**

Therefore, it is important when practicing devotional service to link all our activities to chanting. Srila Prabhupada describes in the *Caitanya-caritamrta* how one who is engaged in the process of devotional service is constantly serving the holy name. Serving the holy name means dedicating ourselves not only to private chanting but to the distribution of the name. A devotee’s life is centered around chanting Hare Krsna because the devotee knows that the holy name and the Lord are nondifferent. A devotee has faith that by constantly endeavoring to serve Krsna, he will reach his desired destination.

Thus, constant chanting is the most basic activity in our Krsna conscious life. The *Bhagavatam* states, “My dear King, although Kali-yuga is an ocean of faults, there is still one good quality about this age: Simply by chanting the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra*, one can become free from material bondage and be promoted to the transcendental kingdom. ” (SB [12.3.51](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-12/3.51))

Chanting Hare Krsna is not just an activity to be performed in the morning, but all day long. Krsna tells Arjuna to perform his prescribed duties and to always think of Him. This means that while performing our duties, we should chant Hare Krsna. There was once a devotee whose service was to sweep the sidewalk in front of the New York temple. Srila Prabhupada was preparing to leave New York, so he walked outside and saw this devotee sweeping the sidewalk and chanting Hare Krsna. Srila Prabhupada pointed him out to the other devotees and said, “This is perfection. He is rendering service to Krsna and at the same time chanting Hare Krsna. ”

Srila Prabhupada was anxious to see his disciples glorifying Krsna. He wanted to hear them chanting the Lord’s holy names. When Prabhupada was preparing to leave this world, he immersed himself in hearing the sounds of the holy name. Many of the disciples who surrounded him were chanting, and when Srila Prabhupada actually left, he departed while bathing in the nectar of the holy name. Therefore, when Krsna says, “Whoever at the end of his life, quits his body, remembering Me alone, at once attains My nature. Of this there is no doubt, ” we can understand that Srila Prabhupada returned to the spiritual world.

Srila Prabhupada describes in many places that by chanting Hare Krsna constantly, devotees can become purified and go to the kingdom of God. Chanting is our meditation. We may think that the purpose of meditation is to remove all disturbing elements from the environment and to become peaceful, but the essence of meditation is to fix the consciousness on Krsna. Because the mind is fickle, we must use force in the beginning to bring it under control. We do not have much attraction to Krsna initially, even though Krsna is all-attractive and can attract everyone. Those of us who are conditioned do not have spontaneous attraction even to the all-attractive Lord. We must force our minds to submit. Then we can chant constantly.

We force our mind to be attentive while we chant, and throughout the day we must force our mind to think of Krsna while we engage in our service. How do we force our mind? We think only of Krsna’s service, Krsna’s pleasure. Service is also nondifferent from Krsna. When a devotee thinks of his service, he is thinking of Krsna. When a devotee hears about Krsna, he is thinking of Krsna. When a devotee speaks about Krsna, he is thinking of Krsna. There are nine processes of devotional service, beginning with hearing and chanting about Krsna. Hearing is the beginning, but then comes chanting. Think of Krsna’s service, and chant His holy name. In that way we will capture the mind and be able to remember the Lord.

## Kiev, April 29, 2001

**How can we overcome the five obstacles to** ***nisthita-bhakti?***

We have been speaking about how to make chanting the pure holy name the primary goal of life. We also spoke about the meaning of *kirtaniyah sada harih* and what it means to chant the holy name constantly. Of course, the words *kirtaniyah sada harih* come from the third verse of Lord Caitanya’s *Siksastaka.* We also spoke about His words, *trnad api sunicena taror api sahisnuna amanina manadena.* But that did not complete our discussion on the verse. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati called this third *Siksastaka* verse the *siddha-pranali* of Gaudiya Vaisnavas. *Pranali* means “the medium by which one achieves, ” and *siddha* means “perfection. ” Sometimes people think that *siddha-pranali* is achieved simply by receiving a mantra. They think that we can immediately know our *siddha-svarupa,* our eternal relationship with the Lord, without proper service.

But the third verse of the *Siksastaka* says differently. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati calls this verse our *siddha-pranali* because it’s not simply by receiving a mantra that we qualify ourselves for such high realization; we must develop the character and qualities necessary to achieve perfection. In other words, we cannot think we have achieved perfection if we are devoid of humility. If we are incapable of giving respect to others, if we are intolerant, full of false pride or prestige, we should know that we are exemplifying the antithesis of perfection. What is perfection? *Kirtaniyah sada harih,* to glorify the Lord twenty-four hours a day.

The chanting of the holy name is the means to achieve this perfection. If we want to achieve constant chanting, we must develop the qualities listed in this verse. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that the *Siksastaka* prayers are related to Rupa Gosvami’s verse describing gradual progress from *Sraddha* to *prema.* Rupa Gosvami’s description of those stages is actually a commentary on the *Siksastaka.*

It has been said that the first verse of the *Siksastaka* is meant to instill faith in the Lord’s holy name. The second verse describes the characteristics of *sadhu-sanga, bhajana-kriya,* and *anartha-nivrtti.* That is, while chanting the Lord’s holy name, we must become free from offenses. As Caitanya Mahaprabhu states, “I am so unfortunate that I still commit offenses in chanting and therefore I have no attraction for the holy name. ”

The third verse of the *Siksastaka* describes the characteristics of *nisthita-bhakti.* When a person comes to the platform of *nistha* and executes his devotional service with steady faith, then the very next quality he will display is humility. “One who thinks himself lower than the straw in the street, is devoid of all sense of false prestige, more tolerant than a tree, and ready to offer all respects to others... ” This verse describes a person at the platform of *nistha.*

Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura describes the characteristics of *nisthita-bhakti* in his *Madhurya-kadambini.* There he says that *nistha* means *niscala,* nonmovement. It refers to steadiness. We have already discussed *anisthita-bhakti,* which has six characteristics (false enthusiasm, indecisiveness, inability to maintain vows, etc. ) and which we more generally know as the stage before *anartha-nivrtti.* When *anarthas* are cleansed from the heart, we attain *nistha,* or steadiness in devotion.

*Madhurya-kadambini* lists the characteristics from which we must become free if our faith is to become steady. One such characteristic is *laya,* the tendency to sleep. Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura describes that just as there are three progressive stages of devotional remembrance, namely, *kirtanam, sravanam,* and *smaranam, so* there are progressive stages in the tendency to sleep. We have less chance of sleeping during *kirtana,* but we often see more of a tendency when we are practicing *sravanam,* hearing. Of course, *smaranam* is an even more subtle form of remembering Krsna and requires greater attention; in this stage the tendency to fall asleep is greatest.

Devotees fall asleep during worship for many reasons. Sometimes they are tired, but more often, it is because they lack spiritual taste. If we were doing something we found stimulating to our senses, we would have less of a tendency to sleep. Because we do not have such taste for hearing and chanting about the Lord, we sleep.

The second characteristic listed by Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura is *viksepa*—distraction. Distraction means the tendency to think of other things while performing devotional activities. The other day, a devotee asked me how he could become more attentive in chanting. It is a common problem for the neophyte devotee. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that we actually have to remove ourselves from distracting elements. Aside from physically removing ourselves, we must also be steady in chanting and determined to persevere despite our lack of taste.

Many of us suffer from *viksepa, so* we are easily distracted during *japa* and *kirtana.* When we are chanting, we stop to engage in *prajalpa* with a friend. Instead of chanting in a place conducive to concentration and free from distractions, we sometimes *choose* to chant where we can be with friends, and whenever the opportunity to speak *prajalpa* arises, we immediately take it.

It is especially distracting to chant in a place where we are normally busy doing other activities. Because of this *viksepa* tendency, as soon as we see something connected to our service, we will immediately think about that activity and forget about our concentration on the holy name. Sometimes devotees chant their rounds in front of their computers. They’re chanting Hare Krsna, but they find themselves thinking about the letters they have to write or the repairs their computer requires. Or they may chant at home where they usually do their chores. They may be chanting, but they will find themselves thinking, “Oh yes, I didn’t clean that yesterday. I had better do it today. ” Or they may chant near their “to-do” list: “Oh, I have to remember to buy such-and-such when I go shopping later... Hare Krsna, Hare Krsna, Krsna Krsna... ” Any area that stimulates remembrance of the activities we usually do throughout the day is not a good place for chanting. Therefore, Bhaktivinoda Thakura suggests that we remove ourselves from all external and internal distractions.

Aside from removing ourselves from distractions, we must be willing to place Krsna in the center of our chanting. If we have a temple room in our flat, then that would be the best place to chant. The temple room is the one place in the home which has been completely set aside for the worship of the Lord. Everything we see in that room will stimulate meditation on Krsna.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura speaks of removing ourselves from external distractions, but he also tells us to remove ourselves from internal ones. This requires controlling our thoughts. In the beginning stage, the most important practice is to listen attentively to the vibration of the holy name. Gradually, when the mind becomes cleansed and controlled, then naturally thoughts of Krsna will begin to enter the mind. This is the natural result of having purified the mind. If we would like this to happen to us, we should be careful to remove ourselves from *all* distractions by fixing our mind on thoughts of Krsna.

Krsna’s form, qualities, and pastimes will manifest in our heart gradually according to our level of advancement, but at least we must be determined to remove distractions.

Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura states further about the *anarthas* that block this determination: “First is *laya,* then *viksepa,* then *apratipatti. " Apratipatti* means the occasional inability to fix our mind in glorifying Krsna, even though we are not suffering from *laya* or *viksepa.* Sometimes a person may be chanting. He’s not distracted or falling asleep, but he still can’t seem to absorb his mind in the holy name. We need determination and patience to overcome this obstacle.

He also mentions *kasaya,* the tendency to be proud, angry, or greedy. Pride, anger, and greed are deeply-rooted tendencies. We have to become free of them if we wish to become *nisthita-bhaktas.* Of course, discussion of these tendencies relates to discussion of the third verse of the *Siksastaka.* This verse tells us that we have to be free of false prestige and ready to offer all respects to others.

The fifth characteristic listed in *Madhurya-kadambini* is *rasasvada,* or the inability to absorb ourselves in *kirtana* whenever we see an opportunity to enjoy our senses instead. In other words, our mind becomes distracted by something we deem more enjoyable than Krsna’s association. This characteristic is similar to *viksepa.*

*Nisthita-bhakti* is defined as the absence of these five characteristics. Then *nisthita-bhakti* is divided into two categories: *Saksat* (steadiness in direct devotional activity) and *bhakty-anukula-vastur-vartini* (steadiness in the elements favorable for *bhakti). Saksat-bhakti* is measured according to steadiness in body, words, and mind. Generally it is said that each of these stages comes progressively. We are steady first in our outward activities, then gradually in our speaking, and then finally in our minds. Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura also explains that sometimes a person may be more attracted to serving in the mind than with his words. Therefore, although these forms of steadiness are considered progressive, they are also measured according to one’s individual nature.

Steadiness in the elements favorable to *bhakti* includes humility, the willingness to always offer respects to others, absence of false pride, etc.

It may be found sometimes that a person is steady in the elements favorable to *bhakti* but is not steady in *bhakti* itself. Such a person may actually be humble but still not fully attracted to *kirtana.* Therefore, Srila Sanatana Gosvami describes that real humility is practiced in relationship to *krsna-prema.* Srila Sanatana Gosvami states that scholars define humility as when a person, although possessing good qualities, considers himself completely unqualified, worthless, and unintelligent. If a *sadhaka* accepts his disqualification and conducts himself humbly, he will gradually become fixed in humility. An intelligent person should not do anything opposed to humility. Factual humility is a mature stage of *prema.* Humility is therefore also a characteristic of *bhava-bhakti.* (This is because *bhava* is the first glimmer of *prema. )* We may not be at such a high platform, so it becomes our duty to practice humility if we wish to advance in spiritual life.

Srila Prabhupada has described humility in his purport to *Bhagavad-gita* [13.8-12](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/13.8). He writes that humility means:

**“One should not be anxious to have the satisfaction of being honored by others. The material conception of life makes us very eager to receive honor from others, but from the point of view of a man in perfect knowledge—who knows that he is not this body— anything, honor or dishonor, pertaining to this body is useless. One should not be hankering after this material deception. ”**

To practice humility, therefore, takes intelligence. We should not do anything opposed to humility. It is a natural tendency in the conditioned soul to want recognition. The desire for recognition is an *anartha.* We previously described the clouds of *anarthas* that torment us and prevent us from achieving our spiritual goals. One of those *anarthas* is called “weakness of heart. ” There are four types of weakness of heart: attachment to things that have no relationship with Krsna, a tendency to find fault in others, envy, and the desire for recognition. It takes intelligence to overcome these obstacles.

A devotee at *nistha* is naturally humble. Even though he has every reason to be proud, he thinks himself worthless. We are the opposite. As soon as we have any small success, we strive to be recognized for it. Usually we want the success just so we can be recognized by others. If we are recognized by others, it will increase our sense of prestige, and that is our motivating force.

Previously we discussed Vallabha Bhatta and his pride. He had written a commentary on *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* and the symptom of his pride was that he wanted everyone to hear it. If no one heard his commentaries, if no one would listen to him, how would he wash the mud of humiliation off? He felt his commentaries were outstanding, but if no one heard them, what value would they have and how would he become recognized as a great devotee? He went from one Vaisnava to another, begging them to hear what he had written. He even said, “How can I go on living? I will die unless someone hears my commentaries! ” The desire for recognition is pride. Caitanya Mahaprabhu saw that pride in his heart and judged his commentaries as useless.

Let us use our intelligence to control our pride. Let us actually be satisfied in service itself, in the desire to please guru and Krsna, and not look for outward recognition. Prahlada Maharaja states that without the desire to render sincere devotional service, everything else we do is simply a show. Krsna will only look at those who have unalloyed devotion for Him; Krsna recognizes devotional qualities.

Devotees sometimes wonder whether a person who possesses all good qualities but who considers himself worthless can still be useful. Such a person can certainly be useful, because whatever qualities such a person possesses will be magnified by the Lord for the benefit of others. Aside from the obvious service value of the qualities, devotional qualities attract others to Krsna. Those who have already become devotees will be attracted to develop similar qualities in themselves. This ability to attract others to serve the Lord is the most useful service. Krsna explains in the *Bhagavad-gita* ([12.13-14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/12.13)):

*advesta sarva-bhutanam*  
*maitrah karuna eva ca*  
*nirmamo nirahankarah*  
*sama-duhkha-sukhah ksami*  
*santustah satatam yogi*  
*yatatma drdha-niscayah*  
*mayy arpita-mano-buddhir*  
*yo mad-bhaktah sa me priyah*

**“One who is not envious but is a kind friend to all living entities, who does not think himself a proprietor and is free from false ego, who is equal in both happiness and distress, who is tolerant, always satisfied, self-controlled, and engaged in devotional service with determination, his mind and intelligence fixed on Me—such a devotee of Mine is very dear to Me. ”**

Actually, Krsna describes these qualities in a number of verses in *Bhagavad-gita,* and He says that those who possess such qualities are very dear to Him. If someone is dear to Krsna, it means that they are giving Him pleasure. If someone is giving Krsna pleasure by their qualities and service activities, then isn’t that useful? Isn’t the perfection of devotional service to give pleasure to Krsna? This is where Krsna actually derives His pleasure—from His devotees.

There is a verse in the fourth canto of the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* that describes that the Lord is pleased with those devotees who are not attached to material possessions and who have nothing but Him. Indeed the Lord relishes the activities and attitudes of such devotees.

We can bring pleasure to the Lord by our behavior. When we manage to do so, we will have attained perfection and we will be perfectly useful. At the same time, we may continue to see ourselves as worthless. Lord Caitanya expressed the same sentiments; He stated that He had no real love for Krsna, and the proof of that was that He was still living: “I have not the slightest tinge of love of Godhead within My heart. When you see Me crying in separation, I am just falsely exhibiting a demonstration of My great fortune. Indeed, not seeing the beautiful face of Krsna playing His flute, I continue to live My life like an insect, without purpose. ” (Cc. *Madhya* 2. 45) Such humility is the symptom of the highest devotee. Lord Caitanya taught us by His own example: *vairagya-vidya-nija-bhakti-yoga-siksartham ekah purusah puranah srikrsna-caitanya-sarira-dhari.* He taught us to become detached from everything that does not foster attachment to Krsna. He taught us devotional service to Himself. Therefore, we offer our obeisances to Caitanya Mahaprabhu, the ocean of transcendental mercy.

## Our progress in life is judged by our chanting

**Moscow, May 1, 2001**

It’s hard to believe it’s already six months since the last time I was here. I am beginning to realize how quickly time moves. Just as these six months have passed so quickly, so our whole lives are flying by. When we feel our lives passing by, we may begin to analyze, “Where am I going? ” That’s a good thought. We should know where we are headed. By nature, everyone needs a goal or aspiration— something to give life meaning. Sometimes we become so caught up by immediate goals that we forget the long-term ones. We hear the expression: “I'm just taking my life one day at a time. ” That’s a common expression in America. People ask, “How are you doing? ” and they reply, “I'm just taking it one day at a time. ” That means their goal is simply to survive until the next day. If they survive until the next day, at least they know they can continue with their goal of getting to the day after that.

But devotees are meant to have higher goals than day-to-day survival. Time *is* passing quickly and taking our lives with it, so we should feel we are making progress toward the real goal of our lives: *vasudeva-param gatim.*

The Supreme Lord, Vasudeva, is the *param gati,* the supreme goal of life. Krsna states this in numerous places. In *Bhagavad-gita* He states that those who make Him the goal of life and who worship Him with devotion will go to Him. We want to be sure that we are actually moving in that direction—toward Krsna—if we want to use our lives to their full potential.

I have been speaking lately about how we should think of our spiritual progress in relation to our ability to chant the holy name.

Krsna is the supreme goal of life. This means that attaining His holy name is also the supreme goal of life. Krsna and His name are identical. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura assures us that there is not the slightest difference between Krsna and His holy name. Although he does mention a secret difference: if we offend Krsna, Krsna will not forgive us, but if we offend His holy name, the holy name will forgive us after some time. What are the circumstances by which we can be forgiven for our *namaparadhas?* We must have a proper attitude of remorse.

If we wish to attain to pure chanting of the holy name, we must maintain a proper attitude. If we can chant on the platform of *namabhasa*, that is better than remaining offenders. One of the differences between *namabhasa* and *namaparadha* is that at *namabhasa* we are trying to free ourselves from offenses. That is the fundamental difference between the two stages. As long as we are endeavoring to chant purely, we have risen to the platform of *namabhasa,* even if we continue to commit offenses due to weakness of heart and our own ignorance. If due to foolishness or bewilderment we continue to chant with offenses, the Lord will take our attitude into consideration because He knows that persons in ignorance do not know better and a person who is suffering from weakness of heart (*hrdaya-daurbalyam)* is not trying to use the holy name to fulfill material propensities, but rather simply lacks taste.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura describes the difference between *chayanamabhasa* and *pratibimba-namabhasa. Chaya* means shadow, and *pratibimba* means reflection. There are actually three levels of *abhasa: kanti* (light—an effulgent object gives off light), *chaya* (shadow—an effulgent object gives off light, and therefore a shadow is cast), and *pratibimba* (reflection).

In *chaya-namabhasa,* a person, either because of bewilderment or foolishness, chants the holy name with offenses, but he is trying to become free from those offenses. At *pratibimba-namabhasa,* a person is attached either to sense gratification *(bhoga) or* liberation and he tries to use the holy name to fulfill his goals. In other words, his goal is not Krsna, but liberation or sense gratification. Therefore, we may see an impersonalist chant Hare Krsna. There is no devotion in his chanting, but only a desire for liberation and the idea that chanting Hare Krsna is the means.

Therefore, the word *pratibimba* is used. We may see the sun’s rays through heavy cloud cover, or the reflection of light upon water, but the light will appear distorted. Similarly, the pure holy name is reflected on the materialistic mentality and appears distorted. When we use the pure holy name to try to fulfill our materialistic goals, the holy name will appear distorted and we will not be able to access the full power of the holy name.

One of the effects of chanting is that we become peaceful, free of anxiety. A person may thus chant in order to become peaceful in the fulfillment of his sense gratification. His goals are all material. Such a person can never achieve love of God and will never attain *suddha-nama.* It is only by some great fortune—either by receiving the mercy of a powerful Vaisnava or of the Lord Himself—that we can come to the platform of pure chanting. One who does not even wish to attain pure chanting will never be able to attain it.

C*haya-namabhasa* is different. A person who chants the shadow of the holy name is not trying to fulfill material desires, but still he is not chanting the pure name. This is because he is weak. Because he is weak, he commits offenses to the holy name. His desire is to become free from offenses. Bhaktivinoda Thakura prescribes three requirements if we wish to become free of our offenses to the holy name: We must associate with advanced devotees and chant in their company, remove ourselves from all internal and external distractions, and remain steady and determined.

We have already discussed in this volume the types of *anarthas* listed along with the internal and external distractions. If we work to overcome these obstacles, we will begin to practice devotional service on the platform of *nistha.*

Certainly in the first stages of chanting we have to listen, listen, and listen some more. Srila Prabhupada says that it is very, very important to hear the name as we chant it. By attentive hearing, the mind will gradually become controlled and our *anarthas* will naturally become cleared. When the mind is controlled and clear, Krsna will begin to manifest the particular form that is worshipable by us. Or, we may meditate on a particular Deity, on Doyal Nitai Saci-Suta, etc. We may do that now by chanting in front of the Deity, but when our minds are purified, that beloved form will manifest whether we are looking at the Deity or not. Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that by chanting the holy name of the Lord, first Krsna reveals His form, then His qualities, then His pastimes, and gradually, everything about our eternal relationship with Him will be revealed.

If the holy name and Krsna are nondifferent, certainly the holy name contains our complete relationship with Krsna. Nothing is missing. Therefore, we can measure our progress in spiritual life by how attached we are becoming to chanting the holy name. Such attachment to Krsna is the goal of our chanting. Bhaktivinoda Thakura states that without affection for the holy name, our lives are useless.

Therefore, when we talk about setting goals, we should at least know the goal of our chanting. Then while chanting, we will be able to think about progressing toward that goal. That desire to progress will keep us off the *namaparadha* platform and situate us on *namabhasa.* On *namabhasa* we may not be chanting the pure holy name, but at least we have hope of gradually becoming free of offenses. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura comments on the *Padma Purana* verse, *namaparadha-yuktanam namany eva haranty agham*, that those who are committing offenses should still chant the Lord’s name. Chanting is the only way to become free of offenses. We should not think that because we are offensive, we would be better off not chanting at all. There is no way to become free of offenses except to chant with the proper attitude.

The attitude of wanting to become free of offenses when we approach the holy name will alone allow us to make progress in our chanting. Therefore, we say there is no difference between Krsna and His holy name except that the holy name is more forgiving—if we have the proper attitude.

Please know that the holy name contains everything. The Lord’s holy name is our life. We are meant to perform our devotional service in relation to the holy name. All our progress is gauged against our relationship with the holy name. Srila Prabhupada taught us that we are actually servants of Krsna’s holy name. We render service to the holy name by chanting it with love and distributing its glories.

If we wish to see ourselves in this way, we will need to develop faith in the Lord’s holy name. Faith is the preliminary qualification for advancement. The descriptions of *namaparadha, namabhasa,* and *suddha-nama* can also be understood as gradations of faith. We’ve oftentimes heard Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s discussion from *Harinama Cintamani* about how the spiritual master should not initiate people into the holy name unless they have faith. One of the definitions of the ninth offense in chanting is to preach the glories of the holy name to the faithless. That includes initiating someone into the holy name who does not possess faith. Bhaktivinoda Thakura considers doing so the same as throwing pearls before swine or giving a monkey an elegant piece of clothing. The swine will trample your pearls and the monkey will simply tear the cloth. Similarly, if we initiate a faithless person into the chanting of the holy name, he will simply commit offenses, and the guru who initiated such a faithless person will be dragged down too.

Why would someone take initiation into chanting if he had no faith? Some people think it will make them more respectable. If they are respected, people will serve them. “All my bad past reputation can immediately be reversed and now I’ll get a good reputation. ” Or, “Yes, now I’ll be able to *really* enjoy my senses. The holy name is able to give me anything I want. ”

Therefore, it is a preacher’s duty to serve the holy name by spreading its glories. While spreading the name’s glories, we are also supposed to cultivate an atmosphere of faith. We must first preach the holy name’s glories, and by such preaching inspire faith in others. They will become attracted to the chanting by our own attraction to it and by our own faith, and they will want to make Krsna the goal of their lives too. We are meant to inspire them that if they take to the chanting, they will be able to attain Krsna, who is the goal of life.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura describes faithful chanting as the conviction that all one’s duties can automatically be fulfilled simply by chanting the Lord’s holy name; all one’s pious activities will be complete by chanting. Srila Prabhupada explains that the most important instruction the spiritual master gives is that we chant the Lord’s name. He gives no other instruction that is more important. All the profound secrets of Krsna consciousness and devotional service will be revealed through faithful chanting.

However, we have to chant with the intention to become free of *anarthas* and to attain the pure name. We should know that time is passing. How much progress are we making in our chanting? As we realize that we are not actually making any progress, then we should take Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s advice and seek out the association of devotees who *are* making progress in their chanting. Without such association, it will be difficult for us to come to the platform of *namabhasa*, what to speak of *suddha-nama.* It is through the association of advanced devotees that the secrets of the holy name become revealed to us and we are able to think more seriously about chanting.

A devotee who thinks about the goal of chanting will always emphasize the importance of his *japa.* He’ll know that although he is progressing slowly, his chanting has purpose. Those who continue to offend the holy name without thought will lose their taste for chanting. If we don’t associate with advanced chanters in order to stop our offenses, we may even give up our *japa.* We will forget the purpose behind the chanting and think that there are more important things to do. When we give up the holy name, we will fall into materialistic consciousness. But Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura has called serving the holy name in all humility the *siddhapranali* of the Gaudiya Vaisnava. Where will we be if we give up our chanting?

## Now you have no excuse

**Sri Siksastaka-bhasya Moscow, May 2, 2001**

There are different types of offenses: Offenses to the holy name, offenses to Vaisnavas, and offenses committed while serving the Deity. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that these offenses obstruct our ability to directly experience the holy name just as fog obstructs our ability to see the sun. The sun is self-effulgent, but our vision of the sun may be obstructed by clouds and fog. The holy name is also self-effulgent and full of transcendental potencies. Srila Prabhupada often cited one of the many verses stating this fact: *nama cintamanih krsnas, caitanya-rasa-vigrahah, purnah suddho nitya-mukto, ’bhinnatvan nama-naminoh.* The holy name is transcendentally blissful and can bestow all spiritual benediction because the name is nondifferent from Krsna Himself. Krsna is the reservoir of pleasure, and like Him, His name is neither material nor lacking transcendental bliss. The holy name carries all of Krsna’s potencies, because that’s what it means for the name to be nondifferent from the Lord.

Therefore, devotees know that Krsna’s name is complete. If it is complete, it means that it is the source of all transcendental mellows. As Krsna is the reservoir of transcendental mellows, so His name is the reservoir of all mellows.

Krsna’s name is never contaminated by material qualities. There is no question of Krsna’s name ever being in contact with *maya.* Krsna’s name is forever eternal and always free from matter.

We have previously discussed the secret difference between Krsna and His name: While Krsna does not forgive offenses, Krsna’s name will forgive those who chant with offenses if they maintain a proper attitude. We have also discussed the characteristics of that proper attitude. We have also stated that even if a devotee out of bewilderment or foolishness commits an offense, but wishes to become free from *aparadha,* Krsna will overlook the mistake and help him make progress.

Of course, once we begin to learn the nature of the various offenses, we can no longer claim we are ignorant. As soon as we understand what the offenses are and how to avoid them, then we must be said to possess knowledge. But ignorance is dense like fog, and *anarthas* are thick like clouds, so even when we possess knowledge, we may not have the strength to act on it.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura describes four categories of ignorance (*tattva-vibhrama):* (1) Ignorance of one’s own identity; (2) ignorance of the Supreme Lord’s position; (3) ignorance of the process of *sadhana-bhakti* leading to *prema-bhakti*; (4) ignorance of the obstacles that prevent one from making progress in devotional service.

Certainly we should try eradicating these four categories of ignorance if we wish to make progress in spiritual life. By understanding the nature of the obstacles, we can endeavor to overcome them. By endeavoring to overcome *anarthas,* we can approach the platform of *namabhasa.* Even though we may still commit some offenses, we can work to gradually overcome all of them by continuously chanting the holy name.

When we chant *namabhasa,* we accumulate stockpiles of pious credits. By accumulating those pious credits, we get *bhakty-unmukhi sukrti,* which blesses us with the association of those who are serving the holy name.

Association with advanced devotees is essential to our progress in chanting. Srila Prabhupada translates *namabhasa* as the “clearing stage. ” At present we are not clear in our chanting, but at *namabhasa,* we are trying to clear away the fog. When we come to *namabhasa,* it means we are conscientiously making a deliberate effort to overcome the obstacles on our path. One of the *anarthas* is *hrdaya-daurbalyam,* weakness of heart.

There are four types of *hrdaya-daurbalyam.* The first is attachment to things that have no relationship with Krsna. We may say that everything has a relationship with Krsna, and we would be correct, but that doesn’t mean just any attachment is conducive to the development of love of God. Actually, if we were to truly see the relationship between Krsna and matter, we would find it impossible to be attached to anything *but* Krsna. We say we’re attached to matter because of its relationship with Krsna, but that doesn’t seem to be the case for most of us. If we understand matter and spirit properly, we will know that everything exists only for Krsna’s pleasure, and we will not feel ownership toward anything.

Actually, one of the primary characteristic of a devotee (s*varupalaksana)* is *krsnaika-sarana,* that he is surrendered to Krsna. Surrender includes seeing everything as both connected to Krsna and meant for His pleasure; a surrendered devotee does not try to enjoy matter him or herself. Therefore, it is said that one who suffers from *hrdaya-daurbalyam,* weaknesses of heart in executing *bhakti,* is attached to things that have no direct relationship with Krsna. How can something *not* have a direct relationship with Krsna? That would be whenever we see the particular object as meant for our own pleasure. If we are seeing something as meant to please our senses, we will find it impossible to simultaneously see it as meant for Krsna’s pleasure. That includes ourselves; *we* are meant for Krsna’s pleasure too.

Thus, such attachment is one of the major obstacles on the path, and it can prevent us from making progress in our chanting. The spirit of enjoying matter is nicely explained by Bhaktivinoda Thakura: “The root of all offenses which prevent us from incessantly chanting the holy name is enjoying the position caused by the false ego and resulting in the bodily conception of life. Impelled by this attitude, one turns away from serving Krsna and regards the whole world as an instrument to satisfy his senses. At the same time, one is dragged into subordination of the lower laws of ignorance and passion. ”

Therefore, the “enjoying spirit, ” as Prabhupada called it, is the root of all our offenses. It causes us to turn away from Krsna. We commit offenses due to material attachment. The etymology of the word *“aparadha”* is described here: Not only does material attachment cause us to turn away from Krsna, it turns us away *(apa)* from the service of Radha. The *Bhagavad-gita* states, *bhogaisvarya-prasaktanam, tayapahrta-cetasam* / *vyavasayatmika buddhih, samadhau na vidhiyate (Bg.* [2.44](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/2.44)): “In the mind of those who are too much attached to sense enjoyment and material opulence and who are bewildered by such things, the resolute determination to engage in devotional service does not take place. ” This is our first weakness.

Our second weakness is our tendency to find fault in others. We have spoken about this point many times, and everyone knows what we mean. We are all already quite experienced in this subject matter.

The third weakness is envy, and the fourth is the desire for recognition. These four items fall under the category, “weakness of heart. ” Why do we have this weakness? Because we are foolish, bewildered, or both. Still, if we sincerely try to overcome our offenses, then at least we can chant on the platform of *namabhasa,* which will eventually lead us higher.

We want to develop pure devotion. Therefore, it is important to understand what exactly the offenses and obstacles are. All of them are caused by ignorance in some form; knowledge can help us overcome such ignorance. With knowledge, we can deliberately work to overcome all obstacles to pure *bhakti.*

It is not necessary for us to remain on the platform of *namaparadha.* Aside from cultivating knowledge about the particular *anarthas,* we should also continue chanting. *Namaparadha-yuktanam, namany eva haranty agham:* “The chanting of the holy name of the Lord is recommended for those who are still committing offenses. ” We should try to chant continuously, and, in the meantime, increase our knowledge about the nature of our offenses so that we can gradually free ourselves from them.

Just by reading this page, we are each becoming more knowledgeable and less able to claim ignorance in chanting. Hearing will save us. With knowledge comes choice. Do we want to give up the *aparadhas?* Choice means taking responsibility for our own progress. How can we make progress in our chanting? It is our responsibility to find out.

Let us hear about the other *aparadhas.* They are divided in four groups: Offenses to the holy name, offenses to the Deity, offenses to the devotees, and offenses to all living entities. Again, the only way to save ourselves is to adopt the mood of Lord Caitanya’s third *Siksastaka* verse: *trnad api sunicena taror api sahisnuna amanina manadena kirtaniyah sada harih.* We have to become humble and prepared to offer all respects to others.

Lord Caitanya describes a devotee as one who thinks himself lower than grass on the street. What does that mean? A blade of grass has an ego; that is, in relation to material nature, a blade of grass perceives itself as grass. That is its sense of self. When the grass is trampled, it bends, then gradually stands erect again. If we squeeze it, it will give juice. It’s a lowly entity. In relation to matter, however, the living entity has no connection whatsoever to matter. When the living entity is illusioned, it may think itself a blade of grass or an intelligent human being, but we have absolutely no connection. By thinking ourselves to be lower than a blade of grass, we can give up our inflated sense of self and see ourselves as free of all connection with matter.

From that position, we will be able to offer respect to all living entities. Respect is important. Lord Caitanya states in *Caitanyabhagavata,* “With My hands raised, I declare, please, devotees, from this day on, no longer commit offenses to any living entities and simply chant the holy name of the Lord. ” We fail to respect other living entities because we are envious of them in some way, and that usually results in our committing violence toward them.

Devotees sometimes wonder about the difference between envy and faultfinding. Certainly envious persons find fault with others, but being envious can include physically harming another entity as well as criticizing them. Faultfinding is a symptom of the envious mentality, but because it is such a common form of envy, Bhaktivinoda Thakura has addressed it separately.

Aside from the fact that faultfinding proves an envious mentality, we should know that envy is the root cause not only of faultfinding but of our entire existence in this world. If we can simply overturn that envy and transform it into love of Krsna, then we would become pure lovers of God. Again, it’s helpful to understand the various ways in which envy manifests so that we can overcome them one by one. One of the most common is the tendency to find fault.

The *Caitanya-caritamrta* defines faultfinding and other forms of disrespect toward living entities as *jiva-himsa,* violence toward others. Srila Prabhupada explains that *jiva-himsa* means the kind of envy that obstructs the dissemination of the holy name’s glories. This envy manifests in a number of ways, and it must be uprooted.
