One other *anartha* that is important to mention is *asat-trsna*— thirst for material objects and the desire to enjoy sense gratification. This also includes the desire to be elevated to the heavenly planets or for liberation. All these aspirations are considered material. Such material desires are compared to a cloud. Although the sun is self-effulgent and cannot be covered, the clouds appear to cover the sun from our vision. Similarly, although Krsna’s holy name is self-effulgent and the reservoir of all pleasure, why do we not experience that pleasure? Our minds and hearts are covered by the clouds of *anarthas.* Burn up the clouds and clear them away if you want to see Krsna.

In order to clear *anarthas,* we have to cultivate knowledge without becoming discouraged. Rather, we should remain positive. Bhaktivinoda Thakura states at the beginning of *Harinama Cintamani* that his book is not for everyone. The book is meant only for those who are interested in relishing the Lord’s holy name. The first positive step we can take to help ourselves is to accept the association of qualified devotees who have a taste for chanting. Such association will help us. If we think we can overcome our *anarthas* alone, we are wrong. We will give up long before the job is finished. If we can associate with devotees who can help us understand where to start to improve, nothing is more important.

By association I mean that we should associate with those who are more advanced than we are and who understand our hearts. These devotees will not be interested simply in pointing out our defects but in sharing their enthusiasm and determination in chanting. We will be able to draw strength from them in the process of purification.

## ‘‘ Kirtaniya-sada-hari’’

**Sri Siksastaka-bhasya Moscow, May 8, 2001**

We have spoken about how we turn away from Krsna’s service when we adopt the enjoying mentality. This enjoying spirit, rooted in envy of Krsna, is the cause of all the offenses we commit in spiritual life. We also discussed the etymological structure of the word *“aparadha, ”* meaning, “being turned away from the service of

Radha and Her servants. ” Therefore, it should be clear that when we offend the Vaisnavas, we are turning away from service to Krsna.

We must learn to practice detachment from the material body. Four qualities are described in the third *Siksastaka* verse—humility, tolerance, freedom from the desire to be honored, and the ability and willingness to respect others—and all of these qualities are obstructed by bodily identification. People are usually proud of their material assets, their wealth, beauty, strength, and knowledge, but these assets are related only to the body and do not usually survive death.

Recently a devotee asked me the difference between self-esteem and pride. She said that the process of *anartha-nivrtti* is so difficult that unless we maintain a sense of self-esteem, we will despair. At the same time, we have been taught not to be proud of anything we do. How can we maintain a sense of self-worth and yet not become proud? I told her that we become proud when we identify with external characteristics or qualities. *Srimad Bhagavatam* ([4.3.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/3.17)) lists the six material qualities as wealth, education, respectable parentage, physical beauty, youth, and knowledge. One who is proud of possessing these qualities generally loses *all* good qualities along with his good sense. When we lose our good sense, we become both blind to ourselves and cannot recognize the good qualities in the hearts of advanced devotees. The material qualities help us forget the value of spiritual qualities and therefore we have a different standard than the Vaisnavas. Vaisnavas value internal qualities.

Aside from that, we do want to maintain a sense of self-worth. In order to do that, however, we must understand who and what the self is. He should be seeking something completely internal and not all based on bodily identification. A devotee develops self-worth by discovering that he is pleasing when he pleases the Lord. The value of our lives is directly proportional to our ability to give the Lord pleasure. Not only will a pleasing devotee develop self-worth, he will also achieve the perfection of life.

The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* ([4.31.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/31.21)) describes that the Supreme Lord is pleased by those who have no material possessions but who are satisfied to possess devotional service. Indeed, the Lord relishes the activities of such devotees. That is, the Lord derives pleasure from those who are attached to serving Him. Everyone in this world is looking for self-esteem, but what greater sense of self-esteem can we gain other than realizing that we are pleasing to the greatest personality?

Those who are proud of their material possessions usually like to display them to gain the respect of others. Such foolish persons think that by displaying their qualities they will attract others who will then worship them. They don’t realize that they are attracting exploiters. They also don’t realize that Krsna is not interested in their material possessions. To gain the Lord’s recognition provides the greatest sense of self-worth. Therefore, Queen Kunti states that the Lord can only be approached by those who are materially exhausted. Those, who are trying to improve themselves through higher education, physical beauty, or respectable parentage, cannot approach Krsna with sincere feeling.

If we really want to please the Lord, we should develop the qualities listed in the third verse of the *Siksastaka.* We can do that through *kirtaniya-sada-hari,* always chanting the holy name. If we are successful, we will receive the topmost benediction, which is Krsna’s personal association. To those who are deeply absorbed in chanting the holy name, Krsna personally appears and reveals His form, qualities, pastimes, and His personal relationship with them. Then the devotees will experience all the ecstatic symptoms and manifest pure love of God.

Krsna says He descends willingly: At*ah sri-krsna-namadi na bhaved grahyam indriyaih sevonmukhe hi jihvadau svayam eva sphuraty adah.* Krsna cannot be understood by our blunt material senses; He can only be understood when we develop purified, spiritual senses. Chanting is the only way to actually realize the Lord’s form. This is what this verse actually means: That we cannot realize anything about Krsna with our blunt material senses. Chanting purifies our mind and senses and allows us to see Krsna.

In *Bhagavad-gita,* Krsna gives Arjuna the eyes by which he can see Him. The devotee with purified senses not only sees Krsna but experiences Him with all of his senses. Therefore we can purify our senses by associating with Krsna *through* our senses. We can purify our eyes by gazing at the Lord’s beautiful Deity form; when our eyes are purified we will see the Lord directly. We purify our sense of taste by honoring *krsna-prasadam* and by speaking only about Krsna; the purified tongue always relishes the taste of Krsna’s lips. Our ears are purified by hearing about Krsna; our purified ears will hear the sound of Krsna’s flute.

The sound of Krsna’s flute captures the minds of the *vraja-gopis.* It’s described that Krsna shoots arrows just like Cupid. Krsna shoots the arrow of His flute song. He shoots another arrow of His bodily fragrance. Imagine what it would be like to feel those arrows with purified senses? The scriptures tell us that Krsna’s body smells like a mixture of sandalwood, aguru, and camphor. This fragrance will completely conquer our senses.

But we are still identifying with the material body. We cannot smell Krsna’s bodily aroma. Instead, we are trying to purify our senses by smelling those things that have been offered to Krsna, especially flowers and incense. The four Kumaras became great devotees simply by smelling the fragrance of the *tulasi* leaves on the Lord’s lotus feet. This fragrance was so powerful that it turned their minds from impersonalism to devotion.

If we want to purify our senses, we must begin by purifying the tongue. Why is the tongue so important? Because with our tongue we can chant Krsna’s holy name. If we are fortunate, we can chant constantly. If we try to chant constantly, we will actually achieve the topmost benediction: The Lord’s name will remain always on our tongue. *Srimad-Bhagavatam* ([3.33.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/33.7)) states, “Glorious are they whose tongues are decorated with the holy name of Krsna. Such persons are considered to have performed in their previous lives all austerities, visited all the different places of pilgrimage, and performed all the prescribed duties for Aryan civilization. ” Saintly devotees are said to have surpassed all austerities simply because they always keep the holy name on their tongues. Why? Because constant chanting is perfection. All the other items listed in the *Bhagavatam* verse are simply means to achieve the goal of constant chanting.

The ability to chant constantly is the characteristic of a pure devotee. *Prayena munayo rajan nivrtta vidhi-sedhatah nairgunya-stha ramante sma gunanukathane hareh. (SB* [2.1.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/1.7)) Topmost transcendentalists derive pleasure from this one activity. They are called *nivrtta vidhi-sedhatah*, above the rules and regulations. All rules and regulations are meant to bring us to the point of constant chanting. Once we have attained it, we will have transcended the rules.

The qualities of such persons are not only attractive to the devotees, but to the nondevotees as well. They are ornamented with the qualities of the *sadhus* (*sadhu-bhusanam*). What makes their qualities attractive is that they are attractive to the Lord. The Lord and His devotees are attracted to one another. Krsna says that the *sadhu* is in His heart and that He is in the heart of the *sadhu.* He specifically says that in relation to Maharaja Ambarisa.

Maharaja Ambarisa was expert at engaging his senses in Krsna’s service. He cleaned the Lord’s temple with his hands, walked to see the Deity with his legs, saw the Lord’s beautiful form with his eyes, and used his tongue to glorify the Lord. He engaged all of his senses fully in the Lord’s service. He became so dear to the Lord that the Lord said He could not act independently of Maharaja Ambarisa; Maharaja Ambarisa controlled Krsna. When Durvasa Muni approached the Lord to get relief from the Sudarsana *cakra,* the Lord told him he would have to seek relief from Maharaja Ambarisa because He would not act independently of him. Such is the power of a devotee; a devotee controls the Lord.

Lord Brahma prays that although Krsna is unconquerable, He is conquered by the love of His devotees. *Jnane prayasam udapasya namanta eva, jivanti san-mukharitam bhavadiya-vartam, sthane sthitah sruti-gatam tanu-van-manobhir, ye prayaso ’jita jito ’py asi tais trilokyam (SB* [10.14.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/14.3)). The Lord is conquered by complete dedication.

We should know that devotional qualities develop automatically in one who has the primary ornament of a Vaisnava, surrender to Krsna. Surrender to Krsna is a *svarupa-laksana,* a primary Vaisnava characteristic. Humility and all the other devotional qualities are called *tathasta-laksana,* or secondary qualities. If we could surrender to the Lord immediately, we would attain all the *tathasta-laksanas.*

Unfortunately, that doesn’t seem likely. Therefore, we must practice chanting and hearing and working through our *anarthas.* Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that when the holy name, the full embodiment of transcendental *rasa,* appears in a *sadhaka’s* heart, his mind is transformed. He then thinks, “I am constitutionally an infinitesimal servant of Krsna. I have no material needs. But alas, because of my estrangement from Krsna’s lotus feet, I now find myself in my present position. Bound to the wheel of repeated birth and death, I am suffering many miseries. Now by the grace of my spiritual master and the Vaisnavas, I have come to realize that only by devotional service to the Supreme Lord can I find release from my distress, become reinstated in my constitutional position, regain my spiritual identity, and attain love of God. Otherwise, I am obliged to embrace the path of *yukta-vairagya. ”*

*Yukta-vairagya* means actively engaging the senses in the Lords’ service. As long as we are connected to the material world, we must practice the renunciation of *yukta-vairagya.* Liberated souls don’t have to think about renunciation of matter because they have no connection with the body. As long as we are following the path of *yukta-vairagya,* we must study the meaning of *sambandha-jnana* and keep body and soul together. *Sambandha-jnana* includes knowledge of the living entity’s position in relation to the Supreme Lord and how to practice the *sadhana* that will lead to higher stages. It is the knowledge by which we initially establish a connection with the Lord.

The *Caitanya-caritamrta* describes the awakening of *sambandhajnana* in the story of the impoverished man who is told by an astrologer that his father has left him great wealth. The astrologer then tells him not to dig on the northern, western, or southern sides—each of which stands for a different type of philosophy—but rather to dig on the eastern side, the side of *bhakti,* if he wishes to find the treasure. The knowledge of the treasure came to the impoverished man through words from a superior source. Similarly, if we wish to forge a connection with the Supreme Lord, we must hear from the spiritual master. Bhaktivinoda Thakura states that while developing *sambandha-jnana,* he will accept nothing but that which will keep body and soul together.

We cannot imitate liberated souls and skip over the stage of *sambandha-jnana,* which inevitably includes *anartha-nivrtti.* We actually have to learn to engage body, mind, words, and all our possessions in Krsna’s service. *Pranair arthair dhiya vaco, etavaj janma-saphalyam dehinam iha dehisu: prana* (life), *artha* (wealth), *dhiya* (intelligence), and *vaco,* (words) all should be engaged in the service of the Lord. Of course, this includes engaging our senses in Krsna’s service too.

A devotee asked me recently whether one who commits *namaaparadha* can even achieve constant chanting of Krsna’s name. Yes, he can—if he is determined. If a person is determined to chant constantly, he will have to be determined to overcome the offenses, and then he will gradually become free of the *aparadhas.* Everything depends upon attitude. We can sincerely pray to Krsna for help in this regard. If we are sincere in wishing to reach *namabhasa,* Krsna will help us. From *namabhasa,* we will be able to clear all our offenses through continuous chanting.

What are the symptoms that show our desire to become free of *nama-aparadha!* We feel repentant. Repentance means we feel the pain of our mistakes and become determined not to commit them again. Isn’t it true that when we suffer for something, we learn something from the suffering and try to avoid repeating the mistake that led to the pain? If we look within our hearts and see the offenses, do we actually understand what we have done wrong? Or do we just feel the pain of suffering without that understanding? Often we think more like Gopala Capala, who prayed to Lord Caitanya, who wanted first to be freed from his pain, *then* to inquire about how to avoid bringing such suffering upon himself again. Maharaja Pariksit was in a different mood. After garlanding the *rsi* with the dead snake, then hearing how he had been sentenced to death, he was repentant. He was willing to suffer everything including the loss of his kingdom and life, because it would purify him from ever acting in such a way again.

Genuine repentance means accepting the pain of realizing one’s mistakes followed by the determination to become rectified. Jagai and Madhai were fully repentant and Lord Caitanya gave them two orders: “Always chant the holy name and never again commit sin. ” When they agreed to these two orders, the Lord accepted all their sinful reactions and they became completely purified. That’s genuine repentance. If we are not genuinely repentant, we are compared to the elephant that bathes in the lake, then comes to the shore and throws dust upon itself. In *The Nectar of Devotion,* Srila Prabhupada gives the example of someone who contracts a venereal disease. The treatment is painful enough that the man decides he will never engage in illicit sex again. But if his atonement is not deep, he will again go out, have sex with an infected person, and become infected himself, only to again undergo the painful treatment.

Ultimately, real atonement means to awaken to Krsna consciousness. It means we will no longer commit sinful activities. As long as a person atones only because he doesn’t want to suffer, he will not become fully purified. He must understand his *anarthas* in relation to his spiritual aspirations. He must understand how they are holding him back from his goal. He must also want the goal enough that he sees his *anarthas* not as temporary stumbling blocks on his path of pleasure but as real obstacles on his path of spiritual development. Therefore, *The Nectar of Devotion* also states that although we may be cured of a particular sinful tendency, if the seeds of sinful desire are not burned up in the heart, we will again be forced to act sinfully. Real atonement means to uproot all sinful desires.

That can be painful. The roots of sin are quite deep. *Kecit kevalaya bhaktya vasudeva-parayanah agham dhunvanti kartsnyena niharam iva bhaskarah*: only rare persons, who attain unalloyed devotional service to the Supreme Lord Vasudeva, can uproot the weeds of sinful desires. (SB [6.1.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/1.15)) Krsna consciousness may not always be joyfully performed in the beginning stages, but if we want *kevala-ananda,* unending and unadulterated bliss, we will have to perform the austerity of uprooting *anarthas.* Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityananda came to deliver the most joyful process, but the full joy comes only to those who are pure in heart. For those of us who maintain deep-rooted material attachments in the heart, the process of Krsna consciousness may not always feel so joyful.

Prabhupada defines austerity as the voluntary acceptance of pain for the sake of pleasing the Lord. As Lord Rsabhadeva states, “My dear sons, of all living entities who have accepted material bodies, one who is embodied in a human form of life should not waste it for sense gratification, which was meant for the hogs and dogs. ” (SB [5.5.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/5.1)) Rather, he should perform *tapo-divyam* (penance) to achieve the divine position of devotional service. When we accept this path and purify our hearts, then we will come to the platform of real happiness. Real happiness is transcendental to material happiness and continues forever.

## Become suitable instruments for hearing and chanting the Lord’s glories

**Moscow, May 9, 2001**

We have spoken about the constant chanting of the holy name. Of course, the instruction to chant constantly is given in different ways according to the development of the chanter. For someone on the platform of *suddha-nama*, constant chanting defines his life, which is full of glorification of Krsna. He speaks *only* of Krsna and has no connection with the material world. He is absorbed only in the spiritual world and therefore has no need to discuss anything in this world. He makes no endeavor for personal maintenance because he is fully surrendered to Krsna and depends only on Him for all his needs. He never even asks the Lord for anything; he trusts that Krsna will supply what he needs. (This is also part of the characteristic of one who has realized *taror api,* being more tolerant than a tree. Even when a tree is drying up due to lack of water, it will not ask to be watered. ) The Lord, accepting such a surrendered soul, provides all his necessities.

Of course, we cannot practice this artificially or imitate it. It is a stage of realization. One who is firmly situated in such realization has no doubt that the Lord is personally maintaining him. That’s true even if it means fasting for several days or living in the most uncomfortable circumstances. Such a devotee is satisfied in any condition of life. If we were to imitate this realization, we would not be able to maintain the show. We may think we will not endeavor for our maintenance and that we will accept only what comes of its own accord, but when the refrigerator is still empty several days later, we will find that our faith has significantly weakened. But one who realizes Krsna and sees Him everywhere will see Him in the empty refrigerator as easily as in the feast. Such a person feels no fear, and is equal in both happiness and distress. The *Bhagavad-gita* calls such a person *sthita-dhi-muni,* one whose mind is not disturbed even when there is cause for disturbance. To have such evenness of mind, we must be free from attachment to the causes of happiness and distress, attachment, fear, and anger. We must never waver in our determination to attain Krsna. “Those who are on this path are resolute in purpose and their aim is one. ” (Bg. [2.41](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/2.41))

If we are not *sthita-dhi* and our minds are disturbed by material considerations, what can we do? We can avoid speaking about ordinary, mundane affairs connected to the body. If we don’t speak about such affairs, what will we say? A devotee has only one subject: Krsna. Therefore, *kirtaniyah sada harih,* we should always chant Krsna’s names and always speak about His glories. A pure devotee who has actually achieved this level of consciousness will manifest all the qualities listed in the *trnad api sunicena* verse.

These qualities are very attractive to the Lord and give Him pleasure. A devotee who possesses them is always within the Lord’s vision: “For one who sees Me everywhere and sees everything in Me, I am never lost, nor is he ever lost to Me. ” (Bg. [6.30](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.30)) Because these qualities give the Lord so much pleasure, they attract Him to want to associate with the devotee who possesses them. Therefore, the pure devotees are always experiencing the pleasure of the Lord’s association.

For those who are not on this stage of realization, constant chanting means maintaining the body according to the rules prescribed for our particular status. These rules are connected to the body, and because we still have connection with the body, we are encouraged to follow them. The prescribed duties come under the designations of *brahmacarya, grhastha, vanaprastha, sannyasa, brahmana, ksatriya, vaisya,* and *sudra.* These designations are not, of course, eternal. The devotees in the spiritual world also accept such designations, but there they are accepted only to enhance the Lord’s pleasure. Here, they are temporary and useful as a foundation upon which we can build our devotion.

I have heard of some devotees who came to think that Krsna was only a v*aisya* or a *ksatriya,* and who misunderstood Krsna’s pastimes. Krsna is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. He has no connection with any material designation. But because the material world is a perverted reflection of the spiritual world, that which exists in the material world also exists in the spiritual context. Here we have parents. What defines “parents” is their relationship with our body; we were born through them. Krsna also has parents, but He was not exactly born through them. We have friends and Krsna has friends. In Dvaraka, Krsna has many wives. These appear to be bodily relationships, but they are not. The material world is simply a perverted reflection of the actual, eternal relationships of the spiritual world. In the spiritual world, these relationships are eternal; in the material world, they are temporary. Here we may be called v*aisya* or *ksatriya* while we live in a particular body, but that designation will change when we change our body. We may be parents here, but upon leaving the body, we may not become parents in a next life. The spiritual world is a completely different place.

The spiritual world is not devoid of variety; it is filled with unlimited variety. Impersonalists think that where there is variety, there is matter. They think that the spiritual platform is devoid of variety, since variety seems to be the cause of so much material suffering. The devotees understand the superior position of the Lord and His world of unending variety.

But while we live in this world with incomplete realization of the spiritual world, we are bound to carry out our designated duties according to the body we inhabit. People in the spiritual world are not bound by the duties the way we are bound here. In the spiritual world, the only binding force is love. In the material world, where we are devoid of pure love, we must act like the bull who is bound by a rope through its nose and forced to go wherever the rope pulls it.

How should we execute our duties? Most people execute them out of fear. Everyone needs a reason to perform their duties. If that reason is not love, it will have to be fear. They have not discovered the platform of love. A *ksatriya's* duty is to ascertain whether people are performing their prescribed duties. If he sees deviation, he wields his sword. People may be afraid of the government’s punishment; therefore, they pay their taxes and perform other duties. Some people are similarly afraid of Krsna. This fear binds us to the material world.

All activities performed in the spiritual world, however, are performed out of spontaneous love. Whatever bonds the devotees feel are only the bonds of love of God. Out of love for Krsna, Mother Yasoda prepares food for her son. Out of love for Krsna, she goes into the forest to call Him home for lunch. Out of love for Krsna, she chastises Him. Out of love for Krsna, she is able to bind Him with rope. Krsna was not bound by Mother Yasoda’s rope, but by her affection. Similarly, Krsna is conquered by the affection of every pure devotee.

If we wish to make progress toward the spiritual world, we must act according to our present realization. That means following our prescribed duties and trying to always chant the holy name. We should make the ultimate purpose of performing such duties Krsna’s pleasure. The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* explains that the highest perfection we can achieve by discharging our *varnasrama* duties is to please the Supreme Lord Hari. Therefore it is also said that our occupational duties are considered useless labor if they don’t provoke attraction for hearing and chanting about Krsna. No one is meant to simply maintain the body so that they can enjoy their senses. We are meant to maintain ourselves only so that we will be suitable instruments for hearing and chanting the Lord’s glories as long as we are in the material world.

One who performs his duties in this consciousness is engaged in *kirtaniyah sada harih.* We must work in this consciousness. If we don’t, then we will tend to direct our life’s desires toward sense gratification. But that would be sinful. The value of the human body is that it enables us to hear and chant about Krsna. We should therefore make hearing and chanting the goal of all our work. The *Bhagavatam* states that all occupational engagements are ultimately meant to help us attain liberation. They should not, therefore, be performed simply for material gain.

This comes down to our needing to practice *yukta-vairagya* as long as we are not free of the bodily concept. We will have to accept matter for our bare necessities and thus keep body and soul together. Miseries stemming from paucity, disease, calamity, and old age; happiness from wealth, health, beauty, and education, are all *prarabdha* karmic reactions. They must be experienced as inseparable features of the present body. We will not be able to avoid these reactions. In any case, loss and gain, birth and death, suffering and joy are left behind when one ascends to the spiritual plane. To steady our minds in the meantime, we should know that the happiness and distress that come in relation to the body are unavoidable. They have nothing to do with our eternal existence. The *Bhagavatam* tells us that those who are intelligent and philosophically inclined do not endeavor for happiness, which is attainable by going to the highest planet, Brahmaloka, down to the lowest planet, Patalaloka. They know that happiness derived from sense enjoyment can be obtained automatically in due course simply by remaining in the material world. Similarly, we can obtain distress. We do not have to ask for these conditions; we will experience them whether we like it or not. Therefore, intelligent persons do not waste their human energy either on obtaining material happiness or avoiding material distress.

Of course, this is not an excuse for inertia. Sometimes people wonder why they should endeavor for anything if everything will come to them (or not) automatically. People who avoid their duty without having advanced spiritual realization of their dependence on Krsna, however, seem expert in using the fruits of others’ labor. In the third chapter of *Bhagavad-gita,* Prabhupada calls them parasites and a social disturbance. They neither contribute to spiritual realization, having little themselves, nor to material social welfare. They think of themselves as renounced, although their hearts are filled with material desires.

According to the laws of nature, everyone must contribute to social development in some way. Every living entity who is connected with a body must execute his or her duty. Only liberated souls are free of this constraint. As Krsna explains in the *Bhagavadgita,* liberated souls have no duties to perform because they do not endeavor to maintain their bodies. They accept only what is necessary to maintain body and soul together—whatever the Lord gives—and spend all of their time hearing and chanting. Whatever they receive, they consider *prasadam.* Devotees like Raghunatha dasa Gosvami relished a small pat of butter every other day. To teach us, Raghunatha dasa Gosvami, while still living in Jagannatha Puri and eating the rice from the temple drain, rejected even by the cows, would quote a *Bhagavatam* verse before honoring this rice: “If one’s heart has been cleansed by perfect knowledge and one has understood Krsna, the Supreme Brahman, he then gains everything. Why should such a person act like a debauchee by trying to maintain his material body very carefully? ” (SB [7.15.40](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/15.40)) He condemned himself even for accepting a few grains of rice. He condemned himself for eating even to maintain the body. Such was his renunciation.

We are not on that platform and should not misuse these teachings to think that we do not need to work for personal maintenance or to maintain our families. Although those who are intelligent and philosophically inclined do not endeavor for material happiness, that doesn’t mean that they don’t endeavor. They always endeavor to please the Lord. If they do have material attachments, their attitude allows them to water the root of the tree of *bhakti* and to gradually give up those attachments. Eventually, they find that they possess nothing but the Lord’s service and they are fully satisfied. Then the Lord becomes completely satisfied with them.

They also find that when they are left with nothing but the Lord’s service, they are rendering the greatest social welfare work. The Lord magnifies the devotional service they render for the welfare of human society. He glorifies His devotees so others can learn from their example.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura goes on to explain how this dependence on Krsna is humility: “Therefore these mundane matters are inconsequential to my actual life. I will approach the Lord with utmost humility and pray, ‘O, Krsna, O Govinda, O Lord of my heart, when will I be engaged in Your divine service? Kindly be merciful upon this lowly creature now and quickly accept me as Your servitor. ’ ” Such a devotee is no longer connected to the happiness and distress related to the body, but is fully situated in *yukta-vairagya,* offering everything in Krsna’s service. Bhaktivinoda Thakura adds, “In this mood I may either renounce my family or remain at home living frugally. It doesn’t matter, for by Krsna’s grace I will somehow sustain my life. ”

Most of the ISKCON devotees renounce their families because they are troublesome. That is an abandonment of duty, not an act of renunciation. As Krsna states in *Bhagavad-gita,* those who renounce their duties because they are troublesome are renouncing in the mode of passion. Such renunciation causes only distress.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura goes on to explain about straw in the street: “A dead straw in the street is simply matter. Accordingly, its ego or identity naturally corresponds to its factual existence, for a straw is but a straw. But my present ego is *ahankara,* which is made up of gross and subtle coverings. It does not at all tally with my original spiritual self. The straw’s *abhimana* or ego is factual. But my material ego is unreal. Therefore it is only correct that I become more humble than the straw in the street. ” Because of *ahankara* we think we are our bodies. A straw, however, is simply what it is. It has no false ego left. Therefore, we should consider ourselves lower than a straw and never become proud of our position.

Difficulties arise when we accept both position and designation as nondifferent from ourselves and then feel pride because of them. Pride, of course, is the opposite of humility. If we are always conscious of the superior order, however, and act in accordance with that order, then we will attain real humility. It is not that humility means that we are always passive. Srila Prabhupada was quite assertive. Still, he always displayed such humility that he immediately won others over. He could disarm people. By his example, Prabhupada proved that humility and passivity were not necessarily synonymous.

## We must never abandon our chanting for any reason

**Sri Siksastaka-bhasya Moscow, May 15, 2001**

We have been speaking quite a bit about the holy name, and in the course of these lectures, we have come to realize that so much more can be said. I recently received a letter from a devotee whom I have known for quite some time, although I have not seen him in about two years. But somehow he has a somewhat friendly relationship with me, although previously I was more in a position of authority for him. At the end of his letter, the devotee asked, “Maybe you can share with me some of your thoughts about what you want to accomplish in this life? ” I think this devotee asked me that because he himself has recently undergone a major change in his lifestyle. He had once been living closely in the association of devotees, and now he has separated himself somewhat, although he maintains friendly connections with the other devotees. I responded to his letter yesterday. It did not take much thought on my part to know what I wanted to say. I think that’s because I have been speaking so much lately about the holy name. I told him that what I wanted to accomplish was to be able to chant purely, and that if I wished to achieve this, I was going to have to spend the rest of my life doing the following: chanting in the association of devotees who have a taste for chanting, and preaching the glories of the Lord’s holy name according to my realization and by setting the example in my own practice of chanting.

After saying that, I realized that some devotees are not necessarily preaching the glories of the holy name. There are some devotees who are more concerned with the benefit they can personally derive from chanting. There are many other devotees who are inspired to distribute the glories of the holy name and to distribute it for the benefit of others. These devotees also have to give relevant instructions about how to achieve perfection in chanting. Therefore, in my letter I summarized some of the points about which we have been speaking. Over the course of my giving these classes, I have also come to realize just how important it is for me to emphasize the chanting for my disciples. I want them to become attentive in their chanting, and to strive for the goal of chanting purely. I am feeling a great responsibility in this regard, both to preach and to set a good example by my own chanting practices and my willingness to glorify the Lord’s holy name by speaking about it.

This is one of the reasons why I have being speaking so much about chanting since coming to Moscow. Actually, we did not complete discussing the third *Siksastakam* verse. We spoke about *trnad api sunicena taror api sahisnuna* and *amanina manadena*, but we did not speak about *kirtaniyah sada harih.* These verses contain so much, and there is much more we can say.

Still, in these meetings with my disciples, I also want to address our own experience in chanting and how to practice it in terms of time, place, and attitude. I have noticed a tendency among my disciples to be slack about their chanting. Chanting Hare Krsna is hard work. To chant successfully requires determination, concentration, and a willingness to fulfill the commitment made at the time of initiation. This is especially true for *sadhakas* who are still practicing their chanting but have not yet achieved a high stage. If we were actually feeling the presence of the Lord in our chanting, we would not be able to live without it.

Sometimes devotees wonder how they can live *with* it. They think that they have so many other things to do. Of course, we have to balance our lives. We expend so much energy to maintain our lives in these bodies. Our chanting is simply part of that endeavor. We think about the arrangements we have to make to maintain the body, but we forget that those who are fully experiencing the Lord’s presence in His holy name do not care whether they are living in a material body or not. Such questions are irrelevant. If they make an attempt to maintain their lives in the body, it is simply to fulfill the Lord’s desire and to use their bodies to serve Him.

I spoke about the characteristics of *kirtaniyah sada harih* in Kishinev. The main point we discussed was that liberated souls make no effort to maintain the body. If the Lord wishes to take them, they are ready to leave their bodies behind. If the Lord wishes them to remain in this world, then they are prepared to stay. Whether they stay or go they consider to be the Lord’s decision. Their only business is *kirtaniyah sada harih,* completely absorbing themselves in chanting Krsna’s names and preaching His glories. We cannot imitate this level of consciousness. Rather, the *sadhaka* who is still practicing to chant purely must concentrate on rising above the *nama-aparadha* platform to at least attain *namabhasa.* This requires both work and attention, and each and every devotee who is trying to chant will experience obstacles on his or her path.

Some devotees complain that they cannot experience taste in chanting. Others say they have lost faith in the holy name. There are many reasons why it is difficult to chant, but ultimately there is only one reason: We commit offenses. Bhaktivinoda Thakura states in his *Bhaktyaloka* that the path is easy if it is the path taken by the previous *acaryas.* That path requires that we never abandon our chanting for any reason. If somehow we do give up chanting, it is a sign that we are suffering from offenses to the holy name.

Of course, many devotees abandon their chanting only to take it up again later. But it is better never to have given it up at all. As has been explained, one of the secret differences between Krsna’s holy name and Krsna Himself is that the holy name will forgive us if we want that forgiveness, and will elevate us to a higher platform. Therefore, we see devotees who abandon their chanting only to resume it later after having gained realization by the experience. Their experience usually comes in the form of suffering, and in a mood of repentance they are able to again chant the Lord’s name. Whether they have committed offenses to the devotees, the Deities, other living beings, or the holy name itself—all of which are listed as the four *aparadhas*—they can become rectified if they seek forgiveness. We should take this seriously. If we have committed offenses to the Lord’s name, we should beg the name for forgiveness. The Lord’s holy name will forgive us.

Therefore, there is no real reason to abandon our chanting. If we have not abandoned our chanting, but continue to struggle with lack of taste or commitment, then we should be careful about committing offenses and beg forgiveness for the offenses we have committed.

Now I would like to speak about the actual practice of chanting. Weeks ago, a devotee couple asked me how they can concentrate better when they chant. It is our tendency to be inattentive when we approach the holy name. We also tend not to chant in the best environment. It is best to chant in whatever place is most conducive to our fixing the mind on the Lord’s name. That place may be among other devotees or in a quiet place at home that is reserved specifically for *japa.* Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that three things are required if we wish to increase our attention: we have to chant in the association of advanced Vaisnavas who are relishing the holy name, we have to withdraw ourselves from external and internal distractions, and we have to agree to chant steadily and with determination.

The *Madhurya-kadambini* lists the distractions as *laya* and *viksepa. Laya* refers to the tendency to sleep when one is practicing hearing, chanting, and remembering the Lord. *Viksepa* refers to the tendency to be distracted while engaged in those activities. The *Madhurya-kadambini* gives the example of devotees engaging in *prajalpa* while chanting. We may be sitting down to chant, and then walk over to another devotee and begin a conversation. Then *apratipatti* is mentioned, the tendency not to absorb oneself in *kirtana,* even though one is neither sleepy nor inattentive. There is also *kasaya,* or the propensity for greed, anger, and pride. Then there is *rasasvada,* which means that when we see the opportunity to gratify the senses, we take it. All of these are given as obstacles to be overcome if we actually want to attain steady faith, *nistha.*

If we understand these distractions, we will realize how much effort sincere chanting requires. Therefore, I have asked my disciples not to chant while they are driving—or they can chant, but they should not count those rounds toward their quota of sixteen. I know that whenever I mention this, a certain number of devotees will say they have no other time in which they can chant. They have to go to work, and the only time they can chant their rounds, they say, is when they go back and forth to their jobs. I do realize that there are sometimes extenuating circumstances that make chanting difficult, but even if devotees are forced to chant in the car, they should not become complacent about it. Instead, they should immediately seek opportunities to chant the holy name at appropriate times and in an appropriate place. It is good to chant on the way to work—it is good to be chanting as much as practically possible throughout the day—but it should not be the time when you chant your sixteen rounds.

I have a householder disciple in Boston. He has three children and a full-time job. He chants sixty-four rounds a day and has been doing so for years. I have never seen him without his hand in his beadbag. He chants at least sixteen rounds before leaving for work in the morning. He is an older man, passing through the final stages of household life. His household life has lost its glitter, for sure. So he chants. He chants on his way to work, but by then he has already counted between sixteen and twenty-five rounds.

I am not necessarily recommending that everyone chant sixtyfour rounds a day, but I am presenting this example to show that while you can chant sixteen rounds on the way to work, you can also find a way to chant sixteen concentrated rounds at some other time. We should chant when we are able to make an endeavor to concentrate.

I have been reading Sacinandana Maharaja’s book on the holy name. He has given many seminars on chanting, and in his book, *The Nectarean Ocean of the Holy Name,* he has compiled references from other books and added some of his own commentaries as well. He writes, “Prabhupada recommends the following two verses before chanting. ” The first is from *Sri Krsna Namastaka* by Srila Rupa Gosvami: *nikhila-sruti-mauli-ratna-mala...*

“O, Sri Hari-nama, the entire galaxy of *Upanisads,* which are like a necklace of most precious gems, are serving the toenails of Your lotus feet with their brilliance. In such a way the Vedic scriptures are constantly glorifying Your lotus feet. Great *yogis,* sages and liberated transcendentalists are engaged in worshiping You. Therefore I fully surrender unto You and humbly pray for Your shelter. ”

The second verse Prabhupada recommended is a well-known verse from the *Bhagavatam* ([10.1.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/1.4)):

“Glorification of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is performed in the *parampara* system. It is conveyed from spiritual master to disciple. Such glorification is relished by those who are no longer interested in false, temporary glorifications of this cosmic manifestation. Descriptions of the Lord are the right medicine for the conditioned soul who is undergoing repeated birth and death.

Therefore who will cease hearing such glorification of the Lord except the butcher and one who is killing his own self? ”

According to Sacinandana Swami, Srila Prabhupada recommended that we meditate upon these two verses before beginning to chant.

The *Bhagavatam* verse states that those who are no longer interested in the false, temporary glorification of this world are able to relish the chanting. If we want to relish the chanting, we have to take the right medicine. The chanting is the right medicine. Sacinandana Maharaja presents his own experience of what has helped him become attentive in his book. He writes that he often tells himself:

“The Lord is present in three forms in the material world— the Deity, the holy name, and sometimes directly as an *avatara.* Among these three forms, the holy name is the most merciful. While chanting I have the possibility to associate with the Lord directly in this form. By meditating in this way, I cannot remain indifferent or inattentive to the chanting, because after all, I am aware that at this time I am associating with Krsna. ”

In the later stages, when the mind is actually clear and purified, then a particular form of the Lord will manifest, although perhaps not consistently. In order to get to that stage, we have to first learn to control the mind by hearing the sound vibration of the holy name. This is not easy. It is quite difficult to conquer the mind. But conquering the mind is required. If we want to be successful, we should chant in the association of devotees, withdraw ourselves from external and internal distractions, and chant with steadiness and determination. Srila Prabhupada said that the most important instruction the spiritual master gives to a disciple is to chant the holy name. Everything else is subsidiary, meant only to help us develop taste for chanting.

We often quote the verse, *susrusoh sraddadhanasya, vasudevakatha-rucih:* “O twice-born sages, by serving those devotees who are completely freed from all vice, great service is done. By such service, one gains affinity for hearing the messages of Vasudeva. ” *(SB* [1.2.16](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.16)) Chanting Hare Krsna is our *sadhana* and our *sadhya*, our goal. The goal is *kirtaniyah sada harih.* We mentioned previously that Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati considered chanting with love our *siddha-pranali.*

We should devote this one lifetime to achieving this goal. We must chant our prescribed number of rounds and associate with devotees. These acts are essential if we are to make progress in Krsna consciousness. Sometimes when devotees meet other devotees after they have been away from association for some time, they are irritated by the question, “Are you chanting your rounds? ” If they are not chanting, this question is irksome. I hope that some of the things I am saying here will inspire devotees who have given up chanting to take it up again. It is my duty to encourage devotees to chant and to make advancement in spiritual life.

Since at our stage, committing *nama-aparadha* seems almost inevitable, we can chant Rupa Gosvami’s *Namastaka* and Lord Caitanya’s *Siksastakam* to help ourselves. The Lord has made Himself so easily accessible through His holy name. Prabhupada also recommends that since this is Kali-yuga and we are all so fallen, before chanting Hare Krsna we chant *sri krsna-caitanya, prabhu nityananda, sri advaita gadadhara, srivasadi-gaura-bhakta-vrnda.* It is possible to offend Krsna’s holy name, but Lord Caitanya is so merciful that He does not accept offenses. It is by Lord Caitanya’s mercy that we are able to chant Hare Krsna at all. Therefore it is recommended that we first chant Lord Caitanya’s mantra to invoke His mercy and to help us avoid the ten offenses to the holy name.

Still, it is an intrinsic part of the holy name’s nature that it will forgive us if we want forgiveness. Bhaktivinoda Thakura states in his *Navadvipa-dhama-mahatmya,* “In Navadvipa one may commit an offense to the Lord, but by that offense one will still get love of Godhead, whereas in Vrndavana if one offends the Lord, he will be punished. ” I think he uses the example of Chand Kazi to make his point. Chand Kazi was Kamsa in *krsna-lila,* who was punished and then absorbed into the *brahmajyoti* for his offenses. In Navadvipa-dhama, however, although Chand Kazi committed offenses to Lord Caitanya, he still attained love of God. This is why we say that Lord Caitanya is the most merciful incarnation: He will not accept offenses. We are chanting the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra* by His mercy, and this chanting is the *yuga-dharma.* Lord Caitanya is the *yuga-avatara,* and therefore, we can take shelter of His feet when we chant.

But still, while receiving Lord Caitanya’s mercy, we can trust that Krsna’s holy name itself is merciful. If we continue to offend the name and do not try to free ourselves from our offensive natures, then we will remain at the stage of *nama-aparadha* and will never attain love of God. B*ahu janma kare yadi...* “Even if one chants the holy name of the Lord for millions of births, if he continues to commit offenses, he will never get love of Godhead, which is the ultimate goal of this chanting. ” We have to make the effort to at least rise to the *namabhasa* platform. From there we can make progress toward *suddha-nama.* We have to overcome weakness of heart and ignorance.

Some devotees asked me about my experience of chanting at Govardhana this year. I had some wonderful realizations while I was at Govardhana. Every morning I did *parikrama* of the hill, and every afternoon I sat on the roof for two hours to chant while taking *darsana* of Giriraj a. The nicest part was that while chanting, I was able to meditate on particular *Mas* that took place at Govardhana. This was made possible because I walked around Govardhana in the morning and was able to see all the pastime places. Then, when I returned from *parikrama,* I would spend some time reading about the same *Mas.* Then when I went up to the roof to chant, the *Mas* would come to mind. For me, this ability to meditate like that was the highlight of my visit.

It was also wonderful to be in the inspiring association of my godbrothers, because we spent a lot of time in the evenings discussing Krsna’s pastimes. Every evening we were also reading *Brhad-Bhagavatamrta*, which is just now being published by the BBT. I found that my appreciation of my godbrothers increased during this visit to Govardhana.

Of course, Mahamantra *\[now Bhakti Visrambha Madhava Maharaja\]* was there, and every morning he accompanied me *on parikrama.* This was the one time I appreciated talking during *japa.* I thought, “Here is Mahamantra circumambulating Govardhana Hill with me, and I know at this place and at this time, only one thing will come out of his mouth. ” Then he stopped and said, “Look, Maharaja! ” We would stop whenever he said that, and speak about the pastimes or significance of the place. He pointed out a variety of *silas.* I remember one morning we were walking through the section where there are a lot of *silas,* and Mahamantra was swimming in ecstasy. “Look at these Balarama *silas*! Look, Maharaja! ” That place is before Jatipur, on the inner road.

Anyway, the trip gave me an opportunity for increased meditation on the pastimes described by Srila Rupa and Raghunatha Gosvamis. I also read the tenth canto chapters about Krsna lifting Govardhana Hill along with commentaries by Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura and Srila Sanatana Gosvami. It was wonderful to hear their descriptions of how the cows ran back to Krsna when they saw Him and how they did not want to leave Him.

## Our connection with the holy name is our only connection with Krsna

**Kiev, November 15, 2001**

Today, devotees will be accepting either *harinama* or *gayatri* initiation. I would therefore like to speak about the connection that develops between guru and disciple at the time of initiation. I recently had a discussion on this topic with a devotee, and this discussion helped me realized that we need to speak about this more.

What *is* the connection between guru and disciple? It is generally understood that the primary connection between them is based on *siksa.* That is, the spiritual master instructing the disciple and the disciple accepting those instructions. But which instructions should the disciple be most dependent upon? A disciple should be most dependent upon permanent instructions. If we want to make a permanent connection, we must be dependent upon those instructions that will not change. If we become dependent upon only the more temporary instructions we receive, then what will happen when our lives and situations change? What will become of our connection with the spiritual master?

Sometimes disciples are waiting for an order from the spiritual master, as if that in itself is the only requirement for an eternal bond. Certainly a disciple should be eager to render service on the order of the spiritual master, and often the spiritual master does give such orders. However, we should understand that the most relevant instructions are those which will carry us through our journey in the material world all the way back to Godhead.

Therefore, a disciple must understand how all instructions given by the spiritual master have a relationship with the holy name. Why? Because the connection between guru and disciple is formed on the basis of the holy name. It is the holy name which purifies our hearts; it is the holy name which delivers us from the material world; it is the holy name which establishes our relationship with the Lord. We are forever dependent upon our relationship with the holy name. Although we chant the holy name in this world as a means of purification, we understand that the holy name and Krsna Himself are nondifferent. Our connection with the holy name is our only connection with Krsna. If our connection with the holy name is eternal, and the holy name is nondifferent from Krsna, then our connection with Krsna is eternal. We are Krsna’s eternal servants: *jivera ’svarupa’ haya—krsnera ’nitya-dasa’* (Cc. *Madhya* [20.108](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/20.108)). Therefore, we should want to establish and maintain our eternal relationship with the Lord. Unfortunately, we have forgotten that relationship. At the time of initiation, the spiritual master helps us awaken our remembrance by giving us the holy name—the means to remember Him—and instructing us to chant the holy name with great faith.

We often speak about the faith required to chant as *‘sraddha’. Sraddha* refers to the firm conviction that by chanting and rendering devotional service, all our subsidiary duties will be fulfilled. *Sraddha* is favorable for the execution of *bhakti.* But in what should we place our faith? We must place our faith in the pure holy name. It is that pure holy name that will deliver us from the material world and bring us to the lotus feet of Krsna. *Krsna-nama haite pabe krsnera carana:* “Indeed, simply by chanting the Hare Krsna mantra one will be able to see the lotus feet of the Lord. ” (Cc. *Adi* [7.73](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-adi/7.73)) The chanting of Krsna’s holy name brings us to Krsna’s lotus feet. Therefore, the relationship between the spiritual master and the disciple is based upon those instructions which are related to the Lord’s holy name.

The *Caitanya-caritamrta* states:

**“One has to learn about the beauty and transcendental position of the holy name of the Lord by hearing the revealed scriptures from the mouths of devotees. Nowhere else can we hear of the sweetness of the Lord’s holy name. ” (Cc.** ***Antya*** [**1.101**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-antya/1.101)**)**

Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami wrote this verse after describing Lord Caitanya’s reading of Rupa Gosvami’s famous verse on the holy name:

*tunde tandavini ratim vitanute tundavali-labdhaye*  
*karna-kroda-kadambini ghatayate karnarbudebhyah sprham*  
*cetah-prangana-sangini vijayate sarvendriyanam krtim*  
*no jane janito, kiyadbhir amrtaih krsneti varna-dvayi*

**“I do not know how much nectar the two syllables ‘Krs-na’ have produced. When the holy name of Krsna is chanted, it appears to dance within the mouth. We then desire many, many mouths. When that name enters the holes of the ears, we desire many millions of ears; and when the holy name dances in the courtyard of the heart, it conquers the activities of the mind, and therefore, all the senses become inert. ” (Cc.** ***Antya*** [**1.99**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-antya/1.99)**)**

When Lord Caitanya came upon this verse in Rupa Gosvami’s manuscript, He read it aloud to the assembled devotees. Upon hearing this verse pronounced by Lord Caitanya, Haridasa Thakura experienced ecstasy, completely absorbed in the meaning of the verse. Therefore, Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami explains that we must hear about the beauty and transcendental position of the Lord’s holy name “by hearing the revealed scriptures from the mouths of \[His\] devotees. Nowhere else can \[one\] hear of the sweetness of the Lord’s holy name. ”

What does that mean? In his purport, Prabhupada quotes, *atah sri-krsna-namadi na bhaved grahyam indriyaih* (*Brs*. [1.2.234](https://vedarama.com/en/books/rupa-brs1/1.2.234)): Krsna cannot be understood by our blunt material senses. Similarly, we cannot use our blunt material senses to understand the Lord’s holy name. Chanting and hearing must be done with our purified, spiritual senses. However, those who do not possess purified senses must hear the holy name from the spiritual master.

Hearing the holy name includes hearing of the name’s glories. By hearing the name’s glories, we can gain a proper understanding of the holy name. If we don’t hear the name’s glories continuously, what understanding will we have when we go to chant it? Even if we were to continue chanting for a long time, our consciousness may not become purified. Without the right conception, we will not be able to maintain a proper attitude. Therefore, hearing of the holy name’s glories from the spiritual master is essential.

Hearing is the process by which we water our *bhakti* creeper. If we do not water the creeper of devotion, even if we chant and chant and chant, we will not feel inspired. We will feel as if nothing were happening. If we feel this way, we should question ourselves: “When I am chanting, what am I hearing? Am I hearing my mind? Am I hearing the dictation coming from my *anarthas*? Or am I actually *hearing* the holy name? If I'm hearing the holy name, why am I not experiencing what Rupa Gosvami experiences when he chants? ” Rupa Gosvami has claimed that there is incredible ecstasy in the two syllables, “Krs-na. ” Do we agree?

Although I said that without hearing about the holy name, we cannot gain a proper understanding of it, that does not mean that if we chant anyway, we will not derive benefit. The holy name can give *dharma, artha, kama,* and *moksa* as well as *prema.* But the word used in this verse by Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami is *madhuri,* sweetness. Rupa Gosvami states that the Lord’s name, form, character, and pastimes are as sweet as sugar candy, but those who are afflicted with the jaundice of ignorance (*avidya*) will taste only bitterness.

There are various kinds of ignorance. One form of ignorance is to be ignorant about the glories of the holy name. In such a condition, we may continue to chant for many births, but we will also continue to commit offenses. If we continue to commit offenses, we will gradually lose our taste for chanting. We may even stop chanting altogether.

When we chant, we water our *bhakti-lata-bija.* Alongside the devotional creeper, however, the weeds of *anarthas* also grow. Lord Caitanya explains to Srila Rupa Gosvami that the seed of devotion is first nurtured by the spiritual master when he offers the disciple the Lord’s holy name, but it is the disciple’s duty to water the seed and to make it flower. The disciple waters the seed by hearing and chanting.

Therefore, the hearing and chanting process is the real connection between guru and disciple. The disciple is dependent upon having the seed of *bhakti* watered. Without the guru’s care, the seed will not flourish. The disciple may hear and chant, thus watering the seed, but he will not be able to distinguish the anartha-weeds from the young *bhakti-lata.* Lord Caitanya teaches that we must be careful in discriminating between the two.

To begin the process of learning the difference between the two, we must first ask ourselves about our aspirations in chanting the holy name. Do we actually want the pure name? Do we realize that we cannot receive the pure name that Lord Caitanya delivered until we have uprooted our *anarthas* and overcome all obstacles? If we wish to uproot *anarthas,* we must first develop an honest desire to do so. That will make it possible to cleanse the heart.

The process of cleansing the heart, of course, is hearing and chanting. However, without the proper understanding of the holy name as it has been delivered by the spiritual master, what will we chant? Will Krsna be present in our chanting or not? We want to know that Krsna is present. Chanting is therefore the real connection between the spiritual master and the disciple. When the disciple establishes his permanent connection with the spiritual master on the basis of chanting, he will gain knowledge. The spiritual master will not leave him in ignorance, because one who is afflicted with the jaundice of ignorance cannot taste the holy name. If, under the spiritual master’s order, we chant daily and with attention, we will naturally develop relish for the holy name by the guru’s mercy. Gradually the disease of ignorance will become destroyed at its root.

But to have that disease destroyed, we must chant carefully. Carefully means that we know the difference between our *anarthas* and our spiritual aspirations. It means we are chanting in the right consciousness and are always aspiring to hear appropriate instructions related to our chanting. The more we hear revealed scripture from the mouths of pure devotees, the more we learn about the beauty and transcendental position of Lord Krsna’s holy name and the more our faith increases.

We must have implicit faith in the process when we take initiation. Haridasa Thakura told the Kazi, “I will never give up the holy name. Never! You can cut my body to pieces—you can do whatever you want—but I will never give up chanting Krsna’s name. ” Srila Vrndavana dasa Thakura explains that by this example Haridasa Thakura taught that even if we suffer calamities, we should never abandon our chanting.

If we do abandon the holy name, we should know that we were able to give up chanting because we committed many offenses at Nama Prabhu’s feet. However, Nama Prabhu is extremely merciful. This is one of the confidential differences between the holy name and the Lord’s form. The holy name is even more merciful than Krsna Himself. If we offend the Deity, which is Krsna Himself, we will not be forgiven, but if we offend the Lord’s name, the Lord will forgive us. “O holy name! You appear in two forms: *vacyam*, the Deity form of the Supreme Lord, and *vacakam,* Your transcendental name. Of these two, we consider the form of the holy syllables to be infinitely more compassionate than the form of the Deity. If a person, having taken shelter of the Deity form, commits *aparadha,* the only recourse he has is to become the servant of the holy name and chant incessantly Krsna, Govinda, and so on. By Your immeasurable transcendental potency, all his offenses disappear and he experiences uninterrupted joy, submerged in an ocean of ecstasy. ” (*Namastaka*, Srila Rupa Gosvami)

If we offend the Deity, we return to the Deity for forgiveness. Our only recourse is to become a servant of the holy name and to chant incessantly. We must take shelter and beg forgiveness by continuously chanting the Lord’s holy name.

If we offend the holy name, we can also receive forgiveness from the holy name, but first we must sincerely repent our offense. Along with feeling remorse, we must take shelter of a devotee who can tell us how to renew our faith by continuous chanting. By receiving both proper instruction and permission to vibrate the Lord’s name constantly, we will continue to water our creeper of *bhakti* despite our offense, and will learn to become dependent on the Lord’s name.

If we want a lasting connection with our spiritual master, we must learn to depend upon *these* instructions. We must increase our understanding of the holy name’s glories so that we will stop committing offenses. When we can chant offenselessly, we will be able to realize the full glory, beauty, and transcendental position of the holy name of Krsna, Himself.

The holy name bestows all spiritual benedictions. What greater benediction can we receive than the gift of the pure holy name? This is what the spiritual master wants to give us. Every disciple must therefore strive to make progress in his or her chanting. If disciples do not hear and simply continue with the attitude, “I simply have to chant my sixteen rounds and then I’ll be a good disciple, ” they will not make a permanent connection with the spiritual master.

We have to ask ourselves what we are hearing. If we are not hearing the holy name, if we are not hearing sufficiently the Lord’s glories, then we must increase our hearing from Krsna’s devotees. This is real *siksa.* Our relationship with the spiritual master is based on *siksa.*

Along with hearing of the holy name’s glories, we must also hear of the Lord’s pastimes from the spiritual master. The Lord’s pastimes are nondifferent from the holy name. When devotees chant the pure holy name, they experience Krsna. When they see Krsna, they also see His form, pastimes, and qualities. Then, they experience ever-increasing pleasure—a continuous wave of ecstasy. Sometimes they are submerged in that wave, sometimes they are floating on top of it, and sometimes they are again submerged. This is what devotees experience when they chant *suddha-nama.* If we want to experience this, we must continue to chant in the association of devotees.

Therefore, let us always continue to water the seed of *bhakti* we have received and try to understand the nature of the real connection between spiritual master and disciple. Real initiation is a beginning and leads further into the deeper connection based on the holy name. Gradually, the proper conception of chanting should manifest within our hearts, and eventually we should begin to get a glimpse of the various stages in chanting.

The continuous effort to reach *suddha-nama* is what differentiates offenders from real disciples. Offenders remain in ignorance; those who strive to become free from offenses do not. Real disciples seek appropriate instruction in the holy name’s glories and endeavor to become free from their *anarthas* by proper discrimination between the *anarthas* and the *bhakti-lata-bija.*

When devotees understand what their *anarthas* are, they try to overcome them by hearing and chanting. Such devotees have faith that even though they may not be able to overcome their *anarthas* quickly, due to weakness of heart, the holy name will forgive their failures. Such disciples try to hear proper instruction and to rectify themselves if they deviate. Throughout, they continue to chant.

Today’s candidates for initiation will be accepting vows. These vows should be accepted on the strength of their faith in the power of the Lord’s holy name. Each one of you should think, “I will be able to maintain these vows only by maintaining my firm faith in the holy name and in the instructions I am receiving in relation to the holy name. ” We will maintain our vows if we have this consciousness; otherwise there may be difficulties. If the day ever comes when there is a discrepancy in our behavior, we should then seek appropriate instruction in relation to the holy name and continue to chant with a proper attitude. The holy name will reinstate us. All glories to that holy name of the Lord!

Srila Haridasa Thakura praises the holy name: “Just as the sun can dissipate all the darkness of this world, just once chanting the holy name of the Lord offenselessly can eradicate all the reactions of sinful life. All glories to that holy name of the Lord. ” We should strive to become offenseless chanters. Therefore, on days when devotees received the holy name from Srila Prabhupada, Srila Prabhupada would always recite the ten offenses. In that way, devotees could understand the offenses properly.

## What is the right consciousness to have to be able to chant the holy name?

**Sri Siksastaka-bhasya Moscow, May 17, 2001**

In his commentary on Sri *Siksastakam,* Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes:

“The word *amani* reveals the third quality of the devotee whose chanting is decorated with offenselesness. His heart is spotlessly free from mundane ego and false prestige. The false designations of the *jiva’s* gross and subtle body are grown up out of nescience. Yogic mystic power, opulence, good looks, high birth, strength, position, and influence are all features of his *ahankara.* They are incompatible with his real spiritual identity. Indeed they are totally alien and false. Having a spotless heart devoid of false ego and prestige means specifically that the devotee completely distances himself from all unreal designations. But what about the pride that may follow the subjugation of false designations? In spite of his being highly respected, the devotee who demonstrates tolerance, humility, and a pure heart is a fit candidate to chant purely. He will take care to avoid becoming proud of being honored as a *brahmana* if he is the householder, or a sannyasi if he is a renunciate. He concentrates on the lotus feet of Lord Krsna and chants His holy name. ” (Sri *Siksastakam)*

*Amani* means one whose heart is free from false ego and false prestige. Earlier we spoke about how persons become proud of their external designations and try to project a particular identity in order to attract others—whether that identity be based on wealth, beauty, or whatever. Such persons think, “Let me project my external designation to attract others’ attention. ” We also gave the example of how under illusion, a person thinks that if he attracts someone’s attention toward himself he will increase his pleasure. We also explained how such persons become victims of exploitation. Others tend to believe them to be what they project, and then try to develop relationships with them based on the projection of wealth or beauty, etc., solely for their own enjoyment. If someone projects an identity of wealth, others will develop a relationship with him in order to enjoy his wealth. If someone projects an identity of beauty, others will develop a relationship with him in order to take advantage of his beauty. This is ultimately not very satisfying to the person who is projecting the identity, and therefore he suffers. No relationship based on the body can be ultimately satisfying, especially when that relationship is based on false designations.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura describes the different designations we tend to accept—designations based on our opulence, good looks, high birth, strength, or position. All of these are external designations, and identifying with any of them can cause us to become proud.

As devotees, we are interested in developing a sense of selfworth based on our ability to please Krsna. As we develop devotional qualities, we become more and more pleasing to Krsna, and therefore more and more pleasing to ourselves. It is natural for everyone to want to please someone else and to base our feelings of self-worth on our success or failure in that regard. But when we concentrate on trying to please others by projecting the identity of a wealthy person, a beautiful person, a person of high birth, etc., we find that not only are we exploited by others, but none of these qualities actually satisfies the Lord. If the Lord is not satisfied, it is impossible for us to feel completely satisfied.

The *Bhagavatam* explains this well. It gives the example of a person decorated with a heavy turban or crown:

“The common man who is puffed up with his material position and does not bow down before the Deity of the Lord in the temple, or who defies temple worship without any knowledge of the science, must know that his so-called turban or crown will only succeed in further drowning him in the water of the ocean of material existence. A drowning man with a heavy weight on his head is sure to go down more swiftly than those who have no heavy weight. ” (SB [2.3.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/3.21), purport)

The scriptures also give the example of kings who were not proud of their material position and who were eager to serve the Lord. The *Caitanya-caritamrta* describes the qualities of Maharaja Prataparudra. Although he was the king of Orissa, he was free from pride, and because he was so eager to render service to the Lord, he personally accepted a broom to sweep the street before Lord Jagannatha during the Ratha-yatra festival. Because of his service to Lord Jagannatha, his desire to meet Lord Caitanya was fulfilled. In other words, the Lord was pleased because Maharaja Prataparudra wanted only to please Him.

There are many other examples in the *Bhagavatam* and *Caitanyacaritamrta* of devotees who deserved recognition but who had no interest in receiving it. This is similar to the point Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura is making when he says that a devotee is devoid of false ego and false prestige, and he should always try to distance himself completely from these unreal designations. That is, those who are not completely free from false ego should try to distance themselves from “unreal designations. ” When a person is completely free from such designations, of course, there is no question of needing to distance himself; he does not identify himself with such designations in the first place. Those who are not completely free have to try to push false designations away. That begins with not seeking personal recognition.

That is difficult to do. It is more our tendency to want people to recognize us for even small achievements, because if no one sees what we have done, how will we feel good about ourselves?

We often give the example of Vallabha Bhatta in this regard. He wrote a commentary on the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* and was quite proud of it. He considered his commentary the best of all the commentaries, and he began to meditate on only one purpose— getting Lord Caitanya to hear it. The Lord knew, of course, that Vallabha Bhatta’s heart was dominated by pride and that there was no benefit in hearing the commentary of someone so proud. Therefore, every time Vallabha Bhatta tried to speak his commentary before the Lord, the Lord cut down his pride. Lord Caitanya did this first by glorifying various Vaisnavas. Vallabha Bhatta glorified Lord Caitanya and asked Him to hear his commentary, but Lord Caitanya said, “Actually, I have no qualification. I am just an impersonalist *sannyasi.* How can I possibly understand *Srimad-Bhagavatam*? But if I have any hope of understanding *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* it is because I am associating with Lord Nityananda, who is distributing love of Godhead. ”

Lord Caitanya then described the qualities of Lord Nityananda, Advaita Acarya, Ramananda Raya, Haridasa Thakura, and Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya, saying that it was only by their mercy that He was able to understand anything. By this he was trying to teach Vallabha Bhatta that the only qualification anyone has to speak *Srimad-Bhagavatam* is that he has received the devotees’ mercy.

Later, when Vallabha Bhatta tried to recite his commentary before the devotees, the devotees took Lord Caitanya’s lead and also refused to hear it. Vallabha Bhatta’s heart was so filled with pride that he was ready to die. He thought, “How can I clean away this mud of shame which is covering me? Nobody will hear my commentary on *Srimad-Bhagavatam.* How can I live? ” This is exactly the prideful mentality: we think, “I am doing something great, but no one recognizes it. I will feel good about its greatness (and my own) if someone simply recognizes the weight of my achievement. ”

When our hearts are filled with such pride, we spend time trying to convince others about how great our achievements are. Bhaktivinoda Thakura warns that we should distance ourselves from seeking this kind of recognition. Instead, we should leave the measuring of our achievements up to Krsna. Krsna knows our heart; He knows exactly what we are thinking. He recognizes the qualities of a devotee who is free of pride, and He derives great pleasure from devotees who do not look for recognition. When the Lord is pleased, we too will become pleased, because achieving the Lord’s pleasure is the perfection of all our endeavors.

Then what happens to a person who does not try to project his image as opulent, good-looking, strong, or influential? What about the pride that may follow such subjugation of false designations? We so easily become proud: “Just see, I have distanced myself from wealth, and bodily beauty. ” We then begin to project the image of humble Vaisnavas, thinking that humility is a quality that will attract others to us.
