This is the trap of pride. A devotee does not try to project an image. Rather, he is naturally humble, never seeking personal recognition. He considers pride as poison. He is not interested in the false glorification coming from the material world.

Srila Prabhupada states that we should chant this third verse of the *Siksastakam* to help us set our minds in the right consciousness before beginning to chant Krsna’s names. The *Bhagavatam* ([10.1.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/1.4)) states, “Glorification of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is performed in the *parampara* system; that is, it is conveyed from spiritual master to disciple. Such glorification is relished by those no longer interested in the false, temporary glorification of this cosmic manifestation. Descriptions of the Lord are the right medicine for the conditioned soul undergoing repeated birth and death. Therefore, who will cease hearing such glorification of the Lord except a butcher or one who is killing his own self? ” Only those who are no longer interested in the false, temporary praise of the material world can relish glorification of Krsna.

Freedom from interest in worldly praise is real humility. Therefore, Bhaktivinoda Thakura states that in spite of his being highly respected, the devotee will take care to avoid becoming proud of being honored. He concentrates on the lotus feet of Krsna and chants His holy name. Instead of concentrating on self-glorification, he will concentrate on glorification of the Lord. Such concentration will help those who are not completely free of pride to become humble.

It is natural for the living being to want to glorify someone, but all glorification is meant for the Supreme Lord. This quality is described as one of the characteristics of a devotee on the platform of *bhava-bhakti.* It’s called *mana-sunyata. Mana-sunyata* is defined as “prideless. ” Even when a devotee has all reason to be proud of his spiritual advancement, he remains prideless. Rather, he always feels himself humble, even though he is highly elevated.

Pridelessness is listed among the nine qualities of steady *bhava.* Srila Rupa Gosvami states that these nine qualities are important indicators of spiritual advancement because they cannot be imitated or concealed. The other eight qualities are as follows. *Avyarthakalatvam,* utilization of time: A devotee is eager to use all twenty-four hours in the Lord’s service. *Ksanti,* cessation of material desires: A devotee remains tolerant even when there is cause for disturbance. *Virakti,* detachment: A devotee is completely indifferent to sense gratification. A*sa-bandha,* hope against hope: A devotee is always confident that by the Lord’s mercy he can be elevated to the highest perfectional stage of devotional service, even though he feels himself to be low, fallen, and unqualified. *Samutkantha,* very eager to achieve the stage of loving devotional service; *nama-gane sada ruci,* addiction to the glorification of the Lord; *asaktis tad-gunakhyane*, intense affection for glorifying the Lord’s qualities; and *pritis tadvasati sthale,* love for living in the Lord’s *dhama.* Pridelessness is listed as the fourth quality of steady *bhava.*

Aside from *amani* in this third verse of the *Siksastakam* there is *manada. Manada* means to offer respect to others. This is important.

“The word *manada* means to offer all respect to others. This is the fourth symptom of the devotee who chants without offense. He understands that all living entities are Krsna’s eternal servants, so he is never envious or spiteful toward anyone. He pleases everyone with sweet sublime words and exemplary actions that bring benedictions to the world. He offers respect to distinguished personalities such as learned *brahmanas:* To Brahma, Siva and other demigods he offers obeisances with utmost humility, praying to them for increasing his devotion to Lord Krsna; and to elevated Vaisnavas and pure devotees he offers service with heart and soul. The chanting of the holy name that is accompanied by the above-mentioned four qualities is the highest success in human life. This is the declaration of Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu, the most magnanimous *avatara* and savior of the fallen souls of Kali-yuga. ” (Sri *Siksastakam)*

Lord Caitanya confirms that if a devotee can chant constantly, decorated with these four qualities, that is the highest perfection. The *Caitanya-caritamrta* states:

“These are the symptoms of one who chants the Hare Krsna *maha-mantra.* Although he is very exalted, he thinks himself lower than the grass on the ground, and like a tree, he tolerates everything in two ways. When a tree is cut down, it does not protest, and even when drying up, it does not ask anyone for water. The tree delivers fruits, flowers and whatever it possesses to anyone and everyone. It tolerates scorching heat and torrents of rain, yet it still gives shelter to others. Although a Vaisnava is the most exalted person, he is prideless and gives all respect to everyone, knowing everyone to be the resting place of Krsna. If one chants the holy name of Lord Krsna in this manner, he will certainly awaken his dormant love for Krsna’s lotus feet. ” (Cc. Antya [20.22-26](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-antya/20.22))

Sometimes devotees ask how a neophyte can recognize an exalted Vaisnava and take shelter of him. But it is not true that neophytes are completely incapable of recognizing an exalted Vaisnava. The *sastras* list the nine qualities of *bhava-bhakti,* and assure us that these qualities can neither be hidden nor imitated. This information is given by Srila Rupa Gosvami to help us recognize whether someone is actually elevated or not. In the case of a neophyte’s inability, the real inability is his ignorance of these qualities. He does not know what to look for. Srila Prabhupada often warned us about this, telling us who were the *sadhus* and who simply wore the beards of *sadhus.* We cannot judge by appearance or by the look such a person has in his eyes. Rather, we must measure a *sadhu* against the symptoms listed by Srila Rupa Gosvami, which are given by Krsna Himself.

Arjuna asked Krsna the same question: “O Krsna, what are the symptoms of one whose consciousness is thus merged in transcendence? How does he speak, and what is his language? How does he sit, and how does he walk? ” (Bg. [2.54](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/2.54)) Krsna provides lists in the scriptures which we can use in our search for a pure devotee. We should be looking for that devotee from whom we can hear in order to achieve an elevated stage in Krsna consciousness. If we want to become elevated, we must hear from an elevated devotee. As Prabhupada said, “It is not simply by the eyes that one can recognize. ” The most important sense to use is the ear. By hearing, we can discern whether the person before us has the qualities Krsna lists in *Bhagavad-gita* and other places.

By hearing from elevated devotees, we can become convinced of Krsna conscious truth. If the speaker is potent and the hearer receptive, such speaking and hearing will act to remove the clouds of *anarthas* and the fog of ignorance. An exalted Vaisnava can clear our vision and clear away the dust of misconception. He can enable us to see both the goal of *suddha-bhakti* and the means to attain it. He will also speak on the authority of scripture. The characteristic of an *uttama-bhakta* is that he understands scripture so well that he is able to convince others of the scriptural conclusion. So if we wish to find an exalted devotee so that we can make progress, we must first look, then hear. Only by hearing will we actually recognize another’s qualities. Then we can begin the process of freeing ourselves from pride and offering devotion to the Lord.

## Chanting is the perfect spiritual activity

**Lecture I Minsk, May 19, 2001**

Srila Prabhupada was pleased when he saw the devotees chanting enthusiastically. Whenever I was with Srila Prabhupada, I could see how much he enjoyed watching his followers chant. That enjoyment is at the heart of a Vaisnava’s nature. Vaisnavas are satisfied to see others pleased by chanting Hare Krsna. Srila Prabhupada would have a big smile whenever he watched us in *kirtana.*

This seemed especially true in Mayapur. Prabhupada used to circumambulate the Deities and then stop and ring the bell before reentering the temple room. Whenever he stopped, he would lift his hand, indicating that we should jump higher and higher in our dancing. Prabhupada knew that our enthusiastic dancing, our hand clapping, and our fervent chanting was cleansing us of the many sinful reactions that lived in our hearts. Because it is the Vaisnava’s nature to be free of envy and pride and to wish only good for others, Prabhupada was our genuine well-wisher. He was genuinely happy to see us becoming purified. He wanted that our chanting should go on forever.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that chanting Hare Krsna is both an activity for this world and an activity for the spiritual world. It is a perfect, spiritual activity. Srila Prabhupada did not want us to stop glorifying Krsna. Bhaktivinoda Thakura had a similar feeling, which he described in his song, *Narada muni bhajaya vina:* “I will be very happy to see that this chanting of *hari-nama* will go on like this forever. ”

Chanting is our most important activity. Srila Prabhupada said, “The most important instruction given by the spiritual master to the disciple is to chant Hare Krsna. ” We should always think about how we can continue chanting with enthusiasm. Enthusiastic chanting is not something that simply takes place on festival occasions. Enthusiastic chanting has to take place every day of our lives. The more we begin to relish the chanting, the more we will develop the qualities necessary for us to continue relishing the holy name.

The third verse of Lord Caitanya’s *Siksastakam* states, *trnad api sunicena, taror api sahisnuna, amanina manadena, kirtaniyah sada harih:*

“One can chant the holy name of the Lord in a humble state of mind, thinking himself lower then the straw on the street, devoid of all sense of false prestige, and ready to offer all respect to others. In such a state of mind one can chant the holy name of the Lord constantly.”

Srila Bhaktisiddhanta called this verse the *siddha-pranali* of Gaudiya Vaisnavism. It is our means to achieve perfection; the perfection is *kirtaniyah sada harih,* constant chanting of the Lord’s names. To achieve it, one actually has to be ornamented with the four qualities described in this third verse of the *Siksastakam.* If we cannot develop these qualities, then we will not be able to remain absorbed in chanting.

It is difficult for proud persons to constantly glorify Krsna, because they are too busy thinking of self-glorification. Self-glorification goes completely against the principle of glorifying the Lord. A proud person may chant Hare Krsna—it is not that he cannot chant the holy name—but if he actually wants to absorb himself in chanting in the mood of glorifying the Lord, he will have to free himself from his self-absorption.

Sometimes people chant Hare Krsna so that they will be recognized as great devotees. If they are chanting for personal recognition, then how can they simultaneously concentrate on glorifying the Lord? The two states of mind are opposed. Glorification of the Lord means not only to glorify the Lord’s names but His qualities and pastimes. To give one’s heart to such glorification, one must first free it of pride.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura tells us of those proud persons who wish to receive initiation into the chanting simply to gain respect from others. If such persons gain respect from others, then they will also be able to extract service from them. Such persons are considered offenders to the holy name. Persons who offend the holy name are struck down by their own offenses. Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains that if we give a valuable piece of cloth to a monkey, the monkey will tear it up. If we throw pearls before swine, the swine will trample them. This is because these animals do not know the real value of what we have presented. Similarly, those who do not understand the value of the Lord’s holy name will offend the holy name.

This does not mean that we shouldn’t distribute the holy name to everyone we meet. Vaisnavas in Lord Caitanya’s service are forever absorbed in spreading the holy name. Aside from the benefit preaching has for others, spreading the holy name’s glories helps us advance in the process of chanting. In this way, everyone is given the chance to receive the holy name. We should strongly desire to advance in our chanting. Unless we actually become free of offenses in chanting, we will never be able to achieve love of God, which is the goal of the chanting. We will never chant purely, and thus never fully relish the holy name. We will not experience the pure holy name but rather the shadow or some reflection of the name.

The reflection of the holy name is called “*pratibimba*”. *Pratibimba* is compared to the light we receive when the sun reflects upon water. Such a reflection is distorted; it is not the real sun in its full potency. Similarly, although the holy name is completely pure, the reflection of the holy name is distorted by the materialistic conceptions in our hearts caused by our offenses. We chant, but we are not chanting the pure or real holy name.

The first step to clearing this problem is to develop a desire to advance in our chanting. As long as we continue to offend the holy name, we will not actually be able to experience the real holy name.

Therefore there are different stages of chanting. The first stage is *nama-aparadha,* then *namabhasa,* then *suddha-nama.* If we wish to reach *namabhasa,* we must want to free ourselves from committing offenses against the holy name.

We have been reading Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s commentaries on the verse, *trnad api sunicena.* We have also been reading about each quality listed. We spoke about *kirtaniyah sada harih,* which is the stage of perfection. We then spoke about humility, tolerance, freedom from pride, and the ability to offer respect to everyone.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura then goes on to describe how we can attain the pure name. He states that the *sloka, trnad api sunicena,* was composed solely to clearly establish how to avoid *nama-aparadha* and surpass *namabhasa* to attain *suddha-nama.*

“One whose intelligence avoids Krsna and rushes headlong after the intoxication of material enjoyment can never perceive his true position as but an infinitesimal spark of consciousness. One who thinks he is the enjoyer can never admit his insignificance, nor be tolerant by nature. ” (Sri *Siksastakam)*

Such a person will not want to renounce false ego. As a gross sense enjoyer, he will be totally unwilling and incapable of offering respects to another enjoyer like himself. It is a materialist’s habit to be insincere in his dealings with others, sometimes to the point of becoming obnoxious. But a Vaisnava who finds ecstasy in the holy name is more humble than a blade of grass, more tolerant then a tree, and is unconcerned with or even unwilling to receive respect from others. His only interest is to offer them respect. “Only such an elevated soul is awarded the constant association of the holy name in *kirtana. ”*

In this section, Bhaktivinoda Thakura is pointing out that it is the enjoying spirit that is at the root of all offenses to the holy name. The enjoying spirit is the desire to enjoy recognition, material profit, or gross sense gratification. This spirit prevents us from experiencing *suddha-nama.*

Devotees sometimes taste a little of the holy name, but then find that the taste deserts them. They become bewildered and wonder what happened. Often, they think there is something wrong with the chanting process. Bhaktivinoda Thakura clears that misconception: We have no taste because we continue to commit offenses. Perhaps when we first began to chant, we were committing fewer offenses.

We at least want to reach the *namabhasa* platform. At *namabhasa,* we are still too weak-hearted and ignorant about the name’s glories to chant purely, but we are less addicted to sense gratification. To be situated on *namabhasa,* we also have to have a desire to become free of *nama-aparadha.* For those who are too addicted to sense gratification, we find that even their chanting is done to please their own senses. In this category are persons addicted to gross and subtle sense gratification and persons addicted to the concept of achieving liberation. Such persons do not try to free themselves from offenses to the name because their goal is to enjoy the name, not to serve it. They think that chanting will bring them peace, freedom from anxiety, and that that will increase their enjoyment of life in other ways. These people are *aparadhis* and cannot even reach the platform of *namabhasa.*

Those striving for *namabhasa* tend to become aware of the obstacles on the path and the offenses they tend to commit. They then work hard to overcome the obstacles and to avoid the offenses. These persons may still commit offenses, but it is not their intention. The gross sense enjoyers do not care about offenses as long as they can enjoy. They do not understand the nature of actual happiness.

We must therefore be careful not to become complacent in our chanting, because if we continue to commit offenses, what will happen to us? We will lose any taste we have for chanting. If we lose our taste, then chanting will become more difficult. It is very difficult to continue doing an activity that does not give us taste. If we are not tasting the chanting, our minds will reach out to sense gratification. That will make it even more difficult to continue chanting the Lord’s holy name.

If we want to always advance in chanting, we have to follow a process for chanting. That process includes developing the four qualities mentioned in the third verse of the *Siksastakam:* humility, freedom from pride, tolerance, and the ability to offer all respects to others. These qualities will be very difficult to develop as long as we maintain the enjoying spirit. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes, “The gross sense enjoyer is unwilling and incapable of offering respect to someone else. ” He is interested only in his own material qualities.

We already discussed how someone absorbed in false ego always tries to project an image of himself to others. That image may be based on his wealth, bodily beauty, or education, but he uses it to gain respect for himself from the people around him. A wealthy person is always trying to show off his wealth by wearing expensive clothes and driving fancy cars. He thinks by doing this that people will notice him and think well of him. Being respected is part of his pleasure. “What is the value of all this wealth if I cannot show it off and thus enjoy it? ” He doesn’t realize that by showing off this wealth he attracts people to him who wish to exploit him for his money. Similarly, a beautiful person may show off his or her body in order to get pleasure from the respect people show, but he or she also attracts people who wish to exploit that beauty for their own pleasure.

That is why the *sastras* state that Vaisnavas are not interested in material opulence. A Vaisnava’s only interest is to attract the Lord. Material qualities do not attract the Lord. What attracts the Lord is humility, pridelessness, tolerance, and simplicity.

Genuine humility means that a person never becomes proud of his humility. Bhaktivinoda Thakura states that one whose heart is devoid of false ego and prestige completely distances himself from unreal designations. “But what about pride that may follow the subjugation of all false designations? ” Sometimes a person may think, “Oh, I am not trying to project my material wealth, beauty, strength, position, or influence anymore and have distanced myself from these things. Now I can project humility. ” Bhaktivinoda Thakura responds:

“In spite of being highly respected, the devotee who demonstrates tolerance, humility, and a pure heart is a fit candidate to chant purely. He will take care to avoid becoming proud or being honored; he concentrates on the lotus feet of Lord Krsna and chants His holy name. ” *(Sri Siksastakam)*

He does not plan how to receive the respect of other people. Instead, he concentrates on his chanting. A person who wants to be recognized for his humility does not have time to concentrate on chanting the holy name, and only a devotee who concentrates fully on the holy name, glorifying the Lord and feeling dependent upon Him can actually attract the Lord’s attention. The Lord looks at a devotee’s qualities. Thus our desire to attract attention can be fulfilled by renouncing the desire to attract attention. Does that sound like a contradiction? The contradiction is resolved by focusing our desire to attract attention on attracting the attention of Krsna. That should be the goal of everything a devotee does.

By attracting the Lord’s attention, a devotee invokes His presence. This means that Krsna will appear in His holy name. Then the devotee will actually experience the Lord’s presence in His name. If the Lord invests all His potencies in His holy name, then one who chants it purely will experience all the Lord’s potencies. This is *suddha-nama.* When one chants in this pure state of consciousness, he has actually perceived Krsna’s presence in His name.

Therefore, Bhaktivinoda Thakura states that Lord Caitanya composed this third *sloka* in order to clearly establish how to avoid *nama-aparadha* and *namabhasa* in chanting. We approach *suddhanama* by clearing ourselves of offensive behavior toward Krsna’s name.

Of course, *namabhasa* is still auspicious chanting. Even at that stage, we still receive so much benefit from the chanting. Bhaktivinoda Thakura lists the seven effects of chanting *namabhasa:* The first is faith, then extinction of sinful reactions, then liberation from the effects of Kali-yuga, then the disappearance of fear and the advent of peace, then protection from the influence of ghosts and adverse planets, then the attainment of the four goals of life *(purusartha*), and then the experience of bliss.

Even if we chant *namabhasa,* we can still experience a certain amount of bliss. We can even be elevated enough to attain Vaikuntha. But if we want to achieve the Lord’s full presence, imbued with our *rasa or* relationship with Him, and thus become qualified to enter into the topmost abode, Goloka Vrndavana, then we have to chant *suddha-nama.*

Bhaktivinoda Thakura prays, *kabe ha’be bolo se-dina amar, aparadha ghuci’, suddha name ruci:* “When will I become free from offenses and thus be able to chant *suddha-nama,* the pure holy name? ” We rise to *suddha-nama* from *namabhasa.* As long as we remain on the platform of *nama-aparadha,* however, which is described as *“bahu janma”,* we will not be able to achieve love of God even after millions of lifetimes of chanting. We must become free of the ten offenses.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura continues: “When these pure souls offer glorification and obeisances to the spiritual master and other senior Vaisnavas, they do so because of their quality *manada,* the ability to respect others according to their status. ” When such persons “advise and enthuse disciples and *sadhakas* in the process of chanting, showering them with appreciative and even laudatory words spoken in affection, ” they manifest their *amani,* their freedom from the desire to be honored. The pure devotee never exploits the material benefits that come with his elevated status. Instead, he remains tolerant, not easily moved to anger or even dismay at the fools who resent his prominence. He exhibits forbearance. The offenseless chanter sees himself as lower than the straw on the street. He never thinks of himself as a Vaisnava or guru. “Indeed he always thinks himself a disciple of everyone, most meek and fallen. ”

Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains further the quality of *manada,* offering respects to others: “Even though a person may be offered so much respect for himself, he never exploits that position. He can gain so many advantages from that position, but he is not interested in any of these things. He does not expect respect for himself. ”

Prabhupada also commented on this point: “A devotee should command respect by his behavior, but he should never demand respect. ” A devotee does not want to be respected. If such respect comes, he accepts it, but he does not seek it out. Sometimes people offer so much respect that envious persons are disturbed. Still, the devotee does not seek such respect offered toward himself.

Haridasa Thakura is a good example of this. The local people respected him because he manifested ecstatic symptoms. He did not manifest these symptoms to draw people’s attention to himself; he was absorbed in Krsna’s pastimes and holy name. He did not always maintain external consciousness. Although everyone around him could see that he was experiencing divine consciousness, an envious *brahmana* in attendance thought, “I am a *brahmana* and he was born in a lower family. He took birth as a Muslim. The *brahmanas* are the most respected class in society, and just look how much respect this low-class man is being offered. ” The *brahmana* then fell to the ground and began to also manifest ecstatic symptoms. But a snake-bite physician, who had patiently and reverentially waited for Haridasa to regain external consciousness, could not tolerate the *brahmana’s* envy towards Haridasa, and began beating the *brahmana* with a stick. Bhaktivinoda Thakura states that sometimes people will become angry with a devotee who is offered respect and resent his prominent position. An elevated Vaisnava will respond to their envy with forbearance. Such a Vaisnava does not even consider himself a Vaisnava, and therefore does not take offense when envy is directed his way.

Recently, a devotee asked me how we can realistically distance ourselves from pride. He said, “When a preacher goes to some place and people say, ‘Please come back, ’ how does he *not* feel the natural rise of pride in his achievement of attracting them to Krsna? ” This is easily reconciled by the devotee’s unwillingness to claim the credit for his preaching, and for his overwhelming desire to share the glories of the Lord with others. A Vaisnava always offers his obeisances to his superiors in his mind before speaking to others, then offers obeisances to his audience. Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami does that often in the *Caitanya-caritamrta.* He offers his obeisances to the readers, because they are eager to drink the nectar emanating from the lotus feet of Lord Caitanya. He thinks as he writes, “By their blessing and by the blessings of my superiors, I will be qualified to say something worth saying. ” In this way he remains a servant, free from pride. He never thinks of himself as a great orator but only as a humble conduit for the Lord’s mercy.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes, “Knowing every atom and every infinitesimal spirit soul to be the residence of Lord Krsna, he never treats anyone as inferior to himself. Nor does he require anything from anyone in this world. Even if others are envious and spiteful toward him, he never retaliates. On the contrary, he prays for his tormenter’s well-being. ” Of course, this is the characteristic of one who is chanting *suddha-nama,* the Lord’s pure holy name.

Having said this, we know that in order to preach, it is sometimes necessary for an elevated Vaisnava to see others differently. Otherwise, how could he instruct those who are in need? If he does not see the discrepancies in their behavior and practice, he will not be able to help them elevate themselves.

This is how we think of Srila Prabhupada. Srila Prabhupada was once speaking strongly to a group of Indians. He was speaking so strongly that he was sometimes calling them rascals and fools. One man retorted, “Swamiji, how is this *‘Panditah sama-darsinah’* \[seeing everyone equally\]? Why you are speaking so harshly? ” Prabhupada replied, “I am not on that platform. That is a higher platform. My duty is to see what is Krsna’s instruction, and when others are not following Krsna’s instructions, to point out, ‘You, rascal, why you are not following Krsna’s instruction? ’ But I do not discriminate: I give these instructions to everyone. That is *Panditah sama-darsinah.* That is how I see everyone equally. I do not discriminate and therefore everyone gets Krsna’s instruction. ”

In order to instruct others, a Vaisnava must agree to see their faults. Bhaktivinoda Thakura also states this in his *Bhaktyaloka,* where he says that a spiritual master sometimes has to point out faults in order to teach others. This information is contained in the chapter on *prajalpa.* There, Bhaktivinoda Thakura speaks very strongly about the importance of not speaking about others. But then he states that sometimes it is necessary, such as in the case of the spiritual master with his disciples.

His proof of this is Sukadeva Gosvami. Sukadeva Gosvami is obviously an elevated Vaisnava who is engaged in *krsna-katha,* not *prajalpa.* Still, in the *Bhagavatam* Sukadeva Gosvami describes the materialistic householders, who work hard during the day and spend the night in sex enjoyment. Why should a Vaisnava criticize them? Bhaktivinoda Thakura points out that Sukadeva Gosvami makes the criticism so that others would understand the difference between material and spiritual life. That criticism does not erase the fact that Sukadeva Gosvami is a perfectly humble devotee.

Srila Prabhupada also had the ability to speak critically and to remain humble. Therefore, almost everyone to whom he spoke accepted him and offered him respect. Even the Indian man who told Prabhupada that he wasn’t seeing equally ended up with some respect. After telling Prabhupada that and after Prabhupada’s response, the man continued, “But Swamiji, you are not doing anything so great. You are simply repeating what others have said.

But what are you *doing! ”* Prabhupada replied, “I am simply repeating the statements of the previous *acaryas.* We always stand on the statements of those who are previous *acaryas.* That is our greatness— that we do not manufacture or concoct. ” In Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s analysis of *Siksastakam,* he heads the next section, “Real humility means never concocting something new. ” Prabhupada made that point. The man was disarmed by Prabhupada’s statements, and although he didn’t say anything at the time, he later admitted that Prabhupada was *Panditah sama-darsinah,* and especially that he was genuinely humble.

These are some of the qualities which must be manifest in order to be able to chant the holy name constantly. Devotees sometimes feel that there is little hope for them in purifying their hearts. They are chanting with offenses, and see no way to stop. But the *Padma Purana* assures us that chanting Hare Krsna is recommended even for those who are committing offenses. If we go on chanting, we will gradually become purified of the offenses (on the *namabhasa* platform), as long as that is our desire. *Namabhasa* refers to the shadow of the holy name, but it is also known as the “clearing stage. ” It is the stage at which we purify ourselves of offenses.

It is important, if we wish to become purified, that we seek out the association of Vaisnavas who are chanting purely. Bhaktivinoda Thakura tells us that there are three requirements for those who actually want to reach pure chanting: We must chant in the association of devotees who relish the holy name, we have to remove ourselves from all distractions (external and internal), and we must be steady and determined in our chanting. We are dependent upon hearing the holy name in the association of advanced devotees who actually relish the name.

If we chant in good association, we will develop taste for chanting by the mercy of the advanced Vaisnavas, and thus we will strengthen our determination. Our desire to become free from offenses will become stronger, and we will hope to one day experience the full holy name. We do not base that hope simply on our own efforts, but on the attainment of mercy—mercy both from the holy name and from those who are purely chanting it. Devotees should always be eager to receive such mercy.

But mercy is also not enough: We must also follow strictly. Sometimes devotees take an extreme position, saying that they are “mercy cases” and are too fallen to even endeavor on their own. It’s almost as if they plan to continue committing the offenses, while maintaining a hope that someone will eventually lift them out of the muck. They are correct in assuming that they can only be rescued by mercy, but wrong in the sense that they choose to do nothing to attract that mercy. Krsna wants us to seek His mercy by endeavoring to please Him. We must want to become free of offenses. Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes, “Sometimes by the mercy of a very powerful Vaisnava, a person who has this mentality can actually become cleared from offenses—or sometimes even by the mercy of the Lord Himself, but it is very rare. ”

Recently a devotee asked me why we say devotees “enjoy” the holy name. But actually the *acaryas* have used the word “relish. ” To relish the holy name means to experience Krsna’s presence in His name. The Lord’s holy name is first revealed to those who are faithful, or on the platform of *Sraddha,* but as Bhaktivinoda Thakura explains, the holy name is actually revealed proportionally according to the degree of one’s faith. Those who chant on the platform of *nistha, ruci, asakti, bhava, or prema* experience not only the Lord’s name, but His form. As their faith deepens, they experience not only His name and form, but His qualities. As their faith deepens further, they also experience His pastimes. At that point, their relationship with Krsna is revealed, and on the platform of *prema,* the experience of ecstatic emotions and the ability to relish such emotions is added in. One who is actually relishing the holy name realizes the Lord and all His features manifest in the name. Certainly one who hears the holy name with such realization experiences pleasure. That is why it is important for us to seek out those who are actually experiencing the Lord in His holy name to help us in our chanting.

The scriptures make so many statements about the glories of the holy name. According to the *Bhagavatam,* devotees who remain absorbed in hearing and chanting about Krsna do not suffer material miseries—not because the miseries are not present in this world, but because they are so absorbed in spiritual relishing.

“The topmost transcendentalists or the liberated souls realize Him by advanced experience of transcendental knowledge, and therefore they take pleasure in the discussion of the transcendental qualities of the Lord’s pastimes. In the *Bhagavad-gita* ([4.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/4.9)), the Personality of Godhead declares that His appearance and activities are all *divyam,* or transcendental. The common man, who is under the spell of material energy, takes it for granted that the Lord is like one of us, and therefore he refuses to accept the transcendental nature of the Lord’s form, name, etc. The topmost transcendentalist is not interested in anything material, and his taking interest in the matter of the Lord’s activities is definite proof that the Lord is not like one of us in the material world” (SB [2.1.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/1.7), purport).

Thus that a devotee relishes the holy name means that there is no other source of pleasure in his life. Srila Rupa Gosvami lists the characteristics of those who are experiencing such pleasure in *Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu,* and adds that these characteristics cannot be imitated or hidden. I have already given this list in a previous essay, but in summation, a devotee never wastes time, but always uses his time in the Lord’s service. He remains tolerant and patient despite difficulties, detached, indifferent to sense gratification, and prideless. He is always anxious to glorify the Lord, to chant His holy name with love, and to reside in the places where the Lord had His pastimes. Above all, he maintains hope against hope that he will receive the Lord’s mercy. We should seek out the association of such Vaisnavas.

## How can we develop real humility in chanting?

**Lecture II Minsk, May 19, 2001**

A devotee wants to give all respect to others and receive none for himself. Even if he meets people who are envious of the respect he naturally receives, a devotee is tolerant of their anger and doesn’t retaliate. On the contrary, a devotee prays for his tormenter’s well-being. This attitude requires real humility. A devotee must be humble, tolerant, and not want respect for themselves. Part of his giving respect is to pray for the well-being of others.

I would like to read from Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s *Harinama Cintamani* from the section, *Real humility means never concocting something new:*

“The devotee who chants purely never rejects the devotional process he received from the spiritual master by introducing new methods, such as replacing the *maha-mantra* with some concocted and imaginary rhythm. A Vaisnava’s humility is not to be questioned. If he preaches the holy name’s glories by writing new books, for example, we can know that as long as those books are written strictly in accordance with his guru’s instructions, they are not concoctions. If a Vaisnava tries to trick people, however, making a show of humility only to gain adoration, then he is barely accepted as a Vaisnava and certainly not as a humble man. ”

A pure chanter never sees the world as meant for his own enjoyment. Rather, he sees the world and everything in it as meant for Krsna’s pleasure. Because a devotee takes a submissive position in hearing and chanting, never abandoning the *maha-mantra*, which he received from his spiritual master, he is neither eager nor interested in propagating new ideas or opinions. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura describes this as a characteristic of humility. A sincere disciple never thinks of introducing something that has not been sanctioned by his spiritual master.

This does not mean that a sincere disciple cannot expand the instructions he has received. Every Vaisnava wants to expand people’s awareness of the holy names mercy, so they may write many books or preach widely in some other way, even if their spiritual master did not. As long as a devotee is preaching in accordance with *parampara*, it still qualifies as a humble act.

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura is writing, of course, at a time when people tended to criticize or reject their spiritual masters or try to introduce something completely new with no basis in *sastra.* In his *Kalyana-kalpataru,* Bhaktivinoda Thakura wrote a section called *Upadesa,* “spiritual instructions. ” There is another section called *Upalabdhi,* which explains that one attains the perfectional stage by strict adherence to the instructions given in *Upadesa.* The final stage is called *Ucchvasa,* the outpouring of spiritual emotion.

But in *Upadesa* he gives detailed instructions about how careful we should be in different areas, including a list of things to avoid if we actually want to attain perfection. For example, he tells us to avoid greed to accumulate wealth, about the danger of the voidistic conception, and about the nature of lust. He provides eighteen instructions. One of them is called, “Faultfinders of the disciplic succession rectified. ” In this particular section, Bhaktivinoda Thakura preaches to his mind in order to preach to all his readers.

“My dear mind, I have this message to convey to you. Alas, how remorseful it is that you have sold your own individuality and freedom to choose intelligently, due to being cheated by other cheaters who dissuade you from the path of spiritual life given by your guru. In your tender, immature stage of spiritual realization, you listen to such cheaters only to be misled from the true path. You now have a new talent for finding faults with the society of devotees and the whole disciplic succession as well. Constantly trying to be aware of these faults that you find, you become careful to try to ‘purify’ yourself. ”

He places the word *purify* within quotation marks for a reason; by finding fault with the disciplic succession due to pride, we try to purify ourselves from everything we have accumulated by following the *parampara.* He continues:

“Wearing neck beads no longer, marking your forehead with *tilaka* no longer, you have now made up your own set of rules and regulations. You have rejected your initiating spiritual master because you now imagine his influence to be a burning sensation in your heart. Dear mind, you artificially agree with your former opinion, but you really do not accept or follow it. Instead, you broadcast your whimsical philosophy just to establish yourself as some sort of incarnation. Trying to find mistakes in the spiritual process and activities of the great devotees, you completely toss out your previous spiritual path as rubbish, neglecting all of your former vows and practices. You become most displeased, because you think that only sly, cunning cheaters accept *tilaka,* initiation, and neck beads. You become angry when you find some insignificant fault with the process of the great souls. Thus you reject all attachment to the path.

“Now just see here, dear brother, your present life as well as your future life are at stake. You have renounced pure gold simply to take some worthless ashes. Everyone says that you are bogus. If you do not accept the process of the bona fide devotional service, then how will you be delivered at the time of death? ”

Bhaktivinoda Thakura is specifically talking about pride here. A proud person looks for insignificant faults and then becomes angry when he finds them. His anger magnifies the fault and he thinks, “Because I see a fault, why should I accept the process? ” Then he rejects everything. What does he do after that? He concocts a new process. Why? Because a proud person thinks that his finding of a fault shows that he is more intelligent than the *parampara.* He now has to show others how wrong they have been to accept a process that contains a fault. He feels he has to show them the real path.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura equates humility with acceptance of the process given by the spiritual master. Only the prideful introduce new paths. He adds that a path cannot be manufactured. Whatever path is there should be accepted by *sadhus.* Those who are proud and want fame vigorously try to discover new paths. The desire for fame and recognition is an *anartha. Anarthas* obstruct our view of Krsna and His pure holy name, just as clouds block our ability to see the sun. If we are fortunate, we will give up our pride and respect the established path. Those who are unfortunate promulgate a new path and thus they cheat the world. *Sruti-smrti-puranadi-pancaratravidhim vina:* “Devotional service of the Lord that ignores the authorized Vedic literature like the *Upanisads, Puranas,* and *Naradapancaratra* is simply an unnecessary disturbance in society” (*Bhaktirasamrta-sindhu* [*1.2.101*](https://vedarama.com/en/books/rupa-brs1/1.2.101)*).* Such activities are born of speculation and therefore only superficially resemble unalloyed devotion. Instead, they are disrespectful to Vedic injunctions.

Disrespecting Vedic injunctions falls into the category of atheism. We cannot practice devotional service if we disrespect the scriptures, because the scriptures contain the Lord’s instructions. Therefore, we should all take Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s warning seriously and be careful of pride.

Another way people become proud is when they consider themselves the doer. A devotee is always submissive to authority, especially to guru and Krsna, and he always gives credit for his successes to his superiors. This is another meaning of Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s statement that a humble devotee hears submissively. Remaining submissive, of course, frees us from acting on pride.

When we forget our subordinate position under God, we begin to think ourselves independent of His mercy. In the *Bhagavatam,* Yamaraja’s servants, the Yamadutas, thought Yamaraja to be independent, but when they approached him after failing to carry away

Ajamila, Yamaraja told them, “Know that I, Lord Brahma, Lord Siva, and all the *mahajanas* are all subordinate to the will of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. ” *Dharmam tu saksad bhagavat-pranitam:* “Real religious principles are enacted by the Supreme Personality of Godhead. ” (SB [6.3.19](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/3.19)) None of us can manufacture a new religious system, but are all subordinate to His will.

If we remain always conscious of our subordination under God, we can become free of the influence of false ego. *Bhagavad-gita* states, “In all activities just depend upon Me and work always under My protection. ” None of us are independent. “In such devotional service, be fully conscious of Me. If you become conscious of Me, you will pass over all the obstacles of conditional life by My grace. ” (Bg. [18.57-58](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/18.57)) If we act only out of false ego, we will be lost.

A preacher who follows authority is acting humbly even if he is actively teaching others how to see. He is not concocting, but following the way of *parampara.* Thus he is neither eager to propagate new ideas nor interested in forming new opinions. This is his humility.

Another aspect of humility mentioned by Bhaktivinoda Thakura is that he never sees himself as anyone’s guru.

“One who regards himself to be the guru of any Vaisnava devotee rings humility’s death knell. The plain truth is that he who does not heed Lord Caitanya’s instructions in this Siksastaka is doomed to pursue material gain with the sole intention of appeasing the senses at the cost of his real spiritual identity. Even if he is a spiritual master, he will simply hanker for recognition in that position. The holy name can never be chanted by such an offender. No matter how sincere and faithful his disciples may be, they will never become worthy of receiving and chanting the pure holy name if they hear his offensive chanting. ”

This is similar to the point expressed earlier. No one should assume the position of guru simply for recognition. A devotee should never aspire to enjoy his position. Rather, he should think exclusively about giving pleasure to his own guru and to Krsna. Such an attitude will protect him from fall down. If he forgets his subordinate position, he will inevitably try to enjoy himself. Srila Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura calls this *taranga-rangini,* enjoying the benefits and respect offered by one’s position in devotional life. By its nature, devotional service is attractive; those who practice it become attractive. Others will naturally offer them wealth and followers. If we become attracted to those things and try to seek enjoyment in them, we will lose our *bhakti.* Instead of making further progress on the path of renunciation we will do whatever is necessary to keep our position, even if it means compromising the pure teachings. When we are attached, we can be easily manipulated. Therefore Bhaktivinoda writes that such a person is doomed to pursue material gain if he does not hear Lord Caitanya’s instructions in His *Siksastaka.*

This concludes Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s instructions on the third verse of the *Siksastaka.* He concludes his explanation by citing a few verses relevant to this third *sloka.*

Maintaining this type of determination and being compassionate to all living entities, while always chanting the holy name of Krsna, is the path of devotional service exhibited by the previous *mahajanas.*

One should strictly follow the easy path, which the previous *mahajanas* have accepted. That path bestows great auspiciousness and is devoid of all misery. By discussing the path of the previous *sadhus,* one gets determination, courage, and satisfaction.

Then, when we discuss the devotional service of Sri Rupa, Sri Sanatana, Sri Raghunatha, and Sri Haridasa Thakura, we feel such happiness that it cannot be described. In the *Caitanya-bhagavata,* Haridasa Thakura states: “Even if you cut my body into pieces, I will never give up the chanting of the holy name of the Lord. ” We need to hear such examples. Haridasa Thakura set the ideal example. We must try to follow it and always chant the holy name under all circumstances. “O King, constant chanting of the holy name of the Lord after the ways of the great authorities is the doubtless and fearless way of success for all. ” (SB [2.1.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/1.11))

The last verse of M*ukunda-mala-stotra* states: “O fool, O rascal, this constantly changing body is under attack from innumerable attachments and diseases that are given by the law of karma, and death is a certainty. What made you want to take it? Simply drink the medicine of Krsna’s name, which is the cure for all diseases. ”

## The Importance of Preaching

## Only those who serve real Vaisnavas receive the empowerment to preach effectively

**SB 1.5.36 Kiev, March 10, 1991**

**“While performing duties according to the order of Sri Krsna, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, one constantly remembers Him, His names and His qualities. ” (SB** [**1.5.36**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.36)**)**

“Vaisnava” means servant of the Vaisnava. A Vaisnava always meditates on serving guru. Everything a Vaisnava does is intended as an act of service, and because a Vaisnava is always serving the guru’s mood, preaching becomes successful. When this dynamic is intact, the Lord agrees to come. Srila Prabhupada was such a glorious Vaisnava that he fulfilled this criterion and worked tirelessly in his Guru Maharaja’s service, always meditating on his guru’s order.

This verse (SB [1.5.36](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.36)) was spoken by Narada Muni and explains how a devotee should execute his duty according to the Lord’s order. But performing our duty is not enough; we must also remember the Lord and His names and qualities.

Srila Prabhupada elaborates on this point in his purport. While writing, he considered his readership. Srila Prabhupada had amazing vision about the importance of spreading Krsna consciousness. He could understand that his books would be read by people for years to come. He knew that his books would be distributed widely even after his physical disappearance. He had to make his writing relevant for changing times and circumstances. Therefore, the instructions he delivered in his purports were written with that in mind. In fact, he stated that his books would become the law books for the next ten thousand years.

Anyone who reads Prabhupada’s purports will get both a basic understanding of how to practice *bhakti* as well as advanced realizations in devotional service. Because he was trying to teach something so important, Prabhupada was concerned that his books not be distributed independently of the pure devotee’s sanctions for personal motive or gain. We have often seen that people use God and His interests as a way to manipulate others into serving them. We have seen this frequently in many places in the world, with people proclaiming ambitious projects in God’s service, and then asking for people to volunteer money and support. Such so-called religious leaders have often been exposed.

We have seen it many times in the United States, especially in recent years among televangelists. These preachers maintain television programs, which they broadcast to a wide audience of Christians. These evangelists are often fundamentalist Christians, and, because of the financial support of their viewers, are able to both build their networks and bring in millions of dollars. They describe what they are doing with the money they receive, as “God’s work, ” however many have been exposed for living better than most of the secular wealthy in America. They maintain big bank accounts, beautiful homes in various places around the country, expensive boats and cars, and it is often for engaging in illegal activities. Only a small percentage of what they collect is used in charity work. Some of these evangelists have been convicted of crimes, and people in general no longer trust such preachers.

ISKCON will not be an exception to such corruption. Srila Prabhupada understood the potential for people to use the *Bhagavatam’s* message to manipulate people into giving them money or to attract followers. People who are unaware of the proper standard will assist them. But Prabhupada’s books contain many important instructions, and anyone who reads them carefully will not be misled by cheaters.

When we read Prabhupada’s books, we should learn to discriminate between right and wrong according to the checks and balances system that *Vaisnavism* provides: guru, *sastra,* and *sadhu.* No one should blindly accept teachings of self-proclaimed gurus who teach against the *sastra's* conclusive statements or the statements of the previous *acaryas.*

If we read Prabhupada’s books, we will see how careful Srila Prabhupada is to present his instructions according to the actual statements of scripture and to quote the previous *acaryas* in his purports. He always remained consistent with guru, *sastra,* and *sadhu.*

Similarly, in accepting others’ direction, we must be careful to make sure that the person from whom we are hearing represents guru, *sastra,* and *sadhu.* This means that the spiritual teacher must be a servant of a Vaisnava.

We began by saying that a Vaisnava is one who serves another Vaisnava. Specifically, a Vaisnava serves his or her spiritual master. To serve the spiritual master means to act according to the guru’s order. This was the example Srila Prabhupada set. Although it may have appeared that Srila Prabhupada was acting independently of his spiritual master—that he was free to go wherever he pleased and to act in any way that he wished—anyone who was ever in Prabhupada’s presence would see that he always made his plans based on his preaching mission. He always acted in ways that were connected to the preaching mission he had received from his spiritual master. Did Prabhupada always have the final say in everything? Although he had the final say with his disciples, he was constantly consulting the knowledge he had gathered from his guru and the *sastra.* He always served his guru’s order. Neither did he claim personal credit for his success; he always said that his achievements belonged to his spiritual master. At the end of his translation of the *Caitanya-caritamrta,* Srila Prabhupada writes:

“Today, Sunday, November 10, 1974—corresponding to the 10th of *Kartika,* Caitanya Era 488, the eleventh day of the dark fortnight, the Rama-ekadasi—we have now finished the English translation of Sri Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami’s Sri *Caitanyacaritamrta* in accordance with the authorized order of His Divine

Grace Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura Gosvami Maharaja, my beloved eternal spiritual master, guide and friend. Although according to material vision His Divine Grace Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura Prabhupada passed away from this material world on the last day of December, 1936, I still consider His Divine Grace to be always present with me by his *vani,* his words...

... Previously, in 1936, just before His Divine Grace passed away at Jagannatha Puri, I wrote him a letter asking what I could do to serve him. In reply, he wrote me a letter, dated 13 December 1936, ordering me, in the same way, to preach in English the cult of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu as I had heard it from him.

... I think that His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura is always seeing my activities and guiding me within my heart by his words. As it is said in *Srimad-Bhagavatam, tene brahma hrdaya adi-kavaye.* Spiritual inspiration comes from within the heart, wherein the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in His Paramatma feature, is always sitting with all His devotees and associates. It is to be admitted that whatever translation work I have done is through the inspiration of my spiritual master, because personally I am most insignificant and incompetent to do this materially impossible work. I do not think myself a very learned scholar, but I have full faith in the service of my spiritual master, His Divine Grace Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura. If there is any credit to my activities of translating, it is all due to His Divine Grace. Certainly if His Divine Grace were physically present at this time, it would have been a great occasion for jubilation, but even though he is not physically present, I am confident that he is very much pleased by this work of translation. He was very fond of seeing many books published to spread the Krsna consciousness movement. Therefore our society, the International Society for Krsna Consciousness, has been formed to execute the order of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu and His Divine Grace Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura. ”

When we see someone rendering service to a Vaisnava, we can be confident that he or she is not self-serving; he or she is not interested in becoming a so-called religious leader for personal fame or profit.

It is possible to become materially profitable by worshiping Krsna— or seeming to. People tend to respect those who practice religion. But if we are actually interested in becoming free from the material world, we must serve a Vaisnava and help him in his preaching. We may still gain respect, wealth, or followers, but attaining these things will not be our purpose.

A genuine Vaisnava wants nothing more than service to another genuine Vaisnava. Lord Caitanya said, *“Na dhanam, na janam na sundarim"* (*Antya*, [20.29](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-antya/20.29)): We do not want wealth, followers, or beautiful women. We only want to be engaged in Krsna’s devotional service birth after birth. A Vaisnava’s thinking is that simple.

Those who are materially motivated may get wealth or prestige, but they will not be given the authority to spread the holy name. Only Krsna can give the potency by which the holy name can be spread to every town and village. Followers and wealth are useful in preaching, but they are not the criteria by which we measure a successful preacher. Only those who serve real Vaisnavas receive the empowerment to preach effectively. This is the real standard against which a preacher’s success is measured—how much he distributes the Lord’s holy name.

In that regard, we can also see the effects of preaching in how much people are becoming willing to cleanse their hearts and give up their bad habits. Beyond that, we can see how much people who contact a Vaisnava are willing to develop their love for Krsna. This is an internal change. A Vaisnava’s success is measured by how much he or she is able to bring about this internal change in others. Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura states that a Vaisnava’s success is seen in terms of how many people he brings to Vaisnavism.

Therefore, it is important to understand the standards Srila Prabhupada has set for us. If we want to spread the holy name, we must teach it to Krsna’s devotees. We may not preach among those who have performed no austerities, charity, or who have no spiritual education. Other people simply will not be interested or able to understand. If we want to convert unwilling people to Vaisnavism, we will have to use Lord Caitanya’s own formula of chanting, dancing, and feasting. Therefore, Srila Prabhupada requested all of us to become authorized to spread Krsna consciousness and then to distribute Krsna’s glories. He wanted us to invite people to take *krsna-prasadam.* Srila Prabhupada spoke not only to the devotees living in the temples in his day but to all devotees in the future, including the ones who would live outside the temples and practice Krsna consciousness at home. No matter who or where we are, we should celebrate Krsna consciousness with chanting, dancing, and *prasadam,* and we should share that celebration with others.

Prabhupada knew that in the future, not all devotees would be able to live in his temples. He also realized that not only would people be forced to live far from temples, but the devotees may have difficulty in maintaining the temples that he opened. He did not restrict his followers to practice Krsna consciousness only in temples. Rather, he wanted us to spread the Krsna conscious message no matter what our living situation.

However, Srila Prabhupada says that the condition under which we can successfully execute our spiritual activities is that we perform them under the guidance of a pure devotee who is completely free of all mundane desire, fruitive activity, and dry speculation about the Lord’s nature. Prabhupada didn’t say that just anyone could spread Krsna consciousness; we have to become servants of the Vaisnava if we wish to have the necessary qualification. If we gain this qualification, the Supreme Lord Himself will agree to come. Krsna told Narada:

*naham tisthami vaikunthe yoginam hrdayesu va*  
*tatra tisthami narada yatra gayanti mad-bhaktah*

**“O Narada, I am not found in the Vaikuntha planets, nor am I found in the hearts of** ***yogis.*** **But I personally go to that place where My devotees are discussing My glories. ”** ***(Padma Purana)***

Similarly, Yudhisthira Maharaja told Vidura:

“My Lord, devotees like your good self are verily holy places personified. Because you carry the Personality of Godhead within your heart, you turn all places into places of pilgrimage. ” (SB [1.13.10](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/13.10)) Wherever a pure devotee goes, it becomes a place of pilgrimage because the devotee carries Krsna within him. Carrying Krsna within him implies that the devotee is also glorifying Krsna. Therefore, by Krsna’s own words, we know that Krsna is present.

We can carry Krsna with us by constantly chanting His holy name. Krsna is nondifferent from His name. We must develop the qualities to chant it honestly and constantly. To do so, we should read Prabhupada’s books. When we read and speak from his books, we will receive realization. That realization will be fresh, because the pure devotee’s words are absolute truth, and when we hear them, our heart becomes cleansed. The *Bhagavatam* assures us ([1.2.18](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.18)):

*nasta-prayesv abhadresu nityam bhagavata-sevaya*  
*bhagavaty uttama-sloke bhaktir bhavati naisthiki*

**“By regularly hearing the** ***Bhagavatam*** **and rendering service unto the pure devotee, all that is troublesome to the heart is practically destroyed, and loving service unto the glorious Lord, who is praised with transcendental songs, is established as an irrevocable fact. ”**

We must hear and we must serve. If we continue this process, we are guaranteed success. We will become purified and we will have the power to purify others by our connection with the disciplic succession.

Srila Prabhupada gave the example of an electric wire. If it is connected to the source of power, the electricity will travel through the wire. If not, the wire will not generate its own power. One who accepts this process and serves a genuine Vaisnava can be assured of returning back home, back to Godhead.

Practicing devotees are often enthusiastic to engage in service in the beginning, but after some time, they find obstacles in their service. They may even be serving according to their own inspiration, which they have presented to the spiritual master and had sanctioned, but then lost their enthusiasm for their activities. The important thing to remember is that we are engaging in service for Krsna’s pleasure. If we lose our attraction for a particular service, we must admit that our motivation for performing that service was not completely pure. We were not acting solely for Krsna’s pleasure but rather because we liked to perform the particular activity. There was still an element of, “I am doing this because I like it” involved. Krsna is kind enough to remove that taste by creating obstacles on our path. He wants us to perform pure devotional service. If we no longer like doing something according to our own taste, that shouldn’t matter, because we are engaged in service for Him. This is a test that all devotees face at some time or other. It is the way we free ourselves from mixed or confused desires in our service. This is how Krsna frees us from the desire to enjoy matter.

When we face the impediment and turn to Krsna, we will begin to execute a purer form of devotional service. A dutiful devotee will feel responsible to carry out the tasks he has been given; therefore there will be no question of not doing his duty. If he were to think that the particular duty he has taken on was his own idea in the first place and not actually assigned to him (although it was sanctioned by the spiritual master), and he decided to give it up because it is difficult, he will simply become a disturbance to society. Such behavior will also weaken his resolve. When he begins a new duty and then again meets obstacles, he will again want to change. We purify our desires by acting on the platform of duty. Duty must be performed. Therefore in the eighteenth chapter of *Bhagavad-gita,* Lord Krsna states that those who perform their duties simply because they should be done are situated in renunciation in the mode of goodness. Those who give up their duties because they are troublesome are practicing renunciation in the mode of passion.

If we wish to continue to execute our duties with determination, we need knowledge and a proper philosophical understanding. It may be that our attraction to the particular services was spontaneous, but once having received the services as duty, we should stick to them. Lord Krsna has spoken so many instructions on this point. We have a right to perform our prescribed duty but are not entitled to the fruits of action. Neither should we ever consider ourselves the cause of the results of our activities, and we should never become attached to *not* performing our duty. Krsna’s instructions can carry us through any test if we follow them.

## How can we increase the preaching?

**SB** [**4.11.8-10**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/11.8) **Kaunas, September 20, 1995**

To increase the preaching we have to increase our desire to preach. It is very difficult to preach when we don’t have a strong desire to do so. When we increase our own desire to preach, we will also increase the desire of others; because by preaching, we will both give them the message that others need to hear and increase their impetus to spread it. In general we see that it is not usually compassion that motivates devotees to go out and preach. Sometimes we are motivated by the desire for recognition more than by our compassion. Perhaps we are motivated by the desire for profit. There may be so many impure motivations. If we want to preach effectively, we will have to strengthen and purify our desire to share Krsna consciousness with others.

What inspires us to preach is when we see the preaching is becoming effective. It’s not that we want to be motivated only if we get a good result—effective preaching isn’t measured only by the number of books we distributed, for example—but part of effective preaching is the increase in our own Krsna consciousness. Recently someone asked me, “If book distribution is the only way to increase love of God, then how can we increase book distribution? ” I tried to explain that yes, book distribution certainly can help increase love of God, but Srila Prabhupada was once asked, “What pleases you most? ” As he asked the question, the devotee held up a book, expecting Prabhupada to say, “When you distribute my books. ” The devotee asking the question was a book distributor, and he wanted Prabhupada to recognize his service and assure him that it was pleasing. Instead, Prabhupada said, “What pleases me most is that you love Krsna. ” Therefore, if we want to be effective in spreading love of God, just think how much more effective we’ll be if we distribute Prabhupada’s books with love! This is what Prabhupada wanted.

Prabhupada wanted us to love Krsna. If we love Krsna, everything we do will be imbued with that love. When our desire to preach is motivated by love for Krsna, our preaching will be effective because we will be pure instruments in the Lord’s hands. We can see in the example of Srila Prabhupada how powerful an impact a pure Vaisnava can have. Just see how Prabhupada changed the lives of so many conditioned souls. Not only Srila Prabhupada, but Srila Prabhupada’s books impact people. Many of those who receive Prabhupada’s books have come forward to find out how to alter their lifestyles, and how to feel the love for Krsna that Prabhupada exemplified in his own life. The books themselves are filled with details of devotees loving Krsna and returning to the spiritual world.

Srila Prabhupada expected all of his followers to show by their example how to practice what he taught in his books. If we read Srila Prabhupada’s books, we will realize the high standard of behavior he set for us. If we want to distribute his books with enthusiasm, with the desire to change the world’s course, we will have to be inspired in our personal Krsna consciousness too. We also have to ask ourselves, “What will happen when someone reads one of Prabhupada’s books? What is he or she going to see? ” Are we reflecting in our ISKCON communities the spiritual society that Prabhupada discusses in his books? Are we living the kind of spiritual love that Prabhupada teaches in his books? Will they find us living the compassionate spirit he taught us to have? Are we strictly following the rules and regulations, controlling our senses, and chanting our rounds? Having found some or all of these factors is what drew us into the Krsna consciousness movement. Now it’s our turn to exemplify them.

Thus, it is not only distributing Prabhupada’s books that will spread love of Godhead, but it is by distributing them and preaching with love. Prabhupada wanted us to kick out all other motivations. He wanted us to be free from the desire for profit, fame, and followers. He wanted us to make our hearts one with the spiritual master’s order. He wanted us to manifest symptoms of love. This behavior will change the course of the world. It will have a powerful effect on the nondevotees because they will taste our *bhakti.* Whenever someone tastes love, he wants to reciprocate with it. Every living entity has a heart, and in that heart is the dormant desire to love Krsna. When we preach selflessly, with love, our preaching will reach that dormant center in each person’s heart and awaken it.

A devotee is prepared to give up selfishness. He is prepared to accept any inconvenience, even if it means his own suffering. He is prepared to accept that the selfless giving of Krsna consciousness is the highest form of worshiping the Lord. When we preach in this way, kicking out everything else, and when others experience our real love, they will want to reciprocate with Krsna. Anyone who becomes an object of such love will want to do something to reciprocate.

What can they do? How can they serve? Those questions are awakened. But how do we serve a *sadhu*? We must know the *sadhus* heart. We must first know that Krsna is within the heart of a *sadhu* and the *sadhu* is experiencing Him. Therefore, the *sadhu* is one who wants to give Krsna to others. A *sadhu* is one who wants to reveal the confidential topics about the Lord. Although confidential topics are difficult to understand, a *sadhu* wants to share details of Krsna’s nature with others.

Krsna states in *Bhagavad-gita, ya idam paramam guhyam, madbhaktesv abhidhasyati, bhaktim mayi param krtva, mam evaisyaty asamsayah:* “For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, pure devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come back to Me. ” (Bg. [18.68](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/18.68)) A *sadhu* doesn’t have to make any other endeavor to attain devotional service. He simply concentrates on revealing the secret of Krsna consciousness to others and knows that he is guaranteed to develop devotion for Krsna.

Preaching is a *sadhus* heart. If we want to please a *sadhu,* we can practice what Srila Prabhupada asked of us—that we come to love Krsna. Loving Krsna means serving Him selflessly. We don’t please a *sadhu* simply by bringing him his shoes, but rather by helping him in his preaching mission. This is how we reciprocate with a *sadhu*— especially that *sadhu* who has awakened love for Krsna in our own hearts. The *sastras* say that even a moment’s association with a *sadhu* can awaken love of God in us. How can we ever repay such a gift? We were heading for hell, planning millions of more births in the material world. The *sadhu* changed our course and awakened the small desire in our hearts to serve and love Krsna. We can never repay such a debt. Even if we were to take birth a million times, the pure devotee would still have awakened devotional service to Krsna in our hearts. It’s an impossible boon to repay. Therefore a devotee is prepared to accept birth after birth in the material world to try to repay that debt. Devotional service gives meaning to life.

What can we do to please a *sadhu?* How can we repay him? We should become crazy trying to answer that question. There is nothing we can possibly do.

Still, we have to try. We must give our lives for him, even though we cannot fulfill the commitment. All he wants of us is that we love Krsna, and he instructs us that the best way to do that is to give Krsna to others. All he wants is that what has been awakened in our heart should be given to others. He wants us to preach Krsna consciousness.

We need to learn to preach with this realization, with this desire in our hearts. Let us give every breath of this life to this mission. Let us pray to our spiritual master and to Krsna to please give us the opportunity to try to serve. Let us preach as our spiritual master has preached. We will never compare as preachers to our spiritual master, but let us beg Krsna for the strength and desire to preach as our spiritual master has preached. His preaching saved us. We should go out and save others. With this mentality, we will increase the preaching of the Krsna consciousness movement.

## How can we become effective preachers?

**SB 4.23.12 Kishinev, June 1996**

**“When he became completely free from the conception of bodily life, Maharaja Prthu realized Lord Krsna sitting in everyone’s heart as the Paramatma. Being thus able to get all instructions from Him, he gave up all other practices of yoga and** ***jnana.*** **He was not even interested in the perfection of the yoga and** ***jnana*** **systems, for he thoroughly realized that devotional service to Krsna is the ultimate goal of life and that unless the** ***yogis*** **and** ***jnanis*** **become attracted to** ***krsna-katha*** **\[narrations about Krsna\], their illusions concerning existence can never be dispelled. ” (SB** [**4.23.12**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/23.12)**)**

Here it is described that Prthu Maharaja became free of the bodily concept of life and realized the Lord within everyone’s heart. Thus, he was thoroughly realized in the practice of devotional service. He understood that Krsna is the supreme goal. Unless *yogis* and *jnanis* become attracted to *krsna-katha,* their illusion cannot be destroyed. As Srila Prabhupada explains in the purport, “When one understands that the ultimate goal of life is to approach Krsna, he realizes Krsna within everyone’s heart and therefore he helps everyone who is interested in Krsna consciousness. ” This is the result of coming in contact with Krsna.

This is also the secret of how we can exist in the material world yet remain fearless in any condition. If someone wonders how Krsna’s devotees cannot be interested in their own liberation, the answer lies herein: The devotees are attracted to *krsna-katha* and are satisfied to be absorbed in thoughts of Krsna. They are so satisfied to hear and chant about Krsna that it doesn’t matter to them where they are. That is perfection.

Srila Prabhupada writes, “The perfection of life depends on one’s inclination to hear about Krsna. ” When Srila Prabhupada speaks about perfection, he is not speaking about the perfection of mystic or *jnana-yoga.* When Prabhupada titled his book, *The Perfection of Yoga*, he was referring to *bhakti-yoga.* Whenever Srila Prabhupada uses the word *perfection*, we can know that he is speaking about perfection in terms of the ultimate goal. That goal—the topmost platform of existence—cannot be surpassed because there is nothing beyond that perfectional stage. When Srila Prabhupada writes, “The perfection of life depends on one’s inclination to hear about Krsna, ” he is stating specifically that if we do not have an inclination to hear about Krsna, we cannot achieve perfection.

While executing all our duties, therefore, we must also evoke an attraction for hearing and chanting. Lord Krsna explains in *Bhagavad-gita,* “The thoughts of My pure devotees dwell in Me, their lives are fully devoted to My service, and they derive great satisfaction and bliss from always enlightening one another and conversing about Me. ” We can evaluate our progress in devotional service by how much we see this interest in ourselves.

If we wish to achieve the perfection of yoga, we must develop an attraction for hearing *krsna-katha. Krsna-katha* means hearing the Supreme Personality of Godhead’s instructions and about His pastimes and qualities, and discussing service rendered for the

Lord’s pleasure. *Krsna-katha* also means discussing the words Krsna Himself has spoken or words spoken by His pure devotees. At the beginning of the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* it is described that Srila Sukadeva Gosvami spoke the essence of the *Bhagavatam* and that the *Bhagavatam* was therefore considered the ripened fruit of Vedic literature. The *Bhagavatam* emanated from the lips of Sukadeva Gosvami, and therefore the nectarean juice became even more sweet and relishable for liberated souls.

Liberated souls derive pleasure from *krsna-katha.* Discussing topics about Krsna is not something performed only in the conditioned state. If someone were to wonder what happens when one achieves liberation, here is the answer: A liberated soul is interested in devotional love of God and engages in *krsna-katha.*

Few people understand liberation. Most people are interested only in becoming free from suffering, so their conception of liberation is limited to that state. Sometimes people try to become free from suffering by practicing austerities. Others try to gain liberation by working for fruitive results. Such persons think that their material opulence will save them; but liberation is not even the perfection of karma or *jnana.* Whatever states one achieves through karma and *jnana* are temporary. Real release can only be achieved by realizing one’s spiritual identity.

We can understand that those who are simply seeking liberation have not developed their attraction to hearing and chanting about Krsna.
