Because their concept of liberation is not fully satisfying to the self, they are unable to remain in a liberated position. A devotee knows that real liberation is achieved by constant hearing and chanting. This is why devotees don’t care about liberation— they are simply interested in the wonders of *krsna-katha.*

Prahlada Maharaja describes in his prayer to Lord Nrsimhadeva:

**“O best of the great personalities, I am not at all afraid of material existence, for wherever I stay I am fully absorbed in thoughts of Your glories and activities. My concern is only for the fools and rascals who are making elaborate plans for material happiness and maintaining their families, societies and countries. I am simply concerned with love for them. My dear Lord Nrsimhadeva, I see that there are many saintly persons indeed, but they are interested only in their own deliverance. Not caring for the big cities and towns, they go to the Himalayas or the forest to meditate with vows of silence** ***\[maunavrata\].*** **They are not interested in delivering others. As for me, however, I do not wish to be liberated alone, leaving aside all these poor fools and rascals. I know that without Krsna consciousness, without taking shelter of Your lotus feet, one cannot be happy. Therefore I wish to bring them back to shelter at Your lotus feet. ” (SB** [**7.9.43**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/9.43)**)**

Prahlada Maharaja can pray like this because he is satisfied in his own devotional service to the Lord. Liberation is meaningless to him. It doesn’t matter to him that he may have to stay in the material world. He is *already* liberated from material life. Liberated activity means to glorify Krsna. Prahlada Maharaja exemplified liberated behavior in his life. No matter what the situation to which Prahlada Maharaja was subjected, he had only one business: To glorify Krsna. Nothing could impede the flow of his devotion. His father made many attempts to stop that flow, but Prahlada Maharaja simply thought of how to please the Lord. He wasn’t interested in his own happiness.

In the seventh canto, Lord Nrsimhadeva tells Prahlada, “My dear Prahlada, may you live a long time. One cannot appreciate or understand Me without pleasing Me, but one who has seen or pleased Me has nothing more for which to lament for his own satisfaction. ” In the purport to this verse, Srila Prabhupada explains that we cannot be happy under any circumstance unless we please Krsna. If we know what pleases the Lord and how to execute it, then we don’t have to worry about our personal satisfaction. We don’t have to ask for liberation or any other perfection, because the perfectional stage of life has already been achieved by engaging in activities that satisfy Krsna.

The Lord takes care of His devotees who are always absorbed in thoughts of Him and who glorify Him. Those devotees exist in this world by His grace. Practically speaking, *only* devotees can survive the material world. No one else can survive, because only a devotee can be peaceful and find shelter at Krsna’s lotus feet. All others, although existing, do not have a final goal in life, and live only for the fulfillment of a goal that cannot give them fulfillment. Survival must mean more than existing without meaning. Real survival means to exist for Krsna’s service. Such devotees will never die. They preach here and will continue to serve Krsna as He wishes upon giving up their mortal bodies.

As Srila Prabhupada explains, “As long as one is too much absorbed in the bodily conception of life, then he becomes interested in different processes of self-realization. However, when one understands that the ultimate goal of life is to approach Krsna, he realizes Krsna within everyone’s heart and therefore he helps everyone who is interested in Krsna consciousness. ” Those who are satisfied in Krsna consciousness preach.

Sometimes devotees wonder, “How can we preach if we are not actually experiencing satisfaction in Krsna consciousness? ” Sometimes they complain that they do not have the conviction to preach, since they are not experiencing Krsna consciousness themselves. How can we preach that Krsna consciousness is a joyful process when we are suffering? Those who express this concern often find their own ways to seek liberation. The desire for salvation is a subtle attachment left over from *jnana or* karma mixtures. If we find ourselves trying to avoid misery, it means we are in the bodily concept of life. It means we have not learned to find shelter in Krsna consciousness.

If we want to be effective preachers, we must learn to experience shelter in Krsna consciousness. Those who find that shelter can convince others of the glories of devotional service. Others become inspired by their words and example and hope that they too can one day achieve Krsna consciousness.

If we want to experience something we are not currently tasting in spiritual life, it is essential that we associate with real devotees. This is how we cultivate faith. Srila Prabhupada explains that faith is attained in the association of devotees. When we see that others are progressing in Krsna consciousness and becoming advanced, not caring for their personal needs but depending on Krsna, and preaching to others in a selfless way, we can become inspired to follow them. We realize that it is not by seeking our own spiritual satisfaction that we advance but by willingly giving whatever we have to others.

Our business in this world is not to look for our own satisfaction but to seek to please Krsna. Krsna will satisfy us Himself. Those who seek only their own satisfaction cannot unlock the mystery of real spiritual happiness. Therefore, they ask, “How can I preach? I am dissatisfied. ” They forget what it is to try to please Krsna.

Krsna clearly explains that anyone who tells others about Him automatically achieves devotional service. It is guaranteed. When we realize that Krsna is the ultimate goal, then we will see Krsna within everyone’s heart. Then we will want to help everyone become Krsna conscious. We will want to give Krsna to others, even though we may not have fully realized Him ourselves, simply because we know this activity is pleasing to Krsna.

Liberated souls always think about Krsna and are therefore not interested in accumulating material wealth or position. They are experiencing a superior society. What is that superior society? The one that Srila Prabhupada established: The International Society for Krsna Consciousness. This society is for spiritual exchanges. Although any society needs money to survive, economics is not the basis of our association. Still, we think that if we want to expand a spiritual society, we need material assets. However, if we wish to spread Sri Caitanya’s *sankirtana* movement in this world, we will have to gather those assets and use them wholly in the Lord’s service. This is how we expand the preaching: We use everything to ensure that Krsna’s glories are being spread.

*Krsna-katha* should be vibrating from the lips of all the devotees. We should be discussing Krsna among ourselves. This is Vaisnava *sanga*; this is the society about which we read in the *Caitanyacaritamrta.* Along with discussions among themselves, Vaisnavas expand their family by giving everyone the opportunity to contact Krsna.

Anyone who engages in this society loses his taste for dry, speculative philosophy and fruitive gain. Krsna consciousness provides a higher taste. Therefore, devotees who want to preach with confidence have to take shelter of the process we have been given to gain confidence. If we do not take shelter of hearing and chanting, then we will not be Krsna conscious and it will be very difficult to preach.

A spiritual master becomes pleased when he sees his disciples advancing in Krsna consciousness by displaying Krsna conscious symptoms. This is why the spiritual master tries to bring others to the spiritual platform. He doesn’t like to see pride, envy, or greed in his followers. Rather, he likes to see them satisfied in their lives and willing to give spiritual understanding to others. He knows that when devotees preach, they lose their bad qualities. Srila Prabhupada once told us that Lord Caitanya doesn’t like to see pride in His devotees. He used to cut down pride wherever He saw it. He didn’t like pride because He knew that the only way a devotee can be an effective preacher is to think of himself as lower than the straw in the street. We can glorify Krsna constantly if we are devoid of false prestige and ready to offer all respect to others. The spiritual master is anxious to see his disciples advance to this platform.

To accomplish this, he gives his disciples two orders: Among themselves, the disciples should hear and chant about Krsna, and go out and preach Krsna’s message. Why do we go out on the spiritual master’s order? Because by following the spiritual master’s orders, we please Krsna. When we know how to please Krsna, we no longer have to worry about our own satisfaction and we are willing to make personal sacrifices. We will give our mouths for speaking about Krsna, our ears for hearing His glories, our hands for cleaning His temple, and our legs to carry us to the temple or out to preach. The Lord is pleased by such sacrifices and thus offers such devotees His pure devotional service.

Srila Prabhupada emphasizes that those who realize Krsna as the goal of life will help others to become Krsna conscious. This is the perfection of life. If we want to achieve life’s topmost perfection, we will not look for our own satisfaction but to Krsna’s pleasure. The *Bhagavatam* states:

*sa vai pumsam paro dharmo yato bhaktir*  
*adhoksaje ahaituky apratihata yayatma suprasidati*

**“The supreme occupation** ***(dharma)*** **for all humanity is that by which men can attain loving devotional service unto the transcendent Lord. Such devotional service must be unmotivated and uninterrupted to completely satisfy the self. ” (SB** [**1.2.6**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.6)**)**

*krsna-bhakta—niskama, ata eva ’santa’*  
*bhukti-mukti-siddhi-kami, sakali ’asanta’*

**“Because a devotee of Lord Krsna is desireless, he is peaceful. Fruitive workers desire material enjoyment,** ***jnanis*** **desire liberation, and** ***yogis*** **desire material opulence. Therefore, they are all lusty and cannot be peaceful. ” (Cc.** ***Madhya*** [**19.149**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/19.149)**)**

Srila Prabhupada never thought of his own liberation. Rather, he tried to serve his spiritual master’s mission. “By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Krsna. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. ” *(Gurvastaka* 8) Prabhupada gave every breath to execute Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura’s order. Therefore, he was the perfect example of a perfect disciple. He never thought of retirement. He preached up to his last breath. He taught us the perfection of life by his example. If we want to please the spiritual master, look to Prabhupada’s example.

Srila Prabhupada was so successful in his preaching because he thought only of his spiritual master’s order. Then, automatically, he was able to show compassion for the fallen souls. Such devotees continue to glorify the Lord wherever they are. Let us follow his example. That is the meaning of becoming a Prabhupadanuga.

## Why do we preach?

The Lord inspires the living entity to enjoy the material world according to his own desire. Although nothing can happen without the Lord’s sanction, the living entity still has responsibility. If we want to remember Krsna, the Lord must sanction our desire. If we want to forget Krsna, Krsna will also have to sanction that desire. The Lord will inspire us according to our desire. For those who want to forget Krsna, the Lord inspires them to enjoy the material world, by which they can suffer the threefold miseries.

Devotees preach because they know that although Krsna is inspiring the living entity in this way, the Lord does not actually want material enjoyment for the living entity. The Lord would prefer the living entity to turn to Him. This is why Krsna accompanies each living entity within the heart. Krsna remains as the witness, and He never abandons His post. He waits patiently for the living entity to turn to Him.

What does it take to turn a living entity toward Krsna? It requires divine grace. Divine grace is distributed by Krsna’s devotees. To distribute this divine grace, the devotees preach. In this way, they try to help others turn to Krsna in the heart, knowing that as soon as they make that turn, Krsna will inspire them with remembrance of Himself. If we can awaken the conditioned souls from their spiritual sleep so that they turn to Krsna, then Krsna will help them come to Him. How will He help them? He will bring them to the lotus feet of guru.

## We must see Krsna in every living entity’s heart

**SB** [**4.4.12**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/4.12) **Novgorod, September 1995**

Even one moment’s association with a Vaisnava can start us on the path back to Godhead. A Vaisnava is one who sees the good in others, who sees Krsna in every living entity’s heart, and who always prays to Krsna to be allowed to fan the spark of Krsna consciousness. If we want to preach Krsna consciousness, we must be able to see Krsna in others’ hearts. If we see Krsna, we will also see the good. If we can’t see the good in others, how will we preach? We *must* be able to see it. If we see only faults, we will not be able to elevate anyone. Elevation in Krsna consciousness does not mean pointing out faults. When we can finally see the good in them, then we will be capable of pointing out their faults. That will become our duty.

## A preacher accepts austerities

**Bg. 6.32 Moscow, January 8, 1997**

**“He is a perfect** ***yogi*** **who, by comparison to his own self, sees the true equality of all beings, in both their happiness and their distress, O Arjuna! ” (Bg.** [**6.32**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.32)**)**

Here, Krsna describes the characteristics of a perfect *yogi.* Those who are perfect in yoga practice are concerned for the welfare of others. The purpose of yoga is not necessarily salvation or even to elevate oneself to some perfect state. Srila Prabhupada specifically explains that the far superior *yogi* is one who is interested in others’ welfare. For a *bhakti-yogi,* this specifically means he is willing to preach Krsna consciousness.

What is the foundation of a *yogis* desire to preach? This verse states, “By comparison to his own self, \[he\] sees the true equality of all beings, in both their happiness and distress. ” He has tasted happiness and distress, and he understands that all living entities are similarly tasting these dualities. The *yogi* doesn’t want them to suffer.

Those who have Krsna conscious realization find that their realization extends across a broad spectrum. They don’t only realize their own position as constitutionally servants of Krsna who have been lost in the material world but that this is the situation with all beings. A *yogi* sees his own relationship with Krsna and realizes that all living beings have a relationship with Krsna.

Of course, each living entity’s relationship with God may be different in terms of their *svarupa-siddhi,* and we know that varieties of relationships with the Lord exist. The spiritual world has unlimited variety; the variety we see in this world is simply a reflection of some of that variety. Aside from these differences between the living entities, we are all basically the same—constitutionally servants of Krsna who are bound in the material world by the three modes of nature.

For the most part, people do not know anything about their relationship with God. This is because it is not possible to realize this relationship without first becoming the Lord’s obedient servant. Therefore, a preacher knows that the first step in helping someone reconnect with the Lord is to help them become submissive to Krsna’s will. A preacher also knows that such submission will make the living entity happy. Seeing a person submit to the Lord also makes the devotee happy. He knows that all living entities are parts and parcels of the Lord and that real happiness cannot be achieved independent of Krsna.

There are different grades of happiness. There is happiness derived from sense gratification, happiness derived from liberation, and happiness derived from pure devotional service. The pure devotee who is experiencing the pleasures of pure devotion knows that this is the kind of happiness ultimately sought by every living being. He also knows that the living beings don’t know what they are looking for. He hopes to show them.

The living entities are convinced that happiness is found in material sense gratification. This is because they do not have any knowledge or experience of the spiritual realm. A devotee knows by realization that seeking happiness in matter brings only misery. In that sense, devotees can easily see into the future. A devotee who sees anyone tasting sense pleasure in this world knows that that person will eventually be due misery. He also knows that anyone who finds happiness in giving Krsna pleasure will eventually attain Krsna. There is no way to avoid these two destinies.

I remember once I was in the United States talking to someone who told me that the weather in a certain area of the United States was always perfect. That place was like paradise; everyone who lived there could enjoy. There were no heavy snows and no cold. Of course, there are other types of problems that arise in warm places. But this person was convinced that if he went to this paradise, he would be happy. In fact, many people go to such places thinking the same thing. Such persons may not have to shovel snow, but they have to fight traffic in the heat. So many people move to such places where the traffic is terrible. Instead of snow they may have earthquakes. Those are much worse.

There are unlimited examples of the dualities that make up happiness and distress in this world. Whatever idea we may have of finding happiness separate from Krsna is doomed. We cannot find that happiness because it doesn’t exist. We may taste some pleasure for some time, but we will have to pay the price for our attempts to gratify ourselves.

There is no happiness anywhere in this world that is not accompanied by suffering. Krsna explains this in *Bhagavad-gita,* and the devotee knows that when Krsna says something, it is absolutely true. Krsna says:

*a-brahma-bhuvanal lokah punar avartino ’rjuna*  
*mam upetya tu kaunteya punar janma na vidyate*

**“From the highest planet in the material world down to the lowest, all are places of misery wherein repeated birth and death take place. But one who attains to My abode, O son of Kunti, never takes birth again. ” (Bg.** [**8.16**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/8.16)**)**

There is no way to avoid material miseries for those who try to enjoy their senses. But still people try, and the more they try, the more miseries they create for themselves.

Spiritual happiness is superior to material happiness. Spiritual happiness is not based on sense gratification, and is not involved with material misery. Srila Prabhupada explains in his purport to *Bhagavad-gita* [6.32](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.32):

“The cause of the distress of a living entity is forgetfulness of his relationship with God. And the cause of happiness is knowing Krsna to be the supreme enjoyer of all the activities of the human being, the proprietor of all lands and planets, and the sincerest friend of all living entities. ”

This is the bottom line: The cause of happiness is knowing Krsna, and the cause of distress is forgetting Him. A devotee who has found the cause of happiness will want to share his enlightenment with others. He is willing to pay that price in order to increase his happiness—by increasing the happiness of the Lord. If we compare the price tag of Krsna consciousness to the price tag of flickering sense gratification, we will realize that our austerity is insignificant. By our austerities we are able to break the cycle of birth and death and return to the spiritual world within one lifetime. The living entities pay for their sense gratification with distress, but aside from that, they are unable to break their bondage in this world. They are doomed to millions and millions of births and deaths in this world with no way whatsoever to break the cycle. What do they receive for this high price? Some temporary, flickering pleasure. He does not even taste real happiness, only illusion. But a devotee is willing to pay the price of simple living and sharing his knowledge, and receives so much in return.

Thus, Lord Rsabhadeva explains that we should practice austerity and penance if we wish to achieve the divine position of devotional service. By such activities, our heart will be purified and we will learn how to taste eternal, transcendental happiness. Our austerity is to learn how to satisfy the Lord and then to do it. A devotee centers his life on pursuit of this knowledge. We call it an austerity because it does not give us direct sense gratification. Usually we find that our sense desires are not fulfilled when we are practicing devotional service. But we know that when the Lord is satisfied, then we will be satisfied.

The *yogi* described in this verse also understands this, and his life is one of consistent pleasure. Still, he is not motivated by his own pleasure but by the happiness he gives Krsna when he helps turn others toward God. A Vaisnava who is perfect in his realization is therefore always anxious to see everyone accept austerity for Krsna. Whatever austerity they can accept to ensure Krsna’s pleasure is for their own benefit. Even a little austerity performed for Krsna’s pleasure far surpasses the huge sacrifices people make for sense gratification. Krsna explains: “In this endeavor there is no loss or diminution, and a little advancement on this path saves one from the most dangerous type of fear. ” (Bg. [2.40](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/2.40))

Any little endeavor for Krsna’s service is to the living entity’s eternal credit. A Vaisnava who knows this always tries to encourage others to serve.

If a preacher sees that others are not inclined to austerity, he may try to attract them to Krsna consciousness with chanting or *prasadam.* He knows that they have to start somewhere, and if they want to become genuinely happy, they will have to keep going. What gives a preacher the conviction to push others toward austerity? Prabhupada has said about himself that when he came to the West, he taught people to follow the regulative principles. He didn’t compromise. He didn’t worry that people wouldn’t accept what he said. When you’re selling diamonds, not everyone will be prepared to pay the price. Diamonds are expensive; they are not cheap commodities. If we want something cheap, we should expect to pay little for it. We will not, however, end up with a genuine diamond. A preacher should know that Krsna consciousness is the most valuable gift. People should expect to pay a higher price for it.

A Vaisnava does not want to see people returning to sense gratification. Still, he is the one who will actually satisfy everyone’s whimsical desires, because by giving them Krsna, they will attain everything in life.

When a preacher is successful, he brings a conditioned soul to Krsna consciousness. That person may then go on to practice for years, offering their time and energy to the Supreme Lord. Sometimes such persons say, “I have accepted inconveniences to please Krsna. I have been accepting those inconveniences for years. Now it’s time to accept inconveniences for myself, because I notice that I still have not attained Krsna. If I’m not going to get Krsna out of this, why lose out on everything else too? ”

But it’s also true that Krsna is not easily attained. We have to be prepared to pay the highest price for Him. We have to continue performing our austerities for the Lord’s satisfaction and know that we cannot gratify our senses separately from Krsna. If a devotee is going to gratify his senses, he should do so in an authorized way.

Such discipline is a stage in spiritual advancement. When we become disciplined, we can become disciples. When a disciple accepts initiation from the spiritual master, he makes a firm vow to give up unauthorized sense gratification. He must make that much of a commitment in order to progress in spiritual life. If we have made a firm vow, then even if we experience difficulty or unsteadiness, as we inevitably will, we will stick to the vow. Krsna consciousness does not deny the devotee the right to gratify his senses; it orders that we do it only in an authorized way. If we don’t accept such spiritual discipline, how can we expect to advance in Krsna consciousness to the point where we attain Krsna? The material world is a dangerous place. If we don’t come to Krsna consciousness and accept austerities on Krsna’s behalf, then we will not be able to negotiate this world safely.

Actually, austerities performed on the Lord’s behalf deliver nectar. Narada Muni states that one who has actually experienced the taste of devotional service will not forget that ecstasy ever again. He may foolishly go out and try to again enjoy separately from Krsna, collecting the seeds of karma in this world, but he will always be haunted by the taste of surrender to Krsna.

The spiritual master directs the disciple how to satisfy his senses in an authorized way. If the disciple doesn’t want to follow those instructions, what can the spiritual master do? It reminds me of the story of the husband and his paramour. A man was going out at night to see his lover. His wife found out where he was going and confronted him. She asked, “I know where you’re going. You are interested in another woman. What is it that this woman has that I don’t? I am your wife. I want to give you everything. What could she possibly give you that I haven’t been able to supply? ”

The husband said, “It’s the way she cooks for me. ” The wife then thought, “The next time my husband comes home, I'm going to cook a feast for him such as he has never tasted anywhere in this world. ” And she did. When her husband came home, she served him everything for his pleasure. The husband seemed to enjoy the meal, and the wife thought his wandering was cured.

But the next night he did not come home. Again the wife asked her husband, “What is it? You said it was the way she cooks for you. ” The husband responded, “Well, it’s not *only* the way she cooks. It’s also the way she dresses. ” The wife thought, “Really! ” and she made every effort to adorn herself with the most costly dress, ornaments, and cosmetics. She thought, “When my husband comes home and sees me, he will never go back to this woman. ”

The husband came home and saw his wife in her regalia. The wife asked, “Does she dress like this? ” The husband had to admit that his wife surpassed his paramour in beauty.

But the next night the husband didn’t come home. Again the wife asked, “What is it that she does better than me? If it’s not cooking or the way she dresses, what is it? ” The husband said, “I have to admit one more thing. It’s the way she kicks and bites me. ”

“The way that she kicks you and bites you? You want to be kicked and bitten? I *love* you. I can’t kick and bite you. If you want to be kicked and bitten, you have to go to her. What can I do? I can’t kick and bite you! ”

This story is to help us understand that Krsna will try to do everything to attract us back to Him—He shows us His pastimes, He gives us His holy name—but if we prefer to be kicked and bitten by *maya*—that is, we desire such a low taste—He will let us go. What else can He do?

And what can the spiritual master do if the disciple makes the choice to be kicked and bitten by the material energy? The spiritual master already knows what will happen, and he knows that his disciple cannot be happy by being separated from Krsna. Seeing a disciple make this choice gives the spiritual master great pain. Devotees experience distress to see others forgetting Krsna and happiness to see others connecting with Him.

A perfect *yogi* preaches Krsna consciousness selflessly, and willingly accepts whatever austerities come from that pursuit for the Lord’s satisfaction. Lord Caitanya ordered that regardless of our position, whether we are *brahmacaris, grhasthas, vanaprasthas,* or *sannyasis,* we should simply accept the austerity of giving Krsna consciousness to others. Make others happy by giving them Krsna consciousness. If we can accept this trouble, Lord Caitanya assures us that there will be no obstacles that cannot be overcome in our spiritual lives. *Grhasthas* shouldn’t think that this doesn’t apply to them. Actually, Lord Caitanya gave this instruction to a *grhastha. Grhasthas* will not find their spiritual lives blocked if they accept the austerity of preaching. Give this one life to Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s movement. Simply pay the price. It is not really such a big price; it is insignificant in comparison to what the materialists are paying for their small amount of sense gratification. A preacher returns to the spiritual world.

## The devotee is prepared to accept risks for the satisfaction of the Lord

**Bg. 12.17 October 19, 1993**

**“One who neither rejoices nor grieves, who neither laments nor desires, and who renounces both auspicious and inauspicious things—such a devotee is very dear to Me. ” (Bg.** [**12.17**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/12.17)**)**

When the devotees are preaching that everything belongs to Krsna, they are exposing everyone’s thieving mentality. No, what you possess does not belong to the state; it doesn’t belong to you, either. The communists are exposed by this preaching. Everything belongs to Krsna: *isavasyam idam sarvam, yat kinca jagatyam jagat:* “Everything animate or inanimate that is within the universe is controlled and owned by the Lord. ” A devotee who preaches this exposes the cheating mentality of materialistic leaders.

Therefore, preachers run a risk. Not everyone is going to like what we are saying, because few people like to have their cheating exposed. Still, the devotee must take the risk. Prabhupada explains in his purport to *Bhagavad-gita* [12.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/12.17):

“A pure devotee is neither happy nor distressed over material gain and loss, nor is he very much anxious to get a son or disciple, nor is he distressed by not getting them. If he loses anything which is very dear to him, he does not lament. Similarly, if he does not get what he desires, he is not distressed. He is transcendental in the face of all kinds of auspicious, inauspicious and sinful activities. He is prepared to accept all kinds of risks for the satisfaction of the Supreme Lord. Nothing is an impediment in the discharge of his devotional service. Such a devotee is very dear to Krsna. ”

There is a story in the *Caitanya-bhagavata* about Gadadhara dasa that illustrates this point. One time Gadadhara dasa was experiencing ecstatic symptoms of love for Lord Caitanya. In this ecstasy he thought about how he could do something wonderful to please the Lord. There was a sinful Kazi. Everyone was afraid of him because he threatened people and severely punished them for their infractions. He especially hated Hindus. Sometimes he would imprison those who were chanting Krsna’s names. Gadadhara dasa thought about wanting to please the Lord, so in his ecstasy, he began chanting Krsna’s names all the way to the house of the Kazi. He did not stop at the doorstep, but walked right into his house. The Kazi’s family and friends were struck to see Gadadhara dasa so engaged in his chanting. Then Gadadhara dasa began to walk around the house saying, “Where is that sinful Kazi. I’ve come here to get him to chant Krsna’s holy names. If he does not chant them, I will sever his head from his body. ”

Then the Kazi came downstairs. When he saw Gadadhara dasa, he could not believe his eyes. He was completely dumbfounded. He said, “Gadadhara dasa, why have you come to see me? ”

“I have come to convince you to chant Krsna’s holy name. Don’t you believe that Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityananda have come to deliver the whole planet by distributing the holy name? The only one who has not chanted Krsna’s name is you. But you cannot escape Their mercy! You *must* chant Krsna’s name! ”

Gadadhara dasa was screaming as he spoke, and the Kazi wasn’t sure what to make of him. Everyone else was afraid of the Kazi, but here was Gadadhara dasa marching into his house and ordering him to chant. The Kazi replied, “Gadadhara, why don’t you come back tomorrow and then maybe I’ll chant your ‘Hari, Hari. ’”

Gadadhara dasa answered, “Why come back tomorrow? You have already chanted the names of Hari and your sins no longer exist! ” Gadadhara then began to dance in ecstasy and he left the Kazi’s home, himself chanting, “Hari, Hari! ”

The Kazi began to think. He was astounded by Gadadhara’s behavior. He did not know what to do. But Gadadhara’s mission had been successful and he had enticed the Kazi to chant Krsna’s name. He took such a risk for preaching; he was not afraid of the danger.

The Krsna consciousness movement is meant to attract all types of people. Even those who come because they desire something other than pure devotion can, through the association of devotees, gradually begin to render service. Even the impersonalists, if they are given an opportunity to associate with the Lord’s devotee, lose their attraction to liberation. How is that possible unless devotional service is a superior activity?

## Never turn your back on an opportunity to tell someone about Krsna

**SB 7.6.25 Boston, October 1995**

**“Nothing is unobtainable for devotees who have satisfied the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is the cause of all causes, the original source of everything. The Lord is the reservoir of unlimited spiritual qualities. For devotees, therefore, who are transcendental to the modes of material nature, what is the use of following the principles of religion, economic development, sense gratification and liberation, which are all automatically obtainable under the influence of the modes of nature? We devotees always glorify the lotus feet of the Lord, and therefore we need not ask for anything in terms of** ***dharma, artha, kama*** **and** ***moksa. ” (SB***  [**7.6.25**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/6.25)**)**

Prahlada Maharaja states here that devotional service is superior to any other process of self-realization and surpasses anything else that can possibly be attained in the material world. Therefore, devotees are not interested in *dharma, artha, kama* and *moksa,* because they gain pleasure in always glorifying the Lord’s lotus feet. They do not need to ask for anything else. Those who begin to taste real Krsna consciousness always want to be engaged in glorifying the Supreme Personality of Godhead. This is such a superior form of pleasure that a devotee loses taste for everything else.

In *The Nectar of Devotion* it is described that Krsna conscious happiness includes the happiness of *dharma, artha, kama* and *moksa.* Not only is it included, but the happiness derived from Krsna consciousness excels those inferior attainments. The materialists interested in *dharma, artha, kama,* and *moksa*—practicing religiosity in order to attain economic development, sense gratification, and ultimately, liberation—do not nurture descriptions of Krsna’s transcendental pastimes. Therefore, such a society will never progress to the platform of devotional service.

Within every society are people situated in various social positions of *brahmana, ksatriya, Vaisya,* and *sudra.* Even though they may remain in their positions, if they would only agree to throw away mental speculation and instead dedicate themselves to the transcendental narrations about the Supreme Personality of Godhead vibrated by the Lord and His devotees, they could certainly conquer the Lord. The Supreme Personality of Godhead is unconquerable by anyone in the three worlds, but if we want to conquer Him, we must do it with pure love. If the materialists want liberation, they can remain within their social positions but must somehow conquer the Supreme Lord, the giver of liberation. “For one who is engaged in devotional service to the Supreme Lord, liberation stands at the door with her hands folded, waiting and ready to serve. ”

There is no way to escape the bondage of society unless the society fosters the opportunity to hear and chant the Lord’s glories.

Devotees are not interested in liberation, because if we achieve it, we no longer have the opportunity to hear and chant about Krsna. What value, then, does liberation have? It is explained in the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* that Krsna gives us liberation easily, but He does not give *bhakti* easily because when He gives *bhakti,* He becomes conquered. Obviously *bhakti* is superior to *mukti,* because *mukti* is not eternal or complete; it does not establish our relationship with the Lord. *Bhakti,* on the other hand, immediately establishes our relationship with the Lord. When the Lord is satisfied by our activities, we can attain anything.

The pleasure gardens and groves for devotees are where transcendental narrations about the Lord are being vibrated. This is where the devotees are attracted to go.

The ability to truly relish transcendental narrations is considered beyond liberation. We think of liberation as freedom from suffering, but actually, if we achieve liberation but do not have attraction to hearing and chanting about Krsna, we remain under the influence of material nature. Such “liberation” is not permanent. Real liberation—the kind that gives eternal freedom from suffering— means to relish topics about the Supreme Lord constantly, and continuously. Only then will we lose our attraction to the false glitter of the material world.

Therefore, the highest duty we can render society is to inject it with hearing and chanting of transcendental topics. As Srila Prabhupada explains in his purport to *Srimad-Bhagavatam* ([7.6.12](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/6.12)):

***“Karmis*** **desire material happiness,** ***jnanis*** **desire liberation, but a devotee does not desire anything; he is simply satisfied by rendering transcendental loving service to the lotus feet of the Lord and glorifying Him by preaching, which is his life and soul. This is the position of a liberated devotee. ”**

The greatest service we can render human society is to tell others about Krsna. All other obligations are transcended when a devotee makes preaching Krsna consciousness his life and soul. A devotee considers preaching his most important priority; everything else is secondary. A devotee’s life and soul is to always hear and speak about Krsna.

All other social entanglements are secondary to our life and soul. By preaching, a devotee knows he is fulfilling his obligation to others in the highest way. A Vaisnava is one who creates this hankering to hear about Krsna in others. Therefore, a Vaisnava has to give up the false hankerings of the material world in order to hanker for Krsna and the spiritual world. We can hanker for relief from suffering as the *muktis* do, but they do not know how to hanker for Krsna. Impersonalists are only hankering for relief from pain. Unfortunately, they do not know where to find relief or what real relief or real hankering are. They are unaware of the unique and superior pleasure experienced by devotees who have even once heard the message of Godhead:

**“The intelligent, who have understood the Supreme Lord in the association of pure devotees and have become free from bad, materialistic association, can never avoid hearing the glories of the Lord, even though they have heard them only once. ” (Cc.** ***Madhya*** [**24.98**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/24.98)**)**

If the *muktis* actually want relief from material suffering, they should simply give submissive aural reception to the transcendental message of Godhead, which is spoken by the Lord’s pure devotees. Once that attraction to hearing about Krsna has awakened in them, then their life and soul will be to continue hearing. They will want to remain awake and attentive to the transcendental vibration. As their hearts become purified, they will develop their desire to hear and chant more and more.

This is how *any* of us becomes relieved from material suffering. If we are going to hanker for something, then we should hanker for that which will provide the most pleasurable experience. By a devotee’s mercy, we will be able to understand what that is. Without a devotee’s mercy, we may simply hanker for relief from our suffering without ever having the chance to taste pleasure.

People usually hanker for sense gratification. That requires economic development. Or so people think. If they are suffering, they think that making money will help them relieve the discomfort. They think money can fulfill their needs. As soon as we think we need to satisfy our own needs, however, we think we have to sacrifice what every living entity ultimately needs: The time we would spend hearing about Krsna. Then our life becomes a series of useless endeavors in pursuit of economic development.

Even if we are engaged in economic development, or if we are in some other social position—even one based on sense gratification— we should allow ourselves to be awakened by *krsna-katha.* Only then will we be happy. We don’t have to change our social position. It’s all right to stay where we are. Srila Prabhupada often stressed that. But from wherever we are, we should both hear about Krsna and tell everyone we meet about Him. Accept the *krsna-upadesa* (instruction given by Krsna) of hearing, and then, “Whomever you meet, tell them about Krsna. ”

When Krsna becomes our life and soul, then preaching will be our life and soul; then we will be devotees. Every other form of striving is secondary. Srila Prabhupada gave such a wonderful, merciful opportunity to make preaching our life and soul. Devotees can have access to such superior pleasure when they preach. Never turn your back on an opportunity to tell someone about Krsna. That is our most important duty.

If we satisfy Krsna by both glorifying Him and hearing about Him, we can conquer Krsna, just as Prahlada Maharaja conquered Krsna by his unalloyed love. Prahlada demonstrated that even in the face of incredible disturbance he was able to instruct his demonic classmates. He ran the risk of angering his teachers and especially his father, but still he preached. We too should take all risks and thus conquer the Lord.

## \[Parting comment before leaving for Russia\]

All of you take shelter of this most wonderful *sankirtana* process that Srila Prabhupada has given us. Don’t miss out on any opportunity available to work cooperatively. Make expanding Srila Prabhupada’s book distribution and the glories of the holy name your priority. It doesn’t matter what other duty you may have; it doesn’t matter whether you are washing pots, distributing books, working in administration, or whatever—never forget that the actual purpose of these duties is to awaken the desire to hear and chant about Krsna.

## To be a successful preacher, we must maintain Srila Prabhupada’s preaching spirit

**Caitanya-caritamrta Boston, February 1997**

Srila Prabhupada has spoken about Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s movement from the position of those who are envious of actual preachers of Krsna consciousness. Srila Prabhupada gives two important criteria for the Krsna conscious preacher. The first is that he is not satisfied to *only* maintain temples. A person with superior vision doesn’t see Krsna only in the Deity but in everyone’s heart.

This does not mean that Srila Prabhupada did not encourage us to engage in Deity worship. Srila Prabhupada certainly encouraged worship of the Deity. Those who may not be sufficiently advanced, Prabhupada says, who may still be at the neophyte stage of devotional service, can purify themselves by worshipping the Deity. Otherwise they may preach a concoction due to their unpurified motivations. They may, for example, twist the philosophy simply to gather followers. Srila Prabhupada insisted that we shouldn’t measure the success of our preaching by how many followers we have gathered. Srila Prabhupada did not use that as the only criteria. He gave the example of lambs going to the slaughterhouse—one will blindly follow behind the other. This does not mean that we have created devotees. This leads to the second criteria: If others are becoming Vaisnavas, then the Vaisnava preacher is successful.

We should learn to see with a preacher’s eye. With a preacher’s vision, we will see Krsna in the heart of each living entity. If he sees Krsna, he will be concerned for other living entities. Preaching means to strictly follow Mahaprabhu’s order. It means to see the superior order coming down and agreeing to carry it out. That is what keeps a preacher free of impure motivations.

There are many traps and *anarthas* on the Krsna conscious path, and if we are not careful, we will fall into them. For example, when we preach, people give us money, we gather followers, and we become famous. How can we remain pure in Krsna’s service despite those facilities? We have to think of Prabhupada’s criteria for successful preaching: To see Krsna in the heart of each living entity and to care that others actually become Vaisnavas.

Srila Prabhupada himself preached with these two points in mind. Therefore, he remained pure despite the immense facility he was offered for his movement. Srila Prabhupada did not claim that he was successful because he had temples and money; rather, he pointed to the Vaisnavas who had come to surrender to Krsna. He thought only about how they could become more and more Krsna conscious. That was his vision. Envious people saw Prabhupada’s success in terms of his wealth and followers, and they tried to criticize the quality of his attainments. They said Prabhupada’s Western disciples were not qualified to worship the Deity, that they couldn’t become *brahmanas,* and that they were disqualified in other ways too. Their example shows us the importance of not becoming envious of those who are preaching successfully.

Srila Prabhupada wanted only to elevate others. He saw Krsna in their hearts and wanted them to become happy by seeing Him too. Srila Prabhupada left this vision behind for his followers to preserve. He didn’t want us simply to maintain temples or even the institution. That is certainly important, but it is not everything. We have to also maintain Srila Prabhupada’s preaching spirit.

If we do maintain Prabhupada’s preaching spirit, we will see the institution and the temples expand. We *will* gather more followers, build more temples, and make other advances. Of course, Srila Prabhupada warned us not to use these things as the sole criteria by which to judge the success of our preaching, but devotees do care about them. Srila Prabhupada was himself keen to see, as he traveled around the world, that his disciples were advancing in Krsna consciousness and that the temples were flourishing.

But he wasn’t concerned *only* with his disciples. He also cared about the nondevotees. Such a Vaisnava is of the caliber of Vasudeva Datta, whom Prabhupada said was in a superior position even to Jesus Christ. Jesus was prepared to accept the sinful reactions of his followers, but Vaisnavas like Vasudeva Datta are prepared to accept not only the sinful reactions of their own followers but of the whole world if it will give them the freedom to love God.

Thus Srila Prabhupada wanted his disciples to preach. If he saw them preaching, he knew they were healthy and that those who were forgetting Krsna would have a chance to become healthy too. This is a Vaisnava’s vision—always planning how to satisfy Lord Caitanya by following His order, *yare dekha, tare kaha ‘krsna’-upadesa, amara ajnaya guru hana tara’ ei desa:* “Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Sri Krsna as they are given in *Bhagavad-gita* and *Srimad-Bhagavatam.* In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land. ” (Cc. *Madhya* [7.128](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-madhya/7.128))

Those who are caring for the Deity in the temple can also be preachers. When people come forward, they shouldn’t show the Deity just so that they can collect donations for Them; they should care for the donors’ advancement in Krsna consciousness. Everything we do to maintain the temple and institution should be done in a spirit of preaching. As soon as we plan only for our maintenance, everything deteriorates. No one becomes inspired to render service simply to maintain themselves. There is nothing more inspiring than the opportunity to serve a great cause. When we preach with the mentality of giving Krsna to others, of carrying out the higher order of the disciplic succession, then this is not material maintenance; this is maintenance on the *spiritual* platform—real maintenance. Then we are maintaining the temples, our own Krsna consciousness, and the Krsna consciousness of the conditioned souls.

Thus the scriptures say that we are being maintained by the mercy of the Vaisnavas and of Krsna. Krsna maintains us by engaging us in His service. We must accept this preaching spirit if we want to make spiritual advancement.

All devotees, especially those on the neophyte platform, should be careful to give proper respect to the preachers who share Prabhupada’s vision. In so many purports in the *Caitanya-caritamrta,* Srila Prabhupada mentions the envy of some of his godbrothers. Prabhupada was successful at spreading Krsna consciousness because he had a vision. How could anyone envy Prabhupada? However, he had temples, money, and followers, and his godbrothers had little of anything. Srila Prabhupada didn’t let their envy stop him. He knew they couldn’t understand what he was doing, because they saw only their own definition of success: Temples, money, and followers. For Prabhupada, success went far beyond that. His success was in the quality of his disciples. He was able to give them Krsna consciousness, and they were able to share that with many, many others.

We should remember Prabhupada’s criteria for preaching success and offer respect to those who are able to keep it in the forefront. We should know that everything else comes from that—our maintenance, temple growth, and opulent Deity worship. Everything else will become perfect through its connection to fulfilling the highest order. This is how we will strictly follow Srila Prabhupada’s example. That will be our perfection! Krsna is no poor man. He will certainly reciprocate with Prabhupada’s faithful followers.

## How can we avoid the tendency to compromise when we preach?

If we strictly follow the spiritual master’s order, we can preach. We don’t need to concoct anything. Sometimes devotees have the tendency to “pack” their preaching in such a way that will attract followers, but often such “packed” preaching omits Krsna. We may think we have to package Krsna consciousness to make it more attractive, but Krsna is *already* attractive. Sometimes we can become so absorbed in making a package that when people see it and become attracted, they open it and find only an empty box. Krsna doesn’t care to stay in such packaging. If we think first of followers and then of delivering the Krsna conscious truth, we may never get past the packaging stage: “Yes, this makes us look attractive. ”

Srila Prabhupada once said in Mayapur that a devotee should not placate the whims of the general populace simply to gather followers. If we want followers, why not dance naked in the street? That would attract so many people. We do not need to concoct when we present the philosophy. We should simply present it as it is.

We also don’t need to try to become big preachers. Better we’re sure that Krsna is actually within our packaging. Even a neophyte devotee can preach Krsna consciousness if he doesn’t concoct.

**SB** [**4.7.28**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/7.28) **Boston, January 1998**

From what you have seen during your preaching in Siberia, how do the devotees there deal with the extreme climate and other difficulties? Do they think, “This is Krsna’s arrangement that I live here and let me figure out how to preach, ” or do they think, “Let’s preach for a while and figure out how to go to a place with a warmer climate and which is more conducive for spiritual practices”?

The interesting thing is that many people from warmer climates go to Siberia to preach, because it is a lucrative place to distribute Srila Prabhupada’s books due to the oil industry there. That’s the irony of it. Now that Siberia has switched to a market economy, they are able to sell their oil on the international market instead of using it to heat their own houses in some places.

Aside from that, I find that most of the Siberian devotees accept where they are as their lot in life. After all, it’s not only Siberia that’s cold. I arrived in Kiev three weeks ago. When I stepped off the plane, it was minus thirty-five degrees centigrade. Again, the devotees in Kiev accept the cold as part of their lot in life. There are some who are eager to go to greener pastures, but it is usually not the weather that is attracting them. Most people have learned to cope with the weather. But it’s other forms of distress that drive the devotees out of the former Soviet Union, especially difficulties in the economy. Nowadays people from the former Soviet Union have learned about the West’s standard of comfort. Many people now watch television every day and have come to desire what they see— all of which is only available on the “heavenly planets” known as the United States, especially New York City. Actually, I should send Yajna-purusa over there and let him preach the “glories” of New York City. He can tell them what 42nd Street is all about. Otherwise, most of the devotees are still performing preaching austerities in these difficult places and are not going off to other places.

## How can we preach when we ourselves are not so inspired in devotional service?

**SB** [**3.29.9**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/29.9) **Dnepropetrovsk, June 1996**

If we want to preach even when we feel uninspired or unadvanced, we can draw people’s attention to those who are inspired and satisfied by devotional service. Preaching means drawing people’s attention not to ourselves but to the Lord or His purified devotees. Although we ourselves may not be ideal examples, we can still show people devotees who are exemplary. Preaching means providing people with the opportunity to associate with advanced devotees so that they can one day become devotees themselves. We should also associate with such *sadhus* so that we too can develop taste in devotional service. Just because we are not experiencing full Krsna consciousness does not mean we cannot preach. We can preach on the order of those who are strong devotees.

## Real samadhi is real absorption

**SB** [**3.28.25**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/28.25) **Moscow, March 31, 1996**

We achieve full absorption in the Lord’s transcendental form by following the spiritual master’s instructions. We must come to the stage of full absorption eventually, and that will require that we engage in meditation. But what is the meditation of a *bhakti-yogi*? It is strict adherence to the guru’s instructions. Srila Prabhupada explains in the fourth canto of the *Bhagavatam* that “one should not try to see Krsna behind some bush in Vrndavana. He should know that Krsna personally manifests His form through the instructions of the spiritual master. ” This is a devotee’s meditation.

When we meditate on the guru’s order, we will gradually become detached from the body. I saw this in action recently. Just before I left the U. S. recently, one of my Godbrothers was hit by a car. He had been out distributing books with three other devotees—he was training them—and all four of them were crossing the street. They looked up and saw a car moving at about 45 miles (70 km) an hour. What did my Godbrother do? He immediately pushed the other three devotees out of the way and took the full brunt of the car’s weight himself. He was thrown seven meters in the air and then landed on the concrete.

The devotees who were there called me to tell me what had happened. I immediately wanted to go and see my Godbrother. I wondered how he could have survived such an accident. By the time I reached the hospital, he was just coming out of surgery. The doctors had had to reconstruct his legs and shoulders. When I walked into his room I wondered whether he was conscious. As I came in I said, “*Haribol*, Prabhu. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. ” He couldn’t turn his head, but he responded, *“Haribol,* Maharaja. All glories to Srila Prabhupada. How is the preaching going in the former Soviet Union? ” I wanted to ask him how he was, but I couldn’t. He didn’t allow me. He just kept asking me more and more questions about the preaching in Russia.

What kind of *yogi* was this devotee? He went on for another fifteen minutes asking me questions. He tried to drink a little water, but then his body went into convulsions. Then he looked up at me and said, “Maharaja, can you do one thing for me? Can you please give me your blessings and beg the other Vaisnavas on my behalf so that I can go out on book distribution for the rest of my life? ” This is all he asked of me!

I couldn’t render him any service; he wasn’t even thinking about his body. If I tried to ask him about it, he treated it like it was irrelevant. All he wanted was my blessing to go back out on book distribution. I talked to him for about an hour, and all we discussed was preaching. He wouldn’t let me discuss any other topic. I realized preaching was real meditation for him. Srila Prabhupada states, “Real *samadhi* is real absorption. ” I began to realize that this devotee’s meditation is the type that enables us to transcend the material body. This devotee was actually practicing yoga. This was not a *yogi* sitting in a posture and meditating on the Lord in the heart, but a preacher meditating on his spiritual master’s order to spread Lord Caitanya’s *sankirtana* mission. Caitanya Mahaprabhu says that such meditation will extinguish the fire of repeated birth and death.

## The easiest way to realize the Lord is to preach to everyone

**SB** [**7.6.24**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/6.24)

Srila Prabhupada states that if one is imperfectly educated but sincerely tries to spread Krsna consciousness by preaching the Lord’s glories and supremacy, he will become the Lord’s dearmost servant. There is no material qualification required to preach the Supreme Personality of Godhead’s glories. Therefore, we should not think that our material education is inadequate even when we meet people in our preaching who appear to be at a higher academic standard. We should understand that a devotee who understands the Supreme Lord’s position and who glorifies the Lord by his words and actions is immediately elevated far beyond the platform of the materialistic academics. Education is not required. We automatically become educated by practicing devotional service, pleasing our spiritual master, and serving devotees. Real knowledge is not achieved by academic pursuits, but through surrender to Krsna—the practice of *bhakti.*

The easiest way to realize the Supreme Personality of Godhead is to preach to everyone. All other methods are extremely difficult, what to speak of all the austerities exalted personalities have performed. Srila Prabhupada made *bhakti so* accessible; he gave everyone the opportunity to partake in this easy and sublime process of self-realization.

## Preach while being dependent upon the Lord

**The Nectar of Book Distribution Hartford, 1992**

*Para-upakara:* To do good unto others. When we go out and distribute books, there will be many difficulties, but this gives one the impetus to pray to Krsna and helps one become Krsna conscious and enjoy life.

A devotee has to learn to feel helpless while executing his duty. Why does a devotee perform a particular duty? He knows he must be dutiful in order to please guru and Krsna. Duty purifies us. We carry out so many self-imposed duties, thinking we must do them, but the greatest, most compelling duty is to offer whatever we are doing to Krsna. We must work for Visnu’s satisfaction.

How to feel helpless in our activities? Srila Prabhupada described his own feelings of helplessness. He realized that he was carrying his spiritual master’s order on his head but that he could not possibly carry it out by his own efforts. He couldn’t imagine what the results of his endeavor would be. We may not fully understand Krsna’s plan, but we know one thing: We must do our duty. We are pushed by the spiritual master’s order. We don’t know how we will carry it out, but we must try. That will make us feel helpless.

Helplessness and dependence on Krsna is vital to the spiritual execution of duty. We do not come to the Krsna consciousness movement and immediately understand our mission. We begin by being concerned with our own welfare. After a while, we understand that we should share Krsna consciousness with others. The spiritual master directs us. But it’s a big task.

Oftentimes people come to Krsna consciousness seeking relief from distress. They take shelter among the devotees without realizing that as they become devotees, they too will be required to help others relieve *their* distress. People don’t understand the welfare mission of this movement in the beginning. They are too absorbed in themselves and their individual, selfish needs.

Most of us have not been able to break through the barrier of false ego to understand Lord Caitanya’s mission. As we become purified and cleansed, we may begin to understand. If we want to purify ourselves, we have to follow Lord Caitanya’s instructions: *ceto-darpana-marjanam.* A sincere devotee takes advantage of this method of purification. We do not simply practice rules and regulations to become purified. Rather, the rules and regulations are meant to help us become fixed in remembrance of Krsna and to realize our actual service.

Our service is to satisfy the Lord. The best way to do that is to preach. Then our liberation is guaranteed. We don’t have to look to be delivered; we just have to serve. Service is the most important activity.

## Advancement means we will someday develop an attraction for telling people about Krsna

**SB** [**1.8.30**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/8.30) **St. Petersburg**

*susrusoh sraddadhanasya*  
*vasudeva-katha-rucih*  
*syan mahat-sevaya viprah*  
*punya-tirtha-nisevanat*

**“O twice-born sages, by serving those devotees who are completely freed from all vice, great service is done. By such service, one gains affinity for hearing the messages of Vasudeva. ” (SB** [**1.2.16**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.16)**)**

We may not have spontaneous attraction to hearing and chanting. We may find ourselves feeling forced to engage in those activities. We may feel uncomfortable in such activities, because we have no spontaneous attraction. But if we wish to *develop* spontaneous attraction, we should voluntarily accept situations that will force us to speak about Krsna. Preaching is such a situation.

A devotee may think, “I have no spontaneous attraction to tell people about Krsna, but here I am, standing on a street corner holding these books. Here is a person who knows nothing about Krsna. What am I doing here? I’m supposed to preach to this person. Maybe I didn’t want to come—maybe I was forced to come by my temple authority. I don’t even like to be out here trying to stop all these people who don’t want to hear about Krsna. Still, here I am. How can I save myself from *maya?* I’ll have to say something about Krsna. It’s either that, or back to sleep or to the *prasadam* room. ”

We may feel forced, but our spiritual master has asked us to preach. We know that if we want to advance in devotional life, we must serve the guru’s order. Advancement means we will someday develop an attraction for telling people about Krsna. We may not have that attraction now, but if we continue to serve, it will come.

## Book distributors have the responsibility of representing the guru and the Lord properly

**The Nectar of Book Distribution Moscow, December 5, 1993**

Sometimes devotees think they want to get a bigger result on book distribution. That’s natural, because the moment we walk through the temple door, devotees want to know how we did. Sometimes we didn’t distribute very many books, so the question begins to embarrass us, especially if we didn’t do well for some days in a row. We begin to plan how to avoid embarrassment by getting a higher score.

That is how material consciousness begins to sneak in. We begin by making a few twisted statements when we distribute the books. Often we see that our twisted statements get a better response than our previously straight ones. Then we become addicted to these statements and are unable to break the habit of not speaking straight Krsna conscious philosophy. We come to think we have discovered a superior method of distributing books. We continue in this mentality as long as we get a good result.

But the devotees should be very, very careful about this, because they are representing Srila Prabhupada and Lord Caitanya. Every one of you who goes out into the streets with one of Prabhupada’s books is responsible to represent the Lord properly. When we begin to think only of results, we lose sight of the reason we’re out there in the first place: To make a humble effort to satisfy guru and Krsna.

Why should devotees feel embarrassed to say they did not distribute so many books? There is no need for embarrassment at all. If they went out and made an honest effort to please Krsna and Prabhupada, then the devotees back at the temple should glorify them for their endeavor. Book distribution is austere for devotees who do not get big results, because they work very hard for not much. They should therefore be glorified for their sincere efforts.

We should not always think that the criterion for success is a big result. The criterion of success is actually that we are developing attachment to Krsna. Prabhupada explains this in *Srimad-Bhagavatam:*

“Anyone who takes pride in doing responsible work but does not give credit to the Supreme Lord is certainly falsely proud and cannot execute anything nicely. ” (SB [3.9.29](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/9.29), purport)

Read these statements and let them be your guide in your humble effort to please Srila Prabhupada.

“So \[as far as\] making some false story for collecting money or selling book, of course we may do anything for Krsna, but that is supposed to be reserved for very advanced experts in Krsna Consciousness—they know how to catch the big fish without themselves getting wet. So it is not very much advisable to tell lies just to sell a book. If we simply stick to describing how wonderful is Krsna, then whatever we may lie or exaggerate, that will not be a lie! But other things (lies) they will not help us to train ourselves in truthfulness. Lie to some, not to others, that is not a good philosophy. Rather the *brahmanas* are always truthful, even to their enemies. There is sufficient merit in our books that if you simply describe them sincerely to anyone, they will buy. That art you must develop, not the art of lying. Convince them to give by your preaching the Absolute Truth, not by tricking—that is more mature stage of the development of Krsna consciousness. ”(Letter *to Sri Govinda dasa, December 25, 1972)*

This is a statement from a letter by Srila Prabhupada to one of his temple presidents. Prabhupada makes it clear what he expects from his book distributors. Of course, he says, “Those who are experts in Krsna consciousness know how to catch a big fish without getting wet. ” But that ability is a sign of advancement; not all of us are capable of doing this. Therefore, we shouldn’t imitate it.

We should not think we are so advanced that we can accept this statement out of context and justify telling lies in order to distribute books. In fact, there are other quotes here in which Srila Prabhupada specifically chastises devotees for things he heard that they had said on book distribution. A devotee is trained in truthfulness. Truthfulness is a brahminical quality. If we understand that distributing Krsna consciousness to others is brahminical activity, then certainly we must take up the banner of truthfulness.

We could say so many things while presenting Krsna conscious literature. The Krsna consciousness movement is performing welfare activity for human society and improving moral standards in a variety of ways already. We don’t have to concoct ways to get people to give money.

How can we convince people that we are improving the moral standard of society if we ourselves are lying? It’s a contradiction. If we become known as people who tell lies, then how can we convince people that we have something elevated to offer to them? In the *Bhagavatam,* Bhumi Devi says she can tolerate any heavy burden except a liar. It is very important that we present Krsna consciousness appropriately. Then when we tell people we are improving society’s moral standard, it will have real impact. Even if people do not respond favorably immediately, they will at least recognize us over time as honest and straightforward.

Prabhupada tells us how to preach: If we simply describe how wonderful Krsna is, “then whatever we may lie or exaggerate, it will not be a lie. ” In other words, there is no question of lying or exaggerating when we are talking about Krsna’s wonders. We simply have to speak as if we are convinced. How can we exaggerate Krsna’s wonderful qualities? Is there a limit to His glories? Is there a limit to what Lord Caitanya can do?

There is no limit to Lord Caitanya’s pastimes. If we stick to speaking about Him, then there will be no question of exaggerating or telling lies.

Along with glorifying Krsna, we can tell people what Krsna is doing through the Krsna consciousness movement. If our movement represents the Lord’s will, then we should represent that will to others.

We are not short of things to present when we are distributing books. We should simply depend on Krsna for the result of our distribution. Srila Prabhupada writes:

“If we simply speak nicely to a person and try sincerely to get him to take the book, he will take it. Why should we adopt unfair means? We should not do anything, which will create a bad impression or make us unpopular. People are after these books. They are hankering for them. ”

Prabhupada’s statements are clear: People are hankering for his books. We don’t need to cheat. I never had to cheat when I began distributing books. I simply presented the truth of what they contained. Practically speaking, Srila Prabhupada writes:

“Everything done sincerely for Krsna’s pleasure is perfect. But that doesn’t mean we shouldn’t be careful in our dealings with others so that they will not take the wrong view. The *gopis* left their homes in the dead of night to meet young Krsna. They wanted to kiss and embrace Him. To ordinary persons, this is sinful, but to a devotee it is worshipable and considered the highest form of devotion. Even Lord Caitanya considered it the topmost *rasa,* even though He was a strict sannyasi. So we do not discuss *rasa-lila* in the presence of ordinary men because they will only misunderstand and commit offenses. While dealing with the ordinary men we must spread this movement in such a way that they will not misunderstand us and take offense. Try to sell as many of my books as possible to your best ability. ”

Srila Prabhupada is, of course, emphasizing that we must simply present the real thing. Basically, everything done sincerely for Krsna’s pleasure is perfect. If we present Krsna consciousness in a thoroughly honest way, trying our best to please Krsna, then we will become perfect. But give the real thing. Prabhupada himself spread the Krsna consciousness movement by giving the real thing.

We want people to become attracted to Krsna. Then we have to give them Krsna. We should never distort Prabhupada’s statement, “By hook or by crook, ” when we are thinking about how to distribute books. We should learn the context of that statement and not think that the end always justifies the means. Just because a person takes a book, we shouldn’t think it doesn’t matter how he or she got it.

“By hook or by crook” means that we have to make every effort possible to convince someone to take a book *without resorting to lies.*

Srila Prabhupada says:

“Regarding the controversy about book distribution techniques, you are right. Our occupation must be honest. Everyone should adore our members as honest. If we do something which is deteriorating to the popular sentiments of the public in favor of our movement, that is not good. Somehow or other we should not become unpopular in the public eye. These dishonest methods must be stopped. It is hampering our reputation all over the world. Money collecting for feeding people in India should be collected under the name ISKCON Food Relief, not any other name, and every farthing of that money must be sent to India or better yet, buy food grains there and ship them here, and we will distribute, but every farthing collected for that purpose must be used for that purpose. ”

We have to somehow try to balance our efforts during the Prabhupada Marathon with careful thought. It’s not that we should go out during the marathon and distribute tons of books at the expense of our reputation. That would not be balanced. I’ve seen devotees go out and collect for Food-for-Life but using the money for other purposes. Prabhupada clearly states that if we collect for food relief programs, every farthing of what we collect should be used for that purpose. Devotees use that line to make money so that they can give out free books. But how can they give out books with the money they have collected for food distribution?

What *can* we tell people? We can say we are distributing food at Food-for-Life programs. We can tell people we are raising society’s moral standard. We can tell them we are preaching to people in prisons. We can tell them how we are placing Krsna consciousness literature in libraries and prisons, how we are building temples, and engaging in so many other activities. We can say all these things, but we should not be interested only in collecting a donation. That we have that interest is why devotees are claiming to collect for food relief.

I hope you have a successful marathon. Certainly if you continue to read from this book daily and make an effort to participate, then everyone will become enlivened and surcharged with enthusiasm to distribute Krsna consciousness. That enthusiasm is what will impact material society. When the devotees go out enthused, then the people they meet, those people with their dull, dreary lives, will actually change. Enthusiastic devotees can be straightforward, because people will be caught more by their enthusiasm than by their actual words.

I have had this experience. I used to distribute books with Pragosa and Vaisiseka Prabhus. Vaisiseka was known especially for his bubbling enthusiasm. With every book he distributed, he would say, “We are giving these books out to distribute love of God. Do you want to learn how to love God too? ” That’s all he would have to say. People would take the books because of his enthusiasm.

If we are enthusiastic, we can convince people that it is wonderful to develop and distribute love of God. Shouldn’t we be enthusiastic to distribute it? If we are not enthusiastic, then it means we have forgotten what we’re distributing. We have begun to see ourselves as booksellers. We are not booksellers; we are meant to distribute love of God. That is what the devotees are doing when they go out with Prabhupada’s transcendental literature. We have every reason to be enthusiastic. If everyone could simply develop this kind of enthusiasm to serve Lord Caitanya and become detached from the result, we would have such wonderful results. We wouldn’t be able to calculate them, because they would include our personal advancement in Krsna consciousness.

Have faith in the order of guru and Krsna and go out armed with transcendental knowledge to give Srila Prabhupada’s books to everyone you meet. This is the Prabhupada Marathon; this is your opportunity. If Krsna is kind, maybe He will allow me to come back for your *sankirtana* festival and we can relish the transcendental nectar as devotees share their realizations. That’s another wonderful thing about the *sankirtana* festival after the Prabhupada marathon: all the devotees have an opportunity to speak the realizations Krsna gave them.

## Become qualified to be a preacher

**The Nectar of Book Distribution (2) Moscow, December 6, 1993**

Yesterday we spoke about the importance of the Prabhupada Marathon and how it helps to invoke a certain consciousness and commitment to Srila Prabhupada’s mission by connecting our efforts throughout the year’s preaching to our actual objective of disseminating transcendental literature. Whether a devotee is on the front lines distributing books or worshipping the deities or educating our future preachers, it’s all part of the *sankirtana* movement. I just came from Vrndavana where there is a nicely developed *gurukula.* I spoke to many of the *gurukula* children as well as to the headmaster. All of them have fixed their minds: They are training to be preachers. They are consuming the Krsna conscious philosophy at a young age. I listened to a six-year-old boy from China as he began his memorization of *slokas* from a variety of *sastras.* He quoted *sloka* after *sloka* with perfect Sanskrit pronunciation, and then gave the translation of each word. He was only six years old! Perhaps there will be some Ramanujacaryas and Madhvacaryas coming in the future. These children will be powerful preachers.

Therefore, education is also part of expanding Srila Prabhupada’s mission. *Every* service is part of that mission—whether it be distributing *prasadam,* administering the temple, or organizing the *sankirtana.*

Srila Prabhupada said that organizing the *sankirtana* was an important service, but everything in ISKCON should be performed in the mood of *sankirtana.* We should always be thinking like that, even when we are lecturing. We want to preach in such a way that people become attracted to what we are saying and want to give their lives to Krsna. We want them to desire to understand more about the subject matter by reading Srila Prabhupada’s books. Thus we should always speak in such a way as to be speaking from Prabhupada’s books, because if someone becomes a little attracted to Krsna consciousness, we can then direct them to the books: “Everything I have said is available in these books. Actually, I have only scratched the surface of the information available.
