If you really want to know what Krsna consciousness is about, then go to the source of information: Srila Prabhupada’s books. ”

When people take our advice, they will become more and more convinced about God’s existence. It is our duty to hear and repeat what we have heard, so that people have that opportunity to become convinced.

Srila Prabhupada on book distributors:

“If your stomach is supplied nutritious food and is clear of all disturbances, then you keep good health. If you pour water on the root of the tree, then you take care of all the branches, leaves, flowers and twigs automatically. So if you take care of Krsna, then you do the best service to all others automatically. These boys are going on the *sankirtana* party because they are Krsna conscious. It is not that they are sitting idly in this temple. They are going outside and preaching this philosophy so that others may take advantage of it. A Krsna conscious person cannot sit idly. He thinks, ‘Such a nice philosophy of life. Why should it not be distributed? ’ That is his mission. A *yogi* may be satisfied with his own elevation to transcendental life. That is his personal concern, but a devotee is not satisfied simply elevating himself. We offer our respect to the Vaisnavas:

*vancha-kalpa-tarubhyas ca, krpa-sindhubhya eva ca*  
*patitanam pavanebhyo, vaisnavebhyo namo namah*

A devotee is very compassionate to the conditioned souls. *Krpa* means mercy and *Sindhu* means ocean. A devotee is an ocean of mercy; he wants to distribute the mercy. Just like Lord Jesus Christ, he was God conscious, Krsna conscious, but he was not satisfied in himself. If he would have continued his God consciousness alone, he would not have met crucifixion, but he wanted to take care of others also, and make them God conscious, Krsna conscious. He was forbidden by the king to do that and at the risk of his life he did it. That is the nature of a devotee. Therefore a preacher is a dearmost devotee of the Lord. They go outside, they preach, they meet opposing elements; sometimes they are defeated, sometimes disappointed, and sometimes able to convince. Not that every devotee is well-equipped—there are three classes of devotees—but that endeavor to preach Krsna consciousness is the best service to the Lord, because they are trying against opposition to elevate people to the highest standard of self-realization. One who has seen, who is in the stage of self-realization, cannot sit idly. He must come out. ” (Lecture, February, 1969)

This is a forceful statement by Srila Prabhupada about the nature of a devotee. We know that Srila Prabhupada spoke from the full realization of his own position. This is what makes a person’s statements forceful: He has realization. He understands the exact consciousness of a Vaisnava as one who cannot sit idly. Srila Prabhupada did not make such statements to justify his own activities. He doesn’t have to justify himself to others. He already knows preaching is a fully justifiable activity because it has been ordered by the Lord. The Lord has specifically stated that preaching is the best way to please Him.

The Lord tells every one of His devotees how to engage themselves fully in His service. Devotional service to the Lord is *ahaituky apratihata,* unmotivated and uninterrupted. It is an eternally sustainable activity. It cannot be destroyed by any material distraction.

Of course, sometimes impediments may appear on a devotee’s path, but the devotee does not see them as impediments. Rather, he accepts them as an opportunity to strengthen his conviction in devotional service, especially in preaching, which he knows will satisfy the Lord. Who can impede Krsna’s will?

Prabhupada said that purity is the force, and purity is certainly powerful. Purity means to represent Krsna’s will. Who can stop His will from being carried out? Therefore, a devotee has the conviction to preach Krsna consciousness. There may be few persons interested in taking Krsna consciousness from a devotee, and we may even meet opposing forces. Prabhupada assured us that the more devotees push to expose atheistic propaganda, the more resistance to their preaching they will feel. A devotee is convinced of Krsna’s power of will and takes whatever impediments arise as a test of his own determination.

Lord Caitanya wants us to be qualified before He hands us the world. How do we become qualified? We have to learn how to confront obstacles by increasing our determination and purity. Lord Caitanya can give us so much. He is ready to give it to us. He is waiting for us to be ready to take it. When He sees that we are ready,

He will give us more and more. That is His will; He wants it that way. At the same time, He wants His devotees to do the work; He wants them to receive the credit.

A devotee is not interested in credit, but simply in pleasing the Lord. Therefore, he cannot sit idly, because he knows the Lord is expecting him to preach. A devotee never thinks the Lord will do the work Himself. He knows that the Lord wants him to do it. How can he neglect that order?

On the strength of the Lord’s order, Srila Prabhupada preached strongly. On the strength of that order and his firm faith in his guru and Krsna, he was tireless to meet all obstacles. With every reverse, he only increased his determination to preach. He was the Lord’s dearmost devotee. Sometimes devotees are defeated— sometimes Prabhupada appeared to be defeated or disappointed— but those conditions were temporary. There is no real defeat for one who is serving Krsna.

What are those defeats? Sometimes we may sit down and speak to someone about Krsna, but we are unable to convince them. The inability to convince others is the symptom of a *kanistha-adhikari. A kanistha-adhikari* is not firmly convinced of scriptural conclusions himself, so he is unable to convince others.

Srila Prabhupada mentions the three classes of devotees. The neophyte (*kanistha*) does not have strong faith, but because his spiritual master is anxious to see him go out and preach, he makes an effort to present whatever he has heard. Sometimes he may be successful and sometimes not.

We often see that among the devotees. They ask, “I don’t know what to say in such-and-such situation. What should I say? ” If we meet such challenges when we are preaching, we have to be careful not to act out of false ego and become angry or insolent with them. We may not be able to defeat all arguments, but our service will still be perfect if we are making the effort to please our spiritual master. Our aim of developing Krsna consciousness will not be defeated, and we will make advancement to the next platform of devotional service. Our faith will develop and we will become stronger when we meet resistance. A devotee uses the opportunity to confront challenges as an opportunity to increase his faith. It’s not that he becomes discouraged and thinks, “This philosophy cannot answer challenges. ” Or, “I am incapable of answering challenges. Something must not be true here. ”

Prabhupada encourages his preachers to take risks when trying to convince others about Krsna consciousness. Even if we are not fully equipped with faith, knowledge, or advancement, we can still push ourselves rather than sitting idly. That is how we live Krsna consciousness and not just philosophizing about it.

We live Krsna consciousness by applying it in our efforts to surrender to Krsna. This is how Srila Prabhupada’s books come to life for us. As we advance, all the realizations will begin to unfold one after another. A devotee lives Krsna consciousness. It’s important to hear the philosophy every day and to discuss it with one another, but hearing means acting. We must act upon what we hear if we want to develop strong realization of it. Then we will begin to catch glimpses of what Lord Caitanya’s movement is about. If we simply take a drop of the nectar and analyze it, we will find its sweetness. Then we can understand that the whole ocean of Lord Caitanya’s *sankirtana* movement must taste as sweet. We will develop the desire to discover the whole ocean of realization available.

We know that there is an ocean of realization because Krsna is unlimited. Srila Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami writes in his *Caitanyacaritamrta* that it is not possible for him to write down all of Lord Caitanya’s glories because every moment of the Lord’s pastimes requires a full description. Yet the Lord is engaged in newer and newer pastimes at every moment. It is therefore impossible to plumb the depths of the ocean of Lord Caitanya’s unlimited pastimes of reclaiming fallen souls. Each pastime contains oceans of meaning and compassion. It is not possible to empty such an ocean.

The Lord is called *purnam*, completely full. *Sad-aisvarya-purnam:* He is completely full of six qualities and opulences. He can never be emptied.

There is a story that reminds me of this quality. There was a young boy who went to Dinabandhu to get yogurt. The Lord had given him a small pot of yogurt to satisfy his classmates. When the boy presented this small pot of yogurt to his teacher, the teacher cried, “What?! You expect this little pot of yogurt to satisfy all the students in this class? ” and he threw the yogurt out of the pot. But when he placed the pot back on the table, it was completely full. The teacher was astonished. Again he threw the yogurt out and again turned it over to find it full again.

Prabhupada called this *sad-aisvarya-purnam,* completely full. He can never be emptied, never exhausted. His pastimes are unlimited. When a devotee realizes the depth of the Lord’s pastimes, he knows he can never come to the limit of realization; there will always be more. This becomes the source of continued devotional experiences and service to the Lord.

Since Lord Caitanya’s pastimes are inexhaustible, if we want to unleash the realizations that come from participating in them, we will have to preach. Prabhupada writes, “That endeavor to preach Krsna consciousness is the best service to the Lord, because they are trying against opposition to elevate people to the highest standard of self-realization. ” This description helps us understand some of the qualities a book distributor must have. He must have faith in guru and Krsna’s order and he must try to elevate others to the highest standard of Krsna consciousness, facing all impediments. He must cultivate a desire to always be engaged in the preaching mission and to be prepared to take risks for the Lord. It’s not that we have to seek risks out; they will always be available. Still, the devotee places himself in Krsna’s hands with firm faith that Krsna will always protect him.

## To what extent is it our choice to become devotees?

**SB** [**2.8.26**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/8.26) **Moscow (Kokoshkino gurukula), October 25, 1996**

It is not exclusively our choice. Certainly we have to choose to accept Krsna when Krsna’s representative tries to give us Krsna. But by Lord Caitanya’s mercy, He helps us make that choice as He distributes Krsna consciousness. Lord Caitanya wants to attract us to devotional service. If Lord Caitanya did not do that for us, how would we know we had such a choice? Rather, we would be in ignorance, thinking that the only way to live is to seek sense gratification. That would limit our choices to suffering and the temporary relief of suffering. Lord Caitanya was kind enough to present us with another option.

We should never think that it is by our efforts alone that we come to Krsna consciousness. Ultimately, we are dependent on Krsna’s choice. We have a choice, but Krsna’s choice is supreme. If Krsna chooses to manifest Himself to us through His devotee’s mercy, then that is His supreme choice. Krsna has kindly chosen to manifest Himself to the fallen people of Kali-yuga through the *sankirtana* movement. We may choose whatever we want; Krsna has already decided to enact His *sankirtana* pastimes as Lord Caitanya.

It is Lord Caitanya’s desire that we have the chance to come in contact with His *sankirtana* movement. If He didn’t spread the *sankirtana* movement, what choice would we have had? That’s what Prabhupada says. In one purport he writes, “The disciplic succession from Brahma, Narada, Vyasa and Sukadeva Gosvami is particularly different from others. The disciplic successions from other ages are simply a waste of time, because they are devoid of *acyuta-katha. ”* Unless one comes in contact with the bona fide disciplic succession, he will only hear mental speculation. How will he benefit? He will have simply wasted his time.

But the Lord is so kind that He gives us the chance to find the bona fide *sampradaya* by spreading His *sankirtana* movement. Then we are able to hear from bona fide gurus. Srila Prabhupada says: “There may be so much counterfeit currency, but even though there is so much counterfeit money... it means that somewhere there must be something real. ” What is the meaning of counterfeit if there is nothing real that is being copied? Similarly, divine instructions come down from the Lord through the disciplic succession. This is the real message of Godhead. Following anything else is not really a choice, but if we choose it, then we have to accept the reaction for making such a choice. We each have free will. We have been given mercy. If we choose to reject it, we will face the consequences as part of free will.

As far as how we can use our freedom in a positive way, we can spend our time hearing the message of the *Bhagavatam.* That is the best use of freedom. The more we hear the *Bhagavatam,* the clearer our life’s mission will become to us. Hearing is our highest duty. That is stated at the beginning of the *Bhagavatam* itself, when the sages of Naimisaranya asked Suta Gosvami, “Please, tell us what is the highest duty for all mankind? ” The sages knew that everyone in society had particular duties, but what was the best duty *(paro dharmo)* of all? Suta Gosvami answered that the highest duty for all humankind is that by which man can attain loving devotional service to the Supreme Lord. He goes on to explain that this duty in life is revealed by hearing the transcendental messages of *SrimadBhagavatam,* because the *Bhagavatam* kicks out all other cheating processes of religion. The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* is *param-dharma*; it kicks out *kaitava-dharma.* Therefore, when we hear the *Bhagavatam,* our most important duty in life is fulfilled. We may have other duties to family, society, and friends—we might have to work to support our families, for example—but as Lord Brahma states in the tenth canto, if those who are established in their particular social positions dedicate themselves to hearing transcendental narrations about the Lord, which are vibrated by Krsna and His pure devotees, then they can conquer Krsna, although Krsna is otherwise unconquerable. In other words, we can remain in our social stations, but we must still perform the duty of hearing the *Bhagavatam’s* message. All other duties are secondary.

All duties within *varnasrama* are meant to lead us to the point of hearing only about Krsna.

## Why is it that some people, although they have money and touch Prabhupada’s books, do not take them?

**Ista-gosthi**

**Vinnitza, Summer 1995**

Every living being has individual choice. Therefore, those who are too attached to sense gratification and material opulence cannot develop the determination to buy one of Prabhupada’s books. Thus it is our duty to make their desire grow and grow. We do that by increasing our own purity. Then even though a person may not have the determination to buy a book, our desire that they do so will be so strong that Krsna will hear our prayers and inspire them to develop the determination.

We have to understand that the success in preaching is not based only on the nondevotee’s desire but on our own as well. We have to feel the weight of responsibility. Maybe *our* desire that they take a book is not pure enough. We have to increase our desire. We cannot force people to take books, but we can pray to Krsna to inspire them from within the heart.

If a materially attached person declines, we should not think that we are supposed to increase our desire that they take the book, then try to force them to do so. That’s not what increasing our desire means. Increasing our desire means making our desire genuine and pure, developing compassion, and praying to Krsna in the heart to inspire the nondevotees to accept His mercy. We can pray, “Krsna, please inspire this person to accept a book. All these objections he’s offering are material, but he can’t go beyond the mental platform. Krsna, I know You are in his heart and beyond his mind. Please, Krsna, let this person hear what I am saying. ”

## How to elevate human society?

**SB** [**2.3.18**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/3.18) **Mayapur, March 20, 1994**

**“Do the trees not live? Do the bellows of the blacksmith not breathe? All around us, do the beasts not eat and discharge semen? ”** ***(SB*** [**2.3.18**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/3.18)**)**

This verse and the ones that follow describe the distinctions between the materialistic and spiritual societies. They also mention that whenever there is an assembly of Vaisnavas hearing descriptions of Krsna’s glories, they are thinking how to benefit human society. All sacrifices performed by devotees are performed with this purpose. They do not meditate on their own elevation, but on how to elevate society. Devotees are naturally pained to see materialistic society become increasingly degraded. The sages of Naimisaranya performed their sacrifice to help human society. Sukadeva Gosvami and Pariksit Maharaja held discussions to benefit humankind. It’s not that Sukadeva Gosvami thought he was only instructing his disciple; Pariksit Maharaja was also not asking questions only for himself. Both Sukadeva Gosvami and Maharaja Pariksit were thinking of future generations—thousands of years worth of generations.

Whenever Vaisnavas discuss topics of the Lord, they act for the welfare of human society. Suta Gosvami explained to the sages of Naimisaranya, “O sages, your questions are glorious because they relate to Lord Krsna. Therefore they are beneficial for the whole world. Only questions of this sort are capable of satisfying the self. ”

Suta Gosvami stated that because the sages’ questions were related to Lord Krsna, everyone was benefited. Any discussion that relates to Lord Krsna is always beneficial to those who hear them, whether they realize what is being said or not. This is how auspicious topics of the Lord are. *Krsna-katha,* therefore, is not simply an affair for a small group of people but it is meant for the whole world.

We come together in Mayapur and devotees hear and chant about Krsna. This *katha* is meant to help the whole world. We are not meant to keep Krsna’s pastimes and qualities to ourselves. That’s not why Srila Prabhupada established this festival. He specifically established this festival so that preachers from all over the world could come to this sacred place and hear topics related to the Lord. Then they would be inspired to return to their *prabhu-datta-desas* to carry the *Bhagavatam* message to others.

India is full of *asramas* and hermitages. When most people think of India and finding a guru, they imagine themselves going to such a hermitage in the Himalayas or Nepal. They are looking for gurus who perform great yogic austerities with their few disciples, meditating for their own purification.

When I was flying from America, I had a layover in a German airport. I was reading *Srimad-Bhagavatam* when someone came and sat next to me. He looked at the book I was reading and said, “Oh, Hare Krsna! ” I looked up and smiled. He told me he lived in Canada and was on his way to India to see his guru. I asked him where his guru lived, and he said he lived very high in the mountains in Nepal in a small *asrama.* I specifically spoke to him about what I was reading in the *Bhagavatam,* describing the present condition of human society. As I was speaking, he said, “Yes, yes. ” I described materialistic society and he agreed with everything I said. I then asked, “What are you doing about all this? ” He had no answer. Like many people, he had no idea that spiritual life meant not only to seek one’s own salvation but to help others.

Such people think that their contribution to humanity is to avoid becoming materialists themselves. They think that by going somewhere and meditating on their own salvation, they will become purified. The Lord’s devotees think differently. They always think about elevating human society, not about their own salvation. Prahlada Maharaja describes this situation clearly. He says that there are so many so-called *sadhus* who go to the forest to perform vows of silence and think of their own liberation, but as for him, he could not stand to see the suffering of people in material consciousness. How could he go back to Godhead and leave behind all the fools and rascals?

Devotees like Prahlada Maharaja have such deep realization because of their profound understanding of devotional service. When a devotee finds shelter in devotional service, he thinks of nothing else. He doesn’t *need* anything else. He experiences real satisfaction. Then he decides he wants to share that with others.

Deep realization in devotional service can only be achieved by the mercy of a Vaisnava. Although many gurus give transcendental instruction to their disciples, real spiritual instructions lead to devotional service. If they don’t, then hearing them is a waste of time: *bhagavad-bhakti-hinasya jatih sastram japas tapah apranasyaiva dehasya mandanam loka-ranjanam.*

Srila Prabhupada quoted the above *sloka* often to emphasize that if a person is devoid of devotional service, even if he chants many Vedic mantras or is expert in studying the scriptures, even if he performs austerities or takes birth in a great family or nation, these things are like ornaments on a dead body. What value does an ornament have when it is placed on a dead body?

Sukadeva Gosvami asks, “Are not the trees living? ” Well, what’s auspicious about such a long life? What about the blacksmith’s bellows—do they not breathe? What value is their breathing? What about the beasts? Do they not enjoy sex life? What is the value of all these forms of enjoyment? Without the opportunity to engage in devotional service, life in this world is useless.

But if we do have that opportunity, we can then become satisfied and begin to think of the welfare of others. This is a devotee’s consciousness: how can he benefit others? Without the association of devotees, it is not possible to benefit human society. The materialists can gather in so many conventions, but they will not be successful in solving life’s problems.

One time Prabhupada was invited to a “Unity of Man” conference in 1975. Many *yogis* and swamis were planning to attend. They thought this conference would help purify the world. Prabhupada argued with the *yogis* who came to invite him. He said, “You simply come together with all your ideas for unity, but there will be no unity, because everyone has a different idea about how it should be achieved. The only basis for unity is to follow Lord Krsna’s instructions as He has given them in *Bhagavad-gita. ”*

“But Swamiji, so many saintly people will be coming. ” They tried to insist that the conference would bring auspiciousness. Still Prabhupada refused to participate in a conference that was not based on seeking unity according to the Supreme Lord’s order. Srila Prabhupada told one of the *yogis,* who was taking notes, “You write down in your notebook: There will not be unity. Bhaktivedanta Swami said there will not be unity. ”

Another time when Prabhupada was here in Mayapur, a *yogini* came to see him. She wanted to invite Prabhupada to participate in an event and came with two of her secretaries. Devotees brought them in to meet Srila Prabhupada and she began to describe how they would benefit the world. Prabhupada listened to them without speaking. The other devotees could see, however, that Prabhupada wasn’t accepting their arguments. Still, he was patient and listened to everything they said. Then he asked, “Are you finished? ” Then Prabhupada said, “If you take some sweet smelling flowers and throw them in the latrine, will that solve the problem? That makes everything good? ” The *yogini* couldn’t believe Prabhupada said that. She thought she was introducing many spiritual solutions, but Prabhupada was comparing her solutions to throwing flowers in a latrine. She was insulted and immediately left.

Prabhupada just laughed. He told the devotees that this is what all these “spiritual” people think—they can treat the symptoms of material society without getting at the root of the problem. He said that all her solutions were material.

Srila Prabhupada always shared with us his deep, strong convictions. He was uncompromising, because he knew the only way to elevate human society was to help people make actual Krsna conscious advancement. He did not think only about elevating the few people who had gathered around him. He was not interested in personal comforts or prestige. Rather, he was broadminded. He had a Vaisnava’s deep vision and willingness to risk everything to serve the Lord through preaching.

It is a risk to actually take responsibility to elevate human society. When devotees take that risk, they please the Lord. When we preach, we will meet resistance. We are uncovering materialistic attachments, and people don’t like to have their attachments uncovered. We should not become disturbed when we meet resistance while spreading Krsna consciousness. Rather, we should expect resistance. Prabhupada assured us it would be there. If the materialists do not fight back when we preach, then there is something wrong with our preaching.

Of course, everyone *can* come to accept us when they accept Lord Caitanya’s pure message, but such a victory will not come cheaply. We should not expect such miracles. As we look to the future of the Krsna consciousness movement, we should face our duty to preach squarely. When we speak Krsna consciousness among ourselves, we should be thinking of the time when we will again go out and speak it to others.

How can we engage others in devotional service? How can we bring them into the devotees’ association so they can hear the transcendental descriptions of the Lord? If we don’t answer these questions by our actions, then we are, like the *Bhagavatam* describes, broken pots. We are not meant to only increase our own knowledge. If we don’t share what we know, our knowledge will leak from us as water leaks from a broken pot. Although we may continue to pour water into the pot, the pot will not be able to hold it. We will only become full ourselves when we share Krsna consciousness with others.

## Vaisnava Qualities

## Why do we sometimes feel as if we have lost our desire to practice devotional service?

**SB** [**3.29.9**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/29.9) **Dnepropetrovsk, June, 1996**

If we lose our Krsna consciousness, it is because we have committed offenses. If at one time we had desired to become Krsna conscious and now find ourselves without that desire, it is only because we have committed offenses. That is why sometimes it seems that when we associate with devotees, we lose our desire to practice spiritual life. If we offend the devotees, our desire to become devotees will disappear. It is not that the devotees make us lose our desire; it is because we have offended devotees. Therefore, we must be very careful not to commit offenses if we do not wish to lose the association of devotees. There is nothing more dangerous in this world than being devoid of the association of devotees.

## How can we distinguish between genuine and false humility?

If we think, “Let me be humble so others can see how humble I am, ” we should know that we are experiencing false humility. It’s simple to understand. If we were truly humble, we wouldn’t care whether someone saw us being humble or not. We would simply always be thinking of accepting everything as the Lord’s mercy because we would have firm faith that the Lord knows everything about us. We would not feel the need to prove anything—including our humility—to anyone. Genuine humility means to understand that we are lower than the straw in the street. From such an understanding, we can become more tolerant than a tree, devoid of all sense of false prestige, and will be able to offer all respects to others.

One symptom of a genuinely humble soul is that he sees all living entities as potential servants of Krsna. Therefore he always tries to fan the spark of their Krsna consciousness. Doing so is an act of respect; a genuinely humble soul respects everyone.

Those who are motivated by false humility will want others to see their humility. When they see someone who can interfere or compete with their apparent humility, they disrespect those persons: “My humility is superior to your humility. Therefore let me push your humility down so that my humility can prevail. ” Such a show is a lie.

## We cannot be successful in preaching if we are not actually devotees

**SB 3.29.18 Krasnodar, June, 1996**

**“A devotee should always try to hear about spiritual matters and should always utilize his time in chanting the holy name of the Lord. His behavior should always be straightforward and simple, and although he is not envious but friendly to everyone, he should avoid the company of persons who are not spiritually advanced. ”** ***(SB***  [**3.29.18**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/29.18)**)**

In this verse, Lord Kapiladeva explains how devotees should behave. Devotees should always use their time to hear about spiritual matters and to chant the holy name. They should be straightforward and simple, nonenvious and friendly to everyone. They should also avoid the company of those who are too materially attached.

In his purport, Srila Prabhupada takes this verse and breaks it into sections according to the characteristics listed. There are many verses in the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* and other Vaisnava literature describing devotional behavior. These verses help us understand how we should act. There are also various guidelines to help motivate us to rise to a higher standard in our behavior. At the same time, we hear frequently that when one becomes a devotee of Krsna, one automatically develops all good qualities. Therefore, we must find the balance between these two understandings. The balance lies in the words “becoming a devotee. ” We must first become an actual devotee; then all good qualities will manifest.

When striving to develop good qualities, we should first understand what we want. The *Bhagavatam* states that if we possess good qualities but do not have devotion to Krsna, our good qualities have no value. Many people in this world are simple, some nonenvious, and many nonduplicitous, but these qualities only have meaning in the context of devotional life. The seventh canto describes that even if we have all the characteristics of a *brahmana,* if we are adverse to devotional service, we are lower than a dog-eater who has dedicated his body, mind and words to Krsna’s service. A devotee can purify everyone by his association, whereas a so-called *brahmana,* striving only for false prestige, cannot even purify himself.

The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* is full of verses emphasizing the importance of becoming a devotee. Lord Kapila’s instruction above assumes that we are engaged in devotional service. A devotee never thinks himself to be advanced. In fact, he doesn’t even consider himself a devotee. A devotee is always striving to become a devotee, because he sees within himself his many shortcomings.

If we get the opportunity to engage in the Lord’s devotional service by the mercy and association of His pure devotees, then we can trust that the pure devotees will also instruct us on how to behave like devotees. The pure devotees will instruct us both through their words and their examples. That is the value of devotee association: if we have the opportunity to associate with a devotee, not only do we learn how we should behave, but we also get to see how a real devotee behaves.

This is a powerful method of preaching Krsna consciousness. If a preacher’s behavior is consistent with his words, then his words will affect others. We know that if a pure devotee preaches the glories of pure devotional service, his heart is free from envy and he is friendly to all living beings. He is thus always able to be absorbed in hearing spiritual subject matters and uses all of his time to glorify the Lord’s holy name and pastimes. When a devotee possesses such purity, his words will affect those who hear him and they too will strive for purity.

Often devotees ask how they can preach convincingly about devotional service when we ourselves are not Krsna conscious. We often feel inadequate because we are not experiencing devotional happiness ourselves. Or perhaps we see that something in our behavior or the behavior of others in the temple is inconsistent with how a devotee should act. How can we preach if we are not ourselves setting an example?

Example is more powerful than precept. It is by example that we convince others how to behave. Srila Prabhupada said that our example would prove that we were genuinely experiencing Krsna consciousness. It is that genuineness that attracts others and not our words. If we are truly attracted to Krsna, we will be able to attract others. Because Krsna is all-attractive, those who are attracted to Him are also attractive. A devotee keeps his heart pure. A devotee is always thinking of Krsna and is therefore thinking about how he can serve Him. To serve Krsna, he thinks of others’ welfare. That is why Krsna says in the *Bhagavad-gita’s* twelfth chapter that it is the characteristic of His devotees to be nonenvious: “One who is not envious but is a kind friend to all living entities, who does not think himself a proprietor and is free from false ego, who is equal in both happiness and distress, who is tolerant, always satisfied, selfcontrolled, and engaged in devotional service with determination, his mind and intelligence fixed on Me—such a devotee of Mine is very dear to Me. ” (Bg. [12.13-14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/12.13))

A devotee’s example is a very, very powerful principle. Those who see a devotee’s example have the potential of becoming attracted to Krsna. If we want to become effective preachers in Krsna consciousness, we must be empowered by the Lord’s potency. Otherwise, we cannot change others’ hearts. It’s not that a devotee thinks he is changing others’ hearts; a devotee knows that only Krsna can change their hearts because He is situated in the heart of everyone. A devotee knows he himself is not situated in everyone’s heart. Therefore, a preacher must see that the Supreme Lord is sitting in the heart of every living being and that that’s what gives a living entity the potential to turn to Krsna. In fact, this is one of the points most emphasized by Lord Kapiladeva in His teachings: “I am present in every living entity as the Supersoul. ” If someone neglects or disregards the Supersoul and engages himself only in the worship of the deity, he is simply imitating. One who worships Krsna’s deity in the temple but does not recognize the Lord as Paramatma within the hearts of the living beings must be ignorant. Such a person is compared to one who offers oblations into ashes.

A preacher doesn’t see himself as convincing others to become Krsna conscious. Rather, he prays to the Lord within the heart of others and begs Him to please enter into his words so that he may encourage that living entity to turn to Him. That is how a preacher preaches. The Supersoul in the heart fulfills the dictations of every living entity. If the living entity wants to forget Him, the Supersoul fulfills that desire. If the living entity wants to turn to Krsna, however, then Krsna will help the living entity from within the heart.

What causes the living entity to turn to Krsna? The first cause is when he hears the message of Godhead from a devotee. This causes him to turn his attention to the Lord. A preacher understands that the message he is delivering is not his own, but the Lord’s. A devotee who preaches with this understanding invokes Krsna’s presence in his words. Krsna then helps the living entity to hear His message. Again, it is important for a devotee preacher to set a good example so that not only can he invoke the Lord in his words, but that the living entity has something to emulate.

Invoking the Lord’s presence while preaching is crucial to the success of preaching. The *Caitanya-caritamrta* states:

*kali-kalera dharma—krsna-nama-sankirtana*  
*krsna-sakti vina nahe tara pravartana*

**“The fundamental religious system in the Age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Krsna. Unless empowered by Krsna, one cannot propagate the** ***sankirtana*** **movement. ” (Cc.** ***Antya*** [**7.11**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/cc-antya/7.11)**)**

Preaching Krsna consciousness is not ordinary work in the material world. It is the work of the spiritual world and is an activity of Krsna’s internal potency. The Lord’s holy name has been imported from the spiritual world. When a devotee distributes the holy name, he is distributing a piece of the spiritual world. The holy name is not an ordinary sound vibration. It comes from beyond this world. Those who are empowered by the Lord are those who have attracted the Lord. Krsna then manifests Himself in their chanting of His name due to their pure devotion. Bhaktivinoda Thakura says that one who chants the holy name without devotion is mouthing only alphabets; the holy name does not fully manifest in such chanting.

Krsna appears in His name when He is called to be present by a devotee’s pure chanting. A pure devotee gives his disciple *harinama* and tells him to chant and perfect his life. In other words, Krsna becomes manifest by the potency of the devotee to attract Him. Again, one must be empowered to preach Krsna consciousness. We cannot attract others to Krsna consciousness by an external show of devotion. Even if we try to behave like devotees, our imitation will not have the same effect. We have to actually become devotees.

Devotional service is not achieved by imitation. Srila Prabhupada warns in *Caitanya-caritamrta* that even though we may wear Vaisnava dress, we may not really be Vaisnavas. We may imitate preachers, but we cannot be successful in preaching if we are not actually devotees. Those who become devoted, with heart and mind drawn to Krsna’s lotus feet, find it possible to become absorbed in hearing about Krsna. When they become absorbed, Krsna cleanses their hearts. Even if we imitate absorption, Krsna will cleanse our hearts by the mercy of the good example we are following. This is the power of devotee association. The pure devotees always instruct us how to behave. Even if we are not fully devoted ourselves, if we associate with pure devotees, our hearts too will become gradually clean.

Associating with Krsna means associating with the holy name and with *krsna-katha:*

*satam prasangan mama virya-samvido*  
*bhavanti hrt-karna-rasayanah kathah*  
*taj-josanad asv apavarga-vartmani*  
*sraddha ratir bhaktir anukramisyati*

**“In the association of pure devotees, discussion of the pastimes and activities of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is very pleasing and satisfying to the ear and heart. By cultivating such knowledge one gradually becomes advanced on the path of liberation, and thereafter he is freed, and his attraction becomes fixed. Then real devotion and devotional service begin. ” (SB** [**3.25.25**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/25.25)**)**

## “Krsna wants to show everyone: ‘Just see the qualities of My devotee! I want the whole world to see the qualities of My devotee. My devotee doesn’t want honor! ’ ”

**Unknown date, verse, town, country**

Devotees are always completely dependent upon Krsna’s desire. They never try to become independent of the Lord’s desire, and therefore, due to their surrender, Krsna is anxious to protect them. Thus, it is described in many places that devotees are meek and humble. Humility is, of course, an important characteristic of the devotees.

Srila Prabhupada defines humility in the *Bhagavad-gita.* There he says that humility means that we are not interested in personal prestige. In the material world, people are always seeking to be honored. Those who are gentle and humble never try to be honored. This is not what motivates them to act. The materialists are motivated to act by the desire to be recognized by others. They believe that such recognition will satisfy them. Devotees don’t want such recognition. They don’t perform activities simply to become recognized. Rather, they are anxious to satisfy Krsna and to be recognized by Him.

But what qualities does Krsna recognize? Krsna does not recognize the show—the grand performance. He recognizes the heart, and within the heart, our devotion. Krsna recognizes meekness and humility and He becomes attracted to it. We should never think that Krsna is not looking for these qualities. He is present in the heart and sees everything. “I am seated in everyone’s heart, and from Me come remembrance, knowledge and forgetfulness. By all the *Vedas,* I am to be known. Indeed, I am the compiler of Vedanta, and I am the knower of the *Vedas. " (Bg.* [15.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/15.15))

Krsna sees everything. He knows every desire that exists within us even better than we know them ourselves. He knows exactly what we are thinking and why. He knows exactly what motivates us. It is all perfectly clear to Him. The *Bhagavad-gita* explains, “O Arjuna, as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, I know everything that has happened in the past, all that is happening in the present, and all things that are yet to come. I also know all living entities; but Me no one knows. ”

When Krsna sees that we are motivated by the desire for prestige, He gives us what we want. But we won’t get Him because we didn’t want Him. We will be unable to capture Krsna’s attention by our desire for recognition. When He sees that we are not motivated by a desire for personal recognition but by devotion, then we gain His attention.

What does Krsna do for such a purified devotee? He glorifies him. The devotee does not want glorification, but glorifying the devotees gives Krsna pleasure. He wants to show the world, “Just see the qualities of My devotees! I want the whole world to see their qualities. My devotees don’t want recognition. Rather, they want pure love! ” Therefore, Krsna arranges for the world to recognize them.

While the world recognizes the true qualities of the devotees, the devotees remain meek and humble. This is a principle of surrender. Surrender simply means to always depend upon Krsna; surrender is not artificial. Surrender is not something we can talk about so much. What actually has meaning is when we exhibit the symptoms of the surrendered devotee. This is why Lord Caitanya describes the qualities of the surrendered for our benefit. He speaks about surrender without reservation. One who is always absorbed in loving devotional service to Krsna has achieved the ultimate platform of surrender. We should look for those symptoms in the devotees, serve them, and we will then have the hope that someday we can be fully surrendered ourselves.

## Remain humble and become dependent upon Krsna

**SB 4.30.12-13**

**“You will have a nice son, who will be in no way inferior to Lord Brahma. Consequently, he will be very famous all over the universe, and the sons and grandsons generated by him will fill the three worlds. O sons of King Pracinabarhisat, the heavenly society girl named Pramloca kept the lotus-eyed daughter of Kandu in the care of the forest trees. Then she went back to the heavenly planet. This daughter was born by the coupling of the Apsara named Pramloca with the sage Kandu. ”** ***(SB*** [**4.30.12-13**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/30.12)**)**

These verses describe what happened when the sage Kandu, although renowned for his austerities, became the victim of King Indra’s envy. There are many instances when a sage underwent severe austerities to acquire material power, and Indra became envious.

Of course, we understand that performing austerities for material gain is not the purpose of austerity, but the *Bhagavatam* contains a number of examples of sages who practiced austerity in order to achieve a material goal. The Pracetas themselves are an example. They underwent austerities on their father’s order so that they could create progeny.

Kandu was performing austerities to acquire material power. Whatever austerities are performed, of course, even for material gain, are successful only by the Lord’s grace. Although materialistic penance is considered less intelligent, the Lord still provides the result of one’s austerities. Hiranyakasipu is an example of this. He performed austerities, worshipping the demigods, for a material boon, and although his boon was so destructive to the living entities in the material world, by Krsna’s grace it was fulfilled.

King Indra, the king of the heavenly planets, is attached to his position of great opulence, power, and independence. Therefore he was observing Kandu. He knew that he had acquired his post by dint of his pious activities and that Kandu was performing austerities that would give him the same kind of pious credits. King Indra demonstrated our tendency to become fearful of potential intruders; he did not want anyone to become more opulent than him. Gripped by a similar fear, he stole the one hundredth horse which Maharaja Prthu was about to offer in order to prevent him from doing the same number of sacrifices that Indra himself had performed. In Maharaja Prthu’s case, although he made an attempt to recapture the horse, Lord Brahma came and asked him to be satisfied with what he had already done. Lord Brahma said, “My dear King, do not be agitated and anxious because your sacrifices have not been properly executed due to providential impediments. Kindly take my words with great respect. We should always remember that if something happens by providential arrangement, we should not be very sorry. The more we try to rectify such reversals, the more we enter into the darkest region of materialistic thought. ”

Srila Prabhupada describes that because the demigods are ordinary *jivas* who are in responsible posts for the management of the universe, they are not pure in their motivation. They are devotees, but they are not necessarily pure. King Indra has often revealed his tendency to make grave errors, even with Krsna Himself. The difference between someone in Indra’s position and an ordinary conditioned soul is that because he is a devotee, he willingly subjects himself to Krsna’s chastisement and accepts it as mercy. He stands corrected when there is a discrepancy in his behavior.

This was true when King Indra offended his spiritual master, Brhaspati, by not offering him proper respect. Indra repented his error after Brhaspati reprimanded him by disappearing and not being available to receive his apologies. Due to his offense, Indra temporarily lost his opulence and was defeated by the demons.

It is difficult to be in an opulent position: opulence leads to false pride. Pride creates danger for us. Therefore, sometimes devotees find themselves impoverished because it enables them to remain humble and to become dependent upon Krsna. When we are surrounded by opulence, we think we have power, control, so what need do we have to depend on God? We think ourselves self-sufficient. Then we think we do not want to see anyone superior to ourselves. This mentality is, of course, one of the characteristics of demons.

We entered the material world because we became envious of Krsna. Envy prevails in this world. When we speak of purification in this material world, we mean purification from the envy of wanting to usurp God’s position as well as to keep everyone around us subordinate. As Srila Prabhupada explains in his purport to *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (4. 30. 13), “The whole material world is filled with such envy that everyone becomes afraid of his neighbors. Every businessman is afraid of his associates because this material world is the field of activities for all kinds of envious people who have come here to compete with the opulence of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. ”

When we are envious, we try to imitate God. We do not realize that whatever position we achieve comes only by God’s grace. Instead we think our status comes by dint of our own abilities, and we think that we can therefore surpass everyone—that there is no one better or more intelligent than ourselves. We have to break that tendency if we wish to become Krsna conscious. Krsna very kindly and mercifully breaks the pride of His devotees, because pride and envy stop us from advancing in Krsna consciousness. Srila Prabhupada explains that in a society of Vaisnavas, the devotees are genuinely happy to see others receiving Krsna’s mercy.

Living in the association of devotees is an ideal opportunity for us to overcome our envious mentality. It forces us to learn tolerance. We have to learn to overlook others’ shortcomings. We have to learn to improve our own character and to concentrate more on our own humility than the lack of humility in others. We also have to overcome the tendency to see faults in others rather than confront faults in ourselves. The desire to find fault is out of envy. If we dwell on it and become focused on the shortcomings of our immediate associates, then we will never be able to see our own shortcomings and we will not be able to improve. Better we seek out the good qualities of devotees and overlook the faults. Srila Prabhupada asked us what benefit we would derive from criticizing someone for having dirty hands when he is at the sink washing.

Our aspiration in Krsna consciousness is to live in a society of devotees and to render service together in that society. By serving together, we will learn how to overcome mundane competitiveness and become happy to see one another beneficed by the Lord’s mercy. A Vaisnava’s real mentality is to push others forward in Krsna consciousness. By taking the humble position ourselves, we will find ourselves recognized by Krsna without any extraneous effort on our parts.

## How can we free ourselves from false ego?

**Bg. 3.27 Vilnius, March 25, 1991**

*prakrteh kriyamanani*  
*gunaih karmani sarvasah*  
*ahankara-vimudhatma*  
*kartaham iti manyate*

**“The spirit soul bewildered by the influence of false ego thinks himself the doer of activities that are in actuality carried out by the three modes of material nature. ” (Bg.** [**3.27**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.27)**)**

We cannot defeat false ego on our own, but Krsna can enable us to become free of it. He can empower us so that we will be released from our false ego.

The more we act consistent with our constitutional nature to serve Krsna, the more we will become free of false ego. Lord Kapiladeva states that devotional service is so powerful that it can burn up everything covering the spirit soul. Just as fire can burn an unlimited quantity of fuel, devotional service can burn up the false ego. Therefore Lord Caitanya states, *ceto-darpana-marjanam bhavamaha-davagni-nirvapanam:* “All glories to the Sri Krsna *sankirtana,* which cleanses the heart of all the dust accumulated for years and extinguishes the fire of conditional life, of repeated birth and death. This *sankirtana* movement is the prime benediction for humanity at large because it spreads the rays of the benediction moon. It is the life of all transcendental knowledge. It increases the ocean of transcendental bliss, and it enables us to fully taste the nectar for which we are always anxious. ”

Participating in *sankirtana* is the method by which we can free ourselves from false ego and cleanse ourselves from all material conceptions. Participating in *sankirtana* requires that we act as an instrument for the Lord’s service. Although we may not be free from all attachment, one who surrenders to the process gradually experiences detachment from everything that covers him, of which false ego is the most difficult to overcome. As explained in the *Bhagavatam,* the other material coverings are like the coverings of a coconut. Earth, water, fire, air, ether, mind, intelligence and false ego are the coverings. Each layer is ten times thicker than the previous one. The thickest layer is false ego. It can only be overcome by Krsna’s grace.

## How can we know which devotees to trust in revealing our mind in confidence?

**Bhaktyaloka by Bhaktivinoda Thakura Boston, April 1997**

To be able to reveal our mind in confidence means we have to trust the confidant, but trust is something that takes time to develop. Trust is not something that can be legislated or imposed on us by someone’s position. The best way to learn to trust is to observe him or her. See how the person speaks and behaves. As Sanatana Gosvami told Haridasa Thakura, “Some people preach, but do not behave nicely, and some people behave nicely, but do not preach. You, Haridasa, can become the spiritual master of the whole world, because you both preach and behave perfectly. ”

If we want to cultivate trust, we have to both observe people’s behavior and hear from them. Is their character consistent with their speech? If they speak well but cannot control their senses, how can you trust them? If we speak in confidence and notice that they speak about others in our company, we can know that they will also discuss our confidences when we are not present. Behavior must be consistent with speech. This is the criterion we use to develop trust in the association of devotees. We want to trust those whose behavior is consistent with their instructions.

## How can we remain helpless yet enthusiastic and determined?

**Nectar of Sankirtana**

‘Helpless’ doesn’t mean unenthusiastic or not determined. Actually, it means the opposite. We know we do not achieve results in Krsna consciousness by our own efforts, and in that sense we are helpless and dependent. There is no material formula that can make us successful in our spiritual endeavors. We have to cross beyond the boundary that makes us think that material formulas bring spiritual results. Devotional service is a spiritual activity. When we practice devotional service, we are responding to the heart’s desire to accomplish something for Krsna. We feel helpless. We *want* to feel helpless and dependent on Krsna. How will thinking about a material formula help us achieve the devotional state? We cannot feel spiritually helpless while taking shelter of matter.

Therefore a devotee recognizes the truth: the result is up to Krsna. We can achieve nothing by our own efforts, and certainly not by making any material arrangements. We may have the best mantra, the best technique, the best place for preaching, but Lord Caitanya will still force us to realize our dependence on Krsna.

So we become dependent, but not unenthusiastic or undetermined. We have to be very determined and enthusiastic to invoke the Lord’s mercy. We start by knowing that nothing material will give a spiritual result. Ultimately, the Lord in the heart will answer our prayers. Srila Prabhupada’s purport to *Srimad-Bhagavatam* ([3.9.29](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/9.29)) defines real success as follows:

“Any person authorized by either the Lord or by His bona fide representative is already blessed, as is the work entrusted to him. Of course, the person entrusted with such a responsibility should always be aware of his incapability and must always look for the mercy of the Lord for the successful execution of his duty. One should not be puffed up because he is entrusted with certain executive work. Fortunate is he who is so entrusted, and if he is always fixed in the sense of being subordinate to the will of the Supreme, he is sure to come out successful in the discharge of his work. Arjuna was entrusted with the work of fighting on the Battlefield of Kuruksetra, and before he was so entrusted, the Lord had already arranged for his victory. But Arjuna was always conscious of his position as subordinate to the Lord, and thus he accepted Him as the supreme guide in his responsibility. Anyone who takes pride in doing responsible work but does not give credit to the Supreme Lord is certainly falsely proud and cannot execute anything nicely. Brahma and persons in the line of his disciplic succession who follow in his footsteps are always successful in the discharge of loving transcendental service to the Supreme Lord. ”

Real success, the real result we are seeking, is to increase our attachment to Krsna*—to satisfy the Lord.* The Lord is expert at placing His devotees in helpless, dependent conditions. Sometimes devotees become artificially dependent on their material circumstances, comfortable and complacent, but Krsna is expert at pulling the rug out from underneath us. As soon as we think we are on solid ground with our position, assets, and other comforts, Krsna pulls the rug out from underneath us.

Krsna wants us to feel helpless. If we are too attached to external arrangements, then when Krsna pulls the rug out, we will clamor for everything we just lost. We will meditate on the things we thought were protecting us—our material assets and prestige. But those things weren’t protecting us. Devotees are meant to plant their feet firmly in the realization that there is no other shelter but Krsna. Everything else is temporary. If we want real shelter or solid ground, then we must turn to full dependence on Krsna. Krsna promises to protect the surrendered soul. What could be more solid than Krsna’s promise? Best if we feel helpless and embrace the opportunity to surrender. Take up the challenge to increase your surrender. A devotee is always eager to take up such a challenge because it pleases Krsna. We should always be prepared to accept risks to please Krsna.

## Serve the Vaisnavas!

**SB 4.21.40 Boston, December 1989**

**“By regular service to the** ***brahmanas*** **and Vaisnavas, one can clear the dirt from his heart and thus enjoy supreme peace and liberation from material attachment and be satisfied. In this world there is no fruitive activity superior to serving the** ***brahmana*** **class, for this can bring pleasure to the demigods, for whom the many sacrifices are recommended. ” (SB** [**4.21.40**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/21.40)**)**

In this verse, Prthu Maharaja is instructing his citizens. The *brahmanas* and Vaisnavas are the most integral members of human society. They are essential for society’s elevation toward a spiritual goal. If we serve *brahmanas* and Vaisnavas, we will become peaceful and liberated from material attachment. This is the only way we will become satisfied.

In text 36, Maharaja Prthu says:

“The Supreme Personality of Godhead is the master and enjoyer of the results of all sacrifices, and He is the supreme spiritual master as well. All of you citizens on the surface of the globe who have a relationship with me and are worshiping Him by dint of your occupational duties are bestowing your mercy upon me. Therefore, O my citizens, I thank you. ”

Almost all the citizens were engaged in devotional service through the performance of their occupational duties. Therefore, regardless of their occupation—*brahmana*, *ksatriya*, *Vaisya* or *sudra*— they were all worshiping the Lord, and Maharaja Prthu thanked them for this. Everyone in society was considered a Vaisnava, a worshiper of Visnu. Now Prthu Maharaja is encouraging the citizens to purify themselves by serving the *brahmanas* and Vaisnavas. (Of course, Srila Prabhupada warns in his purport that in Kali-yuga many people will pose as *brahmanas* based on heredity, but they are unqualified. We should serve those who possess the proper qualification. )

Srila Prabhupada writes extensively in this section about the value of Vaisnava association. He also mentions that the spiritual master is the best *brahmana.* Therefore, it is important for us to take shelter of a bona fide spiritual master. We should also serve other Vaisnava associates of the spiritual master. Prabhupada’s instructions are important to note; we should learn from him how to recognize such persons. In previous ages, especially in Maharaja Prthu’s time, almost everyone was rendering service to Krsna through his or her occupation. The atmosphere was pure. Such a spiritual atmosphere was created by society’s leaders. If the leaders of society are strict in maintaining a proper spiritual environment, all the members of society will be able to work cooperatively and become purified from material attachment. Part of creating that atmosphere is encouraging people to serve *brahmanas* and Vaisnavas.

Srila Prabhupada was the kind of leader who created a purified spiritual environment. It is interesting to note that in his purport, Srila Prabhupada instructs us not only to take shelter of the spiritual master, but to serve his associates. The spiritual master’s associates, those who serve the guru in devotional service, are also worthy of our worship. Therefore, a Vaisnava society is the most auspicious society of which to take shelter. By the mercy of the devotees we can advance in spiritual life. We gain that mercy by rendering them service.

The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* states:

**“By regular attendance in classes on the** ***Bhagavatam*** **and by rendering of service to the pure devotee, all that is troublesome to the heart is almost completely destroyed, and loving service unto the Personality of Godhead, who is praised with transcendental songs, is established as an irrevocable fact. ” (SB** [**1.2.18**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.18)**)**

We cannot hear without the mercy of the devotees. Again, we gain their mercy by serving them. We must fix our consciousness upon serving the Lord’s devotees. Without serving the devotees, we will have no opportunity to approach the Lord.

Yesterday I heard Srila Prabhupada speaking on tape. He said that Vedic society means we must overcome and conquer the next birth. Ultimately, we are meant to achieve liberation from birth and death, and to achieve that goal we must become detached. Ultimately, that means returning to the spiritual world.

Spiritual advancement must be the ultimate goal of society. It is not enough to satisfy our economic needs. By serving devotees, we will conquer birth and death. We must concentrate on hearing and serving in their proper balance. This is what Prabhupada tried to teach us.

## Is there an educational process for attaining the madhyama platform?

**Ista-gosthi**

**Vinnitza, Summer 1995**

Our educational process consists of *nasta-prayesv abhadresu, nityam bhagavata-sevaya*, *bhagavaty uttama-sloke*, *bhaktir bhavati naisthiki:* “By regular attendance in classes on the *Bhagavatam* and by rendering of service to the pure devotee, all that is troublesome to the heart is almost completely destroyed, and loving service unto the Personality of Godhead, who is praised with transcendental songs, is established as an irrevocable fact. ” (SB [1.2.18](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.18))

By hearing the *Bhagavatam* regularly, we become devotees. Academic knowledge cannot deliver us to the *madhyama* platform. One who studies many books may still remain on the *kanistha* platform.

Education is something that is carried out under guidance. That is real education. We learn the basic principles of Krsna consciousness under the guidance of advanced devotees. When we place ourselves under their guidance, we can learn more than Krsna conscious academics. The *Bhagavatam* states, *vasudeve bhagavati, bhakti-yogah prayojitah, janayaty asu vairagyam, jnanam ca yad ahaitukam:* “By rendering devotional service unto the Personality of Godhead, Sri Krsna, one immediately acquires causeless knowledge and detachment from the world. ” (SB [1.2.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.7))

## How can we overcome faultfinding?

**SB** [**4.4.12**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-4/4.12) **Novgorod, September 1995**

**“Twice-born Daksa, a man like you can simply find fault in the qualities of others. Lord Siva, however, not only finds no faults with others’ qualities, but if someone has a little good quality, he magnifies it greatly. Unfortunately, you have found fault with such a great soul. ”**

Daksa decided to perform a sacrifice and he invited the demigods. Daksa entered the arena after Lord Brahma and Lord Siva had arrived, however, Lord Siva did not stand to offer respects. This made Daksa angry, and he immediately began to criticize Lord Siva unjustly. He began by calling Lord Siva filthy, because he was always surrounded by degraded, low-class people and found in the crematorium. After insulting him, he cursed him that he would not receive his rightful share of the sacrifice.

Lord Siva was not disturbed by Daksa’s words. A Vaisnava is in an exalted position. There are many things we can learn from this pastime, the first of which is how Lord Siva showed tolerance even when he was unjustly cursed. Rather than become disturbed, Lord Siva maintained his meditation on Krsna’s lotus feet.

The scriptures describe that Vaisnavas are forbearing. “The devotees of the Lord are so forbearing that even though they are defamed, cheated, cursed, disturbed, neglected or even killed, they are never inclined to avenge themselves. ” (SB [1.18.48](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/18.48)) Usually aggression requires a response, but devotees do not feel the need for vengeance. This is because they see that nothing happens without Krsna’s sanction. They know that if they are being cursed or punished in some way, it is because of an offense they themselves must have committed in some past life. They accept the suffering as Krsna’s punishment. A Vaisnava does not blame the agent delivering Krsna’s punishment. There is no question of retribution.

Pariksit Maharaja is another example of forbearance. Because he insulted Samika Rsi, the *rsi's* son, Srngi, cursed him to die in seven days. When Samika Rsi returned to external consciousness from his meditative state and heard what his son had done, he was very disturbed. How could his son curse such a saintly king? He knew that Maharaja Pariksit had no bad qualities and that there was a good reason for whatever he had done. He began to think about how his son could become free from the reaction for cursing the king. If only Pariksit Maharaja would countercurse his son, then his son would be relieved of sin. However, Samlka Rsi knew that Pariksit Maharaja would never utter a countercurse. Maharaja Pariksit was a great devotee of Krsna, and therefore he would be forbearing even when he was in such danger. Srngi, he knew, had no chance of redemption. Samlka Rsi simply prayed to Krsna to somehow forgive his son.

And Samlka Rsi was right; Pariksit Maharaja did not retaliate, although the curse was a heavy punishment for the light offense he had committed. Maharaja Pariksit didn’t think, “Who is this boy to curse me? ” Rather, he saw it as Krsna’s arrangement, renounced his worldly kingdom, and went to the bank of the Ganges to fast. There he met his spiritual master, Sukadeva Gosvami, and asked, “What is the duty of a man who is about to die? ” Pariksit Maharaja accepted the curse as Krsna’s mercy. He did not try to counteract it in any way. He didn’t ask his spiritual master, “What can I do to avenge myself against this Srngi? ” He simply accepted the curse as Krsna’s arrangement.

This is the mentality of a Vaisnava. A Vaisnava is not disturbed by aggressors because he is able to see Krsna in everything. “For one who sees Me everywhere and sees everything in Me, I am never lost, nor is he ever lost to Me. ”

But a Vaisnava does not tolerate offenses against another Vaisnava. Although Lord Siva was not disturbed by Daksa’s curse, his followers were quite disturbed. When Nandisvara heard about the curse, he became angry that so many so-called *brahmanas* were listening to Daksa’s speech without protest. What kind of *brahmanas* were these? How could they tolerate the cursing of such an exalted Vaisnava? Nandisvara countercursed Daksa and all the attendant *brahmanas.* In this way the sacrifice was devastated and Lord Siva simply got up and left. Lord Siva realized that Daksa’s behavior was improper, but still he was not disturbed by it. One who possesses such spiritual vision does not see faults in others but only Krsna working through everyone and everything. Such a Vaisnava also tries to help others and to bring them to a higher understanding. In that case, it is sometimes necessary for him to point out the mistakes others have made, not out of enmity, but out of compassion, as Lord Siva will later do.

Those who find fault out of envy are victims of excessive pride. Such persons cannot see Krsna in the hearts of others. They cannot see the potential spark of Krsna consciousness that exists in all living entities. It is helpful to the envious to find faults in others; it gives him the opportunity to avoid the faults in himself. Those who are envious cannot see the good. Instead of magnifying whatever good is present, the proud magnify others’ faults.

Ramacandra Puri is an example of a professional faultfinder. That was how he maintained himself. He would go uninvited to someone’s home, accept the welcome he would inevitably receive, eat and drink, and then criticize his host. In Vedic culture the uninvited guest is to be welcomed. Such a guest will be offered a place to sit, something to drink and eat, and kind words. Even if the guest is an enemy, he will be treated as a friend. Ramacandra Puri took advantage of this culture to have his meals supplied, but out of envy found fault with those who served him.

When Ramacandra Puri did not go to householders’ homes for his meals, he would go to wherever the *sannyasis* were gathering to eat. He would sit down and accept a meal with them. He would eat very little, but serve the others more and more. Although the other *sannyasis* could not possibly eat the quantity of food he served them, he would continue to serve them and then insist that they ate what was on their plates. When they obeyed him, he would criticize them for overeating. “How can *sannyasis* maintain their vows while eating that much food? ”

Once he tried to find fault with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Caitanya Mahaprabhu, who has no faults. He entered into the room where Lord Caitanya was sitting and saw ants on the floor. He said, “Look! There are ants on the floor. I can understand that You have been eating sweetmeats all night. These ants have come to collect the crumbs You have dropped. What kind of *sannyasi* are You? ” Ramacandra Puri was so proud that he found faults where none existed. We should know that there are no faults in elevated Vaisnavas.

Another example of an envious man is Ramacandra Khan, who was jealous of Haridasa Thakura’s influence. Haridasa Thakura was renounced and full of good qualities. He was always absorbed in hearing and chanting the Lord’s holy names. Everyone thought of him as exalted. Ramacandra Khan, however, was envious. Although Haridasa Thakura had no faults, he tried to create a fault. He imagined that Haridasa Thakura’s heart harbored lust. He couldn’t see it, but since he had lust in his own heart, he imagined that all others shared his bad qualities. This is called *atmavan manyate jagat:* we think that everyone is like ourselves. He thought he would be able to expose it by sending a prostitute to seduce Haridasa Thakura in the middle of the night.

Ramacandra Khan selected the most attractive prostitute to send to Haridasa Thakura. He hoped that she would be successful and that he would then be able to show all those who worshiped Haridasa Thakura that they were mistaken and that Haridasa Thakura was no different than themselves.

What was the result of Ramacandra Khan’s plan? Haridasa Thakura’s glories were expanded even further because he converted the prostitute into a Vaisnavi. After listening to Haridasa Thakura chant Hare Krsna for three days in his cave, even while petitioning him and making suggestive gestures, her heart became purified and she became a devotee. She would say to Haridasa, “O my great Haridasa, I have come here to unite with you. Please fulfill my desire. ” Haridasa Thakura would reply, “I will certainly satisfy your desire, but for now, sit down and wait. As soon as I have finished my prescribed number of rounds, I will satisfy you. ” But Haridasa Thakura never finished chanting. Each morning the prostitute would go home, and each evening she would return. At the end of the third night, she realized her sinful nature and renounced it. Then she asked Haridasa Thakura what she could do to surrender to Krsna. Haridasa Thakura instructed her to give her possessions to the *brahmanas.* She then returned to the cave and began to chant three hundred thousand names daily. Then Haridasa Thakura left. Soon word spread that a Vaisnavi, a woman manifesting wonderful devotional qualities, was living in the cave where Haridasa Thakura used to live. People began to take her *darsana.* Simply by seeing her exalted qualities, people realized that this Vaisnavi had received Haridasa Thakura’s mercy and they began to glorify him all the more.

The world contains such personalities as Ramacandra Khan, Ramacandra Puri, and Daksa—persons who spend their lives trying to magnify others’ faults. But the Lord’s devotees are powerful. We see that in the examples of Haridasa Thakura, Maharaja Pariksit and Lord Siva. They are also kind. Haridasa Thakura did not tell the prostitute, “Get out of my sight, you degraded woman! ” Rather, he delivered her. In fact, he told her later that he understood why she had come to him from the beginning, and that he had only stayed in his cave to deliver her. Haridasa Thakura allowed her to stay because he could see her innate goodness. He didn’t waste time pointing out her faults but saw her potential to become Krsna’s servant. He knew that Krsna was in her heart and that if she heard the holy name vibrated—and if he prayed for her—she would be able to surrender her life to the Supreme Lord.

This is how a Vaisnava thinks. Vaisnavas desire only to deliver others. They desire this so intensely that when they preach, they pray to Krsna to help the conditioned souls. They beg Krsna to allow them to speak in a way that the conditioned souls can hear them. “O Krsna, You are in their heart. Somehow if they can hear Your message and their desire to approach You be awakened, then You can show them how to come to You. ” A Vaisnava’s desire to save others is intense.

Because of this desire, a Vaisnava is happy to see others advancing in Krsna consciousness and developing all good qualities. A Vaisnava’s desire is so pure that his desire can take precedence over the desire of the living entities who want to remain in the material world. Srila Prabhupada gave the example of a thief who prayed to Krsna to be allowed to steal a particular person’s possessions. That person was also petitioning Krsna to protect his possessions. What should the Lord do? How can He satisfy both desires?

The conditioned soul is so attached to enjoying the material world that Krsna fulfills his desire. He allows him to stay in the material world for millions of births. But a Vaisnava prays to Krsna to save the conditioned souls. To whom will Krsna be more inclined? Krsna is inclined toward His devotees—so much so that even though the conditioned soul thinks he wants to remain in the material world, Krsna listens to the Vaisnava’s prayer and responds by opening the nondevotee’s heart.

Therefore, a Vaisnava never thinks he is delivering anyone. Rather, he simply prays to speak in a way that allows the living entity to hear. Krsna responds to the Vaisnava’s pure prayers and awakens spiritual interest in the nondevotees’ hearts just to please the Vaisnavas. Even a moment’s association with a Vaisnava can deliver Krsna’s mercy to a conditioned soul. Thus Vaisnavas are merciful toward others. The *Bhagavatam* states:

“The symptoms of a *sadhu* are that he is tolerant, merciful and friendly to all living entities. He has no enemies, he is peaceful, he abides by the scriptures, and all his characteristics are sublime. ” (SB [3.5.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/5.21))

What does it mean that a Vaisnava is friendly to all living entities? It means he does not see anyone as his enemy. Prahlada Maharaja taught this. Even though his father tried to kill him in so many ways, Prahlada remained his well-wisher. Haridasa Thakura did not see enemies either. When the prison guards tried to beat him to death, he felt compassion for them because they were in so much anxiety. They had been ordered to beat Haridasa Thakura to death, and if they did not succeed, they would be killed. Haridasa Thakura decided out of compassion to give up the external symptoms of life. But he didn’t die. He had simply entered within himself to chant the holy name. Still, he showed his friendship to those who were trying to kill him by making them think they had succeeded. This is the symptom of a *sadhu.*

We too should develop such qualities. We must develop the ability to see Krsna in others if we wish to be preachers. It is our duty as preachers to elevate human society. Srila Prabhupada created the Krsna consciousness movement to elevate people to spiritual consciousness. A Vaisnava elevates others by being merciful, tolerant, and compassionate, and by seeking out their good qualities and encouraging them to use those qualities in Krsna’s service.

We should also be careful how we interpret this for ourselves. We should not say that others should always seek out *our* good qualities. If someone is in a position to point out our faults, we should submit. Vaisnavas see themselves as fallen. They think they have no good qualities. If someone finds good qualities in them, they think it’s only because of their spiritual master’s mercy. Lord Caitanya instructs, “One should chant the holy name of the Lord in a humble state of mind, thinking oneself lower than the straw in the street; one should be more tolerant than a tree, devoid of all sense of false prestige, and should be ready to offer all respect to others. In such a state of mind one can chant the holy name of the Lord constantly. ” (*Siksastaka*, 3)

Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami describes that we should wear this verse of *Siksastaka* on a string around our neck so that we never forget it. A tree may be dry, but it will never ask for water. That is a tree’s tolerance. A Vaisnava is meant to be more tolerant than a tree.

How does a Vaisnava tolerate? He sees Krsna in everything. If Krsna is present everywhere, there is no reason to be disturbed. If someone points out a small fault in us, we should be willing to look within ourselves and see if that fault exists. We should not be proud or defensive.
