All this time He had been thinking it was Saci’s daughter-in-law who was taking the fruits. “Now, ” He said, “I know it was the Supreme Personality of Godhead eating the offering. ”

After explaining all this to His mother, the Lord asked, “I am going to get Lord Nityananda. Won’t you prepare something for Us to eat? In that way, you will be able to satisfy your desire to feed Lord Nityananda. ”

When Sacimata entered her kitchen, she did so in bliss. She thought she was experiencing the fulfillment of her dream, so she cooked a variety of preparations. Lord Caitanya told Lord Nityananda that Sacimata was preparing a special feast and inviting Him to honor it. “But first I have to tell You to behave Yourself. ” Nityananda was known as an *avadhuta* and sometimes did strange things. Lord Nityananda answered, “Visnu, Visnu! What are You saying? Why are You telling Me to behave Myself? You are only saying this because *You* don’t behave. Just because You don’t behave, You think no one behaves. ” Laughing, the two Lords went to see Sacimata.

When They entered Lord Caitanya’s house, Isana bathed Their feet. When the two Lords sat down to Sacimata’s feast, Sacimata brought in preparation after preparation. When she carried the last preparation into the room, she suddenly saw that Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityananda had become two five-year-old boys—the same boys she had seen in her dream. This was no hallucination, no freeing oneself of a bad dream with a troublesome hallucination. The two boys had four arms and carried conchshells, *cakras,* lotus flowers, and clubs. Sacimata fainted with ecstasy. Tears flowed from her eyes and she experienced all kinds of ecstatic symptoms. When she opened her eyes, Lord Caitanya asked her, “Mother, what did you see? ” Sacimata could not speak. All she could think about was seeing Caitanya and Nityananda in the form of Krsna and Balarama. In this way, Lord Caitanya satisfied His mother by revealing the truth of her dream.

We may not experience such dreams, but we need to understand that the Lord’s pastimes are transcendental reality. When we actually hear His pastimes or the pastimes of His devotees, we can awaken to reality. We can also increase both our affection for and attraction to the Lord. We can begin to understand how the Lord reciprocates with His devotees.

The Lord is always merciful to His devotees, even in their dreams. Pundarika Vidyanidhi is an example of this. Pundarika Vidyanidhi had a dream after the *Odana-sasthi* festival in which the Lord is offered new cloth. The *Odana-sasthi* festival takes place at the beginning of the winter season, and the *pujaris* like to offer Lord Jagannatha warm clothing on that day. Pundarika Vidyanidhi and Svarupa Damodara attended the festival along with Lord Caitanya. Pundarika Vidyanidhi noticed that the *pujaris* were offering Lord

Jagannatha clothes that had not been washed and which still contained some starch. It made him angry, because the *sastras* say clearly that we should not offer unwashed cloth to the deities. One who offers starched clothing to the deities is considered offensive.

The Lord is independent and may do whatever He wishes, and if it is His will to accept starched cloth, we should not question Him. At the same time, as Krsna’s servants, we should serve Him nicely. Pundarika Vidyanidhi found fault with the *pujaris.* It is described that the Lord sometimes bewilders His confidential servants in order to instruct others through them.

Later, after Pundarika Vidyanidhi had gone to sleep, Lord Caitanya appeared to him in his dream in the form of Lord Jagannatha and began to chastise him. “Why do you speak in this way about My servants? Don’t you know that My servants are dear to Me? Why should you criticize My servants? Can’t you see they offer Me service? I must punish you for criticizing My servants. ” Then Lord Balarama appeared and began slapping Pundarika Vidyanidhi’s face. He cried out in his dream, “What are You doing?! ” The Lord did not even remove His rings. He slapped Pundarika Vidyanidhi until he fell down and clasped Lord Jagannatha’s lotus feet to beg forgiveness. The Lord was kind to His devotee and forgave him.

It’s not common to be punished by the Lord in a dream, but just to show Pundarika Vidyanidhi and Svarupa Damodara that it had not been *only* a dream, when Pundarika awoke, his cheeks were swollen—so swollen that he could not even go to see Lord Jagannatha that morning. He was too embarrassed. Still, he went to see Svarupa Damodara as he normally did, and of course, Svarupa Damodara asked him why his cheeks were swollen. Pundarika Vidyanidhi could only repeat how embarrassed he was, and told Svarupa Damodara that the Lord had chastised him for criticizing His servants. Svarupa Damodara immediately understood—he is also the Lord’s confidential devotee—and he celebrated the mercy that Pundarika had received.

Therefore even in dreams the Lord’s devotees experience His presence. (Of course, we have to be careful how we interpret our dreams. There’s a tendency among neophyte devotees to interpret their dreams as special signs from the Lord, but that is not usually true. We are not confidential devotees like Sacimata or Pundarika Vidyanidhi. We should be careful not to put ourselves on the same platform as these devotees. We should awaken fully from the dream of material life before we think that the dreams we have at night are direct communications from Krsna. ) We can experience the Lord’s presence by hearing about these great devotees in their relationship with the Lord.

Narada Muni is clearly recommending for those of us who are *mudha-dhi* that we gradually become detached from material life by hearing about Krsna’s pastimes. This is Narada Muni’s foremost instruction on *grhastha* life. When we understand Narada Muni’s instruction, we will begin to understand the meaning of the phrase, “Ideal family life. ”

## What is “ideal family life”? (2)

**SB** [**7.14.3**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/14.3) **Alma-Ata, October 1997**

There are many important instructions for *grhasthas* that are applicable to other *asramas.* Please do not, therefore, think that simply because we are reading a section about ideal family life that you are exempt from the instructions it contains just because you are wearing saffron cloth. If you listen carefully, you will see that everything in this section applies to all of us—whether because we plan to enter family life or because we wish to avoid it. After all, these instructions are being delivered by Narada Muni, who is an eternal *brahmacari.*

In this chapter, Yudhisthira Maharaja describes himself as *grhamudha-dhi,* a householder ignorant of the goal of life. Srila Prabhupada asserts that *grhasthas* are generally *grha-mudha-dhi.* Therefore, everyone, especially *grhasthas,* should be given the opportunity to hear about Krsna.

Sometimes *grhasthas* think that they have no time to practice Krsna consciousness. They may think that their responsible execution of family duties is sufficient. Srila Prabhupada disagrees. He emphasizes that *grhasthas* should especially find the time to hear and chant about Krsna. He translates the word *yatah-kalam* as “according to time, ” and writes in parentheses, “Generally a *grhastha* can find time in the evening or in the afternoon. ” Of course, we may hear this and think that’s not true. Those who have jobs have to work many hours, and usually they spend time commuting back and forth between home and work. When they return home in the evenings, they are tired. When *grhamedis* return home, they seek out sense gratification to relieve their fatigue. Devotees should seek relief in Krsna consciousness.

So how *can* householders find time to hear and chant? In this chapter of the *Bhagavatam,* Narada Muni answers this question. First, those who hold a house usually do so in order to maintain their attachments to their possessions. As soon as we want more than we require to maintain body and soul together, we will have to work. Therefore, Narada Muni instructs householders to earn a livelihood. Then, instead of trying to enjoy the results of their work, householders should offer the fruits to Krsna. The Lord’s pure devotees can teach us how to satisfy Krsna with the fruits of our work. We should associate with devotees in order to learn how to offer our activities and their fruits to Krsna.

In the next verse Narada Muni explains how when we associate with saintly persons, we must hear about the Supreme Lord’s incarnations. Hearing about the Supreme Lord’s incarnations has a particular benefit: it helps us to gradually awaken our attachment to Krsna and to become detached from family life. Hearing about Krsna awakens us from the dream of material life. Although we may be surrounded by loving family and friends and therefore feel secure, the *Bhagavatam* assures us that such security is temporary. If we take shelter in something temporary, we are bound to suffer when we lose it. As the *Bhagavad-gita* states, “Those who are seers of the truth” understand the difference between the temporary and the real. Live simply and don’t accumulate more than you require.

We generally think that making a livelihood means making money, but that is because this is the basis upon which modern society has been established: you must have money if you want to live. Vedic society was different. Earning one’s livelihood in Vedic society meant only to acquire the basic necessities of life. There are a variety of ways to do that. The *Bhagavatam* will describe this in later verses of this chapter. The point of the verses we have discussed so far is that we should absorb ourselves in *krsna-katha* and thus free ourselves from material consciousness, and at the same time, we should live simply, not acquiring more than we need. These two items are the only way by which we can lose our taste for matter and develop our taste for spirit. Although in householder life we become attached to family and friends, our real object of affection is Krsna. If we don’t understand that, we will look for relationships in the material world and be disappointed.

I saw an example of this recently when I visited a devotee’s house. The devotee had a dog in his house. As I talked with my host, he frequently moved his arm. The sunlight coming through the window was reflecting off his wrist watch. His dog saw the light beam moving here and there and began to chase it, pouncing on it, teeth bared. The dog was trying to catch a reflection—it was seeking taste in a reflection—and each time it pounced, I could hear it gnashing its teeth. But there was nothing there for the dog to catch.

Srila Prabhupada speaks about people chasing reflections in the material world. In the material world, *everything* is reflection and nothing is substance. The real substance is in the spiritual world. How much taste can we get from a reflection? The only way to taste reality is to go to the source of reality, the reservoir of pleasure, Sri Krsna.

“Those who, even while remaining situated in their established social positions throw away the process of speculative knowledge and with their body, words and mind offer all respects to descriptions of Your personality and activities, dedicating their lives to these narrations, which are vibrated by You personally and by Your pure devotees, certainly conquer Your Lordship, although You are otherwise unconquerable by anyone within the three worlds. ” (SB [10.14.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/14.3)) That is what we are meant to do, whether we remain *grhasthas or* take to a life of renunciation.

## We may be externally attached, but internally detached

**SB** [**7.14.5**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/14.5) **Alma-Ata, October 1997**

Narada Muni has explained to Maharaja Yudhisthira that a householder should earn his livelihood with the proper consciousness; that is, with the desire to properly maintain his family responsibilities and to offer the fruits of his work to Krsna. A householder should gradually become detached by associating with saintly persons and by hearing and chanting about Krsna. Attachment is strong; it is the cause of bondage to matter. Lord Kapiladeva states, “Every learned person knows that attachment to material things is the cause of bondage for the living entity, but when that same attachment is applied to self-realized devotees, it opens the door to liberation. ”

When we speak of attachment, we must face that one of our greatest attachments is the attachment to the fruits of our labor. The Lord has three potencies, summarized as follows: the spiritual energy, which is full of knowledge, the living entity, who belongs to the spiritual energy, and the potency of ignorance. The living entity is prone to fall into ignorance, and that ignorance is particularly revealed in attachment to fruitive activities.

Attachment is actually the constitutional nature of the living entity. Attachment means service. If we are not attached to Krsna, we will become attached to *maya.* That is, we will serve matter as the object of our attachment. The *Bhagavatam* states, “By rendering devotional service unto the Personality of Godhead, Sri Krsna, one immediately acquires causeless knowledge and detachment from the world. ” (SB [1.2.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.7)) This is not possible while we are in the grips of illusion.

It is difficult to cultivate detachment while earning a livelihood to support a family, but that is what is required of us. Therefore, Prabhupada told us to appear externally attached while secretly cultivating detachment in the heart. In this way, we will be able to fulfill people’s expectations of us while not becoming actually entangled in their demands. After all, our obligations are based on mutual service in family and society. People will not want us to renege on our responsibilities. We serve family and friends by offering suggestions about how they can become successful in their own lives. If someone has helped us become successful, they will not want us to forget our obligation to them. Even though they may have presented themselves as selfless while they were helping us, they were not actually selfless but selfish. It is not possible to be purely selfless in the material world. Srila Prabhupada said that the closest thing to selflessness in this world is a mother’s affection for her child. Such affection is so strong that even if a child mistreats his mother, she will not reject him.

When we begin to cultivate detachment, those who expect something of us become concerned. They may not understand our detachment and may be afraid that we will not live up to our material obligations. Therefore, especially in family relationships, which tend to be full of obligation, it is good to quietly cultivate detachment while appearing externally attentive. Those who renounce their household duties without experiencing true detachment are false renunciates.

In this chapter of the *Bhagavatam*, Narada Muni describes the process for cultivating detachment. We have already listed some of the ways: hearing the pastimes of the Lord’s incarnations, associating with saintly persons, and maintaining a simple lifestyle. There is a transcendental pastime that can be used to illustrate this point. When Sacimata saw that her young Nimai was going to hear from Advaita Acarya, she became concerned. She was so attached to her son, and she thought of Him as *only* her son. We may know that her son was the Supreme Personality of Godhead, but Sacimata was covered by Yogamaya and did not know this. So when Sacimata heard that Nimai was going to hear from Advaita Acarya, she became concerned because she knew that it was Advaita Acarya who had awoken the desire for renunciation in her first son’s heart. After hearing from Advaita Acarya, Visvarupa lost all interest in material affairs. He was not interested in family life, not interested in earning a living, not interested in social obligations, and eventually, he took *sannyasa.* He was young at that time, but he entered the forest, leaving his family behind. This was difficult for Jagannatha Misra and Sacimata to tolerate, because they loved Visvarupa more than their own lives.

Visvarupa’s departure was so disturbing to his family that one night, Jagannatha Misra had a dream. He saw Nimai leaving home as a sannyasi. Nimai’s head was shaven and He was surrounded by followers. He realized from this dream that Nimai would follow in Visvarupa’s footsteps. In order to prevent this from happening, Jagannatha Misra decided that Nimai should stop studying. He forbade him to read any books. If his son became too learned in *Vedanta,* he too would become detached from the material world.

When Sacimata heard Jagannatha Misra’s order, she could not understand its import. She asked, “How can you forbid your son to study? You don’t want Him to study because you think He will not become attached to family life, but if your son is an ignorant fool, who will marry Him? He won’t have family life either way. ” Eventually, Jagannatha Misra convinced Sacimata that the Lord would take care of Nimai, even if He didn’t go to school.

However, when Nimai was restricted from studying, He became more mischievous than ever. Then the neighbors approached Jagannatha Misra and asked, “What enemy has convinced you to do this to your son? We are trying to convince our children to go to school. Your son *wants* to go, but you forbid Him. You have such a learned son. Give up this foolish way of thinking. ” Jagannatha Misra was affected by his neighbors’ concerned words, and finally he relented.

Soon after, Jagannatha Misra had another dream in which he again saw Nimai as a *sannyasi.* Again he related his dream to Sacimata, but the dream proved so painful to him that he decided he did not want to witness the departure of his son. He thought he would not be able to tolerate the separation. Shortly afterwards, Jagannatha Misra left this world.

Sacimata became a widow. All she had left in the world was her young son Nimai. After Jagannatha Misra’s departure, Sacimata could not tolerate separation from Nimai. If Nimai was not before her for even a moment, she would go blind.

Therefore, when she heard that Nimai was visiting Advaita Acarya, she became fearful that she would lose this son too. In her mind she criticized Advaita Acarya for taking her sons away. She thought, “He calls himself Advaita, non-dual, but actually he is *dvaita,* duplicitous, because he is trying to steal my son from me. ” Later, there is a pastime where Lord Caitanya makes Sacimata go to Advaita Acarya to take the dust from His lotus feet so that she can be forgiven for her offense. In this pastime, we see this principle being described: one who hears the Lord’s pastimes in the association of saintly persons awakens his spirit of renunciation toward matter and his attachment toward Krsna. When such a person’s family members see renunciation awakening, they become anxious.

We can see the difference between material and spiritual attachments. When we are attached to matter, we suffer bondage. When we are attached to Krsna, we become liberated. Therefore Narada Muni explains that while engaging in family affairs we should remain detached, even as we work to maintain them.

What makes this possible is the acceptance of a simple mode of life. The scriptures tell us to gather whatever is necessary to maintain body and soul together. That means food, shelter, and simple clothing. These are our basic necessities, but they are not our ultimate necessities. We must also attend to our higher necessities, the necessities that relate to life outside our body. Govinda dasa describes in his song, “Wealth, sons, wife, youth, family—what are they? They are just drops of water on a lotus leaf, ready to roll off at any moment. At once let me take up the worship of Sri Hari, the ultimate necessity. ”

The ultimate necessity is to be engaged in the service of Lord Hari. Our highest necessity is to absorb ourselves in meditation on the Lord. All other necessities relate to the material body. We need them, but we do not have to give fulfilling them all our heart.

The *Isopanisad* describes, “One may aspire to live for hundreds of years if one continuously works in this way, because that kind of work will not bind him to the laws of karma. ” The author of *Isopanisad* is speaking about material detachment and devotional service. “Work done as a sacrifice for Visnu has to be performed. Otherwise work binds one to the material world. ” Those who do not recognize Krsna’s true transcendental nature and work for His pleasure fall down (Bg. [3.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.9)).

We should all understand this. We have to perform our work for the Lord’s satisfaction. Everyone should know what is required of him in order to satisfy Krsna, and then we should each execute our duties for His pleasure. The *Srimad-Bhagavatam* explains, “All occupational engagements are certainly meant for ultimate liberation. They should never be performed for material gain. Furthermore, according to sages, one who is engaged in the ultimate occupational service should never use material gain to cultivate sense gratification. ” *(SB* [1.2.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.9))

Again, if we are not meant to work for material gain, then we must live simply. Srila Prabhupada writes, “Certainly one requires some means of livelihood, and according to one’s *varna* and *asrama,* this means of livelihood is described in the *sastras.* One should be satisfied with this. Therefore, instead of hankering for more and more money, a sincere devotee of the Lord tries to invent ways to earn his livelihood, and when he does so, Krsna helps him. ” (SB [7.14.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/14.5), purport)

This is an important point: Krsna will help us. Often householders think, “Why would Krsna help me? I'm just an attached householder. I have to take care of these affairs myself. ” Yes, the householder has to accept responsibility, but only as duty. He should give his heart to dependence on Krsna.

Life is difficult nowadays because the current civilization defines life’s necessities at a high standard. Thus those who are bewildered by the Lord’s illusory energy find that there is no end to what must be acquired. There are no more examples—or very few—of people who work simply to maintain body and soul, accepting what comes easily as that which the Lord has provided for them. Srila Prabhupada actually writes that we should accept what comes “automatically. ” We are convinced that *nothing* comes automatically, but this is not true. The Lord is providing everyone’s physical necessities. We need only do our duty and accept what comes from that. That is the Lord’s arrangement.

The Lord has arranged so many things for the smooth functioning of this world. For example, the demigods are in charge of rainfall as the Lord’s agents. When we perform sacrifice, the rain falls. Sacrifice means to perform our prescribed duties for Krsna’s satisfaction. When householders perform their duties in the proper consciousness, they will become satisfied with whatever comes automatically by Krsna’s grace. Even if our livelihood does not come in a way that we were expecting, we will remain dependent on the Lord’s mercy. Krsna will personally help us to maintain this consciousness, and He will personally maintain His devotees.

Who are His devotees? Those who are fully dependent upon Krsna for everything. Those who perform their duties for His satisfaction. Those who remain unattached to the results, so that even when the results do not come according to their expectations, they continue to perform their duties and remain faithful to Krsna. Such devotees do not give up.

Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura told a story to illustrate how the Lord takes care of His devotees. This is the story of Arjunacarya, a learned *brahmana* and Vaisnava teacher of the *sastra.* Arjunacarya was not a wealthy *brahmana.* Rather, he lived in poverty, accepting whatever the Lord provided. His main occupations were teaching scripture and begging for his livelihood. He also wrote commentaries on various scriptures in order to enlighten others about devotional service.

One day, he was working on his commentary on *Bhagavad-gita* when he came across the following verse:

*ananyas cintayanto mam, ye janah paryupasate*  
*tesam nityabhiyuktanam, yoga-ksemam vahamy aham*

**“But those who always worship Me with exclusive devotion, meditating on My transcendental form—to them I carry what they lack and I preserve what they have. ” (Bg.** [**9.22**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.22)**)**

When Arjunacarya saw the word *vahamy,* he thought, “Krsna carries? I don’t want Krsna to carry anything to me. Krsna can arrange everything by His divine will and everything happens according to that will. All the different energies work according to Krsna’s will. By His will all His energies can provide the living beings’ necessities. Why should the Lord personally carry them? ” Therefore he thought the use of the word *vahamy,* “carry, ” was a mistake. He crossed it out and replaced it with *karomi,* which means that the Lord arranges everything through His energies rather than personally carrying it. Arjunacarya was expressing a genuine feeling of humility when he crossed out that word.

The following day, Arjunacarya went out to beg alms and then to bathe. While he was out, two boys arrived at his house. They found only Arjunacarya’s wife at home. The two boys were carrying in their arms a large variety of produce. They told the *acarya's* wife to take it quickly and to prepare an offering before her husband returned. They added, “We have to run quickly, because if your husband sees us, he will strike us again. ”

The *brahmana’s* wife was shocked. “Strike you again? You mean he *struck* you? My husband is a gentle *brahmana.* He would never strike anyone. ”

“We do not have time to listen to your defense. We must run away before your husband returns to strike us. But if you don’t believe that he struck us, just look at our backs. ”

One of the boys then turned and showed Arjunacarya’s wife his back. There she saw marks as if left by a whip. Then the two boys ran away.

Later, when her husband returned home, the *acarya's* wife presented him with the meal prepared from what the boys had brought. He asked, “Why is it that you have already prepared an offering and eaten it before serving me? ” A Vedic wife would never eat before serving her husband. When Arjunacarya asked his wife about her strange behavior, she told him how the boys had come to their home and instructed her to both prepare an offering and to eat it herself. She then described what the boys looked like. When she described their complexion and features, and also the marks on one of the boys’ backs, Arjunacarya began to wonder. Gradually, he realized who the boys were. He told his wife, “Last night I was writing my commentary to *Bhagavad-gita* ([9.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.22)). I thought that the description of Krsna personally carrying what we lack was a mistake. Certainly, I thought, He would use His energies to provide for the devotees and not have to personally carry what we lack. I actually crossed the word *vahamy* out. Now I realize that crossing out that word was as good as striking the Lord. The Lord *does* personally carry what His devotees lack. When that boy showed you his back, that strike came from my pen when I struck out the word *vahamy.* That was Krsna Himself. ”

In this pastime, Krsna showed us that His words in *Bhagavad-gita* are true: for those devotees who are dependent upon Him, who always meditate upon Him, and who always execute their duties in full consciousness of Him, the Lord carries what they lack and preserves what they have.

This is true of renunciates and householders. Srivasa Pandita was a householder, and he had a similar experience with Lord Caitanya. Lord Caitanya ordered Srivasa Pandita to take *sannyasa,* because he could see that Srivasa Pandita was not begging alms for his family. Srivasa told the Lord, “It is not in my power to take *sannyasa. ”* The Lord asked, “How will you maintain yourself? ” In response, Srivasa Pandita clapped his hands three times. When the Lord asked why he was clapping, Srivasa said, “If after three claps nothing comes, I must fast. Because I did not provide my family with adequate meals, I will tie a rope around my neck and drown myself in the Ganga. ” Lord Caitanya exclaimed, “You? Fast! Never! You are dear to Me. Even if the goddess of fortune becomes impoverished and is forced to beg from door to door, I will never allow there to be poverty at your home. I will personally carry on My head everything you need to support your family. Don’t you remember what I said in *Bhagavad-gita*? Did you forget My words? I said I carry what you lack and preserve what you have. There will never be poverty in your home. ”

Srivasa Pandita was such a dear devotee, and he always depended on the Lord. He spent all his time glorifying the Lord. Even if we appear externally impoverished, we will never be poor if we depend on the Lord. The Lord establishes true wealth in His devotees’ homes by giving Himself, our real wealth.

In the *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* Narada Muni describes the Lord as dear to those who have no material possessions. Indeed, the Lord relishes the activities of such devotees. He does not relish the activities of those who are proud of their material wealth, education, aristocracy, or material beauty. Such persons often deride the Lord and His devotees out of their pride, even though they may appear to be offering the Lord worship. The Lord does not accept the worship of those who are offensive in their pride. Rather, the Lord is dear to the materially impoverished.

To be materially impoverished does not necessarily mean that we have to have nothing. Instead, it means that we use everything we have to glorify the Lord. Lord Caitanya established this principle. Lord Caitanya once asked a devotee about his friends and family members and if they were leading an auspicious life. Srila Vrndavana dasa Thakura describes that the Lord asked this question in order to teach the meaning of the word “auspicious. ” Auspicious and devotional service are synonymous. If a person is not performing devotional service, even if he is a king, his life is inauspicious. However, if a person does not even know when he will eat again but is performing devotional service, then his life is fully auspicious and he has all wealth.

After this, the Lord called the *brahmanas* before Him. The *brahmanas* all wanted to invite the Lord to their homes to offer Him a meal. Lord Caitanya told them that He would only accept a meal in the homes of those who were *lakhesvara,* “master of one hundred thousand. ” When the *brahmanas* heard this, they felt great pain. They said, “My dear Lord, why are You making such a condition? We are all poor. None of us possess one hundred thousand coins. How will we be able to fulfill our desires to have You visit our homes? ” Lord Caitanya replied, “You do not know the meaning of ‘master of one hundred thousand. ’ The master of one hundred thousand is he who is chanting one hundred thousand names of the Lord daily. ” When the *brahmanas* heard that, they cried, “Yes! We can do that. One hundred thousand coins we can’t do, but one hundred thousand names we will begin immediately! ”

In this way, the Lord tricked them and they began chanting one hundred thousand names daily. The Lord executed this pastime to specifically establish the principle that the real meaning of “auspicious” was “devotion to Krsna. ” There is no other wealth.

If we have devotion to the Lord, whatever we offer He will accept. The Lord is inclined to accept the offerings of His devotees when they are offered with faith and devotion. He is also always ready to bestow His mercy upon them. That is His nature.

The Lord does not accept the offerings of those who do not have devotion. Even if such nondevotees offer Him millions of gifts, the Lord will not accept them. Devotion is the only ingredient of any value in an offering. Therefore Krsna says in *Bhagavad-gita,* “If you offer Me a fruit, flower, leaf or water with devotion, I will accept it. ” This is how Narada Muni instructs us to execute our household duties in Krsna consciousness. Everything should be offered to Krsna. When we work in this way, Krsna will personally take care of us. As Srila Prabhupada states, “Krsna will help him” and gradually they will be able to become detached.

## Sadhana

## Sadhana-bhakti means the regulated principles of devotional service

**SB 1.13.54 Minsk, March 23, 1991**

“One who has controlled the sitting postures \[the *yogi asanas\]* and the breathing process can turn the senses toward the Absolute Personality of Godhead and thus become immune to the contaminations of the modes of material nature, namely mundane goodness, passion and ignorance. ” (SB [1.13.54](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/13.54))

In the beginning of the *bhakti-yoga* process, it is strongly recommended that we regulate the senses by engaging in devotional activities. It is by regulation that we may achieve restraint. Thus we find that *sadhana-bhakti* means the regulated principles of devotional service.

This verse gives a further description of Dhrtarastra’s yoga practices. At this point in the narration, Dhrtarastra is focusing within, practicing *Pratyahara. Pratyahara* is the process of pulling the senses away from the sense objects and turning them within. This is not an easy process, and to practice it, Dhrtarastra had to undergo great austerities.

There are two ways to control the senses: to restrain them from the sense objects and to engage them in superior pursuits. Srila Prabhupada explains that Dhrtarastra was concentrating on a single objective, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Because he was able to hold the Lord’s image in his mind, and because he was determined not to engage in sense gratification, Dhrtarastra was able to control his senses.

By dint of superior engagement, the mind and intelligence become subordinate to the soul and thus automatically controlled. When the mind and intelligence are engaged in Krsna’s service, the senses follow. Trying to conquer the senses artificially is extremely difficult. Krsna explains that even one of the senses can lead a transcendentalist to lose his renunciation.

We each have five knowledge-acquiring senses: sight, taste, smell, hearing, and touch. The embodied living entity vents his desires through these five senses. As long as we desire to enjoy matter, the senses will seek objects with which to interact for satisfaction. We experience this daily. If we do not focus our minds on Krsna and do not become determined to remain in devotional service, then the slightest distraction from one of our senses can cause us to fall down. We must find superior engagement, in Krsna consciousness, for the mind and intelligence if we wish to control the senses.

For the unfixed mind, the sight, taste, smell, or touch of an attractive object will stimulate us to want to enjoy. As long as we try to enjoy ourselves through our senses, we will not be able to practice yoga. Yoga begins with sensory restraint. Srila Prabhupada explains in *Bhagavad-gita* that the central focus of the yoga system is the mind; the mind is the reservoir of all plans for sense gratification. That is, within our minds are all the ideas of how we can enjoy in this world. The plans are made, and as soon as the object of enjoyment comes within reach of our senses, the senses signal the mind. Our senses reach out like tentacles to grasp the object of our enjoyment.

The senses are the vents through which we seek enjoyment. It is the nature of the soul to seek happiness, and in our conditioned state, to seek it in the material world. That soul’s desire filters down through the intelligence and mind and enjoyment is filtered back through the senses. But the senses are not representing the pure nature of the soul. Just as white light is filtered through a colored glass and takes on its color, our soul’s desires filter through the material intelligence and mind and become colored. When the pure spirit soul vents its desires through the senses and thus comes in contact with the three modes of material nature, its interests become colored by matter.

In contact with the material modes, the soul develops a desire for material sense gratification. When we finally desire to return to spiritual pursuits, we must undergo the yoga process, which begins by restraining the senses. To restrain the senses requires intelligence, because it is the very nature of the senses to seek out material enjoyment.

There are many examples in the *Bhagavatam* of *yogis* trying to control their senses and their senses remaining active despite their endeavors. For example, Saubari Muni was sitting underwater meditating. One day he saw two fish copulating. This agitated his mind and he desired to enjoy sex life so strongly that he married fifty princesses.

Visvamitra provides another example. He too was engaged in meditation, and when He simply heard the tinkling sound of ankle bells, he lost his intelligence. He broke his meditation on transcendence and began to meditate on the woman who was wearing the ankle bells. In the end, he lost complete control and had sex with the heavenly society girl, Menaka.

Even Bharata Maharaja provides an example. Bharata Maharaja was also performing devotional practices alone in the forest, focusing his mind, intelligence, and senses upon the Lord. But he became attached to a deer, lost control of his senses and therefore his meditation, and wherever he looked, he began to seek out the deer. “Is the deer nearby? If the deer isn’t nearby, where is it? ” In the end, he could no longer stop thinking about the deer.

The *Bhagavatam* describes that while meditating, Bharata Maharaja was only feigning attention. Krsna explains this phenomenon in *Bhagavad-gita:* “One who restrains the senses of action but whose mind dwells on sense objects certainly deludes himself and is called a pretender. ” (Bg. [3.6](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.6)) Far superior to such a cheater is he who honestly engages his senses in works of devotion. The pretender may practice yoga and even appear to be detaching himself from matter, but he’s fooling himself because inwardly he is meditating on sense gratification. Real yoga means to control the mind and senses. If the mind is not controlled, the senses will not be controlled.

Srila Prabhupada explains in his purport to *Bhagavatam* ([1.13.54](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/13.54)) that *bhakti-yoga* directly engages the senses in Krsna’s loving service. This prohibits the performer from engaging in material activities. Prabhupada says that the senses are like serpents waiting to strike. When they are engaged in Krsna’s service, they are like serpents with their fangs removed. We must therefore give our mind, senses, and intelligence a superior engagement. If our mind and intelligence are always fixed on devotional service, and if we are resolute in that determination, then we will successfully control the mind and senses.

Srila Prabhupada writes, “However, in the beginning of the *bhakti-yoga* process it is strongly recommended that one regulate the senses in devotional activities. It is through this process of regulation that one may automatically achieve restraint. Therefore we find that *sadhana-bhakti* means regulated principles of devotional service. ” Regulating the senses is essential because we have been in contact with material nature since time immemorial. We are so accustomed to it that we habitually serve the senses and the mind. If we are tired, we are accustomed to say, “I will go to sleep. ” If we are hungry, we decide to eat. We do these things without thought. Whatever we feel, we follow. In that way, we are like bulls being led by a rope through our nose. Without regulation, we find ourselves meditating more on when we will have another opportunity to eat and sleep than on Krsna no matter how much we make a show of meditation.

We have the tendency to think that sense gratification is our shelter. Perhaps we are working hard at our service. We believe we will get relief in two hours when we are able to eat, or in four hours when we are able to sleep. When our mind and senses aren’t controlled, we won’t understand that the very work in which we are engaged has been perfectly designed to regulate us and allow us to practice *sadhana-bhakti.* Unfortunately, we think that the superior activities are eating and sleeping. We actually believe that eating and sleeping will bring us relief. But aren’t these the activities of animal life?

And when those activities fail to bring us relief, we send our senses out looking for other forms of sense gratification. We seek beautiful forms to enjoy, or beautiful sounds, tastes, or smells. All of these things are under the influence of the material modes. Therefore Krsna states in *Bhagavad-gita,* “One who engages in full devotional service, unfailing in all circumstances, at once transcends the modes of material nature and thus comes to the level of Brahman. ” Such a person transcends the modes and experiences self-satisfaction.

A devotee who fixes his mind and senses in devotional service can experience self-satisfaction within his service, because serving Krsna is a superior engagement and the only means we have to control the mind and senses. In *Upadesamrta,* Srila Prabhupada writes that the best way to control the mind is to always think about how to improve one’s service to Krsna. Instead of meditating on the difficulties, we should instead think of how to increase our devotional service. It is the nature of the mind to reject difficulty and to seek pleasure. If we meet difficulty in one service, we think we will avoid it by taking up another. Or perhaps we decide to give up service altogether, move to another location, or change our association. This does not enable us to control the mind and senses because the mind will always find an excuse to be dissatisfied. The mind is the reservoir of ideas for how to engage in sense gratification. It will never voluntarily surrender to Krsna.

If we listen to the mind over the spiritual intelligence, we will become the mind’s servant. This is known in Sanskrit as *godasa.* There are *Gosvamis,* those who control their senses, and *godasas,* those who serve their senses. Both ask, “Please engage me in your service, birth after birth. ” The *Gosvami* makes that prayer to Krsna and defeats repeated birth and death; the *godasa* prays to his senses and is awarded with birth after birth.

The superior engagement of Krsna consciousness is available twenty-four hours a day. Why don’t we take it? Because we have no spontaneous attraction to devotional service. Therefore, we must first become duty-bound and regulated. The scriptures provide us with a number of instructions to help us regulate ourselves. The temples celebrate *mangala-arati* every morning at around 4:30. This means that the Supreme Personality of Godhead is making an appearance at 4:30 a. m. Still, we think, “Let me sleep a little longer. ” That is the mind’s nature. Practicing regulation should help us overcome the mind.

Sometimes devotees are enthusiastic when their spiritual master is arriving at the airport. If they know his flight is arriving at 3: 00, they rush to be there on time. They want to be sure that when their spiritual master clears customs and enters the terminal, they are there to greet him. Srila Prabhupada has told us that Krsna is arriving every morning at 4:30 a. m., but we don’t rush to greet Him. We are so dull. Instead of rushing to greet Krsna, we calculate that no one will miss us if we do not attend His arrival. Do we think that Krsna will not know one way or another? To practice *bhakti-yoga* takes regulated senses. That will help us think that there is no question of *not* attending *mangala-arati,* and gradually by our attendance we will develop a more spontaneous attraction to Krsna.

It is easy to regulate ourselves if we think of Krsna’s pleasure in everything we do. Our rising time is determined by His rising time. Similarly, our eating schedule should be arranged around Krsna’s eating schedule. We are meant to cook for Krsna’s pleasure and not to please ourselves. Then we are meant to offer what we have cooked to the deity. Krsna kindly eats at the same time every day. We can regulate our eating around His offerings so that we are always honoring His *prasadam.* We should not think, “I'm not hungry, so why should I cook? ” We are not cooking for ourselves but for Krsna.

Only those under the influence of the material modes will cook only when they are hungry.

Often devotees, even when they are hungry, don’t cook. Rather, they simply find something to eat. Sometimes what they find is not offered, but they don’t care. They are hungry, so they eat it anyway. Or, even if it’s not time for Krsna’s scheduled offering, they decide to make an offering and eat the remnants. Because he is more concerned with his hunger than Krsna’s offerings, he has already enjoyed the offering before Krsna received it. This is not regulation.

Temple life means regulating the senses. Each morning we rise for *mangala-arati*, prepare an offering for the Lord, hear about Him, and accept His *prasadam.* These activities form the regulative principles of *bhakti-yoga.* One who regulates the senses in this way, who convinces himself that he must at least practice out of duty, learns to control the senses and changes from *godasa* to *Gosvami.* Without that transformation, we will not be able to advance in devotional service.

Even if the mind gives us difficulty when we engage in these simple activities, we should fight it. If we find ourselves thinking, “When will this program be over so I can go back to sleep? ” we should not follow the mind. If we remain strong in our regulated practices, we will gradually find the taste in these simple activities, including attending *mangala-arati,* chanting rounds, hearing *Srimad-Bhagavatam,* performing *guru-puja,* honoring *krsna-prasadam* (everyone has taste for this activity, and therefore when we refer to “taking” *krsnaprasadam,* we mean honoring it; we are not simply eating when we honor *prasadam),* and if we are fortunate to have service, working for Krsna. And if the mind says, “Go to sleep, ” we will ignore it.

We shouldn’t think that full engagement in devotional service means we should never rest. We may rest as required to keep ourselves fit for service. But we should not respond to the mind’s demands that we become, through sleep or some other form of sense gratification, less absorbed in devotion.

We should pray to have service, whether it’s cleaning the temple (those who can clean the *prasadam* room are very fortunate), book distribution, *harinama,* some other form of preaching, deity worship or whatever else. There is so much service to be done. The basic morning program is meant to give us the enthusiasm to serve throughout the day, to meditate on that service, and to expand it in whatever way possible. This full day’s engagement will naturally control the mind and senses.

If we are unfortunate enough not to have service, we should be intelligent enough to approach a devotee and ask for some. Then all of us will be able to regulate and control the senses. When we perform our regulated devotional practices, we will gradually come to the spontaneous platform and will be thinking about Krsna twenty-four hours a day.

## We must get the vaccine against maya’s influence

**SB** [**5.12.16**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/12.16) **Belgium, May 1990**

**“Simply by associating with exalted devotees, anyone can attain perfection of knowledge and with the sword of knowledge can cut to pieces the illusory associations within this material world. Through the association of devotees, one can engage in the service of the Lord by hearing and chanting** ***\[sravanam kirtanam\].*** **Thus one can revive his dormant Krsna consciousness and, sticking to the cultivation of Krsna consciousness, return home, back to Godhead, even in this life. ”**

We may say that varieties arise from the earth planet itself, but we know that although the universe may be temporarily manifest, it has no ultimate existence. The earth was originally created by a combination of atomic particles, but these particles are impermanent. Actually the combination of atomic particles is not the cause of the universe, although some philosophers think it is. It is not a fact that the varieties found in the material world are a result only of atomic juxtaposition.

Jada Bharata explains that it is important for the purification and maintenance of our Krsna consciousness that we associate with that which is *sat,* eternal. What is eternal is Krsna’s pastimes in the spiritual world. To associate with Krsna’s pastimes is, of course, both essential and the most important principle in Krsna consciousness. We associate with these pastimes by *sravanam kirtanam,* hearing and chanting in the association of devotees. Through the association of devotees one can engage in the Lord’s service.

How can we understand that hearing and chanting are the means to purifying the heart? “Sri Krsna, the Personality of Godhead, who is the Paramatma \[Supersoul\] in everyone’s heart and the benefactor of the truthful devotee, cleanses the desire for material enjoyment from the heart of the devotee who has developed the urge to hear His messages, which are in themselves virtuous when properly heard and chanted. ” (SB [1.2.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.17))

Purification comes, according to this verse, when the message is “properly heard and chanted. ” We “properly” hear and chant when we do so in the association of Krsna’s pure devotees. Such Vaisnavas are naturally attracted to glorifying and pleasing the Lord. Krsna has prescribed that to satisfy Him we must perform sacrifice. The real sacrifice required of the living entities is that we glorify Him and work for His pleasure. The benefit we derive from these activities is obvious: “O descendant of King Bharata, one who desires to be free from all miseries must hear about, glorify, and also remember the Personality of Godhead, who is the Supersoul, the controller and the savior from all miseries. ” (SB [2.1.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/1.5))

The reward we receive is that we become free from the miseries of the material world and are able to please Krsna. The material world is a place of suffering, and when we realize that, we will be impelled to find the way out. Devotional service is the only relief. Srila Prabhupada often quoted *Bhagavatam* ([10.1.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/1.4)) in this regard: “Glorification of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is performed in the *parampara* system; that is, it is conveyed from spiritual master to disciple. Such glorification is relished by those no longer interested in the false temporary glorification of this cosmic manifestation. Descriptions of the Lord are the right medicine for the conditioned soul undergoing repeated birth and death. Therefore, who will cease hearing such glorification of the Lord, except a butcher or one who is killing his own self? ”

Krsna doesn’t need us to glorify Him; He is already complete in Himself. But Krsna knows that the only medicine for the material disease is to overcome our forgetfulness of Him and to return to Him. Our forgetfulness of Krsna is the ultimate cause of our suffering. Those who want to become free from misery simply have to remember their subordinate positions under Krsna.

It is important to note that we cannot forget Krsna except by the influence of His external energy. The ability to forget Krsna also comes from Krsna; nothing can exist or happen separately from Him. The Lord covers us with His illusory curtain mainly because we wish Him to. The Second Canto describes that the living entity’s original sin is envy of God. Because of that envy we are placed under the influence of the external energy and spend our lives trying to imitate the Lord. The only medicine for this disease is to hear about and glorify the Lord. It is by this sacrifice alone that the Lord becomes pleased.

When we associate with devotees in order to hear about Krsna, then we are benefited. *Satam prasangan mama virya-samvido, bhavanti hrt-karna-rasayanah kathah:* we should associate with devotees for *krsna-katha.* Lord Caitanya tells us that we should also become detached from the association of worldly people. One point is positive, the other negative. The wonderful thing about Krsna consciousness is that the negative is automatically achieved when we adopt the positive. “Every learned man knows very well that attachment for the material is the greatest entanglement of the spirit soul. But that same attachment, when applied to the self-realized devotees, opens the door of liberation. ” (SB [3.25.20](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/25.20))

This is the process recommended for purification and cleansing of the heart: “By regular attendance in classes on the *Bhagavatam* and by rendering of service to the pure devotee, all that is troublesome to the heart is almost completely destroyed, and loving service unto the Personality of Godhead, who is praised with transcendental songs, is established as an irrevocable fact”.

If we take this medicine in repeated, regular doses, we will become free from the false temporary designations of the material world. These repeated injections of Krsna consciousness vaccinate us against *maya’s* influence. *We all need repeated injections*! And Krsna consciousness is a permanent cure. We simply have to stick with the process. *Sticking* means constantly glorifying the Lord. Lord Caitanya tells us how we can stick to the holy name:

*trnad api sunicena, taror api sahisnuna*  
*amanina manadena, kirtaniyah sada harih*

**“One who thinks himself lower than the grass, who is more tolerant than a tree, and who does not expect personal honor but is always prepared to give all respects to others, can very easily always chant the holy name of the Lord”. (*****Siksastaka,*** **3)**

We must develop this consciousness. In *Caitanya-caritamrta,* Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami explains that a tree never complains, even when someone cuts it down or fails to water it. A devotee should be even more tolerant than a tree. Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami emphasizes that we should take this third verse of the *Siksastaka* and string it around our necks like a garland for constant remembrance. We must develop the qualities of tolerance, humility, and simplicity if we wish to become lovers of God.

These Vaisnava qualities make us attractive even to Krsna. The *Bhagavatam* ([3.25.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/25.21)) lists some important Vaisnava qualities: “The symptoms of a *sadhu* is that he is tolerant, merciful and friendly to all living entities. He has no enemies, he is peaceful, he abides by the scriptures, and all his characteristics are sublime. ”

Why is the *sadhu* peaceful? Because he remembers Krsna. If we see everything in relation to Krsna’s mercy, we too will be peaceful. This kind of peace is natural and self-manifesting. It helps a devotee persevere through all difficulties.

We develop Vaisnava qualities by associating with *Vaisnavas.* Jada Bharata states, “Simply by the association of the devotees anyone can obtain perfection of knowledge and with the sword of knowledge cut through the illusory associations with this material world. ”

The Krsna consciousness movement is meant to facilitate Vaisnava association. We should keep that objective in mind. Why are we each associating with devotees? Because our ultimate objective is to attain Krsna. If we reflect, most of us will find that we joined the Krsna consciousness movement for this facility and because we found the Krsna conscious process so attractive.

The result of hearing and chanting in the association of Vaisnavas is the awakening of the desire to serve Krsna—the spontaneous desire, not the forced or repressed duty. The desire to serve Krsna is natural to the heart. It only has to be brought out. The catalyst that can do that is hearing and chanting.

## Prajalpa

## Favorable and unfavorable prajalpa

**Bhaktyaloka Odessa, July 1999**

Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura’s book, Sri *Bhaktyaloka,* is a commentary on the first three verses of *Upadesamrta.* In it he writes about the six favorable and six unfavorable activities in practicing devotional service. In one essay in particular, he addresses *prajalpa,* one of the faults that can destroy our service.

Devotees often think of *prajalpa* as faultfinding. In a recent *Bhagavatam* class, a devotee asked, “What is necessary criticism? ” Prahlada Maharaja answered this question from a variety of angles in the *Bhagavatam,* and Bhaktivinoda Thakura also answered it brilliantly in his essay on *prajalpa.*

So when *is* criticism allowed? In the *Upadesamrta* as well as *Bhaktyaloka,* the *Vaisnava acaryas* give instructions in two categories—prescriptions and prohibitions. That is, they prescribe activities that are favorable for our spiritual development and prohibit activities that hinder us. Usually prescriptions and prohibitions are given at the same time. When we take our initiation vows, we promise to avoid the four sinful activities—no meat-eating, no illicit sex, no intoxication, and no gambling—and on the positive side, we promise to accept the prescription of chanting at least sixteen rounds of the Hare Krsna mantra every day.

If we were able to constantly follow only prescribed activities, there would be no need to learn about the prohibitions. If we spoke only of Krsna, we would have no need to discuss *prajalpa.* After all, the *Vaisnava acaryas*—even Krsna Himself—state satatam *kirtayanto mam:* devotees always chant Krsna’s glories. Satatam means always. If we were able to speak about Krsna “always, ” there would be no time to speak *prajalpa.*

But that isn’t the case. We do speak about unnecessary subject matters. Therefore, we should understand the categories of *prajalpa* and watch to see if we are speaking it.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura has categorized *prajalpa* as follows: useless talks, arguments, gossips, debates, faultfinding, blaspheming devotees, and worldly talk. He then takes each category and explains in detail what it is. The information he gives is quite useful. Gossip is very well described. We know what it means to blaspheme a devotee, but we try to justify our offenses. However, for our understanding, Bhaktivinoda Thakura presents the real justifications for *prajalpa.*

Talking with one another is called *jalpana or prajalpa. Jalpana* and *prajalpa* are synonymous. Nowadays, godlessness is so prominent that conversing with others always means godless talk, but the literal definition of *prajalpa* does not necessarily carry a negative connotation. It simply means “speaking with one another. ” There are therefore favorable forms of *prajalpa.* Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes, “Generally in this godless age, as soon as people start talking with one another, it is automatically godless talk. That is the symptom of this age. ” Whenever two or more people gather, they begin to spontaneously discuss something unfavorable for the practice of *bhakti.*

It is possible, however, to speak favorable *jalpana*—speak about things that are favorable to the cultivation of devotional service. Rupa Gosvami states, “While chanting the holy name of the Lord, if a wretched person in his life achieves *namabhasa* or a glimpse of offenseless chanting, then all his faults are destroyed and the Lord becomes pleased with him. *Kirtana,* offering prayers, and reciting the scriptures are all *jalpana,* but these are performed with a favorable mood and devoid of material desire. They are all cultivation of Krsna consciousness. Therefore the conclusion is this: *prajalpa* that is unfavorable to Krsna’s service is adverse to devotional service. The practitioner should carefully give up *prajalpa. ”*

Favorable *prajalpa* is *kirtana*, prayer, and scriptural reading, if they are done in a mood favorable to the cultivation of *bhakti.* Bhaktivinoda Thakura goes on to explain that these forms of favorable *prajalpa* were practiced by the *mahajanas* in their own service to Krsna. “Some people advocate *mauna or* silence as a means for spiritual elevation, ” Bhaktivinoda Thakura writes, and we know that many people consider silence superior to speaking. Prabhupada mentions such persons in his purports, commenting that often people who maintain *mauna* these days do so to gain public attention. Often these people do not avoid communication altogether but carry a board with them on which they write their responses to people’s questions. There are some who say *mauna* is superior to speaking *krsna-katha.* That’s why Bhaktivinoda Thakura separates favorable *prajalpa* from the unfavorable, and to substantiate his claim, he quotes scriptural references to show why we follow the path of *mahajanas* like Rupa Gosvami, Prahlada Maharaja, Sukadeva Gosvami, Vyasadeva, and Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu. Each of these *mahajanas* has taught that discussing topics related to Krsna is the best path. Although sometimes puffed-up devotees advise that all types of *prajalpa* should be given up, we follow Rupa Gosvami. Being *rupanugas*, we stick to the path shown by the *sadhus* who were also following him.

Bhaktivinoda Thakura then quotes from *Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu:* “One should strictly follow the easy path of the previous *mahajanas* and accept it. That path bestows great auspiciousness and is devoid of all misery. ” Elsewhere, Bhaktivinoda Thakura further elaborates on this point. After receiving initiation and instruction from a bona fide spiritual master, a fortunate disciple should follow the path of the previous *sadhus.* Those who are proud neglect the previous *mahajanas* and instead create a new path. As a result, they soon ruin themselves.

The need to create something new is a sign of pride in a Vaisnava. A devotee suffering from pride will think that the Krsna conscious path is not original. “If I produce something new, the people will become attracted. I’ll attract followers. ” Prabhupada says that those who practice *mauna* want to attract cheap followers.

We should not try to manufacture a path. Rather, we should follow a path accepted by *sadhus.* Those who have the good fortune from their past lives to give up their pride respect the established path, whereas those who are unfortunate create new paths to present to others.

Godless *prajalpa* is an impediment in devotional service. *Prajalpa* can be summed up as useless talk. Gossip is extremely detrimental. Practicing devotees should discuss Lord Hari in the association of other devotees, remembering the Lord’s name, form, qualities and pastimes. They should not waste time. Bhaktivinoda Thakura quotes *Bhagavad-gita:* “I am the source of all spiritual and material worlds. Everything emanates from Me. The wise who know this perfectly engage in My devotional service and worship Me in their heart of hearts. The hearts of My pure devotees dwell in Me, their lives are always devoted to My service, and they derive great satisfaction and bliss from always enlightening one another and conversing about Me. ” The *Bhagavad-gita* also states, “Always chanting My glories, endeavoring with great determination, and bowing down before Me, these great souls perpetually worship Me with devotion. ” These are the ways by which we can cultivate unalloyed devotion. If we spend our days and nights talking uselessly with materialists, then how will we follow the Lord’s instruction to “always chant My glories”?

What are ways we engage in materialistic talk? We read newspapers. Newspapers contain so much useless talk. For the practicing devotee to read a newspaper is a great waste of time. If the newspaper discussed topics of pure devotion, then we would read it, but otherwise, we are wasting our time. Srila Prabhupada told a story about how people are attached to newspapers. He said that a Christian missionary went to preach in a mining city. The Christian missionaries told the people about Jesus, but the people had never heard of him. The miners asked, “What is his number? ” They thought perhaps he was a miner, like them, and would therefore have a company number. It was an innocent but ignorant question. The missionary was shocked, “You mean you have never heard of Lord Jesus Christ?! ”

“No, we have never heard of him. ”

“If you don’t know who Jesus Christ is, then you’ll go to hell. ”

“Hell? What is hell? ”

“Hell is a very dark place. It is always damp and cold, and you can’t see anything. ”

“Then we are always in hell, because we spend our days in the mine. Why should we worry about going to hell? We’re already there. ”

The missionary became frustrated and said, “In hell there are no newspapers! ”

“No newspapers?! Oh, how horrible! ”

We should not be attached to reading newspapers, because they are filled with godless talk. They discuss only politics, death, birth, old age, and suffering. The topics contained in newspapers never make the heart peaceful.

Similarly, when we read mundane literature, our hearts do not become peaceful. Rather our minds will always be agitated and we will want to talk about the subjects we have read. Such discussions often lead to argument, but the arguments have no conclusion because there is no *siddhanta.* When we actually come to *siddhanta,* our minds can find shelter in Krsna. Otherwise, our minds will always be disturbed. Godless talks simply agitate the mind.

Politicians would have us believe otherwise. Sometimes leaders get together to discuss peace initiatives, and it may even appear that their two countries are at peace because they are not fighting one another. Is the absence of fighting synonymous with peace? Even if two countries are not at war, the people inside each country are usually in conflict with one another. It is the nature of Kali-yuga that someone is always going to be fighting with someone else. It amazes me to see a group of people with a common enemy fight one another. Their minds are so agitated that as soon as one party makes a statement, it triggers conflict in the other party’s minds.

For example, in the United States racism is a big issue. The white people are upset because the black population is increasing. The blacks feel mistreated by white Americans. If a political leader or important figure makes the slightest negative innuendo about black people, it sparks conflict. A few years ago, a black man was mistreated in Los Angeles and people began rioting. They smashed buildings and committed other acts of violence.

In Kali-yuga there is no peace without Krsna consciousness, but we see that even in the Krsna consciousness movement there is fighting. Why? Prabhupada explains that it is due to want of an ultimate goal. When our minds have no shelter in Krsna consciousness, we will always look for something to fight about. If we spend our time discussing the material world and all the changes that take place here, we will find our minds further agitated and ourselves more and more absorbed in the mundane. We will not be peaceful.

Therefore, Bhaktivinoda Thakura states that for a *sadhaka* to waste time in useless talk is a great loss. “After finishing their meal, mundane people normally smoke and engage in useless talk with other godless people. ” It’s certainly difficult to become *rupanugas* if we live like that. Reading novels is *prajalpa.* However, if we find a novel with a story like the one told of Puranjana in the *Bhagavatam,* then reading that is actually beneficial.

Next Bhaktivinoda Thakura discusses *prajalpa* in the form of “argument. ” Argument too is averse to the practice of devotional service. The arguments of the followers of Nyaya and Vaisesika are godless quarrel. Bhaktivinoda Thakura mentions Nyaya and Vaisesika because they are two of the six schools of Vedic philosophy. Gautama Rsi founded the school of Nyaya and Kanada Rsi founded the school of Vaisesika. Both these systems are based on the idea that the material universe is composed of material ingredients or atoms, and that the atoms themselves are both eternal and the source of creation. Both schools argue these points quite thoroughly. In *Bhaktyaloka,* Bhaktivinoda Thakura states, “It is godless quarrel, and they do not reach any result. Rather, they are simply wasting the mind’s strength and increasing one’s restlessness. ”

In the *Katha Upanisad* it is said that pure intelligence cannot be attained by argument. Therefore, argument is described as *prajalpa.* Both argument and debate are ways to discuss. Arguments are created alone, and debates are engaged in by many. Argument means counteracting whatever we hear. When someone says black, we must say white. To be successful, it requires a challenging attitude. In spiritual life, a challenging attitude is symptomatic of pride: we always want to establish our own arguments as truth.

But dry arguments are inconclusive. A *muni* whose conclusion does not differ from that of another *muni* is not recognized as a great philosopher because the word *muni* means someone who can come up with a new philosophy. Therefore, a *muni* tends to think his view of the world is correct.

However, the *Bhagavad-gita* describes the *sthita-dhi-muni,* one whose consciousness (*dhi*) is steady (*sthita*) and focused on Krsna. Such persons are true *munis,* or wise men.

There are examples of argumentative *munis* throughout the scriptures. In one incident, a *muni* approached Rupa and Sanatana Gosvami with the aim to defeat them through argument. Rupa and Sanatana simply signed their names to indicate that they had been defeated; they did not want to argue with this man and thus engage in *prajalpa.* When Jiva Gosvami saw their signatures, he could not believe that Rupa and Sanatana could have been defeated by a fool. In order to protect their reputation, Jiva Gosvami engaged in debate with the *muni* and defeated him. Jiva Gosvami was so merciless in his victory that the *muni* almost died from the shock. Jiva Gosvami was not speaking *prajalpa* because he used the arts of argument and debate to establish the *siddhanta* and to protect the reputation of Vaisnavas.

The usual reason that *Panditas* wandered around arguing and debating with others was to win. Few of them were actually interested in *siddhanta.* None were trying to establish the truth of our loving relationship with Krsna. In order to win, they are prepared to say almost anything. Politicians do this too. They often use slander as a tool. The moderator may blow the whistle and overrule the slander, but often the damage is already done.

Part of winning is attracting followers. Argument and debate are like sports. As soon as two people begin to argue, a crowd gathers. People are attracted to conflict; they want to know who has more power.

Sometimes when a crowd gathers, there is an attempt to break up the argument. When I was in school, it was common for boys at the end of the day to challenge one another to fight. If someone were challenged, the news would spread throughout the school. “Did you hear he will fight me? ” “Really? ” We used to hold our fights in a particular place, and as soon as school was over, the boys would gather to watch the fight, eager to declare a winner.

This was also going on during Lord Caitanya’s time in Navadvipa. The boys would argue philosophically, or claim that their teacher was better than everyone else’s. As soon as an argument began, everyone would gather to watch. Each student’s fellows would then defend their representative. Usually, however, the arguments were about logic, Sanskrit grammar, or anything they were studying at school.

Of course, we know that Nimai Pandita Himself engaged in these debates in His youth; it was one of His youthful pastimes. He was known as the best arguer. Nimai Pandita manifested His argumentative nature because He had a particular mission. Everyone was absorbed in argument, so He wanted to capture their minds by becoming the best debater. In this way, He gathered followers and then taught them devotion and to glorify the Lord.

Here is a story from *Caitanya-bhagavata.* This is not on the subject of *prajalpa,* but it will illustrate Lord Caitanya’s debating propensity.

The Vaisnavas were disturbed because they were so attracted to Nimai Pandita. They could not help being attracted to Him, because He was their worshipable Lord, although at that time they did not know that. They saw Nimai Pandita only as a beautiful young scholar and not as the Supreme Lord enjoying youthful pastimes. At this time there were few Vaisnavas in Navadvipa. The only Vaisnavas present were those who had descended to help the Lord in His pastimes. When Nimai was born, all these Vaisnavas saw auspicious signs indicating divinity on the Lord’s body, but Lord Caitanya had covered them with His *yoga-maya* potency so that they would no longer recognize Him.

When Nimai Pandita was a young scholar, the Vaisnavas would say, “This Nimai Pandita has so many attractive qualities. If only He would become a devotee I would give my life to Him. ” Instead, Nimai Pandita tried to engage them in debate. The devotees were constantly trying to run away from Him. They did not want to argue. But Nimai Pandita treated the devotees just like the nondevotees treated one another. He tried hard to provoke them, and He appeared to be displeased with them because they would not respond to His attempts to argue. Internally, however, He was pleased. He could see His devotees’ glories. When the materialists saw the Lord, they declared, “Glorious, glorious. ” They loved His behavior and considered Him a glorious heretic. They thought He would make a first-class materialist. “How can poverty and misfortune touch the parents who have this boy as their son? ” When women saw Nimai Pandita, they thought He was Kamadeva Himself. When blasphemers and offenders saw Him, they thought He was Yamaraja personified. When Vaisnavas saw the Lord, both joy and sadness entered their hearts. They said, “His body is glorious, but He does not taste the nectar of Krsna. What will He do with all His learning? He is still wasting time. ” The Vaisnavas, bewildered by *yoga-maya,* saw the Lord but didn’t see Him in truth. “O Nimai Pandita, what are You going to gain by spending all Your time studying? ” Whenever the Lord heard their words, He smiled and said, “I am fortunate to be instructed by you. ”

The *Caitanya-bhagavata* continues: “In this way the Lord was absorbed in tasting the nectar of learning. Even His servants didn’t recognize His identity. How could others recognize Him? From the four directions people come to Navadvipa and there were many scholars who were tasting the nectar of learning. Many Vaisnavas came from Catagram. They assembled in a secluded place on the bank of the Ganga. All these Vaisnavas were taking birth by the order of the Lord and all of them were Krsna’s devotees who have renounced the world. They met, spoke, and listened at that secluded place, and they talked about Krsna. The Vaisnavas would always take shelter of talking with one another about Krsna. ”

This is the symptom of a Vaisnava. Vaisnavas go to “secluded” places to discuss Krsna. At that time, the Vaisnavas secluded themselves because they were frustrated by the lack of interest the residents of Navadvipa had in Krsna consciousness. Although they had tried to preach Krsna’s glories, no one wanted to hear them. Such disinterest pained the Vaisnavas’ hearts. The only time they could find solace was when they gathered with other Vaisnavas and discussed *krsna-katha.*

“The great devotee Mukunda Datta was dear to all Vaisnavas. When Mukunda Datta was singing, those great Vaisnavas were melted. In the afternoon the great devotees would assemble in Advaita’s home when Mukunda sang songs about Krsna. Everyone there no longer knew about anything else. Some cried, some laughed, some danced, some rolled on the ground, some called out loudly and clapped their hands, and some of them grasped Mukunda’s feet.

In this way, there was great bliss and the Vaisnavas didn’t know anything about sadness or suffering. In His heart the Lord was very pleased with Mukunda. If by chance He saw him, the Lord would always stop Mukunda. The Lord would always question him about philosophy and logic. Mukunda Datta would answer and the Lord would say, ‘No, it is not right, ’ and they would argue. By the Lord’s power, Mukunda was a great *Pandita,* and he would debate with the Lord. The Lord recognized that His servant was using tricks of logic. The Lord always defeated him.

When He saw Srivasa and the other devotees, the Lord would challenge them with tricks of logic. Fearing His word jugglery, they ran away. These devotees were actually renounced. They tasted the nectar of Lord Krsna. They talked only about Krsna. They didn’t talk about any other topic. Seeing them, the Lord would question them with tricks of logic. When they could not answer, the Lord would make fun of them. If one of them saw Him at a distance, he would run away in fear of His tricky questions. The devotees liked to hear only about Lord Krsna, but the Lord asked them only about logic. He didn’t ask them about Krsna.

One day, the Lord, surrounded by students \[who were\] marked with the signs of pride and arrogance, walked in the street. In other words, when the Lord would walk all His fellow students would walk with Him. They all showed pride and arrogance, \[which\] were the ornaments on their bodies. So they were walking down the street and Mukunda was going to bathe in the Ganga. When Mukunda saw the Lord, he immediately hid and fled. Seeing that, the Lord asked Govinda Dasa, ‘Why did that boy run away when he saw Me? ’

‘O Pandita, I don’t know! Maybe he had some task to do? ’

‘I know why he ran away. He doesn’t want to talk about material things. This boy always studies the Vaisnava scriptures and I only talk about logic and astronomy. I never talk about Krsna. That’s why he is running away when he sees Me. ’ ”

The Lord was pretending to mock Mukunda, but in truth He was pleased with him. The Lord added, “O little boy, how many days will pass like this where you run to escape Me? ” Then He laughed. “After some days, you will see on Me the marks of a Vaisnava. I will become such a Vaisnava that Brahma and Siva will visit My door. O brother, please hear My words. I will become the best of all Vaisnavas. Those who run from Me now will one day sing My glories. I am just speaking these words. ”

“In this way Lord Visvambhara enjoyed His pastimes. He did not reveal the truth. Who has the power to understand Him? In this way, there were some devotees at Nadia. However, everyone else was after tasting the nectar of wealth and sex. Lord Caitanya adopted a mood that was prevalent by all materialists at that time. He would provoke the devotees.
